A slot goes back to the free list (3D: object state freed, +4 = 0). The cell becomes 0xFFFF (flag bit 0 cleared) and so do the footprint's 0xFFFE cells; the aux word is left. Passability.
**Next stage** (`Feature_ReplaceWithNextStage` `0x00423710`, (x, z, reclaimed)): type = reclaimed (or the slot's reclaim-animation flag) ? reclamate : dead. With a slot: remove, then place at the slot's +8 bytes and +0x20 attitude (taken after the removal, i.e. the same bytes, whatever they hold); without: remove and place by default. Owner nibble 10. A failed removal (indestructible) makes the placement fail too.
**Destroy** (`Feature_Destroy` `0x00423550`, (x, z, reclaimed)): the cell must exist (the original dereferences it; off-map is a crash). 0xFFFE → the feature's own cell. A 2D type with a die (reclaim) sequence starts it in a new slot (shadow cursor when there is one, flags: reclaim bits); a feature already holding a slot (burning) is left alone; otherwise the next stage at once.
**Ignite** (`Feature_Ignite` `0x004233A0`, (x, z, remote)): the cell's own type needs a burn sequence and no slot. Slot: burn cursors, flag bit 0, cell; fire countdown = RandMod(spark/2) + spark/2 (u16 spark time, battle RNG; spark/2 < 2 draws nothing); flag bit 3 = remote; sound "treeburn" at (x << 20, ?, z << 20); not remote: packet 0x0F {0xFE, x, z} broadcast to the first human's net id.
**Spread** (`Feature_SpreadFire` `0x004239C0`, (type, &slot cell)): every cell within 3 (z outer, x inner, except its own) holding an unslotted flammable feature catches fire when RandMod(100) < that feature's spread chance (ignite, not remote). Then a walk downwind from (x << 16, z << 16): five steps of 2 × the wind velocity (x, z words of GS+0x37ECC, 32-bit wrapping), each step landing in a new cell tests it likewise. Then the burning type's burn weapon (if any): area damage from a zeroed temporary shot (player slot 10) at the footprint centre (`0x0049A0C0` → `0x0049A120`).
**Weapon damage** (`Feature_ApplyWeaponDamage` `0x004244B0`, (cell, x, z, weapon)), called by area damage and by received packets: needs game option word GS+0x37F2F bit 3; nothing for special values or indestructible types. Network games (map info +0 = 3): a machine whose lobby flag (+0x97 bit 0) is clear sends 0x0F {weapon index, x, z} to the host and changes nothing. Not flammable, or the weapon's fire-starter byte (+0x10B) is 0: an unslotted feature adds the weapon's default damage to the aux word (u32 sum < type damage keeps it, else Destroy); flammable + fire starter on an unslotted feature: Ignite. Slotted 3D features add the damage to slot +0x26 if the slot's cell is (x, z), Destroy at ≥ type damage. Slotted 2D features: nothing. The host broadcasts 0x0F {0xFD, x, z} after a destruction.
**Packet 0x0F** (handled inside `0x00453D40`): byte 1 = 0xFD destroy, 0xFE ignite (remote), 0xFF destroy reclaimed, else weapon damage with that weapon index at Map_GetCell(x, z) as is.
**Feature stage** (`Feature_TickAnimsReproduceAndFire` `0x00424050`, step stage 7):
1. Animating types advance their type-wide cursors (def +0xCC, +0xD8).
2. Reproduction sweep (GS+0x14257, reset to 0 at map load): decrement; below 0 → width × height − 1 and nothing else. Otherwise the cell at that index: an unslotted feature reproduces when RandMod(100) < its reproduce value: a = reproduce area, target = (index % width + RandMod(a) − a/2, **index / height** + RandMod(a) − a/2) (the row divides by the map height, a quirk visible on non-square maps); placed (owner 10) if the target exists, holds no feature, and the **source** cell's unit word is 0.
3. The active list, each slot's next link read before handling it:
   - 3D: zero velocity → moves to the idle list. Else position += velocity; g = average of the cell's min and max corner height under it (`0x00485140`, −1 off the map); above the ground (g << 16 < y): below the sea level the velocity becomes (0, −0x2CCC, 0) (sinking), else vy −= gravity; on or below it: y = g << 16, velocity 0.
   - 2D, not burning: advance both cursors (shadow if flag bit 2); main sequence ended → next stage.
   - Burning: every game tick divisible by 3 a smoke puff from the current flame frame: two C-runtime `rand()` draws give x = footprint-centre x + rand·(w/2)/32768 − x offset + w/4 and y = ground + 2·(y offset − rand·(h/2)/32768) − 2·(h/4) (16-bit arithmetic on the integer parts). Then advance both cursors; burnt out → remove and place the burnt type (if any); else the fire countdown (if not remote) counts down and spreads at 0.
   - **Original crash:** a burnt-out feature that cannot be removed (indestructible) stays burning with no flame sequence, and its next smoke reads a null frame (confirmed by the harness; the engine records a fault).
**Wreck** (`Unit_SpawnWreckageFeature` `0x00486360`, (unit, wreck level, burn), from the kill packet): corpse = type +0x1BC; while level ≥ 2: stop at a special value, else one stage down the dead chain. At the unit's cell origin (+0x76/+0x78, must be on the map): placed with the unit's position, attitude and player slot as owner nibble. Terrain sample at the unit ≤ sea level: a placed 3D wreck sinks (vy −0x2CCC, vz 0; vx keeps the slot's stale value) unless the unit type has the "is a feature" flag, and burn is dropped. Burn adds the wreck fire (`0x472630` with 15, 900; presentation).
**Engine API:** `Battle::reset_features_and_effects()` (map load), `ta::place_tnt_features` (cells' owner nibble 10, void cells, then the TNT's features in row-major order, index < the TNT feature count), `Battle::step_features()`, `Battle::feature_world()` for `place_feature` / `remove_feature` / `replace_feature` / `kill_feature` / `ignite_feature` / `damage_feature` / `apply_feature_packet`. Projectiles reach features through the battle (feature damage, slot positions for area damage); `Battle::spawn_wreck` serves the kill packet. `BattleOutside` keeps only presentation and the 0x0F packet (`feature_packet`, `feature_smoke`, `feature_steam`, `feature_fire_sound`, `wreck_fire`). Reclaiming (`0x004237D0`, construction), the indestructible-feature metal stamp (`0x00422040`, economy), schema features (`Mission_SpawnMapFeatures`, importer) and saved games are built in their own sections.

**Features across the machines of a network game (2026-10-17).** Read in Ghidra (`0x4244B0`, `0x4233A0`, `0x423550`, `0x4237D0`, the resurrect step around `0x405141`, case 0x0F of `0x453D40`, `0x499EB0`, `0x4866D0`, `0x450030`, `0x451BC0`); the feature routines with both network roles run in `feature_harness` (smoke 2026-10-17: 12 scenarios / 1,663 operations identical, routing stubs logging sender and addressee); our machines compared with `net_play features` (below, and "Network and sync").
- **Who changes features.** A detonation deals damage only when the shot's player record is not a remote one (controller 3; see "Projectiles"), so only the machine that owns a shot (or a dying unit, whose death explosion takes the unit's player slot) runs its area damage and reaches `0x4244B0`. Other ways in: the burn weapon of a spreading fire, reclaim, resurrection, the 0x0F packet, saved games and map load, and the debug console (`0x4163A0` removes the feature under the cursor, `0x4163D0` places one, `0x416390` → `0x424840` removes every feature; local only). Units never knock features over in 3.1 (no other caller of destroy, remove or place).
- **One machine decides: the host.** Which machine that is comes from the *viewed* player's side record (GS+0x2A43 → record +0x27 → flags +0x97 bit 0), checked inside the damage routine every time. Another machine changes nothing and addresses {0x0F, weapon index, x, z} to the host only: sender = the first playing record's net id (`0x44FDB0`), addressee = the net id of the first record (slot order) with the host flag if that record has a controller, else −1 (`0x450030`; −1 sends nothing: once the host has gone, nobody decides features). The host runs the damage for its own shots and for each request alike. It announces {0xFD, x, z} to everybody only when that call destroyed the feature; an ignition announces itself ({0xFE, x, z}, `0x4233A0` with remote = 0). Damage below a feature's strength is never sent: the aux word (and the slot damage of 3D features) counts on the host only and stays 0 elsewhere.
- **Applying 0x0F** (every machine, nothing re-sent): 0xFD → destroy (x, z, 0); 0xFE → ignite (x, z, remote = 1): no announcement, slot flag bit 3 set, so its countdown never runs and it never spreads or fires its burn weapon (the countdown's RandMod(spark/2) is still drawn, from this machine's own battle generator); 0xFF → destroy as reclaimed; any other byte → the full damage routine with that weapon (on the host). A request reaching a non-host would be forwarded again; the original never addresses one there.
- **Fires spread on the host only** (only its fires are not remote), on the host's battle generator; every fire it starts is announced. Each machine then burns its copy for the burn sequence's length and places the burnt stage itself, a frame interval or so after the host.
- **Local on every machine, not announced:** the end of a burn (burnt stage), of a death or reclaim animation (next stage), falling and sinking 3D features, type-wide animations and the reproduction sweep (each machine's own battle generator: a type with `reproduce` would multiply differently on each machine; the maps tried have none — `net_play` prints them with NET_PLAY_VERBOSE).
- **Wrecks:** the kill packet carries the wreck level (byte 10 low nibble, from the owner's Killed script) and the death kind (high nibble; the wreck burns unless it is 7); every machine runs `0x486360` with its own copy of the unit: its cell origin, position and attitude. A remote copy trails its owner by a few world units, so a wreck can land one cell over on another machine; nothing corrects it later (no packet carries feature positions). A sinking wreck keeps the slot's stale x velocity, which depends on each machine's slot history.
- **Reclaim and resurrection:** the reclaiming machine destroys the feature as reclaimed and broadcasts {0xFF, x, z} from its unit owner's net id. Resurrection removes the wreck outright and broadcasts the same 0xFF: the other machines destroy it *as reclaimed* — reclaim animation, then the reclamate stage (a wreck with a reclamate stage leaves it on the other machines only).
- **Races the original allows** (kept by the engine): requests reach the host a frame interval late and hit whatever stands there then; the host may destroy a feature another machine still shows burning (its fire started later): destroy leaves a slot-holding 2D feature that has a death sequence alone, so that machine burns it out to its burnt stage instead; wreck cells (above); reproduction; console commands.
- **Engine:** `sim::damage_feature` / `apply_feature_packet` follow these rules (`FeatureEnvironment::network_role`); the battle sets the role in network games from `VictorySide::host` of the viewed player (`StartPlayer::host`, from the room's host flag); `net::NetBattle::feature_packet` addresses a request to the host's net id and announces the rest to everybody. **Bug fixed 2026-10-17:** the battle never set the role, so every machine applied the damage of its own shots and nobody announced destructions (each machine destroyed only what it shot itself); and requests went to everybody (with three or more machines every non-host machine forwarded each one again).

### Debris and effects — smoke-tested within feature_harness (same run): 451 script explodes (232 drawing the RNG), 516 effects stages, 160 direct world effects, up to 121 world effects and 10 debris pieces at once, 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/effects.cpp` (`sim/effects.h`). These are simulation state in the original and the engine models them exactly; only pixels and smoke particles are presentation.

**Why they matter:** script explode draws the battle RNG (6 draws per call, plus 8 per shattered quad), and the number of quads that can still be shattered is limited by the world-effect table, whose occupancy depends on explosion art lifetimes, shards in flight and debris landings. So the effects stage is part of the simulation.

**World effects** (`GS+0x1491F`, 300 × 0x54, count `GS+0x1491B`): +0 shard slot pointer, +4 art cursor, +0x10 glow cursor, +0x1C position, +0x28 carried velocity (half the unit's motion velocity at shattering), +0x34 own velocity, +0x40 spin xyz, +0x4C angles (i16), +0x52 flags (bit 0 explode where it lands).
- **Spawn** (`Fx_SpawnWorldEffect` `0x00420A30`, (pos, sequence or 0, glow index or −1, flags)): nothing when full; else position, art cursor (sequence 0: only the pointer cleared), glow cursor from generated explosion [index] (`GS+0x1AB8F`; 12, 15 and 15 frames of 2 steps, never looping), shard none; flags 0 above the sea (signed short compare) adds smoke (`0x472630` (pos, 7, 15)). The other fields keep the previous occupant's values.
- Spawners: projectile impacts and splashes (weapon art +0x78 or water/lava art +0x7C, glow 0, flags = in water), script bitmap explosions (glow 2), landing shards and debris (the general explosion, glow 0, flags 0) and splashes in deep water (h2oboom2 or lavasplash on lava maps, glow −1, flags 1).
**Script explode** (`UnitScript_ExplodePiece` `0x00481140`, thiscall on the script, (piece, flags)): unless flag 0x20 (bitmap only): RandMod(3000) × 3 (spin), velocity ((20 − RandMod(40)) << 14, RandMod(10) << 16, (20 − RandMod(40)) << 14), lifetime 900, shatter word = flag 1, debris flags: bit 0 = 0x10 (fire), bit 1 = 0x08 (smoke), bit 2 = 0x01 (shatter), bit 3 = 0x04 (fall), bit 4 = 0x02 for falling pieces / bit 5 = 0x02 for shards (explode on landing); the other bits of that word are stale stack and never read. Then `Debris_ThrowPiece`. Flags 0x100, 0x200, 0x400, 0x800, 0x1000, 0x2000: world effects explosion, explode2-5, nuke1 at the piece's world position (as the script's piece position), glow 2. A piece outside the object state corrupts memory in the original (engine: fault).
**Throw** (`Debris_ThrowPiece` `0x00421620`): the piece's visible bit is cleared (only that: no cache reset); shatter → `Fx_ShatterPiece`; else the first empty of the 100 debris slots (`0x00511DF0`) gets a block of the debris cache (vertices × 12 + 0x66 bytes): the spawn arguments, a copy of the object piece (as last synced) and its world position (piece transform + unit position). No empty slot or no block → nothing (the piece is still hidden).
**Debris cache** (`MemCache_AllocBlock` `0x00437A30` on `0x00511F80`, 100,000 bytes, reset at map load to one free block): blocks {owner pointer, size including the 8-byte header} tile the buffer. Allocation of need = payload + 8: if the cursor plus need passes the end, every block from the cursor to the end is evicted and the walk starts at the buffer start; blocks are walked (evicting) until their sizes cover need; a remainder of less than 9 bytes is absorbed (the cursor moves past it, to the start if at the end), otherwise it becomes a free block where the cursor stops. Evicting a block nulls its owner slot only if the slot still points at it. So old debris pieces vanish when the ring wraps.
**Debris step** (`Debris_Step` `0x004213B0`): lifetime runs out → gone. At or below the sea level → gone (explode-on-landing pieces splash on maps with water impacts). Above it: if vy + y reaches the ground average (cell min/max), it bounces (vy = −(vy >> 1), vx and vz halved) and, when the bounce is below 0x20000, lands: explode-on-landing pieces leave the general explosion; gone. Otherwise it moves, spins (turn x += spin[1], y += spin[2], z += spin[0]) and falling pieces gain gravity.
**Shatter** (`Fx_ShatterPiece` `0x00421700`): for each primitive of the piece's model with exactly 4 indices, not flat (bit 0 of its flags after texture binding) and not the selection primitive, while the effect count is below 300: a world effect at the piece's world position (the count is taken first: if no shard slot is free (`GS+0x1AB9F`, 300 × 0x34, free byte 0xFF) the effect keeps stale fields and the whole call stops). Shard: carried velocity = half the unit's motion velocity (units without motion keep stale values), own velocity x, z = (80 − RandMod(160)) << 9, y = the same + 30 × gravity (draw order x, z, y), spin = 800 − RandMod(1600) × 3, angles 0, no art or glow. Points: the quad's transformed vertices as stored, then reversed (8 points). Facing: n = normalize((p2 − p1) × (p0 − p1)) in x87 floats (points × float 0x37800080, ≈1/65536, stored as floats; each difference, product sum and quotient rounded to double then float, sqrt of ((x² + y²) + z²)); vx += RandMod(200) × (short)ftol(n.x × 512), vz −= RandMod(200) × (short)ftol(n.z × 512); the back four points move by −ftol(n × 65535) × shatter word; then all 8 are centred on their (truncated) mean. Its six faces take the source primitive's colour/texture.
**Effects stage** (`Fx_TickWorldEffectsAndDebris` `0x00420F30`, step stage 5): debris slots 0-99; then each world effect (the count is read every time round, so effects added now are stepped too): a shard moves by carried + own velocity, gravity on own vy, spins; with the ground average g: above the sea (or g ≥ sea level): reaching the ground (integer y ≤ g) puts it back, vy = −(vy / 2), and when the new vy is below 1.0 it lands (explosion if flagged) and its shard slot is freed; below the sea: splash if flagged (and the map has water impacts), freed. Then the art and glow cursors advance. Finally effects with no shard, no art and no glow are removed keeping order (the last entry keeps a stale copy).
**Dead code:** `0x00420E50` (explode every piece) and `0x00420920` have no callers.
**Engine API:** `Battle::step_effects()`, `Battle::effects_world()` for `explode_piece`, `spawn_world_effect`, `spawn_impact`; the unit script's explode goes there; projectile impacts call `spawn_impact`. `BattleOutside::explosion_smoke` is presentation.
**feature_harness:** `feature_harness <TotalA.exe> <game_dir> [scenarios] [operations] [first seed]` (32-bit; real unit models from the game files; one process per scenario). **Large verification run should cover:** many seeds and longer scenarios (fires that spread several times, reproduction on dense maps, the slot table running out at 2048, the world-effect table full at 300 while shattering, the debris cache wrapping), maps whose width differs from their height, network roles (host / other machine), units with motion velocities, every unit model (with texture-bound primitives through `bind_pose_primitives` instead of the file flags), and the battle wiring end to end (projectile area damage into features, kill packets into wrecks) against a captured game. The harness leaves one-thread TotalA.exe processes behind when the original crashes (Windows error reporting snapshots, access denied); they go away on their own.

### Construction and reclaim — smoke-tested in orders_harness: 24 scenarios × 40 steps (930 unit passes, 1,581 operations; real behaviour steps: BuildingBuild 43, MobileBuild 45, HelpBuild 31, RepairUnit 33, Reclaim 26, ReclaimUnit 123, Capture 185, BeCarried 23, Park 46, GetBuilt 29; direct calls: 33 build slices, 38 feature reclaims, 23 reclaim pulses, 51 placements with snapping, 20 transport packets, 22 build-count commands, 59 nano streams over 2,251 sweep steps), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/construction.cpp` (`sim/construction.h`), `engine/sim/src/nano_spray.cpp` (`sim/nano_spray.h`), `sim::attach_edge_goal` (`motion.cpp`), `sim::bucket_unlink/bucket_prepend` (`footprint.cpp`); wired into `sim::Battle` (`Battle::construction()`, `Battle::step_nano()`, `Battle::order_build_count`, `Battle::order_mobile_build`, `Battle::order`, `Battle::transport_attach`; structure placement now answers `RouteSystems::structure_fits` and the unfinished decay of `GetBuilt` is built in). Type data `sim::ConstructionTypeInfo` (`BattleUnitType::construction`, filled by `ta::to_battle_unit_type`); feature `metal`/`energy` (`FeatureTypeInfo`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/orders_harness`: the table entries of these kinds now keep their real handlers; creation (`0x485F50`, answers a live stand-in unit), the placement check `0x47DB70`, the edge and ring goal wrappers, the transport sender `0x48AAC0`, feature destroy `0x423550`, the GUI and network calls are logging stubs; rand() is patched onto a known state. Defs are a real 0x249-stride table (footprint, yard map, build options, model box, build distance, slope and water limits), map cells carry heights, height ranges, metal, features (with parts and special ids), occupants and marks, six feature types (one sometimes past the table count). Direct operations call the original build slice, decay, finalize, reclaim pulse and permission, yard placement and snapping, feature reclaim, ownership transfer, the transport packet and the build-count command; the nano stream test builds a stream object in the process, runs its setup and sweep and compares every particle and the rand() state.

**Unit fields used:** +0x104 build fraction left (float; 1.0 = nothing built, 0.0 = finished; NaN counts as 0 in every FCOMP test), +0xBA pending events (building sets 0x8000, the event `GetBuilt` waits for), +0xBC resource slot (+0 energy income, +0x18 metal income, +0x30 player), +0xB8 kills, +0x10F script flags (bit 0 build stance, set by the script through value 5), +0x86 carrier, +0x8A cargo head, +0x8E cargo / bucket link, +0xF9 transport piece, +0x82 spatial bucket. **Type fields:** +0x14A/+0x14C footprint, +0x14E yard map, +0x156 build options, +0x15E.. model box, +0x182 model average size (whole part = reclaim reach), +0x186/+0x18A energy/metal cost, +0x1BE/+0x1C0 max/min water depth, +0x1EA build time, +0x1FE worker time, +0x212 build distance, +0x228 max slope, +0x22C waterline, +0x22F movement (0 = structure); abilities +0x245 bit 10 reclaim, bit 12 capture; type flags +0x241 bit 6 builder, 9 air base, 11 flies, 18 activate when built, 24 is a feature.

**Build slice** (`Unit_ApplyBuildProgress` `0x41BA60`, stdcall builder, target, float work; rate = (float)(worker time / 30) per step):
- Nothing for a finished target (0.0, NaN) or zero work. Work ≥ 0 marks the target's pending event 0x8000.
- next = left − work / build time (53-bit doubles); stored as 1.0 if not below 1, 0.0 if ≤ 0 (or NaN), else (float)next. Slice = (float)(left − stored); energy = (float)(energy cost × slice), metal likewise; hp change = ftol(left × max hp) − ftol(stored × max hp) (max as unsigned).
- Building: the builder's slot books (energy, metal) through the economy's use-if-not-stalled (`0x4011C0`; the stall check is the economy's). Only if paid: hp += change, capped at max (unsigned compare), fraction stored, unit flag 0x2000, result 1.
- Unbuilding (work < 0): the target's own slot gets the metal back (income − (−metal) × −0.5 / −0.7 for an AI player on difficulty 0 / 1, else income + metal), fraction stored, hp = max(0, hp + change), flag 0x2000; at 1.0 the target takes 30000 damage of kind 9 from itself. Result 0.
- Afterwards a target with fraction 0.0 is finalized.
**Decay** (`0x41BCD0`): work = −(float)((build time × steps) / energy cost) (int product, double quotient) on the unit itself; `GetBuilt` calls it every 11 steps when nobody builds.
**Finalize** (`Unit_FinalizeBuiltUnit` `0x41B8D0`): needs a live builder whose type has build options and a live unit. Fraction 0.0, flag 0x2000. If the unit's owner plays: a structure opens the builder menu (user interface), a carried unit is dropped (packet 0x0A, piece 0xFF, layer 1). Activate-when-built types activate; the builder's build menu refreshes if it is the shown one; "is a feature" types die (kind 7, dying flag); a playing owner sends packet 0x12 {0x12, unit id, builder id} to the builder owner's net id; the orders panel refreshes if either unit is selected.

**Order behaviours** (results as in "Unit orders"; "stance" = `0x438700`: build stance set → 1, else wait = mask | 4 → 2; "start building" = `0x438590`: `StartBuilding(rel)` started by index (argc 1, not run), notice `0x456290`, order flag 0x400000; "stop building" = `0x4385F0`: with 0x400000, `StopBuilding` started, notice, flag cleared; rel = (u16)(atan2_scaled(unit − target in x, z) − heading); "reach" = ftol(hypot(dx, dz)) whole part − ftol(hypot(own footprint) × 8.0) + ftol(hypot(target footprint) × −8.0) compared with the build distance; "spray" = nano stream from the nano piece (`QueryNanoPiece`) to the target box, "reverse spray" = from a box to the nano piece):
- **BuildingBuild** (12, factories; param0 type, param1 count; target = the unit on the pad). Event 2: if a unit is on the pad, its unbuilt metal ftol((1 − left) × cost) as an unsigned value is refunded to the factory's slot (AI 50%/70%), it is finalized and takes 30000 damage kind 9 from the factory; activation and building bits off; 5. Event 8: speech "construction stopped", count − 1, 0. Step 0: unlink the target; not a structure → 7; count > 0 → activate, 1; else deactivate, 5. Step 1: stance (2). Step 2: `QueryBuildInfo` piece (starting −1) → world position into the order; site cell (footprint centred, rounded) **into the event argument**; placement check (`0x47DB70`, mode = the factory's layer bits) fails → deadline 15, wait |= 2, 2; create unfinished (owner, type, position, layer 1); none → speech, deadline 300, wait |= 2, 2; else speech, transport attach (cargo at the piece, layer 1), copy standing move/fire bits, enqueue `GetBuilt` (queued, target = factory), building bit on, 1. Step 3: no target → 7; a build slice, spray (box from the feet); finished → 1 else deadline 1, wait |= 0xA, 2. Step 4: speech, building off, finalize, unlink, count − 1, 0 (the next unit starts at once).
- **MobileBuild** (25; param0 type at the order position, param2 waits). Event 8: speech, display, 8; event 2: display, 5. Step 0: site cell **into the event argument**, order position = cell centre, param2 = 0, edge goal (site cell, type footprint), wait = 0xE0, 1. Step 1: event 0x40 → reach check (the distance **into the event argument**), too far → speech, 8; site cell into the event argument; placement check (mode 1) fails → speech on the first wait, after 10 waits speech and 8, else param2 + 1, deadline 30, 2; unrelease weapons, snap the position (`0x47DDC0`), create unfinished; none → speech, deadline 300, 2; else speech, display, enqueue `GetBuilt`, start building, 1. Step 2: stance (10). Step 3: slice, spray (box with the model's lowest point), decloak hold-off tick + 300; finished → 1, else deadline 1, wait |= 0xA, 2. Step 4: speech "building complete", 5. (Step 3 without a target reads a null unit: original crash.)
- **HelpBuild** (23): event 2 → 5; no target → 8. Step 0: needs motion and the builder type flag; ring goal around the target (outer = build distance + h, inner = h, h = ftol(sqrt(fx·fx + fz + fz) × 16) / 2 — the z footprint is not squared); wait = 0xE8, 1. Step 1: event 0x40 → 8; target finished → 5; unrelease, start building, display, 1. Step 2: stance (10). Step 3: slice, spray, hold-off + 300; unfinished → deadline 1, wait |= 0xA, 2; else 1. Step 4: speech, wait |= 2, 5.
- **RepairUnit** (35): no target → speech, 5. Leash param2 ≠ 0: ftol(hypot of the whole x/z offsets from the order's +0x2E/+0x30 origin) ≥ param2 → 5. Target not on the ground layer → speech, 5. Step 0: needs motion, builder flag and a finished target, else 7; announce "repairing", 1. Step 1: event 0x40 → 8; out of reach → edge goal at the target's cells, deadline 30 + RandMod(30) (battle RNG), wait |= 0xE8, 2; else unrelease, start building, 1. Step 2: stance (8). Step 3: whole → 1; target moving (flags & 0xC) → stop building, deadline 15, 0; else hold-off + 150, a heal step (`0x41BD10`), spray on success, deadline 1, wait |= 8, 2. Step 4: speech, 5.
- **Reclaim** (32, features at the order position): no feature (`0x421DA0`: part cells refer back by row/column offsets with bounds-checked cells; outside the map there is a null read) → speech, 8; type not reclaimable (flag 0x80) → 8. Step 0: needs motion and the reclaim ability; edge goal at the feature, wait = 0xE0, 1. Step 1: event 0x40 → 8; param0 = ftol(15 − (energy + metal) × −0.5); the aim point's height draws **RandMod(feature height)** (battle RNG) before the ground sample; start building, 1. Step 2: stance (0). Step 3: speech 11, then step 4: deadline 2, param0 − 2; ≤ 0 → 1; else hold-off + 300 and, while param0 > 15, two reverse sprays from the feature box {cell << 20, ground << 16, ..., + footprint << 20, + height << 16}; 2. Step 5: the yield, 5.
- **ReclaimUnit** (33): no target or event & 0x10008 → 5. Step 0: needs motion and the ability (else speech, 7); `can reclaim` (`0x489960`: worker can reclaim, target not airborne, target cannot capture) → announce, unrelease, 1; else two speeches, 8. Step 1: event 0x20 → 1; else edge goal at the target, wait |= 0x100E8, deadline 15, param0 = pulse (`0x438650`: max(1, ftol((u32)(worker time × ((kills + 5) / 5) × max hp × 15) / (max(metal cost, 10.0f) × 300.0f)))), param1 = 0, 2. Step 2: event 0x40 → 9; start building, 1. Step 3: stance (0x10008). Step 4: speech 11, 1. Step 5: within (build distance + reclaim reach)² (hi32 squares) and still reclaimable: every 15 steps damage param0 of kind 5 (the kill refunds metal to the reclaimer, see "Damage"); hold-off + 900, reverse spray, deadline 2, param1 + 2, 2; else deadline 15, stop building, 0.
- **Capture** (14): no target or event & 0x10008 → speech, 8. Step 0: needs motion and the capture ability; a target that can capture → speech, 8; unfinished target → speech, 8; announce; work = min(ftol(E × 30 × 0.0005f − M × 30 × (−1/140 as float) − (−150)), 1800); then (u32)((hp + max) × work) / (2 max) (unsigned; max 0 divides by zero), then ((kills / 5 + 10) × that × 10) / 100; unrelease, edge goal, wait = 0x100E8, 1. Step 1: event 0x40 → 8; in reach → start building, 1; else 0 (re-runs step 0, which sleeps on its wait). Step 2: stance (0x10008). Step 3: speech 11, 1. Step 4: target with motion and moving → stop building, deadline 30, 0; work done < needed → reverse spray, hold-off + 900, + 2, deadline 2, 2; else 1. Step 5: transfer to the worker's owner, speech 16, 5.
- **BeCarried** (11): no carrier → 5; step 0 unrelease, 1; step 1 deadline 10, 2.
- **Park** (28, what a finished factory unit gets when its builder has no queued moves): step 0: no motion → 7; aircraft: order position = unit position, the order becomes VTOL_MOVE (flags = default with 0x600 kept), 0; else f = footprint x (+3 unless min water depth < 0), corner = (x >> 20 logical, z >> 20) − (4f, 3f) as words **into the event argument**, edge goal of size (8f, 6f), wait = 0xE0, 1. Step 1: event 0x20 or another order follows → 5; else deadline 30, 0.
- Event-slot writers: BuildingBuild (step 2), MobileBuild (steps 0 and 1), Park (step 0), as the stale-word note in "Unit orders" says.
- **The factory pad:** creation → transport attach (the new unit becomes cargo at the build piece; carried units move with the piece, see "Motion") → `BeCarried` prepended by the attach, `GetBuilt` enqueued in front (its default flags have 0x20) → the factory script raises the yard and the build stance → build slices → finalize drops the cargo (it rejoins its bucket) → `GetBuilt` hands over the builder's queued moves/patrols or enqueues `Park`, whose edge goal drives it off the pad.

**Transport attach** (packet 0x0A {0x0A, cargo id, transport id, piece, layer}; `0x48AAC0` checks, sends to the first playing player's net id and applies; `Unit_ApplyTransportPacket` `0x48AB70`): cargo alive, not a structure, carrying nothing; transport (if any) alive, not the cargo, not carried. The cargo leaves its carrier's list (walk) or its spatial bucket (list walk; a unit missing there is a crash). Piece stored. Detach: link cleared, flag 0x20000 cleared, carrier none, pushed on its bucket's head. Attach: carrier set, pushed on the carrier's cargo head, flag 0x20000 = (piece == 0xFF). Motion layer bits = the packet's. A playing owner's cargo on a transport that is not an air base: `0x4384A0` (partial order clear, then BECARRIED on the carrier prepended). A selected cargo that is no longer selectable loses flag 0x10 (user interface refresh).
**Ownership transfer** (`0x488570`, unit, new owner): nothing for the same owner, a dead or dying unit. Victory notice first. Old owner playing: new owner inactive → nothing; watched (3) → transfer hold-off 150, deselect, packet 0x14 (24 bytes: id, new owner's net id (or −1 without a controller), ftol(fraction), hp, pitch|heading << 16, bank, the three stockpiles when slot 0's weapon flag bit 1 is set) to the old owner's net id, then a 30000 kind-4 wipe. Otherwise a playing new owner gets a finished copy (created for the record index byte +0x146, layer = the old layer bits): standing bits cleared, hp, fraction, attitude and the stockpiles of slots with flag bit 1 copied, the old unit wiped (kind 4), then the old activate bits set and the others cleared through the activation routine.
**Placement of structures** (`Map_CanPlaceStructureYardMap` `0x47D2E0` as `0x47DB70` calls it: no exclude, no view context): cell x and z ≥ 1, x + fx < width, z + fz < height. Per yard byte (row by row): 0x08 → min of the cells' min heights and max of their max heights; 0x10 → max of max heights (water band); 0x01 → fail on a yard-marked cell (flag 2); 0x06 → fail on any ground occupant; 0x20 → fail on a blocking feature (type flag 0x40; ids past the table and the special ids 0xFFFB-0xFFFD block; parts take their feature's flag); 0x40 → fail on an indestructible feature (0x200); 0x80 → needs a geothermal feature (0x20) somewhere. Then: height = sea − waterline when no cell sampled the slope, else the min height after a slope test (max − min > the type's max slope fails); fail if the water band > height, if min < sea − max water depth, or if max(max, band) > sea − min water depth. On success the height is kept for `0x47D820`, which recomputes it for snapping (`0x47DDC0`: x, z = footprint centre of the rounded cell, y = height << 16; mobile types unchanged). Metal under the footprint is summed into a global (used by the interface).
**Feature reclaim** (`0x4237D0`, unit, position): cell = C division of x and z by 2²⁰; feature type of the cell (parts by index arithmetic; their feature's id is not checked against the table size); none → 0; a slot-holding 2D feature (busy burning or dying) → 0; the unit's slot income gets the feature's energy, then its metal (AI 50%/70%); destroyed as reclaimed (`0x423550`); provider type 3 sends {0x0F, 0xFF, x, z} to the owner's net id; 1.
**Build queue command** (`Unit_AdjustBuildCount` `0x43B0B0`, kind (BuildingBuild for factories, MobileBuild for mobile builders, BuildWeapon for stockpiles), unit, type, delta): delta < 1: repeatedly the last order of that kind and type shrinks by −delta (when bigger) or is removed (flag 0x10000 unless it is the primary head; destroyed) with delta += its count; delta > 0: the list's last order of that kind and type grows, else a queued order (param0 type, param1 delta) is enqueued.

**Nano spray and the C runtime rand()** (`Fx_SpawnNanoBeamEffect` `0x4720D0` point → box, `0x472200` box → point; stream object vtable `0x4FD5B8`): they **do not touch the battle RNG and nothing happens at draw time**, but they draw the C runtime `rand()` on the simulation thread:
- Spawn (inside the order behaviour, i.e. the unit pass): an object from the shared effect-node pool (`0x51E610`; none or the pool blocked flag `0x51E608` → nothing), setup (`0x473B50`): deadline = tick + 1, both boxes narrowed to their middle band (lo = (d × 4)/11 + min, hi = (d × 7)/11 + min, kept as min and size), then one emission. Then it joins effect list 6; with more than 400 entries the oldest is deleted first.
- Emission (`0x473D50`): five particles; each draws **six rand() values** (source x, y, z, target x, y, z: min + (int64)(rand × size) / 0x8000), whether kept or not. Steps = the high half of ((int64)(int32)ftol(√((dx² + dy²) + dz²)) << 16) / 0x40000 — four world units per step; 0 drops the particle. Velocity = d / steps (int), colour = 0xA1 + i % 7, flags word 0x100, expiry = tick + steps. Then next emission = tick + 1.
- The effect sweep (stage 9, `0x471EB0`, lists 0..9 in order): a stream without particles is deleted; otherwise each particle moves (colour nibble cycles 1..7) and goes once tick > expiry, then the stream emits again when next ≤ deadline (signed) and next ≤ tick (unsigned). So a normal stream draws 30 values when it starts and 30 more in the next step's sweep, and lives until its slowest particle arrives.
- Engine: `sim::NanoSprayState` in `Battle::nano`, `spawn_nano_stream` / `step_nano_streams` on `Battle::crt_rand`; `Battle::step_nano()` is the list-6 part of stage 9. The shared pool (1000 objects, `0x51E610`, also the effect lists') is modelled: a stream spawn needs a free object (`Battle::effect_pool_free`, 2026-10-15); lists 0..5 sweep first (see "Effect particle lists").
- Drawing (`0x472F90`): each particle is one pixel of its colour byte at (x − scroll + 128, z − (y >> 1) − scroll + 32), gated by the viewer's sight; list 6 is drawn after the ground units and tall features, before the projectiles.

**Units under construction on screen** (presentation): the original keeps a two-plane sprite (pixels + depth = height + 50, +75 for diggers) for them. After compositing, `0x458DD0` remaps it by v = ftol(left × 255.0f) and two pulsing colours from palette 0xA0..0xAF (c1 from (id ^ 5) + tick × 33 / 30, c2 from (id ^ 9) + tick × 57 / 30: low nibble rising, then falling when bit 4 is set). A band b at depth [b − 4, b): v > 235: b = (v − 235) × 255 / 20, only the band shows (c1); v > 200: b = (v − 200) × 255 / 35, the same; v > 115: b = (115 − v) × 255 / 85 − 1 as a byte, above the band nothing, below it c1, the band c2; v > 30: b = (30 − v) × 255 / 85 − 1, above c1, below the texture, band c2; else b = v × 255 / 30, the texture with a c1 band. Then `0x458FA0` outlines every primitive of every visible piece in c2: the polygon (first point repeated) scan converted without clipping, only the two end pixels of each row, depth tested (`0x4C0820`, `0x4C0A90`). Structures keep this look; a mobile unit under construction outside a carrier is drawn over again with its textures (so it looks finished), while a unit on a factory pad is drawn into the factory's sprite with the look. Engine: `render::rasterize_model_sprite_depth`, `render::apply_nanoframe`, `render::outline_polygon`, `BattleScene::nano`, `BattleScene::game_tick`; carried units are drawn right after their carrier on their own (the depth-tested composite is approximated).

RepairPatrol, Resurrect and the aircraft variants are built ("Aircraft and transports"), as is the effect-node pool (above); packet 0x14's sender side on the receiving machine is the network work's. The resource stall test and the per-player resource state belong to the economy (`use_resources_if_not_stalled`; the incomes the construction routines write are `UnitTickState::metal_make` / `energy_make`, an additive field for the economy work to reconcile with its resource slot).
**Faults the engine flags instead of crashing** (`Missions::fault`): unit types past the type table, a mobile build step without its unit, feature parts pointing outside the map, feature types past the table where the original reads garbage, capture of a type with 0 hit points (division by zero), a transported unit missing from its carrier's list, transport attach without a motion object, nano particle velocity overflow (in `NanoSprayState::fault`).
**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (created where missing; name + plate comment; smoke-verified): `Mission_BuildingBuild`, `Mission_MobileBuild`, `Mission_HelpBuild`, `Mission_RepairUnit`, `Mission_ReclaimFeature`, `Mission_ReclaimUnit`, `Mission_Capture`, `Mission_BeCarried`, `Mission_Park`, `0x41BA60` `Unit_ApplyBuildProgress`, `0x41BCD0` `Unit_ApplyUnfinishedDecay`, `0x41B8D0` `Unit_FinalizeBuiltUnit`, `0x438650` `Unit_ReclaimPulseDamage`, `0x489960` `Unit_CanReclaimTarget`, `0x47D2E0` `Map_CanPlaceStructureYardMap`, `0x47D820` `Map_GetStructurePlaceHeight`, `0x47DDC0` `Unit_SnapPositionToFootprint`, `0x4237D0` `Feature_ApplyReclaimYield`, `0x421DA0` `Map_GetFeatureAtWorldPos`, `0x488570` `Unit_TransferOwnership`, `0x48AB70` `Unit_ApplyTransportPacket`, `0x4384A0` `Unit_EnqueueBeCarried`, `0x43B0B0` `Unit_AdjustBuildCount`, `0x438700` `MissionOrder_WaitBuildStance`, `0x438590` `Unit_StartBuildingScript`, `0x4385F0` `Unit_StopBuildingScript`, `0x473B50` `NanoStream_Setup`, `0x473D50` `NanoStream_Emit`, `0x472EB0` `NanoStream_Advance`, `0x4739B0` `NanoParticle_Advance`, `0x473B30` `NanoParticle_IsExpired`. Read, not named (not run in a harness): `0x4720D0`, `0x472200` (stream spawn wrappers), `0x471EB0` (sweep), `0x48AAC0` (transport sender), `0x438AD0` (edge goal wrapper), `0x458DD0`, `0x458D30`, `0x458FA0`, `0x4C0820`, `0x4C0A90` (construction look), `0x450010` (net id of a player with a controller).
**Large run should cover:** `orders_harness <exe> <game> N 40 <first>` in shards (thousands of scenarios); factory chains reaching steps 3-4 repeatedly (the script's build stance), cancels mid-build (event 2) with AI owners on every difficulty, decay to 1.0 (self-kill), finishing with activate-when-built and is-feature types, capture of watched owners (packet 0x14), reclaim of 2D features busy burning, structures on the map edge and in water (every yard bit), nano streams with very long or zero-length flights and the 400-entry cap, transport packets with piece 0xFF and air-base carriers, and the battle end to end against a captured game (rand() stream with nano streams).

### Draw-time side effects on the simulation
**Line of sight depends on the viewed player** (GS+0x2A43; see "Line of sight and radar"): the radar, sonar and jam marks every player's target lists read are computed from the viewed player's side only; without fog every viewer's visibility uses the viewed player's explored bit; only the viewed player's dead units leave lingering sight (into its counts); lingering sight expires once per drawn frame, so its length depends on the frame rate. None of this depends on the camera. The engine takes the viewed player as an input (`BattleSettings::local_view`) and the frame loop calls `Battle::expire_lingering_sight()`.
**Unit script EMIT_SFX acts only on units the viewed player sees** (`UnitScript_EmitSfx` `0x480EB0`): the visibility test (`0x465AC0` for the viewed player's record) gates both the pose sync (`Unit_SyncObjectStateAnimTree`, base angles the simulation can later observe) and the particle spawns (listener lists stepped in stage 9 with CRT `rand()` draws). So which units the viewed player sees changes simulation-visible state. Not camera dependent. The engine's `BattleOutside::emit_sfx` hook receives every request; an implementation must apply the same test (`Battle::unit_seen_by(local_view, unit)`).
**Drawing advances the simulation's C-runtime random sequence.** `Gfx_DrawProjectiles` (`0x0049BE60`) calls the C runtime `rand()` while drawing lightning shots (render type 7). It runs on the same thread as the simulation. The simulation also draws from `rand()`, for wind, meteors, features, explosion frames, victory polling and battle start (see SIM_INVENTORY "Randomness"). So in the original, that sequence depends on which lightning shots were on screen and on how many frames were drawn. This is a view- and frame-rate-dependent input, like the heap-address cases. The engine keeps drawing free of side effects; whether to feed these draws in is open.
**Drawing advances the simulation's C-runtime random sequence.** `Gfx_DrawProjectiles` (`0x0049BE60`) calls the C runtime `rand()` while drawing lightning shots (render type 7). It runs on the same thread as the simulation. The simulation also draws from `rand()`, for wind, meteors, features, explosion frames, victory polling and battle start (see SIM_INVENTORY "Randomness"). So in the original, that sequence depends on which lightning shots were on screen and on how many frames were drawn. This is a view- and frame-rate-dependent input, like the heap-address cases. Built 2026-10-20: the renderer draws the jitter from a copy of the battle's state and returns the copy's state after the frame's lightning (`BattleScene::crt_rand_after`); the client gives it back to the battle after drawing, so its sequence follows the original's (tools without drawing, and the whole-game replays, which take the between-frame draws from the capture, are unchanged). **Nano lathe particles are not a draw-time effect:** their streams draw `rand()` in the unit pass and the stage-9 sweep and are simulated exactly (see "Construction and reclaim").
**The interface's drawing writes two saved fields** (see "Client"): the top bar moves the viewed player's record display timer (+0xF8, saved as DisplayTimer) on 30 steps when it is behind; the queued-order view (Shift held) writes a seen target's whole x and z into the order (+0x32/+0x34, the behaviour's second word) and sets order flag 0x200000. Both only while drawing (the second only with Shift held). The client keeps its own copies (the simulation's records stay as the simulation leaves them); whether saves must carry the drawn values is open.
**Replaying a captured game** (see "Whole-game replay"): the capture records the end of every frame's steps (so `end_frame()` and lingering-sight expiry run at the same points), the viewed player and the line-of-sight options per frame, and the `rand()` state at every step; the draws made between frames (drawing, CD audio, interface) are taken from it as an input at the first step of the next frame. Debris emitters run in `end_frame()` (`draw_side_effects`). EMIT_SFX follows `local_view`.

### Target acquisition — smoke-tested: 24 scenarios × 60 steps, 1,341 operations (217 table refreshes, 67 enemy listings, 153 best-target searches, 84 fire-at-will, 72 attack-order issues, 147 resolutions over all 16 intent values, 199 retargets, 238 weapon-target passes with 97 slot changes, 2,248 interceptor searches), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/targeting.cpp` (`sim/targeting.h`: `enemies_in_radius`, `find_best_target`, `fire_at_will_target`, `issue_attack_order`, `resolve_mission`, `retarget_attack`, `weapon_target_pass`, `refresh_unit_table`, `create_player_targeting`), type data `sim::TargetingTypeInfo` (`BattleUnitType::targeting`, filled by `ta::to_targeting_type`). Wired into `sim::Battle`: the mission hooks (fire-at-will, attack orders, resolution, enemy lists) and the combat retarget hook call it; `Battle::start_players()` builds the per-player state, `Battle::players_stage()` runs stage 6 of the step. Checked by `tools/orig_harness/targeting_harness`: inside the suspended exe a battle like `orders_harness` (three players, random real unit types with real scripts and object states, the original mission table) plus unit tables, controllers, a sight grid, map cells with features (and parts of bigger features) and random category masks, weapon flags, transport and water data. The original routines below run for real on random operations; stubs: the line-of-sight test `0x465AC0` (answers from a queue), AI rescoring `0x409730`, the under-attack notice `0x47F850`, the interceptor search `0x49D120` (answers from a queue: a fake shot), behaviours (only reached by destroying an order that waits for it), network notices, frees, script requests. After every operation it compares results, the stub log, the battle RNG, unit flags, pending events, activate flags, weapon slots, both order lists field by field, target links, script threads, the unit tables (sight and radar lists, facility flag, stamp) and the controllers (cursor, AI hold tick). Mutation check: replacing the swap-remove of the candidate list by an ordered erase is caught at once.

**Per-player state.** Every player record that is not watched (active with controller 3) gets at battle start (`0x464700`):
- a **controller** (0x3D bytes at player +0x74, constructor `0x408CB0`): +0 the player record, +5 the AI's 30-step countdown, +0xD an AI hold tick, +0x11.. ten AI behaviour objects, +0x39 the weapon-target cursor (a unit address, 0 at start). The AI parts are built (see "Computer player").
- a **unit table** (0x10D bytes, `0x40B320`/`0x409160`) stored in the array at `0x005119C0` **under the player's state index** (+0x146), made for the player record with that index. Callers look tables up by **player slot** (the refresh by the slot being visited, the enemy listing by the unit's owner slot +0xFF); in normal games slot and state index agree. Fields used here: +9/+0xD sight list (unit pointers), +0x19/+0x1D radar list, +0x79 targeting-facility flag, +0xED refresh stamp. The rest is AI bookkeeping (own-unit counts by type +0x81, builder airbases +0x25, builders +0x75, weighted centre +0x35, weights +0xA1, build scores +0x65/+0x8D).

**Players' stage** (`0x464F80`, stage 6 of the step; engine `Battle::players_stage`): first the route search's turn (`0x40EB70`, hook). Then for each slot 0..9 that is active, has controller 1/2/3 and state ≠ 10:
1. if the player has a controller (`0x408C40`): an AI player (controller 2) runs its AI work (countdown → `0x408830` every 30 steps, due behaviour objects; hook) and then the weapon-target pass with AI = 1; any other player the pass with AI = 0;
2. the unit table refresh if due (`0x40B2C0`);
3. sight for each live unit of the slot's range (`0x4827B0`, hook);
4. the rest (view sight `0x466DC0`, watch-mode/victory checks every 30 steps, economy `0x401360`, sensors `0x467440`; one hook `player_stage_rest`).
After the loop a game-mode check (hook `players_stage_end`).

**Unit table refresh** (`0x40B2C0`): nothing without a table or while stamp + 30 > tick (unsigned). Rebuild (`0x40AA40`): the sight, radar and airbase lists are emptied and the facility flag cleared; then every table entry from 1 to the table end (GS+0x1435B) that is alive and not dying:
- owner's state not allied in the table player's alliance bytes (+0x108[state]; a player's own state byte is normally set): appended to the **sight list** when the line-of-sight test (`0x465AC0`(table's player record, unit), hook `unit_visible`) passes and unit flag 0x8000 is clear; independently appended to the **radar list** when unit flag 0x100 is set;
- else, when the owner's state is the table player's own and the build fraction is 0.0 (NaN counts as 0): AI statistics (hook `ai_own_unit`), and the **facility flag** is set by a type with flag 0x400 (`istargetingupgrade`) whose activate flag bit 0 is on.
Then stamp = tick, and **RandMod(30)** is drawn; 0 → the AI rescoring of unit types (`0x409730`, hook `ai_rescore`).

**Enemy listing** (`0x40AD80`, stdcall (table, &pos, radius, unused, &vector)): r² = radius × radius (32-bit). Every sight-list unit with hi32(dx²) + hi32(dz²) ≤ r² (signed; dx, dz the 16.16 offsets) that is alive and not dying is appended. Only when the facility flag is set **and nothing was found**, the radar list is scanned the same way. Missing table: the original crashes (engine fault).

**Best target** (`0x40B7B0`, stdcall (unit, slot, in-weapon-range)):
- AI = owner active with controller 2.
- Candidates: enemy listing for the owner slot around the unit, radius = the slot weapon's range (+0xDC, via `0x49ADF0`) in weapon-range mode, else the type's sight distance (i16 def+0x202).
- Up to **50 picks** while candidates remain: k = **RandMod(count)**, the candidate is taken out by moving the last one into its place (`0x4800C0`). It is skipped unless: alive and not dying; type flag 0x8000 (`shootme`) or AI owner or game option GS+0x37F30 bit 2; in range of the slot (`0x49ABB0`) unless the unit's type is a kamikaze (type flag 0x10000000); in sight mode not in the type's no-chase category (def+0x23D); not (paralyzer weapon and target already paralysed, activate flag 0x10).
- Score = **RandMod(hi32(dx²) + hi32(dz²))** (unit − target; RandMod of < 2 draws nothing). Candidates in the slot's bad-target category (def+0x231 + 4·slot) compete separately; strictly lower scores win (the first of equals stays).
- Result: the best normal candidate, else the best bad-category one, else none. Category masks are 512-bit sets indexed by the target's **type index** (+0xA6).

**Fire at will** (`0x43B700`): fire order bits (0x300000) = 0x200000 → best target (slot 0, sight mode), else none.

**Attack order** (`0x43B1F0`, stdcall (unit, target, forced) → 0/1): 0 when unit = target; unforced, 0 when the move order bits (0xC0000) or the fire order bits (0x300000) are 0 (hold position / hold fire). Kind = resolution (intent 3); 0 → 0. Unforced with move order "maneuver" (0x40000): a move order (resolution intent 2 with no target at the unit position) is built and prepended, then the attack (target, param2 = u16 maneuver leash def+0x214, +0x2E/+0x30 = the unit's whole x and z) is prepended in front of it. Otherwise only the attack (params 0) is prepended. Prepending = in front of the head of the list the order's own flags name, inheriting its 0x4000. Returns 1.

**Mission resolution** (`0x43F0E0`, stdcall (&out byte, intent, unit, target, pos) → out): with a target that lacks the alive flag → 0. Enemy/ally = the unit owner's alliance byte for the target owner's state (0 = enemy). "Air" kinds are chosen when the unit's type can fly (0x800). Abilities are def+0x245 (0x10 attack, 0x20 guard, 0x40 patrol, 0x80 move, 0x100 load, 0x200 repair, 0x400 reclaim, 0x800 resurrect, 0x1000 capture, 0x4000 special attack, 0x80000 not transportable).
- **2 move:** no move ability → 0; no motion object → QMove; with a target: enemy + capture → Capture; enemy + reclaim → (VTOL_)ReclaimUnit; ally: can-repair (below) and build fraction ≠ 0 → (VTOL_)HelpBuild, can-repair and hp (u32 of the signed word) < max damage → (VTOL_)RepairUnit; flier + ally air base (type 0x200) → VTOL_Landing; can-pick-up → (VTOL_/Ground_)Pickup; guard + ally → (VTOL_)Follow/Follow_Ground; else (VTOL_)Move/Move_Ground.
- **3 attack:** no attack ability → 0. With the has-weapons unit flag (bit 31), w0 = slot 0's weapon: no enemy → 0 for an anti-air w0, Suppress for non-fliers, AirStrike if the definition's first weapon is dropped, else AirToGround. Enemy: target not airborne and w0 anti-air → 0; if target top (model height word + whole y) < sea: 0 unless w0 or an enabled slot 1 is a water weapon; else a hovercraft (type 0x1000) with a water w0 or water slot 1 → 0. Fliers: dropped first weapon vs ground target → AirStrike, non-dropped vs flier → AirToAir, vs ground → AirToGround (AirToGroundHover with type flag 0x8000000), else 0. Others: motion object → Attack_Chase, unit flag 0x20000000 → Attack_NoMove. Then (also without weapons): kamikaze type → Attack_Kamikaze, else 0.
- **1 default:** attack + enemy → intent 3. With game option GS+0x37EFA = 1: reclaim + enemy → (VTOL_)ReclaimUnit; ally: can-repair and unfinished → HelpBuild, can-repair → RepairUnit; flier + ally air base → VTOL_Landing; can-pick-up → Pickup; guard + ally → Follow. Otherwise: reclaim + enemy → intent 12; with a target: can-repair and unfinished → intent 8; a finished selectable (flag 0x20) unit of the local player (GS+0x2A42) with no transfer hold-off and no carrier (or one with flag 0x40000000) → 0. Then both: resurrect + position + wreck there → Resurrect; reclaim + position + wreck → (VTOL_)Reclaim; move ability and motion object → (VTOL_)Move, else 0.
- **4** special attack ability → AttackSpecial. **5** load + flier + air-base target → VTOL_Landing; load → (VTOL_/Ground_)Unload. **6** target and can-pick-up → Pickup. **7** guard + ally → Follow. **8** can-repair → finished target: RepairUnit, else HelpBuild. **9** patrol: no motion → QPatrol; without repair → (VTOL_)Patrol, with → (VTOL_)RepairPatrol. **10** Stop. **11** Teleport. **12** reclaim ability required; the cell lookup reads the position before checking it (none → original crash); resurrect + wreck → Resurrect; wreck → Reclaim; target → ReclaimUnit; else 0. **13** capture + target of another owner → Capture. **14** build options (def+0x156 ≠ 0) and motion → (VTOL_)MobileBuild. Other values → 0.
- **Wreck at a position:** the owner player's sight grid cell ((whole x) >> 5, (whole z − (whole y >> 1)) >> 5, unsigned against the player's +0x80/+0x84) must carry the local view's bit (GS+0x2A43) in the grid at GS+0x14273 (hook `position_explored`), and the map cell (x >> 20, z >> 20; `0x4815A0`) must hold a feature whose type flag byte +0xFE has bit 7 (hook `feature_flags`): a direct feature index < 0xFFFB counts only below the feature count; 0xFFFE refers back (low byte rows, high byte columns) to the feature's own cell, **whose index is not checked against the count** (engine fault).
- **Can repair** (`0x4899B0`): repair ability, target hp ≠ max damage (as ints), target not airborne, and (non-flier, amphibious (0x200000) or sea ≤ target top) and (flier or sea − max water depth (i16 def+0x1BE) ≤ target top).
- **Can pick up** (`0x489A90`): target transportable; load ability; fewer units in the cargo list naming this unit as carrier than the capacity (def+0x22B); target has a motion object; transport size (def+0x22A) ≥ target footprint x (def+0x14A); target not airborne; a ground transport only takes units with negative min water depth (def+0x1C0); target y + model height (int) > sea << 16; target finished.

**Retargeting** (`0x406F80`, stdcall (attacker, victim, amount) — amount unused; called by the damage packet for kinds ≠ 11):
1. Every link pointing at the victim with an owner hears event 0x10 (`0x4897B0` → order vtable `0x4FD2C8` slot 0 = `0x438870`: events |= event), so orders targeting it can wake.
2. An attacker whose entry is empty (type index 0) counts as none.
3. A victim able to capture whose owner is an active AI: **RandMod(300)**, controller +0xD = tick + 30 + r, then the partial order clear (orders without 0x4 go).
4. With an attacker, an owner playing (1|2), a type with weapons or kamikaze (0x10010000), a finished victim (0.0, NaN equal) and a non-allied attacker: if the primary head is missing or has flag 0x20000, the attacker's type is in neither the no-chase nor slot 0's bad-target category and slot 0 reaches it → attack order (unforced). Without an order and with any fire order bits: every enabled released slot that reaches the attacker and is not command-fire is pointed at it (`0x48A060`), unless its current unit target is in reach and not in its bad-target category.
5. Unless the head order has flag 0x80: if the previous attacker slot (+0xF4, not yet updated) ≠ the owner slot or the previous damage kind (+0xF5) = 1 → the under-attack notice (`0x47F850`(victim, 2, 0); presentation only).

**Weapon-target pass** (`0x4089A0`, thiscall controller, AI flag): count = GS+0x37EE6 (u16, the unit limit) / 30; count + 1 visits. Each visit moves the cursor: 0 or the last entry of the range → the first entry, else the next entry. A unit with a type, finished (0.0), the has-weapons flag and fire-at-will is served slot by slot (enabled and released slots only; dropped weapons skipped; command-fire weapons only for the AI):
- a current unit target (`0x48A190`) is kept if its owner is not allied with the controller's player, it is not in the slot's bad-target category, and not (paralyzer weapon and target paralysed);
- otherwise a non-interceptor slot gets the best target (weapon-range mode) via `0x48A060`, or is cleared (`0x48A0F0`) when there is none;
- an interceptor slot asks for a shot to chase (`0x49D120`): none → cleared; else the slot aims at the shot's position as a ground point (`0x48A0A0`: whole x and z, a z of −0x8000 becomes −0x7FFF; pending events &= 0x83FF).

**Engine notes:** the line-of-sight test, the explored-grid bit, feature flag bytes, the AI and the interceptor search are reached through `TargetingSystems` (Battle forwards the first four to `BattleOutside`, the interceptor search to `find_intercept_target`). `QuietBattleOutside` treats every unit as visible and the map as explored. Faults the engine flags instead of crashing (`Targeting::fault`): enemy lists of players without a table, category bits past the mask, slot weapons missing, weapon target ids outside the table, reclaim resolution without a position, a 0xFFFE feature reference past the feature table, an AI player without a controller.
**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name, prototype, plate comment; smoke-verified): `0x0040AD80` `UnitTable_ListEnemiesInRadius`, `0x0040B7B0` `Unit_FindBestTarget`, `0x0040AA40` `UnitTable_Rebuild`, `0x0040B2C0` `UnitTable_RefreshIfDue`, `0x0043B700` `Unit_FireAtWillTarget`, `0x0043B1F0` `Unit_IssueAttackOrder`, `0x0043F0E0` `Unit_ResolveMissionKind`, `0x00406F80` `Unit_RetargetAttack`, `0x004089A0` `Controller_WeaponTargetPass`, `0x004897B0` `Unit_NotifyReferrers`, `0x00438870` `MissionOrder_ReceiveEvent` (created), `0x004899B0` `Unit_CanRepairTarget`, `0x00489A90` `Unit_CanPickUp`, `0x004815A0` `Map_GetCellAtPos`, `0x004800C0` `PtrVector_RemoveSwapLast`, `0x0048A0A0` `Weapon_SetTargetPoint`, `0x00438BE0` `Unit_GetHeadOrderFlags`, `0x0049ADF0` `Weapon_GetSlotRange`. Read but not tested on their own (named only in this file): `0x00464F80` players' stage, `0x00408C40` controller step, `0x00408CB0` controller constructor, `0x0040B320`/`0x00409160` unit table creation, `0x00465AC0` line-of-sight test, `0x00409730` AI rescoring.
**Large run to do:** `targeting_harness <exe> <game> N 60 <first>` in shards (e.g. 3,000 scenarios); check every intent reaches every kind (aircraft, hover, water weapons, transports, wrecks), the maneuver path, AI capturers, radar-only listing with a facility, interceptor slots, ties of best-target scores, paralyzer skips, and unit limits near 0 and 65535.

**Not reverse engineered here:** the line-of-sight system (now built: see "Line of sight and radar"), the AI (controller behaviours, rescoring, own-unit statistics), the route search turn, and the movement behaviours that the resolved kinds lead to (Attack_Chase etc.).
**Debris drawing also feeds `rand()` (frame-rate dependent, not camera dependent).** `Fx_DrawWorldEffects` (`0x00420B00`) handles every debris slot before any culling, once per drawn frame (`Fx_EmitAndDrawDebrisSlot` `0x00421550`): smoke pieces (debris flag bit 1) spawn a smoke particle emitter (`0x00472810`), fire pieces (bit 0) a jittered one (`0x00472AB0`), which draws `rand()` four times on the spot. The particle emitters live in the effect listener lists (10 lists, at most 400 entries each, oldest dropped) and are stepped in simulation stage 9 (`Fx_SweepAndPruneListenerSlots` `0x00471EB0`), where their ticks draw `rand()` again (`0x00474DF0`, `0x00475340`). So the C-runtime random sequence the simulation reads (wind, meteors, burning-feature smoke) depends on how many frames were drawn while flaming or smoking debris existed, and on the particle lists' fill. Debris spawning and stepping themselves do not depend on the view; they use the battle RNG only at spawning (script explode). The battle RNG is never touched by drawing. The engine leaves the particles to the presentation and keeps `rand()` an input stream (`Battle::crt_rand`).

### Line of sight and radar — smoke-tested: 36 scenarios (12 × 30 and 24 × 40 steps; 24 ran to the end, 12 ended at an original crash the engine predicted, all confirmed), 4,605 operations (1,349 sight updates after moves, 281 new-unit stamps, 290 deaths with 56 lingering stamps, 366 frame expiries, 515 visibility tests, 281 explored tests, 455 sensor passes, 225 rebuilds, 201 option toggles, 132 map shares, 187 viewed-player changes; 783 reads in front of the ring table), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/sight.cpp` (`sim/sight.h`), wired into `sim::Battle`; importer `ta_compat/src/sight_tables.cpp` (`ta/sight_tables.h`: `gamedata\los.tdf` rings, `anims\vismasks.gaf` masks); type data `sim::SightType` (`BattleUnitType::sight`, filled by `ta::to_battle_unit_type`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/los_harness` (below).

**Options** (`wMapFlags` GS+0x14281; battle init copies them from the game settings: skirmish `nLosType`/`nMapping`/`nLineOfSight`, multiplayer the host's side-data option byte; the console toggles flip one bit and rebuild):
- bit 0 **reveal** ("mapping"): the explored map starts empty and units explore it; off: a rebuild fills it with every bit.
- bit 1 **fog** ("line of sight"): live sight counts; off ("permanent"): every count is 1 after a rebuild and "seen" means "explored".
- bit 2 **true sight** (LOS type): ring rays over the terrain; off: circular mask stamps.
- bit 3: presentation only (the viewed player's fog edges are current); cleared whenever the viewed player's sight changes.

**Data.** The map is in **blocks of 2 × 2 cells** (32 world units): width/height = cells / 2.
- **Explored map** GS+0x14273: a u16 per block, bit = player **state index** (+0x146); `cells·cells/2` bytes, zeroed by the map load. **This is `Battle::explored`, the same data the route search reads.**
- **Sight counts** per player record (+0x7C pointer, +0x80 width, +0x84 height, +0x88 size = (w·h + 7) & ~7): a byte per block, how many sight shapes cover it (wraps). Allocated zeroed for every record with a controller (`0x464700`, battle start).
- **Unit fields:** +0x7A/+0x7C the stamped shape's block (shorts), +0xF8 the stamped frame (circular) or **eye height** (true sight); 0 means "not stamped" for true sight. Creation zeroes them.
- **Height band** GS+0x1428F (`{ptr, width, height, count}`, built by `Map_BuildDerivedLayers` `0x482C20` at map load): per block a {top, bottom} byte pair, `count = (w·h + 7) & ~7` entries initialised {0, 255}. For each cell column x (outer) and row z (inner), height h, `t = 16z − h/2`, `bz = t >> 5`: when bz ≥ 0, `v = ((32·bz + 31)·h) / (t + 31)`; v is folded (top = max, bottom = min) into the two blocks remembered from the previous row, then into block (bz, (x−1)>>1) (if inside) and block (bz, x>>1) (if different and inside), which become the remembered ones; then h itself is folded into the remembered blocks (whether or not bz ≥ 0, so they persist across rows with bz < 0). Finally every entry: top' = max((bottom + 2·top)/3, sea), bottom' = max((top + 2·bottom)/3, sea) (unsigned).
- **Masks** (circular): sequence `vismask` of `anims\vismasks.gaf`, 10 frames (11 × 11 … 29 × 29 blocks); covered = frame byte ≠ the frame's transparency index; the frame's x/y offsets centre it. Frame for a sight distance s: `s/32 − 5` (C division), below 0 → 0, past the last → the last.
- **Rings** (true sight): `gamedata\los.tdf`, `[TABLEINFO] numtables` (9), `[TABLE<n>] numlines`, `line<i> = count, a, b, …` (tokens split on ", ", atoi, 16-bit). A line gives four rays (slices i, n+i, 2n+i, 3n+i): (a, −b), (b, a), (−a, b), (−b, −a). The ring for sight s: r = max(s/32, 0); r < count − 1 ? r : count − 1; the table read is entry **r − 1** — so the last table (radius 9) is never used, and **s < 32 reads the 16 bytes in front of the table** (heap contents: an "entry" whose slice count comes from the allocator's header). The engine stands in an empty ring there (`SightTables::ring_before_first`, configurable) and counts the read (`heap_reads`).
- **Lingering slots** GS+0x1427B, count GS+0x14277: 20 slots of 36 bytes (record pointer, sight, eye, frame, position, expiry tick, block). Slots past the count keep old contents and are reused as they are.

**A sight update** (`Los_UpdateUnitFromPosition` `0x4827B0` → `Los_UpdateUnitVisibility` `0x4825B0`): query = owner record (+0x96), sight (i16 def+0x202), eye = byte 2 of the model max y (def+0x170), position with y raised to (sea+1)·65536.
- True sight: bx = (whole x) >> 5; eye' = clamp(eye + whole y, 0, 255); bz = (whole z − eye'/2) >> 5. If the stored block differs or |stored frame − eye'| > 5: with fog and a nonzero stored frame, decrement the old shape; store the block; outside the band → frame 0, stop (nothing stamped); else frame = eye', with fog increment, with reveal explore.
- Circular: f = mask frame; bx = x/2²¹ − x offset, bz = z/2²¹ − (whole y)/64 − y offset (C divisions); if block or frame changed: with fog decrement the old shape, store, increment; without, store; with reveal explore.
- **First stamp** (creation `0x482AC0`, the rebuild, lingering slots; only with fog): frame = 0, then true sight → a normal update; circular → place the mask, increment, **explore regardless of the reveal option**.

**Counting a shape** (`0x482270` +1 / `0x481D50` −1; first, if the owner record's state is the viewed player, the view is marked dirty):
- True sight, block inside the band: the block itself; then each slice of the ring in order: best = 0/−1 (as a fraction n/d = 0/−1), k = 1, 2, … per point; point = block + offset (16-bit); inside the band: c = d·k, a = (top − eye)·n; **if a > c** (computed as `a − c ≠ 0 && c ≤ a`) the point counts, and if (bottom − eye)·n > c the occluder becomes (n, d) = (k, bottom − eye). Points outside the band only advance k.
- Circular: the mask rectangle at the stored block, clipped to the map (blocks), every covered byte.
- **Exploring** (`0x481930`) walks the same shapes setting the owner's **state** bit (circular: the frame from the sight distance again); a change for the viewed player marks the view dirty.

**Rebuild** (`0x4816A0`, battle start with clear, option toggles, watch mode): clear → explored = 0 (reveal) or all bits; every record active with controller 1–3 and state ≠ 10: counts = fog ? 0 : 1; with fog every unit table entry with a type (alive or not) gets a first stamp; then presentation. Lingering slots are not touched (their later expiry still decrements).

**Deaths** (the kill packet handler `0x4866D0`): a unit **of the viewed player** first gets a lingering slot (`0x482910`, with fog and < 20 slots): the **viewed player's** record, sight, eye, position (y raised), expiry = tick + 60, then a first stamp (true sight compares with the slot's stale block). Then with fog the unit's own shape is decremented (`0x482090`). **Expiry** (`0x482130`) runs **once per drawn frame after the frame's steps** (also with 0 steps): every slot with expiry < tick is decremented, then the expired ones are removed keeping order. (It reads an uninitialised flag when nothing expired; the compaction then removes nothing, so it has no effect.)

**Visibility test** (`Los_IsUnitVisibleToPlayer` `0x465AC0`, viewer record): own unit → yes; activate bit 2 (cloaked) → no; p = (min x + x, max y + y, min z + z): model top under the sea (p.y < sea·65536) without the sonar mark 0x200 → no. Then the corners p, p + size x, then z + size z and y − size y, then x − size x; a corner counts when its block ((whole x) >> 5, (whole z − (whole y >> 1)) >> 5, unsigned against the viewer's count map size) has a nonzero count (fog) or, **without fog, the explored bit of the viewed player** (GS+0x2A43, not the viewer's). The table refresh, hot-unit list, HUD and EMIT_SFX use it.
**Explored test** (`0x408090`, targeting's wreck check): the same block test, explored bit of the viewed player.

**Sensor pass** (`Unit_UpdateSensorRadarAndCloak` `0x467440`), only with 2+ players (GS+0x2A3C); L = the viewed player:
1. Every live unit: flag 0x1000 cleared; if owned by L, or its owner is allied with L's state and shares radar (owner side data +0x97 bit 6), or L is active and watching (L's side data +0x9B bit 6): flags |= 0x300, else flags &= ~0x700.
2. Every live, not dying, activated unit of L's range with radar or sonar: radar² = (radar + 2·whole y)², sonar² = sonar²; every unit in the bucket walk of radius max(radar, sonar) (`Map_ForEachUnitInRadius` `0x47E890`: buckets (centre ± r) >> 23 clamped, units with hi32(dx²) + hi32(dz²) ≤ hi32(r²)) that has a type, is not L's and is not stealth (type flag bit 8): with d = hi32(dx²) + hi32(dz²), y ≤ sea·65536 and d < sonar² → 0x200; model top ≥ sea·65536 and d < radar² → 0x100.
3. Every live, activated unit whose owner **slot** differs from L's **state**: radar jammer → every unit in the walk loses 0x100 and gets 0x400 (no other checks: own and allied units too); sonar jammer → loses 0x200, gets 0x400.
4. Every live unit whose owner is active with controller 1|2 and whose type cloaks for energy (can-do bit 13): if its owner's unit table sight list holds a live, not dying unit within mincloakdistance (hi32 squares ≤ r·r; `0x40B0D0`; a missing table crashes): cloak hold-off tick (+0xB0) = tick + 90, flags |= 0x1000.
5. Every live unit without 0x100 and not cloaked: if L sees its block (counts, or L's explored bit without fog) → 0x100.
**The cloak itself** is applied in the economy pass (`0x401360`, ECONOMY's): for each unit of a player that is not an active watched one: cloaked = wants cloak (flags bit 11) and no 0x1000 and tick ≥ hold-off tick and the player's energy ≥ ftol(cost) (moving (flags & 0xC): cloakcostmoving, else cloakcost; paid: energy −= cost, unit energy use += cost); then `Unit_SetActivateState(unit, 4, cloaked)`. The engine leaves this to `sim/economy.h`.

**Map sharing** (`0x485420`, packet 0x16 / share dialog): explored bit `from` copied to `to` on the first (cells·cells)/4 words.

**Step placement.** Players' stage (`0x464F80`), per playing slot: AI and weapon-target pass, unit table refresh, **sight update of every live unit of the slot**, (minimap redraw for the viewed slot), every 30 steps victory checks and the economy, then **for the viewed slot: the sensor pass**. Moves call the sight update too (`0x43DA59`, `0x48AAA0`). Creation stamps (`0x482AC0`), the kill handler lingers/removes. After the frame's steps: lingering expiry.

**View dependence (all reproduced as inputs):** the original's simulation reads the player this machine shows (GS+0x2A43, `BattleSettings::local_view`):
- radar, sonar and jam marks are computed **from the viewed player's side only** and stored in the units; every player's unit table reads 0x100 (radar lists: AI targeting with a targeting facility) and the sonar mark gates under-water visibility for every viewer;
- without fog, visibility for every viewer uses the **viewed player's explored bit**;
- only the viewed player's own dead units leave lingering sight, into the viewed player's counts;
- lingering sight expires per drawn frame, not per step (frame-rate dependent: with several steps per frame it lasts longer);
- unit script EMIT_SFX acts only when the viewed player sees the unit (see "Draw-time side effects").
Engine: `Battle::expire_lingering_sight()` is called by the frame loop; `settings.local_view` is the viewed player.

**Engine API:** `Battle::sight_tables` (`ta::load_sight_tables`), `settings.fog_of_war` / `explore` / `true_sight` / `player_sight` (shares radar, watching), `types[k].sight`; order: map heights, sea level and player slots → `init_sight()` → `init_routes()` → units → `start_players()` → `rebuild_sight(true)`; per step `players_stage()` (sight and sensors built in), per frame `expire_lingering_sight()`; queries `unit_seen_by(viewer, unit)`, `point_seen_by(viewer, pos)`, `sight` and `explored` for drawing; `sight_faults()`. Until `init_sight()` runs, sight goes to `BattleOutside` as before (tools that do not model it). Faults (the engine skips the access): counts of a record without a count map (the original writes through a null pointer), a missing unit table in the sensor pass, a missing mask table.

**los_harness** (`los_harness <TotalA.exe> <game_dir> [scenarios] [steps] [first seed]`): inside the suspended exe, a random map (16–96 cells, hills, sea), the real masks (the GAF relocated in place) and rings (the vector of vectors at 0x51E6A0, with an empty entry in front), four players (random activity, controllers incl. 0 rarely, alliances, radar sharing, watching, state 10 sometimes), 32 units of 9 random synthetic types (sight 0–800 incl. < 32 and multiples of 32, radars, sonars, jammers, cloak distances, stealth, model boxes), units on and off the map, in the air and under water, random flags; spatial buckets and unit-table sight lists written on both sides. Original routines called: the band build, rebuild, sight update, first stamp, lingering, removal, expiry, visibility, explored test, sensor pass, sharing; stubs: allocator (bump heap), minimap redraws. Compared after every operation: option flags, the explored map, every count map, every unit's block/frame/flags/hold-off, all 20 lingering slots and their count; results of the tests. A predicted crash ends the scenario and is confirmed by running the original.

**Rendering** (battle view; `render/src/battle_view.cpp`, `battle_view --player N [--true-los]`):
- Units drawn: the viewed player's own and those it sees (`Unit_CollectHotUnitIds` `0x48BAE0`, view-culled). Cloaked units (activate bit 2) are drawn blended through the ALP table (`0x459200`; also in crosshair mode).
- Shots are drawn only where the viewed player sees the block of their position (`0x49BE60`).
- Features with `nodrawundergray` are drawn when the cell's owner nibble (cell flags bits 3–6) is the viewed player or a footprint corner block is seen (`0x4658E0`: (16x, 16z − height/2) and (16(x+fx), 16(z+fz) − height/2)).
- **Fog** last (`Map_DrawLosFogTiles` `0x4848E0` with the edge grid of `0x4843C0`): a grid of 32 × 32-pixel cells, `view_w/32 + (2 or 3)` × `view_h/32 + (2 or 3)` (3 when the size is not a multiple of 32), starting at block x0 = scroll/32 − (scroll % 32 < 16 ? 1 : 0) (likewise z0), drawn at origin + (scroll % 32 > 15 ? 16 : −16) − scroll % 32 + 32·cell. Each cell has two corner masks (bit 1 top left = block (x0+gx, z0+gz), 2 top right, 4 bottom left, 8 bottom right): unexplored (no viewed-player explored bit) and, with fog, unseen (count 0). Corners outside the map copy the inside ones (top row, row h−2, column 0, column w−2 as in the original). Draw per cell, row by row: unexplored mask 15 → fill with HUD colour 0; else unseen mask 15 → the rectangle remapped through the **grey table**, partial → frame (mask − 1) of `Gray1..4` (`anims\fog.gaf`, sequence (gx + gz + (scroll x + 16)/32 + (scroll y + 16)/32) & 3) remapping the frame under its non-key pixels; then a partial unexplored mask → frame (mask − 1) of `Black1..4` blitted. The "DitheredFog" option draws checkerboards instead (built, see "Console commands").
- **Grey table** (`Palette_BuildGrayscaleRemap` `0x4BAD30`): for each palette colour, g = (r + g + b)/3; the colours sorted by r + g + b (selection sort swapping whenever a later one is smaller); nearest by squared distance to (g, g, g) among those whose sum lies within ±40 of 3g, first of equals; new draw op `BlitRemap` (CPU and GPU, table bound like the light table). Sample frames: CPU = GPU (D3D12), 0 differing pixels.
- **Radar dots exist only on the minimap** (`0x466DC0`): a unit is shown when no LOS/mapping option is on, or UI option 0x200, or its flags have 0x300, or it is the viewed player's; selected units show radar/sonar/jam circles. The main view has none. The minimap is not drawn by our battle view (not built).
