**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name, prototype, plate comment; smoke-verified): `0x004827B0` `Los_UpdateUnitFromPosition`, `0x004825B0` `Los_UpdateUnitVisibility`, `0x00482AC0` `Los_StampNewUnit`, `0x00482910` `Los_AddLingeringSight`, `0x00482090` `Los_RemoveUnitSight`, `0x00481D50` `Los_DecSightCounts`, `0x00482270` `Los_IncSightCounts`, `0x00481930` `Los_RevealExplored`, `0x004816A0` `Los_RebuildAll`, `0x00482130` `Los_ExpireLingeringSight`, `0x00465AC0` `Los_IsUnitVisibleToPlayer`, `0x00408090` `Fog_IsExploredAtWorldPos`, `0x00467440` `Unit_UpdateSensorRadarAndCloak`, `0x00467840` `Sensor_MarkRadarOrSonar`, `0x00467960` `Sensor_ApplyRadarJam`, `0x00467980` `Sensor_ApplySonarJam` (the last three created), `0x0047E890` `Map_ForEachUnitInRadius`, `0x0040B0D0` `UnitTable_HasVisibleEnemyInRange`, `0x00485420` `Fog_ShareExploredBits`, `0x00482C20` `Map_BuildDerivedLayers`. Read, implemented, not differential-tested (named only here): `0x00433130` ring table load, `0x00433380` / `0x004336F0` table and line loaders, `0x00464700` count map allocation, `0x004843C0` fog edge grid, `0x004848E0` fog drawing, `0x004658E0` footprint visibility, `0x004BAD30` grey table, `0x00429870` (masks and fog GAF names), `0x0048BAE0` hot-unit list, `0x00466DC0` minimap composite (read only).

**Not built / open:** (the cloak application is built in the economy) the minimap and its radar dots (presentation), `Net_DeserializeUnitStateBits` (`0x48B6BF`, network) and the pose packet's stamp; the heap contents in front of the ring table (configurable stand-in); the sight shape of radius-0 units with true sight on real heaps.
**Large run should cover:** many seeds with the real unit types (sight, radar, sonar, jammers on real maps: Moon Quartet and a water map), long runs of moves so counts wrap and lingering slots fill to 20 (and compaction with several expiries in one frame), option toggles mid-battle, viewed-player changes with lingering slots alive, all 10 mask frames and 8 rings at map edges (negative blocks), eye heights near 0 and 255, states differing from slots, watched players, and sensor passes with many jammers overlapping.

### Battle step (integration) — `sim::Battle`
`engine/sim/src/battle.cpp` holds the state of one game:
- the unit table (index = id, entry 0 unused), per-unit states, 11 player records (ten slots plus "no owner"), unit types (entry 0 = the blank type of empty slots);
- the projectile table, the map grid, feature types and settings;
- feature animation slots, world effects, shards, debris and its cache, the animation sequences, the C-runtime `rand()` stream.

It wires the systems built so far to each other. Everything else goes through one `BattleOutside` interface:
- (historical: the systems once listed here as not built are all built now; see "Simulation completeness" for what the interface still carries);
- the network, presentation and user interface.

**`Game_RunSimulationStep` (`0x00495490`), per pending step:**
1. Increment the game tick (GS+0x38A47).
2. Network input `0x00453D40` (when called for a network game).
3. Unit pass `0x0048AD30`.
4. Projectile update `0x0049B720`.
5. Effects and debris `0x00420F30`.
6. Players `0x00464F80`.
7. Features `0x00424050`.
8. Animation cursors `0x00415B30`, wind `0x00490C40`, meteors `0x00437DE0`, camera `0x0041CA10`.
9. `0x00471EB0`, then `0x00466580`.
10. Network flush, for a network game with `0x00506DBC` set.

Between stages it adds GetTickCount deltas to profiling counters, which are not part of the simulation. After the frame's steps it runs `0x00428BD0`, `0x00428BE0`, `0x00428BF0`, `0x00463EF0` and `0x00482130`; see SIM_INVENTORY.

**Whole step: `Battle::step()`** (game flow, 2026-10-14) runs every stage in this order; `Battle::end_frame()` is the per-frame tail (lingering sight; optionally the draw-time debris emitters). Exact stage contents (checked against the disassembly of `0x495490`):
1. tick + 1; 2. network game: `BattleOutside::network_input()` (`0x453D40`); 3-4. unit pass, projectiles; 5. effects and debris; 6. players' stage (route search first, victory turn inside); 7. features;
8. texture animation cursors (`Gaf_AdvanceAllFrameCursors` `0x415B30`: model texture animations only, presentation), wind (`0x490C40`), **meteors** (`0x437DE0`, below), **camera** (`0x41CA10`: follow/ease of the view is presentation, but its shake decay `0x41C6F0` draws `rand()` twice per step while shaking, below);
9. the effect sweep (`0x471EB0`) over lists 0-9 in order: lists 0-5, the nano streams (list 6), lists 7-9 (see "Effect particle lists"); then `Minimap_TickBlinkDirtyFlag` (`0x466580`: an 8-step blink counter of the minimap, presentation);
10. only in a network game **and** with the frame-timing flag `0x506DBC` set (`BattleSettings::net_frame_timing`): automatic sharing (`0x457D30`) and `Net_FlushPendingFrames` (`BattleOutside::network_flush()`).

**Meteor showers** (`Meteor_TickShower` `0x437DE0`, engine `sim/sky.h` `step_meteors`, `Battle::step_meteor_shower`). Configuration (`0x437D60`, from the map info's schema keys MeteorWeapon/Radius/Density/Duration/Interval; an empty weapon name loads `gamedata\meteor.tdf` [Default] and disables showers; a schema with a zero value also takes the default file but stays enabled): spawn interval = ftol(30.0f / density), duration = ftol(duration × 30.0f), gap = ftol(interval × 30.0f), radius as given. At map load (`0x437CD0`) the shower is inactive and the **first schedule is at the spawn interval** (not the gap); the weapon is looked up by name and must have the meteor flag (0x20), else weapon slot 0. Each step: when the schedule step is not after the tick (unsigned), a shower starts **whether or not showers are enabled**: active, end = tick + duration, next schedule = end + gap, next meteor = tick; four `rand()` draws: origin z = r×map height/0x8000, origin x = r×map width/0x8000 (cells), aim z = origin z + r×10/0x8000 − 15, aim x = origin x + r×30/0x8000 − 15; disabled → inactive again. While active and the next meteor is due: next = tick + interval; velocity x/z = ((origin − aim) << 20)/90, y = −15.0; two draws: distance = (r×radius/0x8000) << 16, heading = r×2; position = aim cell << 20 minus rounded sin/cos(heading, distance), y = 90 × 15.0; an ownerless shot announced to the network (`0x49DF10`). Shower over when its end is not after the tick.
**Camera shake** (`sim/sky.h`): an impact adds (`0x41C640`, unless the player turned shaking off, UI flag GS+0x37F2F bit 4): when not shaking the accumulators clear; magnitude = (new + previous)/2; steps left = magnitude; accumulators += x, y; shaking when magnitude > 0. Each step (`0x41C6F0`): while shaking with steps left: amplitudes = accumulator × steps left / magnitude (32-bit product), two `rand()` draws, view moves by r × amplitude / 0x8000 − amplitude/2, steps left − 1; with none left the shake ends. The view position is presentation; the draws are not. All zero at map load (`0x41C2B0`).
**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/flow_harness` (meteor step with the launch stubbed, shake add and decay on the original): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 4,800 operations / 263 meteors / 1,212 shake steps identical.**

`Battle::advance_tick()` and `Battle::step_units_and_projectiles()` cover stages 1, 3 and 4; `Battle::players_stage()` covers stage 6 (weapon-target passes, unit table refreshes, sight; see "Target acquisition"; `Battle::start_players()` once after the players are set up). Other stages run in the caller. Hook mapping:
`Battle::advance_tick()` and `Battle::step_units_and_projectiles()` cover stages 1, 3 and 4; `Battle::run_route_search()` is the first part of stage 6 (the players stage begins with the route search, see "Route search"). Other stages run in the caller. Hook mapping:
`Battle::advance_tick()` and `Battle::step_units_and_projectiles()` cover stages 1, 3 and 4; `Battle::step_effects()` is stage 5 and `Battle::step_features()` stage 7 (the caller runs them in that order, with the players' stage between). Other stages run in the caller. Hook mapping:
- weapons → slot pass; fire → fire routines;
- damage, heal and kill → combat;
- primary orders → order processing, then the stale word = the visited slot's player record;
- secondary orders → order processing; settle → ground settle;
- paralysis, clear orders, weapon-target release → orders;
- projectile removal and death explosions → projectiles;
- retargeting, and the behaviours' fire-at-will target, attack orders, mission resolution and enemy lists → targeting.
- feature damage, feature slot positions, wrecks → features; impact explosions and script explode → effects; burn weapons → projectile area damage.
- repair energy and build/stockpile requests → economy slot bookings; each player's 30-step turn in the players stage → victory hook, settlement, sensor hook; `Battle::step_wind()` = stage 8 (after the features); `Battle::auto_share_resources()` = the network stage's sharing (see "Economy").
- sight updates (moves and the players' stage), creation stamps, lingering sight and removal at death, the visibility and explored tests of targeting → line of sight (`sim/sight.h`, once `init_sight()` ran); the viewed player's sensor pass ends its turn of the players' stage; lingering expiry is per frame (`expire_lingering_sight()`).

- the computer player (controller step before the weapon-target pass, the table refresh's AI part and rescoring, group lists of new and dead units) → `sim/ai.h` (see "Computer player"); `BattleOutside::ai_controller_step/ai_own_unit/ai_rescore` are no longer called.

Orders get stand-in heap addresses (a sequence from 0x01000000 in steps of 0x58), since the original's vary between runs.

**Behaviour seen while testing:** shots pass through units of their own player (`owner_slot` equality in the hit test). Units are found only through the map cells' occupancy, which the footprint system fills (see "Footprints and occupancy"); units enter the battle through `Battle::create_unit` (see "Unit creation").

### Unit creation — smoke-tested: 24 scenarios, 1,675 operations, 440 units created (147 structures, 247 mobile, 129 activated, 86 with contested cells), 309 refused, 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `sim::Battle::create_unit` (`engine/sim/src/unit_create.cpp`, `sim/unit_create.h`), type data `sim::UnitCreateType` (in `sim::BattleUnitType::create`), filled from imported definitions by `ta::to_battle_unit_type` (`ta_compat/src/battle_types.cpp`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/create_harness` (below, with the footprints).

**Entry** (`Unit_CreateFromDefId` `0x00485F50`, stdcall (player, type u16, x, y, z, fully_built, layer, wanted id u16) → unit or 0). The player is the low byte; the definition record is GS+0x1439B + type × 0x249 (record 0 is the blank type).
1. Fails for type 0 and for a type whose flag word (def+0x241) lacks bit 23 (the importer's "version compatible" bit).
2. **Limit:** if def+0x15A (`maxthisunit`, −1 = none) is not −1, every entry of the player's range (+0x67..+0x6B inclusive, dead or alive) with this type index is counted; count ≥ limit (signed) fails.
3. **Entry:** the range must not be empty. Without a wanted id: the first entry with type index 0. With one: exactly that entry, which must lie in the range and be free; otherwise fail.
4. The three weapon slots' aim sinks get their vtable (no state); type index = type.
5. Field set-up (below), script and object state, weapon slots, metal extraction (below).
6. **bmcode** (def+0x22F) = 1: a motion object (`0x43DC00`, a hook for the motion system) and the heading becomes def+0x210 (the build angle) — the random heading drawn in step 5 is thrown away, but its RNG draw stays.
7. Unit flags bits 0-1 = layer bits 0-1. Ground settle (`0x48A870`, see "Ground settle"; the new unit has resettle set). Footprint claim (`0x47CC30`, see "Footprints and occupancy").
8. **Packet 9** (23 bytes, to the owner's net id through `0x451DF0`, always): 0x09, type u16, id u16, x, y, z, pitch, heading, bank.
9. **Only when fully built:** bmcode 0 → packet 0x12 (5 bytes: 0x12, id, id); type bit 18 (`activatewhenbuilt`) → `Unit_SetActivateState(1, on)` (verified here through the real routine: Activate queued, speech, HUD and packet 0x11 hooks); type bit 24 (`isfeature`) → last damage kind 7 and the dying flag 0x4000 (the next unit pass kills it).
10. Line of sight (`0x482AC0`, hook), the player's alive count (+0x144, u16) and units-ever-created (+0x140, u32) + 1, and the victory conditions hear about the unit (`0x490580`, hook).

**Field set-up** (`Unit_InitInstanceFields` `0x00485A40`), in order. "Type bit n" means bit n of def+0x241.
- def pointer; flags |= alive (bit 28); flags bit 14 (dying) cleared; bit 29 = (bmcode == 0) (a structure: claims by yard map); footprint (+0x7E) = def+0x14A.
- flags bit 31 = type bit 16 (has weapons); +0x114 bit 0 = type bit 7 (z-buffer); flags bit 30 = type bit 9.
- Fully built: build fraction left 0.0, hp = low 16 bits of max damage (def+0x1FA). Otherwise: build fraction 1.0, +0x100 = 0, hp 0.
- Script flags &= 0xF0; health % 0; flags: clear bits 1-4, 10, 11, 17, set bits 0, 5, 16 (ground layer, selectable, resettle); position; previous health % 0; **activate flags 0**; cloak hold-off 0; bank 0.
- **Cell origin** (+0x76/+0x78, shorts): ((x − fx·2¹⁹ + 2¹⁹) >> 20) and the same for z (32-bit wrapping, arithmetic shift): the footprint centred on the position, rounded to the nearest cell.
- **Heading** = rand(build angle) + 0x8000 − build angle / 2 (16 bits; battle RNG, a build angle < 2 draws nothing). Pitch 0; line-of-sight cell (+0x7A/+0x7C) 0; damaged timer 0; transfer hold-off 0.
- Flags bit 9 = (owner's +0x146 == the local view GS+0x2A43), bit 8 cleared.
- For slots 0, 1, 2: target = (0, −0x8000) (`0x48A160`), then `Unit_ReleaseWeaponTargets(slot)` (only sets bit 0x10 on enabled, unreleased slots — the target is already gone).
- Net dirty 0; kills 0; last attacker none, its slot 10. **The last damage kind is not reset.**
- Flags bits 18-19 = type bits 0-1, bits 20-21 = type bits 2-3, bit 11 = type bit 4 (init cloaked), bits 26-27 cleared; build menu pages (def+0x22E) > 1 → bits 22-23 set, 24-25 cleared; else bits 22-25 cleared.
- +0xF8 = 0; resource slot (+0xBC, 52 bytes) zeroed and its player = the entry's owner slot (+0xFF) (`0x401070`); transport piece +0xF9 = 0xFF; hover phase = rand(0x10000); group list 0 (`0x480250`, hook).
- **Not touched** (whatever the previous occupant left): the owner (+0x96/+0xFF, fixed per range), the motion pointer of non-mobile types, orders, target links, aim result/heading/pitch of the slots, slot flag bits 5-7, last damage kind, metal extraction of non-extractors.

**Script and object state** (`Unit_CreateScriptAndObjectState` `0x00485D40`): with a COB (def+0x18E): a script object (tick rate 30), the object state reordered by the script's piece names, bound, then `Create` started and all threads run once. Without one: no script, object state without reordering. State +0x10 = 0. The script's statics come from `realloc` **without clearing** (heap contents; the engine starts them at 0, the harness clears its heap to match).

**Weapon slots** (`Unit_SetupWeaponSlots` `0x0049E070`), slot i: reload 0, weapon = def+0x1EE[i], stockpile 0, flags = (old & 0xF0) | i << 2 | 0x10 | (weapon's index byte +0x10A ≠ 0) << 1. Slot +0x10 = ftol((Query-piece z − AimFrom-piece z) × 1.25) (`0x43E240` with piece −1, then `0x43E2E0`; x87 FILD × double, truncated): the z difference only, not a distance. Then the script gets `SetMaxReloadTime(longest reload × 1000 / 30)` (queued). A null weapon pointer or a missing script crashes the original.

**Metal extraction** (`Unit_UpdateMetalExtraction` `0x00437840`): only if def+0x1CE (`extractsmetal`) > 0.0 (NaN and ≤ 0 skip). Sum = Σ (cell metal byte +7, + 1) over the footprint cells inside the map, as 16 bits. Extraction (+0x58) = (float)(extracts × (short)sum) (the sum sits in the high half of a dword loaded with FILD, then × 2⁻¹⁶). Script: `SetSpeed(sum)` queued.

**Engine API:** `Battle::init_unit_table(n)`, `assign_unit_range(player, first, last)` (sets the owner of those entries), `init_buckets()` after the map is set, `types[k]` filled with `ta::to_battle_unit_type`, then `create_unit(player, type, x, y, z, fully_built, layer, wanted_id)`. Hooks in `BattleOutside`: `create_motion`, `join_group`, `stamp_line_of_sight`, `victory_unit_created` (plus the footprint hooks below). **Callers** (names from the reference program, not verified): battle start `0x497180`, the commander spawn `0x496EE0`, map-schema units `0x488310`, saved games `0x487080`, ownership transfer `0x488570`, the players' tick `0x464F80`, the building/mobile-build/resurrect behaviours and a console command. The commander spawn (read, not tested) looks up the start position for the player's start index in the schema (`0x437320`), the side's commander type by name, and calls the routine with (player, that type, start x, y, z, fully built 1, layer 1, any entry); it also sets the player's side bytes and starting resources (at least 200). A tool spawns a commander the same way: `create_unit(player, commander_type, x, y, z, true, 1)`.

### Footprints and occupancy — smoke-tested within create_harness (same run): 160 moves, 57 wanted-cell claims, 79 area re-claims, 81 forced re-claims, 47 yard checks, 46 yard openings, 60 height-range updates, 0 differences
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/footprint.cpp` (`sim/footprint.h`): `claim_footprint`, `release_cells`, `clear_footprint`, `claim_wanted_cells`, `reclaim_area`, `force_reclaim_around`, `yard_cells_free`, `refresh_height_range`, `footprint_origin`; wired into `sim::Battle` (yard opening, death). `create_harness` runs the original routines below against them after random moves, layer and yard-state changes and random cell occupants, and compares all cells, buckets and units after every operation.

**Map cell** (13 bytes, GS+0x14287, row-major, width GS+0x14233, height GS+0x14237): +0 unit (u16), +2 unit2 (u16), +4 height, +5 max and +6 min of the cell's and its +x/+z/+xz neighbours' heights, +7 metal, +8 feature, +0xA feature extra, +0xC flags (bit 0 feature animation, **bit 1 marked by a yard map**). `Map_GetCell` `0x481550` (x, z) gives the cell or 0 outside.

**Spatial buckets:** GS+0x1429F points at a grid of 10-byte records (head unit pointer at +6), GS+0x142A3/+0x142A7 = width/height = map world size rounded up to 128 units (`0x482C20`), GS+0x142B7 = one more record for units off the map. A unit's +0x82 is its bucket (0 none); lists link through **+0x8E, the same field as the cargo list** (carried units are never filed). Newest first.

**Occupation contest** (every claim): a unit U wants a cell field that holds H ≠ 0 (H may be U itself). If H's owner record is active (+0 ≠ 0) and has controller 3: H gets flags bit 27 (wants cells), U gets bit 26 (holds wanted cells) and U takes the field. Otherwise H gets bit 26, U gets bit 27 and the field stays H's. Bit 27 marks a unit to claim again; bit 26 makes a unit's release trigger re-claims around it.

**Claim** (`Unit_ClaimFootprint` `0x0047CC30`):
1. With a motion object: motion +0x26 = game tick (hook `motion_cells_claimed`).
2. Off the map (cell x or z < 0, or x + fx ≥ width, or z + fz ≥ height, int sums): file the unit in the off-map list; done.
3. File it in bucket (z >> 23) × bucket width + (x >> 23) (arithmetic shifts of the 16.16 position). Moving between lists: carried units only record the new bucket; others unlink (walk) and push at the head.
4. **Structures** (flags bit 29): yard byte k per footprint cell (row by row): if byte & (yard open (script flags bit 2) ? 2 : 4): contest `unit`; then if byte & 1: cell flags |= 2. Afterwards the height range of the rectangle (x−1, z−1, fx+2, fz+2) (16-bit sums) and passability for (x, z, fx, fz) (`0x440A40`, hook `update_passability`).
5. Others: layer 1 contests `unit`, layer 2 `unit2`, layers 0 and 3 claim nothing.

**Release** (`Unit_ReleaseFootprintCells` `0x0047D0E0`): unless in the off-map list (a unit never filed goes ahead with its origin): structures clear `unit` where it is theirs and clear cell flag 2 for yard bit 0, then the height range as above (no passability call); layer 1/2 clear their own field. Then clear bit 27; if bit 26 was set: clear it and re-claim the area of the footprint (below). Finally passability after release (`0x440A70`, hook: with a motion object it recomputes the movement classes changed since motion +0x26 and restamps it; without, all classes).
**Clear** (`Unit_ClearFootprint` `0x0047CBD0`, on death): release, then leave the bucket list unless carried; bucket = 0.
**Wanted-cell claim** (`Unit_ClaimWantedCells` `0x0047C790`): only with bit 27, which it clears. The first cell comes from `Map_GetCell` (0 off the map: **the original crashes** when the footprint is not empty); the walk is not bounded (a footprint past the bottom edge writes beyond the grid). Structures: per yard byte, occupied in the current yard state → contest `unit`, else clear `unit` if it is theirs (no cell marks, heights or passability). Others: **layer 1 contests `unit`, every other layer (0, 2, 3) `unit2`.**
**Area re-claim** (`Map_ForEachUnitInCellRect` `0x0047E5C0` with the callback `0x47ED30`, also `Map_ReclaimCellRect` `0x47CAC0`): buckets x from (x0 >> 3) − 1 to (x1 >> 3) + 1 (outer), z likewise (inner), skipping those outside the grid (unsigned compare); every unit of the bucket and every unit of its cargo list whose footprint overlaps [x0, x1) × [z0, z1) gets a wanted-cell claim.
**Forced re-claim** (`Unit_ForceReclaimAround` `0x0047DB20`): bit 27 on, area re-claim of its footprint, passability.
**Yard check** (`Unit_YardCellsFree` `0x0047D970`, value): x1 = (short)(x + fx), z1 = (short)(z + fz); false unless x ≥ 1, z ≥ 1, x1 < width, z1 < height; then false if any yard byte & (value ≠ 0 ? 2 : 4) covers a cell whose `unit` is another unit. **Yard open** (`Unit_SetYardOpen` `0x0047DAC0`, the script value 18): if free: script flag bit 2 = value & 1, bit 27 on, wanted-cell claim, passability.
**Height range** (`Map_RefreshCellHeightRange` `0x00483210`, origin, size as shorts): x0 = max(x, 0), z0 = max(z, 0), x1 = x + w and z1 = z + h, each clamped to width − 1 / height − 1 when beyond; each cell of [x0, x1) × [z0, z1) gets max/min over itself and its +x (if x < width − 1), +z (if z < height − 1) and +xz neighbours.

**Undefined behaviour recorded by the engine** (`FootprintWorld::fault`, the access is skipped): cells beyond the grid, yard map reads past the end, occupants outside the table, buckets outside the grid, a unit missing from its bucket list. In the smoke run the original crashed every time the engine predicted a crash (4 wanted-cell claims with the first cell off the map) and wrote past the grid once without crashing (scenario stopped).

**Not reverse engineered here:** placement checks for building by yard map (`0x47D2E0`; `0x47E2D0` is unused), the start-position code that calls the creation routine. The movement classes' passability and the move placement check `0x47DB70` are built: see "Route search".

**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name, prototype, plate comment): `0x00485F50` `Unit_CreateFromDefId`, `0x00485A40` `Unit_InitInstanceFields`, `0x00485D40` `Unit_CreateScriptAndObjectState`, `0x0049E070` `Unit_SetupWeaponSlots`, `0x00437840` `Unit_UpdateMetalExtraction`, `0x0047CC30` `Unit_ClaimFootprint`, `0x0047D0E0` `Unit_ReleaseFootprintCells`, `0x0047CBD0` `Unit_ClearFootprint`, `0x0047C790` `Unit_ClaimWantedCells`, `0x0047D970` `Unit_YardCellsFree`, `0x0047DAC0` `Unit_SetYardOpen`, `0x0047E5C0` `Map_ForEachUnitInCellRect`, `0x0047CAC0` `Map_ReclaimCellRect`, `0x0047ED30` `FootprintCb_ClaimWanted` (function created), `0x0047DB20` `Unit_ForceReclaimAround`, `0x00483210` `Map_RefreshCellHeightRange`, `0x00481550` `Map_GetCell`, `0x0048A160` `Weapon_ClearSlotTarget`, `0x00401070` `ResourceSlot_Init`, `0x00456050` `Net_SendUnitCreated`, `0x004560C0` `Net_SendStructureFinished`.

**create_harness:** `create_harness <TotalA.exe> <game_dir> [scenarios] [operations]`. Per scenario: three players with ranges 1–8, 9–16, 17–24 (random controllers incl. 3, random view), a fourth with an empty range, four random real types (model, script, all 256 weapon definitions) with random limits, bmcode, build pages, build angles, extraction, footprints and yard maps, type flag flips, settle variants; Moon Quartet heights with random metal. Stubs: motion constructor, line of sight, victory, group list, passability (both), speech, HUD, network (packet bytes logged), script effect requests, the COB checksum cache. A crash the engine predicted (fault set) restarts the process without counting as a failure.

### Route search (movement classes, passability, placement, the pathfinder) — smoke-tested: 30 scenarios (20 × 40 and 10 × 60 steps: 1,400 search steps, 1,622 operations, 359 route requests taken, 100 routes given, 107 no-route answers, 326 placement checks), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/route_search.cpp` (`sim/route_search.h`): `build_passability`, `update_passability`, `update_class_passability`, `passability_after_release`, `can_place_footprint`, `init_route_search`, `run_route_search`, `route_search_forget`; wired into `sim::Battle` (footprint hooks, the motion placement check, the route goals' forget, `Battle::init_routes`, `Battle::run_route_search`, `Battle::refresh_passability`). Importer: `ta::to_move_classes` and `BattleUnitType::route` (`ta_compat/src/battle_types.cpp`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/route_harness` (below).

**Movement classes** (static table of 32 × 0x20 bytes at `0x00512358`, filled from `gamedata\moveinfo.tdf` CLASS0..31 at program start; a class is in use when its name pointer is set): +0 name, +4/+6 footprint x/z, +8 max water depth, +0xA min water depth, +0xC max slope, +0xD bad slope, +0xE max water slope, +0xF bad water slope, +0x10/+0x14 grid width/height (the map's), +0x18 grid, +0x1C **lock step**. The table is program-wide: the lock step survives from one battle to the next (an input; 0 after program start). A unit type's class is its definition's `movementclass`; its motion object keeps the class pointer at +4 (none → the search never serves it).

**Grid**: 2 bits per cell, word (z >> 4) × width + x, shift (z & 15) × 2. The grade of cell (x, z) is for the class's footprint with its corner there: 0 blocked, 1 passable but rough (costly), 3 free (2 is never stored; the search uses it for unexplored ground).

**Obstacles:** a cell blocks a class when (in this order) a feature blocks it, its ground occupant (`unit`, not `unit2`) is a unit without a motion object or one whose motion +0x26 (the step it last claimed its footprint) is older than the class's lock step, its min height is below sea − max water depth, or its max height is above sea − min water depth (signed ints). Slope = max − min (byte): with the min height below the sea the water slopes apply (above bad → 1 if ≤ max, else 0), otherwise the land slopes; else 3. **Features:** id 0xFFFF none; below 0xFFFB the type's flag byte (+0xFE) bit 0x40, a type past the table (GS+0x14253) blocks; 0xFFFE is a part of a bigger feature whose own cell lies (aux low byte) rows and (aux high byte) columns back — its id decides (0xFFFB+ there: not blocking; the type there is not range-checked: engine fault); other ids (0xFFFB-0xFFFD) block.

**Build at map load** (`MoveClass_BuildAllGrids` `0x00440940` → `MoveClass_BuildGrid` `0x00440500`, per named class): grid dims = map dims (allocation uninitialised, every cell written). Pass 1, every row: the cells' own grades (`Map_CellGrade` `0x0047DE60`) in a line buffer with two 0 cells before it and footprint x + 1 zero cells after it; then a running minimum over the window [x, x + fx − 1]: if the window's last cell ≤ the previous minimum it is the minimum; else if the cell that left the window (x − 1) was above the previous minimum that stays; else the window is rescanned. A window minimum of 3 next to a cell below 3 on either side (x − 1 or the cell after the window) is stored as 1. Pass 2 does the same down every column (footprint z) over the pass-1 grades. Quirks kept by doing it literally: footprint 0, map edges (the zero padding makes edge footprints rough).

**Incremental updates** (`MoveClass_UpdateRect` `0x00440830`, class, cell (x, z), size (w, h) as shorts): the corners that can touch the rectangle: x from x − class fx (≥ 0) to x + w + 1 exclusive (unsigned clamp to the width), z likewise; each gets `Map_FootprintGradeWithRing` (`0x0047E1F0`): the lowest grade over the footprint (`Map_RectGrade` `0x0047DFC0`: 0 when x or z < 0 or x + w ≥ width or z + h ≥ height — so a footprint touching the last row or column is blocked; else the min over the cells, same tests as above), and when that is 3 the ring around it (row above from x − 1, column right from z − 1, row below from x, column left from z, each one longer than the footprint) must be all 3, else 1. Note the build and the update are different rules; the grids drift toward the update's rule where things change.
- `MoveClass_UpdateAllRects` `0x00440A40` (every named class): footprint claims of structures, yard opening, forced re-claims, feature placement (`0x423C50`, `0x4246B0`).
- `Unit_PassabilityAfterRelease` `0x00440A70` (end of a footprint release): with a motion object, its +0x26 becomes the game tick and every class (named or not) whose lock step is above the old value updates the unit's rectangle; without one, every named class does.

**Lock** (`MoveClass_AcquireLock` `0x00440AF0`, before a search): lock = max(tick, 30) − 30 (30 when the tick is below 31). The served unit's step is set to the tick while this runs: if its old step was below the old lock, its rectangle is refreshed (now free for it); if the lock moved, every live unit with a motion object (table entries 1..end GS+0x1435B) whose step lies in [old lock, new lock) gets its rectangle refreshed (it has stood still for 30 steps: now an obstacle). Then the unit's step is restored. **Release** (`MoveClass_ReleaseLock` `0x00440BE0`): the served unit's rectangle is refreshed if its step is below the lock. So a class grid counts every unit that has not claimed cells for 30 steps as an obstacle, except the one being routed.

**Placement check** (`Map_CanPlaceFootprint` `0x0047DB70`, stdcall (def, exclude id, packed cell, mode)): x or z < 0, or x + def fx ≥ width, or z + def fz ≥ height → mode == 2. Structures (bmcode 0) → yard-map placement `0x47D2E0` (built: `sim::structure_fits`, construction). Mode ≠ 1 → true. Else every footprint cell: blocking feature or a ground occupant other than `exclude` → false; min < sea − def max water depth or max > sea − def min water depth → false; slope (int) above def max slope → false on land, false above def max water slope under water. The motion move calls it with the unit's id and its layer.

**Search object** (0xC9 bytes at GS+0x14207, `RouteSearch_Construct` `0x0040E9E0` while the battle loads, after the map): +0 node pool, +4 open heap (pointers into the pool), +8 free-list head, +0xC pool count, +0x10 capacity, +0x14 open count, +0x18 **hold** flag, +0x1C cell states (4 bytes per cell: flags, entry direction, u16 pool index), +0x20/+0x24 grid size, +0x28 cell count (rounded up to 8), +0x2C touched bitmap (1 bit per 8 cells), +0x30 start cell, +0x34 goal cell found, +0x38/+0x3C nearest goal cell, +0x40 probe best, +0x44 turn range, +0x48 budget divisor 0x535, +0x4C work, +0x50 estimate scale, +0x54 base 0x18000, +0x58 unit, +0x5C route goal, +0x60 order goal, +0x64 class, +0x68 turn costs {0, 40, 60, 80, 100, 80, 60, 40}, +0x70 direction costs (16 straight, 22 diagonal), +0x78 player whose turn it is, +0x79 unit cursor per player (starts at the range start), +0xA1 credit per player (0). Program-wide statics, not reset between battles (inputs): `0x00511A38` refresh counter, `0x005119E8` estimate scale per player (the constructor sets them to the base), `0x00511A10` turns per player. Node (0x14): heap index (free-list link when free), cell (x low, z high), g, f (heap key), i16 step cost, i16 straight depth.

**Directions:** 0 = −z, 1 = (−1, −1), 2 = −x, 3 = (−1, +1), 4 = +z, 5 = (+1, +1), 6 = +x, 7 = (+1, −1) (tables `0x004FD670`/`0x004FD678`). A unit's start direction = ((heading + 0x1000) >> 13) & 7.

**Step** (`RouteSearch_Step` `0x0040EB70`, thiscall; the first call of the players stage `0x464F80`, i.e. stage 6 of the battle step, after effects): player count (GS+0x2A3C, u16) 0 → nothing. share = 0x535 / count (C division). Refresh counter + 1; at 150: 0, and for every player p: load = turns[p] / units per player (GS+0x1434F, u16; 0 divides by zero) → scale[p] = base × 6 (load < 1), × 3 (load < 2), else base; turns[p] = 0. Every player slot 0..9 active with controller 1/2/3 and state ≠ 10: credit += share; total += its new credit. While total > 0: work = 0, then
1. No unit served: work = 1; while credit[player] ≤ 0 the player index advances (0..9 wrapping); turns[player] + 1; its cursor moves to the next table entry (from its range end back to its range start); if that entry has a type, a motion object and a class: route goal = motion goal's "wants route" (vtable +0x18; stores it at +0x5C even when it says no); yes → serve the unit, work + 100, scale = scale[player], begin (below) with the goal's order goal.
2. Open set empty (open count = hold) → answer "no route" (route goal `0x44F080` with 0), release, unit and class cleared.
3. Else pop and expand until 100 work: (open = hold → stop); work + 1; a held node is freed first (free list, last heap entry into its place, sift down); the top node is copied and held; if its cell has the goal flag: found = its cell, reconstruct (below), release, unit and class cleared; else its cell state = closed (2, assigned) and the neighbours for turns −range..+range (each & 7; range 4 for the start node, so the reverse direction is tried twice, 2 afterwards) are expanded.
Then total −= work, credit[player] −= work.

**Begin** (`RouteSearch_Begin` `0x0040E630`): class = motion +4, order goal, start = unit cell; acquire the lock; cost tables; clear touched cells (`0x0040D900`: the state byte of every 8-cell group whose bit is set). Goal cells = order goal vtable +0x18 (circle: its cell; ring: (cx, cz + (outer + inner)/32); edge: its border; point/manoeuvre: none): inside the grid → state = 4 (assigned) and touched; the one with the smallest squared distance to the start (strictly smaller wins) becomes the nearest goal cell (none: the old value stays). Start inside the goal (vtable +0x14) → report 0x100 to the order, no route. Estimate h(x, z) = low 32 bits of (vtable +0x1C distance × scale) >> 16 (signed 64-bit). Start outside the grid → report 0x200, no route. **Probe** (`RouteSearch_Probe` `0x0040E160`): best = h(start); start not passable → return best. Loop: work + 1; best = 0 → return 0; step toward the nearest goal cell (x first: dx < 0 → dir 2, dx > 0 → 6, else dz ≤ 0 → 0 else 4); passable (≥ 1) → move, mark the cell (touched, entry dir, state |= 8; it was a goal cell → return 0), best = min(best, h). Blocked: from the blocked spot two walkers follow the obstacle: walker A starts at the blocked direction and turns +1 until a passable neighbour (back to its start direction − 1 → return best), walker B the opposite way (direction + 2, steps backwards, turning −1); each move marks its cell (goal → 0) and returns to the probe line (the L from the blocked spot: along x within the goal's x distance, then along z) → the probe continues from there; otherwise best = min(best, h). They meet (same cell, same direction, after A moved) → return best. Probe 0 → report 0x100; else report 0x200 and, if probe best ≥ h(start), no route. Else: open set reset, start state |= 1, entry dir from the heading, start node {g 0, f h(start), depth 100, step = uninitialised stack}, turn range 4. "No route" = route goal answer 0, release, unit and class cleared (the order goal and route goal fields keep their values).

**Expand** (`RouteSearch_Expand` `0x0040DA70`, parent copy, its cell state, turn t): dir = (parent entry dir + t) & 7, neighbour outside the grid → nothing. New cell (state & 3 = 0): touched; passability (`RouteSearch_CellPassability` `0x0040D7B0`: outside the class grid → 0; the 2 × 2 block (x/2 + fx/4, z/2 + fz/4) of the explored map GS+0x14273 (u16 per block, bit = player) without the turn's player's bit → 2; else the grid grade) 0 and not on the probe line → state |= 3 (blocked). Else h; state |= 1, or 5 (open + goal) when h ≤ probe best — **every cell estimated as close as the probe got counts as a goal**; entry dir; depth = t ? 1 : parent depth + 1; step = passability > 1 ? 0 : 30; g = turn cost[t] + dir cost[dir] + parent g + step, + 75 when t ≠ 0 and parent depth < 5; f = g + h. With a held node: it becomes this node (heap index kept), sift down from the top, hold off; else a node from the free list or the pool end goes to the heap end and sifts up. Open cell: g = turn cost + dir cost + parent g + **the cell's own stored step**, + 75 as above; if lower: entry dir, f += g − old g, depth as above, sift up; with a held node, if the held node is no longer at the top it is freed. Closed or blocked cells: nothing. Heap ties: sift up moves past strictly larger parents; sift down prefers the right child only when strictly smaller and stops at ≤.

**Reconstruct** (`RouteSearch_Reconstruct` `0x0040E050`): from the found cell back to the start along the entry directions (16-bit cell steps); whenever the direction changes the cell goes into a 64-entry ring (the found cell is entry 0); then the start. Up to 64 points, start first, each = ((cell × 2 + class footprint) × 8) as u16 (the footprint centre in world units), go to the route goal (`0x44F080` keeps the first 20).

**Forget** (`RouteSearch_Forget` `0x0040E9C0`, from route goal attach/destructors): if the goal is the one at +0x5C, unit, route goal, order goal and class are cleared — without releasing the lock (the class grid keeps the unit's footprint free until something refreshes it).

**Smoke runs** (`route_harness <exe> <game> 20 40 100` and `10 60 5000`): random maps 24..128 cells square (hills, cliffs, sea level, blocking/non-blocking single and 2 × 2 features, markers past the feature table, explored bits), the real 32 classes of moveinfo.tdf with leftover lock steps, three players (random controllers, sometimes out), 18 units with motion objects, route goals and circle/ring/edge/point order goals written field by field, leftover program-wide counters. Per step: routes asked, goals replaced (with the original's forget first), unit moves (release, after-release update, claim, rectangle update), releases without motion, alive flags, rectangle updates, feature and explored changes, placement checks (structure placement stubbed), then the original's step. Compared after everything: the whole search object, node pool, open heap, every cell state and the touched bitmap, every class grid and lock step, the program-wide counters, route goals (points, count, flags, search step), order events and footprint steps. A one-off change of the early-turn depth limit (5 → 4) was caught at once. 0 differences, no engine faults.

**The work budget (checked 2026-10-17 for "is the pathfinder budgeted as in the original?" — yes, exactly).** The search gets a fixed amount of work per battle step, and nothing else limits it:
- Per step: 0x535 (1,333; the constructor's +0x48, `0x40EAD3`) divided by the player count (C division) is added to the credit of every playing player (slot active, controller 1/2/3, state ≠ 10). Credits carry over between steps, negative ones too. The step works while the sum of those players' credits (after the share), less the work done in this step, stays above 0.
- Units are served one at a time, round robin: the turn goes to the next player with credit above 0 (slots 0..9, wrapping), whose unit cursor moves one entry through its own range (back to the start after the end; one entry = 0x118 bytes). Picking costs 1; a unit whose goal wants a route costs 100 more plus the probe's moves, and its search begins.
- A search runs in rounds of at most 100 pops (1 work each, expansions included), each round charged to the player whose turn it is. It stays open across rounds and steps (open set, held node, cell states kept) until a goal cell is popped or the open set is empty. Only one unit in the whole game is searched at a time.
- Every 150 steps the estimate scale of each player is reset from its turns in that time (turns / units per player: below 1 → 6 × 0x18000, below 2 → 3 ×, else 1 ×): players asking for few routes get greedier, cheaper searches.
- So with hundreds of units the routes are handed out a few units per step and a unit stands still until its turn comes; 240 units ordered across Moon Quartet were mostly still waiting 600 steps later. That is the original's behaviour (same budget, same order), not a slowdown of the program.
- Evidence: the disassembly of `RouteSearch_Step` `0x40EB70` (0x96 = 150 for the refresh, 0x64 = 100 per round, the credit loop over slots 0..9, the cursor step 0x118) against `run_route_search` (`engine/sim/src/route_search.cpp`), and `route_harness <exe> <game> 10 40` after the speed work below: the whole search object (credits, work, turn player, unit cursors, pool, heap, cell states, grids) equal to the original's after each of its steps, 0 differences. Its cost is small: with 400 units moving, a few per cent of the simulation's sampled time.

**Engine API and step placement:** set `Battle::move_classes` (`ta::to_move_classes`), `feature_blocks` (`ta::bind_feature_blocks`: the feature types' flag 0x40; without it every part cell points "past the table", the search faults at step 0 and stops serving), `explored` (per 2 × 2 cells; the line-of-sight system will own it; a wrong-sized map becomes all explored), `settings.player_count` and `units_per_player`, types' `route` (`ta::to_battle_unit_type`), then `Battle::init_routes()` after the map and unit ranges, before units are created. Each step: `advance_tick`, `step_units_and_projectiles`, (effects stage), then `run_route_search` at the start of the players stage. Faults (`RouteContext::fault`): feature parts pointing at unknown types, occupants outside the table, a serve without class or order goal, a zero units-per-player refresh, a reconstruction that does not lead back, a unit cursor outside the table.

**Classified (2026-10-15):** the yard-map placement `0x47D2E0` is built (construction); `0x40E9A0` (release the lock and forget the unit) has no callers (dead); `0x40EF20` is the held node's release inside the step's pop loop (built, route_harness); the explored map belongs to line of sight (built). The meaning of GS+0x2A3C (taken as the number of players in the game) and GS+0x1434F (units per player) is from the reference program, not checked. **Large run should cover:** many seeds and longer runs (searches spanning many steps; heap growth), real maps (Moon Quartet and water maps) with real feature definitions, units leaving the off-map area, classes with footprint 0, the tick < 31 lock branch with stamps around it, player counts up to 10 with players dropping out, and the 64-point ring overflow of long winding routes.

### Movement behaviours (ground) — smoke-tested in orders_harness: 2 × 24 scenarios × 40 steps (1,883 unit passes, 3,343 operations; Move_Ground 30, Patrol 63, QMove 136, QPatrol 129 real behaviour steps), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/missions.cpp` (`move_ground`, `patrol`, `queued_move`, `ensure_patrol_return`); the goal goes through the new `MissionSystems::attach_circle_goal` (the battle calls `sim::attach_circle_goal`, `0x00438930`). In `orders_harness` these kinds now run their real handlers, with `0x438930` a logging stub (order, &order position, radius).
- **Move_Ground** (`Mission_MoveGround` `0x004031D0`): step 0: carried (unit +0x86) → 7; acknowledge; circle goal at the order position with radius param0 + 4; wait **=** 0xE0 (reached / no route / replaced); 1. Step 1: event 0x20 → speech(unit, 6), 5; else 9 (finished if another order follows, else idle). Other steps → 7.
- **QMove / QPatrol** (`Mission_QueuedMove` `0x00403160`, one handler): deadline 60, 6 (to the back of the list).
- **Patrol** (`Mission_Patrol` `0x004033A0`): step 0: no motion object → 7; acknowledge; patrol return (`MissionOrder_EnsurePatrolReturn` `0x0043A020`: unless one of the unit's primary orders has flag 0x8000, a new order of the same kind at the unit's position (no target, parameters 0) is appended at the end of the list its flags name — a plain append, no idle-flag inheritance — and the order gets 0x8000); deadline 1; 1. Step 1: release weapon targets (`0x489800`, all); circle goal at the order position, radius 0; deadline 15; wait |= 0xE0; 1. Step 2: event & 0xE0 → step 1, 6; fire-at-will target and a successful (unforced) attack order → wait 0, step 1, 3; else deadline 30 + rand(30), step 1, 4. Other steps → 7.
- **Follow_Ground** is now built (see "Aircraft and transports"; was: `0x406300`; needs the last attacker, no-chase categories, weapon range checks and repair/help-build resolution — target acquisition territory).
- **End to end** (`tests/sim_tests.cpp` `test_route_search`): a unit given Move_Ground across a 35-cell wall gets a route of corners around it from the route search, never stands on the wall, arrives at the goal cell and finishes the order (427 steps).
- **Harness notes:** the orders harness's mission-resolution answers no longer include AttackUType: the AttackUType order it makes has type 0 to look for, which matches empty table entries whose missing owner crashes the original (a crash path of the original that the engine does not flag; open question for the target-acquisition work). Seed index 1001 of `orders_harness … 24 40 1000` makes the original crash during a unit pass with or without these kinds (pre-existing, not investigated).

### Aircraft and transports (and the remaining order behaviours) — smoke-tested in orders_harness: 12 × 30 + 30 × 40 steps (42 scenarios, 1,313 unit passes, 2,380 operations; every one of the 68 mission kinds runs its real handler; 31 transport packets; 36 event-slot writes), 0 differences; 10 runs stopped at an original crash or hang the engine predicted (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/air_missions.cpp`, `ground_missions.cpp`, `unit_missions_common.{h,cpp}` (`sim/unit_missions.h`: `run_unit_mission`, `unit_mission_kind`, `can_land_at`, `units_in_radius`, `air_bases_in_radius`, `script_attach_unit`, `script_drop_unit`, context `UnitMissions`, hooks `UnitMissionSystems`), the air builder variants and Resurrect in `construction.cpp`. `Missions::unit_missions` points at the context; `sim::Battle` wires it (`UnitMissionHooks`). With these, **no mission kind is left on `run_other`**.

**Kinds and handlers** (index = mission kind): AirStrike (2) `0x00411F50`, AirToAir (3) `0x00412D40`, AirToGround (4) `0x00412710`, AirToGroundHover (5) `0x00413470`, Attack_Chase (6) `0x004034A0`, Attack_Kamikaze (7) `0x00403260`, Follow_Ground (18) `0x00406300`, Ground_Pickup (20) `0x00406780`, Ground_Unload (21) `0x00406900`, RepairPatrol (34) `0x00405980`, Resurrect (37) `0x00404DB0`, Suppress (46) `0x004038A0`, Teleport (47) `0x00406AA0`, VTOL_Evade (48) `0x00413BC0`, VTOL_Follow (49) `0x0040FBE0`, VTOL_GetRepaired (50) `0x00415250`, VTOL_HelpBuild (51) `0x00414380`, VTOL_LandIfCan (52) `0x0040F2A0`, VTOL_Landing (53) `0x004118E0`, VTOL_MobileBuild (54) `0x00413D80`, VTOL_Move (55) `0x0040FA20`, VTOL_Patrol (56) `0x00410E70`, VTOL_Pickup (57) `0x004111B0`, VTOL_Reclaim (58) `0x00414770`, VTOL_ReclaimUnit (59) `0x00414A80`, VTOL_RepairPatrol (60) `0x004152F0`, VTOL_RepairUnit (61) `0x00414E70`, VTOL_SeekAttack (62) `0x004103E0`, VTOL_SeekGuard (63) `0x00410850`, VTOL_Standby (64) `0x0040F7D0`, VTOL_Unload (65) `0x00411560`. Each source function opens with a short description of the behaviour in our own words; the points below are the ones that matter for exactness.

- **Goals.** The behaviours steer only through order goals: a goal object is allocated (0x36 bytes for point / follow / dock / face, 0x2C for a manoeuvre), built (`0x0044E2D0` point, `0x0044E190` follow, `0x0044E250` dock on a piece, `0x0044E330` face, `0x0044E740` manoeuvre), adjusted by setters in the order the original calls them (`0x0044E6C0` altitude, `0x0044E720` heading, `0x0044E730` radius, `0x0044EC10` manoeuvre altitude — does nothing, `0x0044EC20` manoeuvre target heading), then attached with `MissionOrder_AttachGoal` `0x004388D0`. Building and attaching are two calls (`make_goal` / `attach_goal`) because the original sometimes builds a goal and never attaches it: VTOL_Pickup step 4 (the climb after loading) and AirToGroundHover's random point after two out-of-range checks leak their goal (heap bump only).
- **Motion layer** changes go through `0x0043D210` (1 lands: velocity and speed zeroed; 2 takes off). Map size for "off the map": GS+0x1422B = width × 16 − 32, GS+0x1422F = height × 16 − 128; the off-map spatial list is GS+0x142B7 (`Unit::bucket == kOffMapBucket`).
- **Can land** (`0x0047E2D0`): footprint inside the map; cells the owner has not explored (grid at GS+0x14273, 2 × 2 cells per 16-bit entry, bit = player) always pass — the entry index uses the footprint x twice ((cx >> 1) + (fx >> 2) + ((cz >> 1) + (fx >> 2)) × (W >> 1)), kept; else no blocking feature, yard mark or other unit and water depth / slope inside the type's limits.
- **Air bases** (`0x0040B530`): the owner's unit table list, activated builders with the air base flag (0x40 + 0x200), within the radius (squared compare). Badly damaged (hit points below 3/4 of max as an unsigned compare of the signed word) aircraft look within 3,840 and take a random one (battle RNG).
- **Units in radius** (`0x0047E890`): buckets in rows of z then x, each list in order, horizontal distance (high halves of the squares) within the radius; the off-map list and carried units are not visited. The behaviours' temporary unit lists grow like the original's vectors (capacity 1, 2, 4, …; each growth is a heap block: `heap_alloc`).
- **Bomb runs** (AirStrike): drop lead = ftol(sqrt(2 × altitude / gravity) × 30 × speed) + 1 + attack run length (def +0x216); 2,240 out to get a run-up, 1,440 past the target. Gravity is read from the schema (+0xD3C).
- **Kamikaze**: distance def +0x218 (at least 16). **Cruise altitude** def +0x21C. **Maneuver leash** def +0x214 (scratch0 holds the leash origin: whole x | whole z << 16). **Transport size / capacity** def +0x22A / +0x22B.
- **Event slot (stale stack word of the secondary list):** VTOL_Follow leaves the resolved help kind in the low byte; Follow_Ground the HelpBuild kind (build path); VTOL_MobileBuild the packed site cell; RepairPatrol the metal amount found (float bits) or 0; **VTOL_RepairPatrol the stack address of its metal-site record** (run-varying: input `UnitMissionEnvironment::air_repair_patrol_site_address`, `BattleSettings::air_repair_patrol_site_address`).
- **Uninitialised read:** the reclaim-site search (`0x0047EA40`, best of three random picks per resource) never writes the sites' y: whatever the stack held (input `reclaim_site_y`, `BattleSettings::reclaim_site_y`).
- **Transports:** ATTACH_UNIT / DROP_UNIT script requests (`0x00481340` / `0x004813B0`) now run in the simulation (`script_attach_unit` / `script_drop_unit`, packet 0x0A through the transport routine); air pickup asks the script `QueryTransport` for the piece, calls `BeginTransport` with the cargo's height and broadcasts it by name (`0x00456200`), docks on the cargo, attaches it; unload checks the footprint fits (`0x0047DB70`), descends, drops, climbs. Ground transports use the scripts' `TransportPickup` / `TransportDrop` (three tries).
- **Air pads** (VTOL_Landing): circle the base until its script offers a free pad piece, approach, settle on the piece, become its cargo; on a finished builder a damaged aircraft gets SelfRepair. Texts: `PadsOccupied`, `NoPads`, `TooLarge`, `LandingAborted`, `LandingFailed`.
- **Resurrect:** the wreck's feature name up to the first '_' is looked up with the original's lower bound over the unit type names (`0x00488B10`, case-insensitive, defs 1..count−1, GS+0x1438F); the importer precomputes `FeatureTypeInfo::resurrect_type` (`ta::bind_resurrect_types`, exact match; the original's search on an unsorted table can miss — open question); the new unit takes the attitude of the feature slot the wreck's origin cell names (GS+0x1420B, 0x30-byte slots, angles at +0x20/+0x22/+0x24, not range-checked); spraying lasts build time × 0.3 / (worker time / 30) steps.
- **Teleport:** every unit table entry (empty ones too) inside the teleporter's model box moves by the order offset (`0x0048A9F0`) with a dot trail (`0x00471FD0`, 30 steps, effect list 5). The trail is simulated (2026-10-15): `Battle` starts `fx_dots(from = the unit's position, to = its destination, 30, list 5)` — its particles draw the C runtime rand() in the effect sweep — then calls the presentation hook `teleport_trail`.
- **Original crashes the engine flags** (`Missions::fault`): unload without a cargo link, air-to-air / hover attack without a target, an attack order on a weapon slot outside 0..2, resurrection of a wreck without a cell.
- **Harness** (`tools/orig_harness/orders_harness`): the goal constructors, setters, layer, unit / air base searches (answering from queues), place, trail, feature removal, broadcast by name are logging stubs; `0x004388D0` and the reclaim-site search run for real (a wrapper writes the known y into the uninitialised stack slot). Types get random sight (+0x202), leash, run length, kamikaze distance, cruise altitude, type id (+0x21E), transport size / capacity, the four category mask pointers (+0x231.. / +0x23D), model size z (+0x17E); players their stored amounts and capacities (+0x8C, +0x98, +0xA4, +0xA8); units last attackers (+0xF0, +0xF4, +0xF5), motion layer (+0x2E), velocity (+8) and speed, one unit in the off-map list; the explored grid and feature names are random. The air repair patrol's stale word is accepted when the original's is any stack-like address.
- **Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (created where missing; name and plate comment; saved): `Mission_AirStrike`, `Mission_AirToAir`, `Mission_AirToGround`, `Mission_AirToGroundHover`, `Mission_AttackChase`, `Mission_AttackKamikaze`, `Mission_FollowGround`, `Mission_GroundPickup`, `Mission_GroundUnload`, `Mission_RepairPatrol`, `Mission_Resurrect`, `Mission_Suppress`, `Mission_Teleport`, `Mission_VtolEvade`, `Mission_VtolFollow`, `Mission_VtolGetRepaired`, `Mission_VtolHelpBuild`, `Mission_VtolLandIfCan`, `Mission_VtolLanding`, `Mission_VtolMobileBuild`, `Mission_VtolMove`, `Mission_VtolPatrol`, `Mission_VtolPickup`, `Mission_VtolReclaim`, `Mission_VtolReclaimUnit`, `Mission_VtolRepairPatrol`, `Mission_VtolRepairUnit`, `Mission_VtolSeekAttack`, `Mission_VtolSeekGuard`, `Mission_VtolStandby`, `Mission_VtolUnload`, `Unit_CanLandAt`, `UnitTable_AirBasesInRadius`, `Map_PickReclaimSites`, `Unit_EnqueueRepairHonouringStance`, `Script_AttachUnit`, `Script_DropUnit`.
- **Large run to do:** `orders_harness <exe> <game> N 100 <first>` in shards (thousands of scenarios); check that every air kind reaches every step (landing on pads with free and busy pieces, bomb runs with gravity 1..4000, transports loading and unloading on land and over water, resurrection of named wrecks, teleport with many units in the box, kamikaze at the distance edge), the event-slot words of VTOL_Follow / Follow_Ground / VTOL_MobileBuild / RepairPatrol in the secondary list, units in the off-map list, and the frequency of the predicted original crashes and hangs.
- **Whole-battle findings (2026-10-14):** the bomber's long runs are the original's: a target closer than 0x1E00000 sends it 2,240 units out (goal radius 960) before the swing-in, and after the drop it flies on 1,440 (radius 128). What froze it in the battle view was the missing link release at death (see "Unit orders", target links): its target died, the order never heard event 8, built a follow goal on the empty table slot and the motion fault stopped every unit's motion. The transport after VTOL_Pickup step 4 waits on its dock goal (the climb goal is built and leaked, as the original does); the dock goal follows the cargo, which now hangs under the transport, so it is reached at once when the piece sits where the dock altitude puts it, and otherwise the transport chases its own cargo (climbing) — the same in the original, as both routines are verified (orders_harness for the behaviour, motion_harness for the goals and air steering `0x43D290` on real flying types: smoke 30 × 30 again 0 differences).

### atof (1998 C runtime string → double) — bit-exact, user rule: keep all quirks
Verified 2026-10-13: `ta::crt_atof` (`ta_compat/src/crt_strtod.cpp`) matches the original `atof` (`0x004E4560`) on **20,022,360 strings**, with **0 mismatches**. That covers all 22,360 distinct value strings in the game's TDF/FBI/OTA/GUI files plus 20M random edge-heavy strings. `atoi` was also checked with 0 mismatches. A modern `strtod` would differ on 3.25M of them, including 27 real game values.
- **Pipeline:** skip C-locale isspace → `Crt_StrToLd12` (`0x004F3D10`) → `Crt_Ld12ToIeee` (`0x004F37C0`, double format descriptor at `0x00510CE0` = {maxexp 1024, minexp −1023, prec 53, expwidth 11, 64 bits, bias 1023}).
- **Scanner:**
  - Skips space/tab/LF/CR, optional sign, digits, `.` (locale decimal point), digits.
  - Exponent letters: `e` `E` `d` `D`.
  - Stores at most 25 significant digits, then keeps 24; **extra digits are truncated**.
  - Explicit exponent clamps at 5201, which gives inf or zero.
  - No digits gives +0. The sign is OR'd in at the end, so `-0` and `-.0` give −0.0.
  - No `inf`/`nan`/hex parsing.
- **Mantissa:** decimal digits → 96-bit integer → normalized 80-bit mantissa (`Crt_DigitsToLd12` `0x004F8C40`, start exponent `0x404E`).
- **Scaling** (`Crt_Ld12MulPow10` `0x004F9390`):
  - Clear the low 16-bit guard word.
  - For each octal digit of |exp| (least significant first), multiply by a table power.
  - The table entry is "un-rounded" (its dword@2 decremented) when its low word > `0x7FFF`.
  - Tables at `0x005116F0` / `0x00511850`: 29 × 12 bytes each, entry = low16(round80(10^p)) | round64(10^p) | exponent. Generated bit-exact by `tools/gen_pow10_table.py`.
- **Multiply** (`Crt_Ld12Mul` `0x004F90D0`): 16-bit word partial products, only those with index sum ≥ 4. Carries bump one word only. Round half-even on the 16-bit guard word.
- **To double** (`Crt_RoundMantissa` `0x004F3610`): round up only if bit 53 is set **and** some bit in 55..95 is set. **Bit 54 is never examined, and exact halves round down.** Denormals re-round after shifting.
- **Test technique (reusable):** `ta_compat/tools/orig_harness` (32-bit) starts `TotalA.exe` **suspended**, injects an x86 stub, and calls the original routine in batches via `CreateRemoteThread`. Use it for any self-contained original routine (no OS calls, static data only).

### Rendering (battle view) — scan conversion differential-tested: 7,000 random polygons, 0 differences
Implementation: `render/` (engine side, no game names), importers in `ta_compat/src/render_assets.cpp`, viewer `render/tools/battle_view.cpp`. The original draws everything in software into an 8-bit offscreen surface; we build a **draw list** (projection, culling, order, CPU-rasterised model sprites) and execute it either on the CPU (reference) or on the GPU (SDL3 GPU API, `render/src/gpu_renderer.cpp`). Both give identical palette indices (checked every frame by `battle_view --gpu`; D3D12 and Vulkan: 0 differing pixels).

- **Frame** (`Render_DrawBattleFrame` `0x00468CF0`, called once per main-loop pass from `0x00496790` with (draw dynamic = 1, present = 1)). Order:
  1. Terrain tiles, clipped to the view rectangle.
  2. (HUD, minimap: not part of the battle view renderer.)
  3. Visible units are bucketed into **rows of 16 world units of z**: row = trunc((z − scroll y) / 16) + 16 (C truncation), kept when 0 ≤ row < row count; within a row, in visible-unit-list order.
  4. Feature cells from cell row trunc(scroll y / 16) − 16 and cell column trunc(scroll x / 16) − 10, clamped to the map minus one cell: every feature with height < 10 is drawn (row by row, column by column); taller ones are marked deferred (cell flag 0x04).
  5. Row by row: the row's **ground units** (unit flags & 3 == 1), then the deferred tall features of the matching cell row.
  6. Projectiles (`0x0049BE60`), then world effects, then the **other (air) units** in row order.
  7. Overlays (order lines, health bars, LOS fog, selection boxes, HUD text) follow; not modelled.
  - Hidden features (`nodrawundergray`) are drawn only when the cell's owner nibble is the viewing player or the LOS footprint test passes (not modelled; we draw all).
- **Projection.** World (x, y, z) in 16.16; screen x = int(x) − scroll x + 128, screen y = int(z) − (int(y) >> 1) − scroll y + 32, where int() is the high 16 bits as a signed short and >> is arithmetic. (128, 32) is the view's top-left corner on the 640×480 screen. **Height only moves things up by half; the terrain image itself is not displaced by height** (the tile art is pre-drawn in perspective). View size assumed 512×416 (left panel 128, top and bottom bars 32): not yet confirmed from the binary.
- **Terrain** (`Map_BlitVisibleTerrainTiles` `0x00483FA0`): the TNT tile map (u16 per 32×32-pixel tile, (width/2) × (height/2) tiles, at header offset 3) indexes 1024-byte tiles (header offset 5, count at offset 6). The tiles covering the view are copied opaque at tile × 32 − scroll + view origin; partial edge tiles go through a clipped copy, inner ones through a fast 32×32 copy. Exact.
- **Palette and tables.** `palettes\PALETTE.PAL` (256 × RGBA, A unused). Translucency and shadows use `palettes\PALETTE.ALP`, a 256×256 table: entry [src·256 + dst] = palette index nearest to the average of the two colours (diagonal = itself); built by `Palette_BuildAlphaBlendTable` `0x004BA750` and cached as the file (ccdata.ccx ships one, which we load). PALETTE.LHT / .SHD (8 KiB each) are light and shade tables (32 levels) used by the shaded model path (not modelled yet).
- **Model sprite** (`Unit_UpdateAndDrawModel` `0x0045AC20` → `Object_DrawPieces` `0x00458810` → `Model_AllocAndRasterizeSprite` `0x004586A0`):
  - Draw first **syncs the unit's pose** (base angle check and transform recompute, as `Unit_SyncObjectStateAnimTree`): see "Render coupling" below.
  - A unit's model is rasterised into a cached 8-bit sprite and blitted at the unit's screen position. The sprite is rebuilt when the cache-draw count is 0 (reset by pose changes to cached pieces), when it has none, or after cache eviction; otherwise the old sprite is reused. Rebuilding every frame gives the same pixels.
  - **Bounds** (`0x004581E0`): over every *visible* piece's transformed points, project x = int(px), y = int(−pz) − (int(py) >> 1); min/max start at 0 (the unit origin is always inside). Sprite = (max x − min x + 4) × (max y − min y + 4), hotspot (2 − min x, 2 − min y), filled with 1, transparency key 1.
  - **Pieces** (`Model_RasterizePiecesTextured` `0x00459830`, called with "cached only"): pieces from the last state slot to the first; a piece is drawn when visible and its cached flag is set (all visible pieces while the unit is under construction). Points are projected as above plus the hotspot; depth = int(py) + 50 (+75 for `digger` units). Primitives in stored order (the load-time sort by average Y is the painter's order), skipping primitive 0 when the piece has a selection primitive. **No depth buffer** in the normal path (1-byte-per-pixel sprite).
  - Primitive kinds (flags at primitive +0x1C, from the file's word there and the texture binder): bit 0 → **flat polygon** in palette index (colour & 0xFF), any vertex count; else only **4-index primitives are drawn, as textured quads**; others are skipped.
  - Texture frame: bit 1 (animated): bit 2 (team colour) → frame = owner player's colour index; otherwise frame 0 in the sprite path, the animation cursor in the direct path. Not animated → the bound frame.
  - **Texture binding** (`Model3d_BindPrimitiveTextures` `0x0042A140`, per model, sibling then child then own primitives): if the primitive has a texture name pointer: clear bit 2; search the texture GAFs (`textures\*.GAF` in enumeration order without `logos.GAF`, `Texture_LoadAllGafs` `0x0042A440`) by stricmp; else `logos.GAF` (a sequence with exactly 10 frames sets bit 2); found → bit 1 = (frames ≥ 2); not found anywhere → colour 0xD1 and bit 0 set. Bit 0 from the file is never cleared, so a coloured primitive stays flat even with a texture.
  - **Blit**: at (int(x − scroll x·65536) + 128, int(z − scroll y·65536) − (int(y) >> 1) + 32) minus the hotspot, transparency key 1 (or translucent when the unit's +0x10E bit 2 / crosshair mode).
  - **Uncached pieces** (visible, cached flag clear) are drawn every frame straight onto the frame after the sprite (`Model_DrawPiecePrimitivesDirect` `0x004584D0`): point x = int(px + unit x − scroll x·65536) + 128, y = int(unit z − scroll y·65536 − pz) − (int(py + unit y) >> 1) + 32 (note the different rounding: unit and point fractions are added before truncation), no depth, clipped to the view. The non-depth textured quad / polygon routines used there (`0x004C7580`, `0x004C0330`) are assumed to follow the same scan rules (not yet differential-tested).
  - Units with the `zbuffer` flag or under construction use a second path with a depth plane (`MemCache_AllocMaskBufferDual`, composite with attached units, water clipping by sea level): not modelled. The "shaded" path (`0x00459C70`, under construction with a visual option) is not modelled.
- **Polygon scan conversion** (`Gfx_RasterizeDepthPoly` `0x004C1000`, `Gfx_DrawTexturedQuadDepthTested` `0x004C8760` + span `0x004C7A20`, flat span `0x004C06E0`) — **differential-tested** (`tools/orig_harness/raster_harness`):
  - Top vertex = first with the smallest y, bottom = first with the largest y. Reject if max x < clip x0 (textured only), min x > clip x1, max y < clip y0, min y > clip y1. Clamp min y up to clip y0 and max y down to **clip y1** (so the last row is never drawn); nothing if they are equal.
  - Left chain: from the top vertex backwards (index − 1, wrapping) to the bottom; right chain: forwards. Each edge with y_a < y_b: x step = ((x_b − x_a)·65536) / (y_b − y_a) (32-bit, C division), x starts at x_a·65536 + 0xFFFF; texture u/v and depth z start at value·65536 with steps ((b·65536) − (a·65536)) / dy. Rows above the clip are skipped by stepping. Rows y_a .. min(y_b, max y) − 1 are written **through a moving row pointer** (not indexed by y), integer x = x >> 16.
  - Rows min y .. max y − 1: span when right − left > 0. Span steps = (right − left attribute) / width (C division); a left end before clip x0 is stepped forward; the right end is clamped to **clip x1** (so the last column is never drawn); pixels left .. right − 1.
  - Texel = texture[(u >> 16) + (v >> 16) · width] (power-of-two widths use shifts with the same result); quads map vertex 0..3 to (0,0), (w−1,0), (w−1,h−1), (0,h−1); texel colours are copied as-is (no key).
  - With a depth plane: write when depth byte ≤ (z >> 16) & 0xFF, and store it.
- **Shadows** (composite `0x00459200`, 1-byte path): when shadows are on (visual flags bit 2) and the type lacks `noshadow`: ground units (unit +0x110 bit 29 clear, or `digger`), with visual bit 3 and not `canhover`/`floater` → copy the sprite, set every non-key pixel to 0 (`Gaf_CopyFrameToShadow` `0x0045A470`) and blend it through the ALP table at (x + 5, y computed with the **terrain height** at the unit instead of the unit's height), before the sprite. Airborne units get a ground-projected shadow built from the model (`UnitTable_RasterizeAndCompressShadow` `0x0045A790`); we use the ground-unit shadow for them (approximation).
- **Features** (`Map_BlitFeatureGaf` `0x0046A610`): x = footprint x·16/2 + cell x·16 + 128 − scroll x; y = footprint z·16/2 − (sum of the 4 corner cell heights >> 3) + cell z·16 + 32 − scroll y. Shadow sequence frame 0 first (when visual flags bit 4), blended if `shadtrans`; then the sequence frame 0, blended if `animtrans`. Animating features use their cursors; 3D features are drawn through a proxy unit with `Unit_UpdateAndDrawModel` (see "Battle features, wrecks and effects" below).
- **GAF frames**: header {u16 w, u16 h, i16 x offset, i16 y offset, u8 transparency index, u8 compressed, u16 layer count, u32, u32 data, u32}; layered frames hold a table of layer frame offsets (drawn in order; blended layers per their flag). Compressed rows: u16 byte count, then codes: bit 0 → skip code >> 1 pixels; bit 1 → repeat the next byte (code >> 2) + 1 times; else copy (code >> 2) + 1 bytes. A frame drawn at (x, y) lands at (x − x offset, y − y offset). Blend blits mix through the ALP table only when the display context's flag 0x20 is set.
- **Projectiles** (`Gfx_DrawProjectiles` `0x0049BE60`), by the weapon's render type (+0x10C): 0 line from the shot to its tail in HUD palette colour [colour] (with a second, offset line in [colour2] when set); 1/3/6 3DO model with a blended shadow blob at the shot's ground height (`Gfx_DrawModel3doProjected` `0x0046BAE0`, see the shards below); 2 explosion-lens frame; 4 shell GAF (cannon / plasma sizes) at frame (tick − spawn) mod frames; 5 flame stream frame by remaining life; 7 lightning: two passes of jittered segments every 5 world units using **CRT `rand()`**. Shots hidden by the viewer's fog/LOS are skipped. Implemented: lines, sprite shots and model shots (as a sprite with all pieces); colours and frames are supplied by the caller.
  - **Lens shots (render type 2; only MINDGUN in the stock data, no stock unit carries it)** (2026-10-20, read, not built): when the shot's screen point (x = int16(pos.x >> 16) − scroll x + 128, y = int16(pos.z >> 16) − scroll y − (int16(pos.y >> 16) >> 1) + 32) is outside the view rectangle the routine **returns** (later shots are not drawn); else `0x4B9360` (frame, lens GS+0x1AB9B, x, y). The lens (`0x4B91B0`(22, 22) in the battle-start routine `0x420620`, "LensFrame", 4·w·h + 0x18 bytes): w 22, h 22, hotspot (11, 11), +0x10 a table of w·h shorts, +0x14 two w·h byte buffers (capture, output); +9..+0xB zero, **+8 (the frame's transparent index) never written** (heap contents). Table (x87, w as double D): with dx = x − 11, dy = y − 11, r = sqrt(dx² + dy²) (FILD of the integer sum, FSQRT): when ftol(r) ≥ w / 4 (5) the entry is 32000; else s = (11 − r) / D, entry = ftol(dx / s) + 11 + (ftol(dy / s) + 11)·w − (y·w + x) (16-bit). Draw: the 22 × 22 rect of the frame at (x − 11, y − 11) copied **unclipped** (`0x4CBDD1`, rows by the frame's pitch: the side panel / bars of the back buffer and the next row when past the right edge) into the capture buffer; out[i] = entry 32000 ? the +8 byte : capture[i + entry] (the index may fall before the capture, into the table's bytes, or past it, into the output being written); the output blitted keyed by the +8 byte at (x, y) with the hotspot (`Gfx_BlitGafFrame`).
- **Lines** (`Gfx_BlitClipped` `0x004BE950`): clipped one-colour line; we use Bresenham (tie pixels not verified).
- **Render coupling — the original's draw mutates simulation-visible state:**
  1. Drawing a unit syncs its pose (base angles within 7 units; transforms) — the sim can later observe base angles that depend on whether the unit was drawn (already noted under "Piece pose"). The renderer draws from a synced **copy** and never writes the unit.
  2. Lightning projectiles (render type 7) call CRT `rand()` while drawing, on the main thread that also runs the simulation, so the CRT random sequence the simulation sees depends on which lightning shots were on screen (and on the frame rate). The renderer does not draw lightning yet; when it does, it must use its own random source.
  3. Feature drawing writes the deferred-tall-feature bit (0x04) into the map cells each frame; cell flags are otherwise simulation data.
  4. Texture animation cursors advance in the simulation step (`Gaf_AdvanceAllFrameCursors`), not in the draw. Model texture cursors (the client's `TextureLibrary` cursors): at model load (`0x42A140`) every primitive whose texture has two or more frames gets its own cursor, started on frame 0 with that frame's GAF duration (`0x4B8B30`), and is put on the global list unless the texture is a 10-frame team-colour one from logos.GAF. Every simulation step (`0x415B30`) walks the list from last to first through `Gaf_AdvanceFrameCursor` `0x4B8B90`: a countdown of 2 or more counts down, otherwise the next frame shows with its own duration; past the last frame a looping sequence starts over and any other stops. Only pieces drawn straight onto the frame (not cached, e.g. a script's dont-cache, as the metal makers' Go does) show the cursor's frame; cached pieces show frame 0. The client first stepped the frame once per drawn frame, which made metal makers flicker.
- **Battle features, wrecks and effects** (battle mode of `battle_view`, from `sim::Battle` state; the original's `Map_BlitFeatureGaf` `0x0046A610` and `Fx_DrawWorldEffects` `0x00420B00`):
  - A feature cell with a slot: 3D → drawn through a stand-in unit at the slot's position and attitude (`Unit_UpdateAndDrawModel`; we draw the model sprite, no shadow); 2D → the slot's shadow cursor frame (if flag bit 2 and shadows are on) and then its main cursor frame, both plain blits. Without a slot: animating types use the type-wide cursors, others frame 0 of their sequence and shadow; blended per `shadtrans` / `animtrans`.
  - After the projectiles: every debris piece (culled by its origin's screen point): the model piece's vertices turned by its angles (`Math_RotatePointByAngles`, z, y, x), projected around the origin like the uncached-piece path and drawn straight onto the frame. Then every world effect's glow (screen point inside the view): the generated noise frame drawn through the **light table** — pixel p in 0x4F..0x6E sets dst = PALETTE.LHT[(p − 0x4F)·256 + dst] (`Gfx_BlitRectShadowLookup` `0x004CC332`; only when the lighting option, display flag 0x80, is on). Then every effect's shard (`Gfx_DrawModel3doProjected` `0x0046BAE0`, (frame, &point, object, angles): each of the object's +4 points (+0x24) turned by the angles as stored (`Math_RotatePointByAngles`; for a shard the effect's +0x4C) and placed at x = int16((rot.x + point.x) >> 16) + 128, y = int16((point.z - rot.z) >> 16) - (int16((rot.y + point.y) >> 16) >> 1) + 32 (point = view-relative x and z, world y); then its +8 primitives (+0x28, 0x20 each; the first skipped when the selection index +0xC is not -1) in order, no culling or depth: flat (flags bit 0) filled, else only quads, textured with the frame at +0x10 (bit 1: the cursor's current frame, `Gaf_GetCursorFrame`)) and its art frame (keyed blit). The shard object (`0x420620`, 300 x 0x34 at GS+0x1AB9F): 8 points, 6 primitives, selection -1, faces {0,1,2,3} {2,1,6,5} {0,3,4,7} {1,0,7,6} {3,2,5,4} {4,5,6,7} (`0x502BF8`); `0x421700` copies the quad's +0, +0x10..+0x1B (texture / cursor) and flags into every face, and a team texture (flags 2 and 4, not flat) becomes the owner's player-colour frame (`0x4B7F30`) with bit 1 cleared. The copied cursor is never advanced: an animated face keeps the frame it had at the shattering. Built 2026-10-20 (`render::draw_shard`, the client's `note_new_shards`); frame_harness `--shards N` compares it.
  - Generated glows (`Explosion_Frame` `0x00420D20`): square frames of size n with hotspot n/2; pixel value from v = ftol((√(dx² + 1.33·dy²) + noise)/c·32) with c = n/2, dx/dy from the centre, noise = rand()·10/32768; with b = (0x20 − v) as a byte: b < 0x20 → 0x4F + b, b = 0x20 or 0x21 → 0x6E, else the key 0xFF. Sizes 64 − 4k (12), 128 − 7k (15), 200 − 11k (15). The original fills them with `rand()` on the loading thread; the renderer uses its own generator.
  - New draw op `BlitLight` (CPU: the light table turned into a 256 × 256 mixing table; GPU: the same table bound in place of the blend table, no shader change). Sample frames: CPU = GPU (D3D12) with 0 differing pixels.
- **Exact vs approximate (current state):** exact by construction or test — projection, tile terrain, polygon scan conversion (tested), sprite bounds/hotspot/key, piece/primitive order, texture binding, flat colours, team-colour frames, blend table, feature placement, draw order buckets. Approximate — view size and row-bucket counts (assumed), line pixels, air-unit shadows, uncached-piece path (rules assumed equal to the tested ones), animated texture frame selection timing, model projectiles, depth-plane/zbuffer and shaded paths, carried units, LOS/fog, overlays; 3D features drawn as plain model sprites (the original's proxy unit has the z-buffer flag), glows only with the light table.

### Economy (resources, stalls, transfers, wind) — smoke-tested: 20 scenarios × 100 operations (660 settlements over 4,765 unit slots, 115 leaving a player short, 2,703 cloak decisions, 330 slot bookings with 51 refused while stalled, 205 immediate spends, 215 transfers, 202 sharing calls with 63 packets and 61 map shares, 198 wind steps with 149 changes), 0 differences (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/economy.cpp` (`sim/economy.h`), wired in `sim::Battle`; importer `ta_compat/src/battle_economy.cpp` and `to_battle_unit_type` (`battle_types.cpp`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/economy_harness`: a fake game state with ten player records, their own slots, a unit table and definitions with random resource values, stubs for the activate routine and the packets; random settlements, bookings, spends, transfers, sharing and wind steps; every player, slot, log entry, both random streams and the wind compared after each operation.

**Resource slot** (13 dwords, inside each unit at +0xBC and one per player through the record's +0xEC; all zeroed when made, `0x00401070`, whose last dword is the player record it spends from — the owner for units, the record of the player's *state* index for the player's own slot). Floats: energy made, energy used, energy use paid, energy stall, then the previous settlement's energy made and used; the same six for metal; then the player pointer.
- *Stall* is the unpaid backlog carried from earlier settlements; while it is above zero the slot counts as stalled.

**Ways resources are spent**
- *Booking* (`0x004011C0` energy+metal, `0x00401180` energy only): the amounts are always added to "used"; unless the slot is stalled (energy stall > 0, or metal stall > 0 for the two-resource form) they are also added to "paid" and the call returns 1, else 0 and the caller makes no progress. Nothing is taken from the stored amounts here. Callers: build progress (`0x0041BC60`, construction), stockpiling (`MissionTick_BuildWeapon` `0x00402C6B` call), repair healing (`0x0041BD10`, energy only).
- *Immediate* (`0x00401220` energy, `0x00401260` metal; `0x004012A0` both, see "Weapons"): if the slot player's stored amount is at least the cost (an unordered compare fails) it is reduced at once and the cost is added to "used".
- *Cloak upkeep* is immediate and lives in the settlement (below).

**Settlement** (`Player_SettleResources` `0x00401360`, stdcall(player)). Totals are 8 float accumulators that start at 0; the player's storage capacities are reset to 0. Every live unit (flags bit 28) of the player's range, in table order:
