1. Structures (flags bit 29) only while activated (activate bit 0); mobile units while activated *or* moving (flags & 0xC). The type's energy use: not ≥ 0 (negative or unordered) is production of its negation; otherwise it is added to "used" and, when the slot's energy stall is not above zero, to "paid" (*paid* flag; mobile units ignore the flag).
2. Activated structures only, at most one of, in this order: extractor (extractsmetal > 0) adds the unit's extraction rate (+0x58, see "Unit creation") to metal made *only when paid*; else metal maker (makesmetal byte ≠ 0) adds that integer to metal made *only when paid*; else wind generator (> 0) adds wind fraction × windgenerator to energy made (no paid check); else tidal (> 0) adds tidal strength × tidalgenerator.
3. Complete units (build fraction left compares equal to 0, or unordered): energymake and metalmake are added; the type's energy/metal storage are added to the player's capacities.
4. Cloak, unless the *settled* player is active with controller 3: if the unit wants cloak (flags bit 11), is not blocked (bit 12) and the hold-off step (+0xB0) is not after the current step, the cost is ftol(cloakcostmoving if moving else cloakcost); it is paid from the slot player's stored energy when the cost is not greater than it (unordered pays), adding to "used". Then `Unit_SetActivateState(unit, 4, paid)` is called for **every** such unit (cloaked bit set or cleared).
5. The slot's 8 values are added to the accumulators (each sum stored as float).
- **Production credit rule** (used for every production above, for transfers, for reclaim and for the deathmatch respawn): when the slot's player is active with controller 2, difficulty 0 adds amount×0.5 and difficulty 1 adds amount×0.7 (written as `make − amount × −0.5/−0.7` with double constants); anything else adds the amount. The product and the sum are double, the result stored as float.
- After the units the player's own slot is added the same way, **except energy made**: that sum stays at double precision (it never becomes a float before it is used below). If the player flags (+0x149) bit 0 is set — the same bit the commander's death clears — the storage bonuses (+0xDC energy, +0xE0 metal) are added to the capacities.
- Recorded: energy income = float(energy made), usage = energy used; metal income/usage likewise; four double totals (produced/consumed per resource) gain the double value (energy made unrounded; the others from the float sums).
- **Paying.** For each resource the pool is float(made + stored). (a) If the stall total exceeds the pool, the stall fraction is float(pool / stall total) and the whole pool is taken; otherwise the fraction is 1 and the stall total is taken. (b) The rest is kept at double precision; if the paid total exceeds it, the paid fraction is float(rest / paid total) and nothing is left, else the fraction is 1 and the paid total is subtracted. The pool becomes float(what is left). Comparisons with unordered operands take the "fits" branch.
- Stored amount = the pool; if it exceeds the capacity it is capped and the excess (double) is added to the wasted total.
- **Slot close** for every live unit and then the player's own slot: "previous" values take this settlement's made and used; made, used and paid reset to 0; new stall = float((paid − paid fraction × paid) + (stall − stall fraction × stall)), each part in double. So a short player stalls every slot that had paid use, in proportion, and a stalled slot is paid back first next time.
- Units that died keep their slot values; a new unit's slot is zeroed at creation.

**Where it runs.** The players stage (`0x464F80`) gives each player (after AI, unit table refresh, sight and the view player's minimap composite) a 30-step turn: when the record's next-turn step (+0xF0, set to the current step by the player set-up `0x00464700`) is not after the current step it advances by 30, then (1) the local player's victory/defeat checks, (2) the settlement if the player is active with controller 1/2/3, state ≠ 10, has live units or never made any, has controller 1 or 2, the game-ending flag (GS+0x3923B bit 2) is clear and the end countdown (GS+0x39239, signed) is negative, (3) for the view player the radar/sonar/cloak detection pass `0x00467440`, fog minimap and network view state. A player whose turn is not due skips (1)–(3). Battle start runs the players stage once and then resets the stored amounts (`0x00465E30`), so the first settlement is effectively discarded except for the slot close.
- Player set-up (`0x00464700`): next turn = current step; doubles, amounts, incomes, usages, capacities and share thresholds zeroed; own slot made (0x34 bytes) and initialised; flags bit 0 cleared; the storage bonuses are **not** touched.
- Starting resources: skirmish commander spawn (`0x00496EE0`) sets flags bit 0 and the bonuses to the start energy/metal (at least 200), the same rule as `0x00496E90` (campaign start and deathmatch respawn); stored amounts come from `0x00465E30` (skirmish: the settings' integers as floats; campaign: the map's values; network: side start × 100).

**Transfers** (`0x00464B30` energy, `0x00464C60` metal, stdcall(from, to, amount, sending)): nothing if either index is 10. When sending, the amount is clamped to the giver's stored amount (when greater). Then a zero (or unordered) amount does nothing. When sending, the giver's own slot spends it immediately (failure ignored). The receiver's own slot gets it as production by the credit rule — so it arrives at the receiver's next settlement. Sending finally emits packet 0x16 (subtype 1 energy, 2 metal); received packets call the same routine with sending = 0. Callers: share dialog, network packets, the cheat command, automatic sharing.

**Automatic sharing** (`Share_AutoShareResources` `0x00457D30`), at the end of each step of a network game, for the local player, only with the net flag (GS+0x2A44 bit 0): every 60 steps, if the side's share-metal flag (side record +0x97 bit 1) is set and stored metal exceeds the metal threshold (+0xE4), the receiver is the *last* slot 0..9 that is active, playing (controller 1/2/3, state ≠ 10, live units or none made), controller 3, side byte +0x94 = 1, allied (+0x108 indexed by slot) and holding less (unordered counts as less). The amount is (stored − threshold) × 0.33333334f, compared at double precision with the receiver's capacity − stored and taking the smaller (stored as float). The energy part follows with bit 2, threshold +0xE8 and factor 0.5. Every 450 steps with bit 5 the explored map goes to every slot passing the same filter.

**Wind** (`Wind_Step` `0x00490C40`, stage 8 of a step, and once while the map loads): when the next-change step (GS+0x37EC4, unsigned) is before the current step: next += (rand()×10 / 32768 + 5) × 30 (one rand(); 150..420 steps); speed = min + RandMod(max − min) (one battle RNG draw unless max − min < 2 or ≥ 2³¹ as unsigned); if the speed is not 0 the heading = RandMod(65536) (second draw); drift x = −2 × rounded-sin(heading, speed), z = −2 × rounded-cos (y is never written); fraction = float(speed / denominator) (integer divide in double), capped at 1.0; changed = 1. Otherwise changed = 0. The denominator is set to 5000 and the next step to 0 just before the loading call. Readers: the settlement (fraction), the unit pass's wind notification (changed, heading, speed), shots and fires (drift).

**Map metal and environment** (`Map_LoadTnt` `0x00483610`): every map cell's metal byte (+7) = the OTA schema's SurfaceMetal when it is ≥ 0 and the TNT is version 0x2000, else 0; 0x1020 maps then take each cell's attribute byte 6. Wind range: the OTA's minwindspeed/maxwindspeed when ≥ 0 on a 0x2000 map, else the TNT header's (0x1020: dwords 10/11; 0x2000: 100 and 2000). Tidal strength: the OTA's tidalstrength unless negative (0.5). The OTA loader (`0x00435DA0`) presets all of these to −1 and reads them with default 0 (wind, gravity from [GlobalHeader]; SurfaceMetal from the selected schema), so with an OTA they are always used. Gravity: see "Simulation completeness" (GS+0x14263 = ftol(g × 65536/900), default 0x1FDB). Finally (`0x00422040`) every cell holding an indestructible feature (flags bit 9) with nonzero metal stamps float→int(metal)'s low byte into the cells of the feature's footprint from that cell (cells outside the map skipped). Extractors sum (metal + 1) over their footprint at creation (see "Unit creation").

**Engine mapping.** `UnitTickState` holds the unit slot (`metal_make`, `energy_use`, `metal_use` and the added fields), `Battle::player_economy[p]` the record's economy and its own slot, `PlayerSlot::energy_stored/metal_stored` the stored amounts, `Battle::wind`, `Battle::end_game` (the victory checks own it), `BattleSettings::tidal_strength`. Booking hooks (`pay_energy`, `use_resources_if_not_stalled`) are now built in; `Battle::step_wind()` is stage 8; `Battle::auto_share_resources()` the network stage; `BattleOutside::player_victory_turn/player_sensor_turn` surround the settlement; `resources_shared` / `explored_map_shared` report sharing packets.

**Open:** schema selection for SurfaceMetal (`0x00436860`: by difficulty for campaigns, by Network N type and start-position count for skirmish/multiplayer) is not imported (the viewer reads Schema 0); the end-countdown's value at battle start was not traced (negative during play); `Ai_TickUnitActivateByEconomy` (`0x00408670`, AI turning units on and off) belongs to the AI.

### Client (playable game: front end, loop, input, interface, sound, music) — smoke-tested: headless scripted runs (Moon Quartet, ARM and CORE, 640×480 and 1024×768), CPU = GPU (Direct3D 12) on every screenshot, window runs with sound
Interface checks against the original (2026-10-17, smoke): frame_harness `--hud` — 17 scenes (640×480 and 1024×768, with and without line of sight): top bar 0 differing pixels in every scene; 16 placements (8 fitting) 0 differing pixels; Shift order view 0 differing pixels among 7,696 changed; pointer animation 13,500 steps against the original's routine, 0 differences — and gui_harness pointer events through the original's own interface pass against ta_play headless frames (panel button looks with either mouse button, page arrows, tabs, an order button on and off, a "+3" count: 0 differing pixels; right release on NEXT / PREV sets the next / previous page flags) — see the "(exact)" entries below. Harness limits met on the way: with line of sight, some scenes with units in view get an all-black fog from the original's frame (also without `--hud`, also with another branch's build; such scenes are left out of the placement / Shift checks); model shots can fault in `0x004B9360` (harness set-up, both pre-existing).
Implementation: `client/` (engine side, no game names: `game_client.cpp` rules, `hud.cpp` drawing, `queued_orders.cpp` the Shift order view, `commands.cpp` the order adapter, `front.cpp` SDL3 window / headless runner, `audio_sdl.cpp` mixer, `canvas.cpp` rectangles and one-bit fonts), importer `ta_compat/src/client_content.cpp` (GUI, FNT, PCX, side data, sounds, build menus, shot looks), `client_battle_setup.cpp` (skirmish set-up **adapter**), `client_scene.cpp` (the battle view's scene), executable `ta_compat/tools/ta_play.cpp`. Nothing here writes simulation state except what the original's interface writes too (unit flag bits 4, 6, 7, 22–25 and the group number, orders through the command routine).

**Main loop** (`0x00496790`, once per pass): the step budget (`SpeedCtrl_BudgetSimSteps`, see "Battle step" and `sim::StepBudget`) gives 0..5 steps from the wall clock (`GetTickCount × 30 / 1000`, game speed 1..20, ×0.1 per unit, 10 normal); `Game_RunSimulationStep` runs them; then the hot keys (`0x00495E90`), scrolling (`0x0041CE90`), the visible-unit list (`0x0048BAE0`), the selection-changed panel refresh (when GS+0x37EBE bit 4: `0x0041B2E0`), and the frame (`Render_DrawBattleFrame(1, 1)`). The frame is drawn after the steps with no interpolation (positions are the last step's). Lingering sight expires once per pass after the steps. Speed setting `0x00490DF0(speed, send)`: clamped 1..20, both the wanted and effective speed set; console text "Game Speed Normal" at 10, else "Game Speed" + two spaces + sign + |speed − 10|; packet 0x19 in network games. Registry defaults: gamespeed 10, scrollspeed 0x20, textlines 10, textscroll 10, damagebars off, Interface Type 0.

**Screen.** Frame w × h (640 × 480 and up); the battle view is x 128..w−1, y 32..h−33. Interface art per side: `sidedata.tdf` [SIDEn] intgaf → `anims\<intgaf>.GAF` sequences PANELTOP (frame 0 at x 129, y 0; at wider screens further pieces are PANELBOT's frame, repeated), PANELBOT (x 129 repeated, y h−32), PANELSIDE (0, 0). Frames are drawn with their top-left at the given point (hotspot added back).

**Interface colours**: `palettes\GUIPAL.PAL` (256 × RGBA); entry k maps to the game palette index with the smallest |Δr|+|Δg|+|Δb| (first of equals) — the table at GS+0xDCB (`0x004AC7D0`, built when a battle loads). Used: [0] black (bar backgrounds, inner drag rectangle), [4] the damaged part of info bars and blocked placement, [10] green (healthy, production numbers, selection outlines, radar circles), [12] red (low health, consumption numbers, jammer circles), [14] yellow (medium health, minimap view rectangle, shot dots), [15] text and the outer drag rectangle. Info-bar unit names use raw palette index 0x53.

**Fonts** (`fonts\*.FNT`): u8 height, u8, u8 raise, u8 first character, then a u16 offset per character from the first (0 = none); a glyph is u8 width followed by width × height bits, most significant first, rows packed back to back. Text at (x, y) starts at row y − raise; set bits take the foreground colour, clear bits the background unless it equals the "skip" colour (transparent). Width = sum of glyph widths up to the end or a newline; a width limit drops trailing characters. Side fonts: sidedata `font=` (console) for bars and console.

**Side data regions** (`0x00431A60`): [SIDEn] name, nameprefix, commander, intgaf, font, energycolor, metalcolor and rectangles {x1, y1, x2, y2}: LOGO, ENERGYBAR/NUM/MAX/0, METALBAR/NUM/MAX/0, TOTALUNITS, TOTALTIME, ENERGY/METAL PRODUCED/CONSUMED (top bar), LOGO2, UNITNAME, DAMAGEBAR, UNITMETALMAKE/USE, UNITENERGYMAKE/USE, MISSIONTEXT, UNITNAME2, DAMAGEBAR2, NAME, DESCRIPTION, RELOAD1–3 (bottom bar, rows for a 480-row screen, moved by h − 480). [CANBUILD] lists build options (see "Unit definitions").

**Top bar** (inside `Render_DrawBattleFrame`) — **compared with the original**, see "Top bar (exact)" below. (Summary: the stored amounts shown ease toward the real ones; energy rates in whole units, metal rates with one decimal; incomes and usages refreshed every 30 steps by the player's display timer.)

**Bottom bar** (`Hud_DrawSelectionInfo` `0x0046A860`) — **compared with the original** (frame_harness `--hud`: 28 scenes at 640×480, 1024×768 and 1920×1080, own and other units hovered, 0 differing pixels over the bar's rows): see "Bottom bar (exact)" below. (Earlier summary: the unit under the pointer: an unseen unit shows "Unidentified object"; the name centred on UNITNAME.x1 in 0x53; DAMAGEBAR; own units' make/use numbers, kills, status; the second line.)

**Marks in the view.** Health bars (option, or units in a group; own units): at (sx, sy + 10): [0] rectangle x−17..x+17, y−2..y+2, then x−16..x−16 + hp·32/max (unsigned), y−1..y+1 in [10] above 2/3 of max, [14] above 1/3, else [12]; the group digit at (sx, sy + 14). Selection outline (option GS+0x37F2F bit 2): see "Selection outline (exact)" below. Drag box: outer rectangle [15], inner (1 pixel in) [0]; placement: two nested outlines in [10] when it fits, else [4] (see "Placement outline (exact)"). Health bars and group digits: see "Health bars (exact)" (the option alone decides).

**Console** (`0x00463CA0` add, `0x00464060` draw, `0x00463EF0` expiry): see "Console (exact)" below (the game's picture font, not plain text).

**Scrolling** (`0x0041CE90`): step = scrollspeed × (scaled ticks since the last pass), at most 128; left/up when the arrow key is held or the pointer is on column/row 0 (right/down on the last column/row); while TALK.GUI is open keys don't scroll. Then clamp (`0x0041C3C0`): a negative scroll becomes 0, else one beyond the **scroll extent** less the view (GS+0x1422B = map width × 16 − 32, GS+0x1422F = height × 16 − 128, minus the view's width / height in pixels) becomes that limit — negative on a map smaller than the view, which the original allows; the minimap rectangle follows. Middle button drag scrolls in whole cells (`0x0041CD50`). Minimap click (`0x0041D0F0`): scroll = (click − minimap origin) × scroll extent / minimap size − view size / 2, then the clamp (checked: frame_harness `--minimap` compares the clamped scroll).

**Keys** (`Input_MapVkAndEnqueue` `0x004C1D50` → `0x00495E90`): letters lower-case ASCII, Ctrl+letter = 0xAA + letter index, digits ASCII, Ctrl+digit = 0xC5.., F1–F12 = 0xE2.., Ctrl+F = 0xCE.., Pause = 0xF8; arrows, Shift, Ctrl, Alt are polled (0xF4–0xFB). Actions: Pause toggles the pause flag (packet 0x19); +/= and −/_ change speed (not past 1/20; not in some network modes); `~ ! # * toggle damage bars; 1–9 select group n (Shift adds; with the alternate option Alt instead), Ctrl+1–9 make group n (selected units join, others leave), Ctrl+A select all, Ctrl+C commander (select and centre), Ctrl+D self-destruct (toggles a pending one), Ctrl+other letters select by the units' CTRL_x categories, Esc cancels the order mode (else deselects), Enter chat, t/T track, n next, F keys squads and help.

**Selection** (unit flags: 0x10 selected, 0x20 selectable, 0x40/0x80 on-screen marks): a unit is selectable when own, flag 0x20, fully built, not just transferred and not inside a carrier that forbids it. Click (`0x0048C7F0`): Shift toggles (selecting speaks "select"); otherwise every unit of the table loses 0x10/0x40/0x80, the visible own units get 0x40, the clicked one 0x10 and speaks. Box (`0x0048C390`): the drag's two corners are kept as world points and projected with the current scroll; units whose screen point lies inside are selected (Shift toggles); afterwards on-screen marks are redone; one unit speaks "select", several play "SelectMultipleUnits". Deselect all (`0x0048BD00`). Group select (`0x0048D9A0`): units with the group number (+0xAC); Shift keeps others.

**Interface types** (registry "Interface Type", GS+0x37EFA). Left click (`0x00498F70`): placement mode: place when it fits ("OKTOBUILD", else "NOTOKTOBUILD"), Shift stays in the mode; select pointer: click-select; another order pointer: the command with the mode's intent (Shift stays); type 1 with the default mode and nothing to select: deselect all. Right click (`0x00499100`): any order mode is cancelled; in the default mode type 0 deselects (with a Ctrl-like flag it starts drag scrolling), type 1 gives the default command. The client defaults to type 1 (`--left-click` for type 0).

**Command routine** (`0x0048CF30`): over the selected units except the unit under the pointer: centre = mean of their whole x and z; each unit's mission = resolution of the intent (`sim::resolve_mission`) or the given kind; standing fire / move orders are skipped for types lacking abilities bit 1 / bit 0; when the mission's default flags have bit 1 and the unit's squared distance to the centre (hi 32 bits of the 16.16 squares) is at most 3000 × count, the destination keeps the unit's offset from the centre. Then `MissionOrder_IssueOrCancelMatch` (`0x0043AFC0`): with Shift, an existing primary order of the same kind, same target (or none given) and position within 16 world units is removed instead (cancel); otherwise the order is queued (Shift) or replaces the list (`Unit_EnqueueOrder`).

**Pointer** (`0x0048D220` → `0x0043E490` per selected unit, smallest id wins): 1 attack in range, 2 air strike, 3 out of range, 4 capture, 5 guard, 6 repair, 7 patrol, 8 air pickup, 9 teleport, 10 resurrect, 11 reclaim, 12 load, 13 unload / air landing, 14 move, 15 select, 16 build, 17 enemy (type 1 default), 18 ally / reclaimable (type 1 default), 19 normal. Looks from `anims\CURSORS.GAF` (cursorattack, cursortoofar, cursorcapture, cursordefend, cursorrepair, cursorpatrol, cursorpickup, cursorteleport, cursorreclamate, cursorload, cursorunload, cursormove, cursorselect, cursorfindsite, cursorred, cursorgrn, cursornormal, cursorhourglass, cursorrevive); type 1 shows the plain pointer, red over enemies, green over allies.

**Pointer rule** (2026-10-20; `0x0048D220` stdcall (intent GS+0x2CC3), called by the interface pass `0x00499200` while the pointer is over the view or minimap and no placement runs; checked by targeting_harness op 11, which calls `0x0043E490` itself against `sim::pointer_look` on its random battles: 620 looks, 0 differing). The list is the local player's unit range with unit flag 0x10 (selected), the unit under the pointer (GS+0x2CBA) taken out; each gives `0x0043E490(intent, unit, unit under the pointer, &pointer world position GS+0x2CAA)` and the lowest number wins (19 when none is lower); an empty list gives 15 when the intent is 1 and the unit under the pointer is the local player's own selectable, finished unit (flag 0x20, build fraction left 0, no hand-over, not carried or carried by a unit with 0x40000000), else 19. The look is the art `GS+0x1487F + 4n` (CURSORS.GAF by number), restarted only when the number changes. Per unit (abilities = type +0x245, type flags +0x241; "enemy" / "ally" by the unit's owner's alliance byte for the target's owner state, as resolution; "wreck" = the resolution's wreck test at the pointer position):
- **1 default**: interface type 1 (GS+0x37EFA = 1): own selectable finished unit 15; enemy 17; ally 18; resurrect or reclaim ability over a wreck 18; else 19. Type 0: attack ability and enemy → as intent 3; reclaim ability and enemy → as intent 12; else a target the unit can repair that is unfinished 6, an own selectable finished unit 15; resurrect ability and wreck 10; reclaim ability and wreck 11; move ability 14, else 19.
- **3 attack**: attack ability and a dropped first weapon (type +0x1EE, weapon flag 0x100) 2; no attack ability 19; a unit that moves (motion object) 1; a target: slot 0 reaches it (`0x49ABB0`) 1, else 3; a ground point: out of range of slot 0 from the unit's position (`0x49AA80`) 3, in range 1 unless slot 0's weapon is anti-air (flag 0x20000) 3. So attacking the ground with a mobile unit always shows the attack look.
- **4 special attack**: ability 0x4000 needed (else 19); the owner's stored energy (+0x8C) below slot 2's energy per shot (+0xC0) 3; metal per shot (+0xC4) at most the stored metal (+0x98) 1, else 3.
- **2 move**: move ability (else 19); resurrect ability and wreck 10; a target and a unit that moves: capture ability → enemy 4 (reclaim not tried); else enemy the unit can reclaim (`0x489960`) 11; ally the unit can repair 6; the unit flies and the target is an air base 13; can pick it up 8 (flying) / 12; guard ability and ally 5; else 14.
- **5 unload**: load ability 13. **6 pickup**: can pick the target up 8 / 12. **7 guard**: guard ability and ally: 5 for a flying unit, else 19 when the target flies, else 5. **8 repair**: can repair the target 6. **9 patrol**: patrol ability 7. **11 teleport**: 9. **12 reclaim**: reclaim ability and wreck 11; a target the unit can reclaim 11. **13 capture**: capture ability and a target of another owner 4. **14 build**: build options (+0x156) and a unit that moves 16. Anything else 19.
- The interface type is one global: the default command's resolution (`0x43F0E0`) reads the same GS+0x37EFA, so the client keeps the targeting environment's command mode equal to its interface setting.

**GUI files** (`guis\*.GUI`, TDF): [GADGETn] { [COMMON] { id (0 header/panel origin, 1 button, 5 label, 7 font, ...), assoc, name, xpos, ypos, width, height, attribs, colorf, colorb, texturenumber, fontnumber, active, commonattribs, help } plus per kind: header totalgadgets, [VERSION], panel (a commongui sequence), crdefault, escdefault; button status, text, quickkey (ASCII); font filename. Gadget positions are relative to the header's. Button art: the gadget name's sequence in `anims\<GUI name>.GAF`, else `anims\commongui.GAF`, else (downloaded units) `anims\<UNIT>_GADGET.GAF` or `unitpics\<UNIT>.PCX`. Attribs: 0x10 tab (ORDERS/BUILD), 0x20 build button, 0x40 order mode, 0x100 staged switch, 2 page arrows, 0 immediate (STOP). Frames (`Gui_DrawGadget` `0x004A5F40`): plain buttons 0 normal, 1 pressed, 2 disabled; staged buttons (FIREORD 6, MOVEORD 6, ONOFF 5, CLOAK 5 frames) show their stage (0..n−1), then the "mixed" frame (selection disagrees), then disabled (count−2) and pressed (count−1).
- Panels: `<prefix>GEN.GUI` the orders panel (all order buttons; unavailable ones disabled by the selection's abilities, `0x0041B2E0`: move 0x80, attack 0x10, guard 0x20, patrol 0x40, load 0x100, repair 0x200, reclaim 0x400, capture 0x1000, special 0x4000, on/off bit 2, cloak bit 13, standing orders bits 0/1, merged over the selection with "mixed" stages); `<prefix>MAIN2.GUI` with nothing selected; one selected unit with build pages shows `<UNITNAME><page>.GUI` when its flags bit 22 is set (page in bits 23–25, 1-based; page 0 with HAS_GUI0 shows `<UNITNAME>0.GUI`). BUILD sets bit 22 and page 1; ORDERS clears it; PREV/NEXT wrap the page.
- Build menu pages: see "Build menus (exact)" below (pages from `<UNITNAME><p>.GUI` or the viewing side's `<prefix>DL.GUI`; a download entry's MENU is the page + 1 and its BUTTON the gadget record − 4). Factory buttons: left +1 (Shift +5, "ADDBUILD"), right −1 ("SUBBUILD") on the build-count order; the queued count is drawn on the button; mobile builders enter placement mode.

**Minimap (exact)** (`0x004669B0` set-up at battle load: `0x00466780` picture; `0x00466C20` fog picture; `0x00466DC0` marks; `0x00466B70` view rectangle; `0x00466B00` blit; checked with frame_harness `--minimap`, the original's own routines on a pattern against `GameClient::draw_minimap_onto`: Coast To Coast (wide, 640×480, mapping), Great Divide (tall, 1024×768), Moon Quartet and Painted Desert (square, 1920×1080 / 1280×1024), Ice Scream (very tall, 800×600, no line of sight), 25 scenes with selections, the pointer's unit, up to 32 shots and both blink phases: 0 differing pixels, the clamped scroll equal). Its shape comes from the **scroll extent** (above), not the whole map: when the extent is narrower than tall the picture is 126 high and extent-width × 126 / extent-height wide, centred across (x origin (126 − w)/2), else 126 wide, centred down. (The client used the whole map's size, 128 rows too tall: the bottom of the box showed the map file's filler colour — the reported bleed.) **Picture**: the map file's own picture (header word 10 its offset: u32 width, u32 height, pixels; word 11 bit 0 says it is there) taken as stored, else one sampled from the tiles at twice the minimap's size (pixel (x, y) is the map point extent-width × x / (2w), extent-height × y / (2h); tile numbers past the tile count read tile 0); then each minimap pixel mixes the 2 × 2 block (2x, 2y) of that source with the mixing table: top = T[p(2x,2y)][p(2x+1,2y)], bottom = T[p(2x,2y+1)][p(2x+1,2y+1)], pixel = T[top][bottom] (reads past the source are undefined). **Fog picture**, rebuilt in the viewed player's 30-step sensor turn (after its settlement; also by a whole sight rebuild): per pixel the 32-pixel block (row y × (map height/2) / h, column x × (map width/2) / w of the whole map's blocks): not explored by the viewed player → interface colour 0; explored but no sight count → the picture's pixel through the grey remap (the display's +0xCC table); else the pixel. **Marks**, drawn every step onto a copy of the fog picture right after the viewed player's sight updates in the players' stage: every unit in table order with a type, shown when the radar cheat (option 0x200) is on, or neither line-of-sight option is on, or it carries the radar/sonar marks 0x300, or the viewed player owns it: position (x·w / extent-width, (z − y/2)·h / extent-height) (16-bit whole parts, truncating division); radlogo frame [the owner's colour] unless its damage timer (+0xFA) runs and the blink bit is clear (the bit toggles every 8 steps, counted from the battle's load at the end of each step); radlogohigh over the pointer's unit; a selected unit that is switched on or not on/off-able draws 32-segment circles (points at angles k·2048 with the rounded trig, joined by the line routine) of radar and sonar reach in interface colour 10 and jammer reaches in 12, radius = w × reach / extent-width; an antiweapons type (type bit 29) also draws, for each weapon slot holding an interceptor (weapon bit 30), a circle of (coverage − 512) scaled in colour 15, dashed (every other segment, starting with the second, or the first when the blink bit is set) while the slot has stockpiled shots; each unit drawn goes to the pick list (id, minimap x, y). Then every shot of the table (deleted ones too): weapons without bits 29/30 and without bit 6 draw a pixel of colour 14 when the viewed player sees the ground block under it (z − y/2) (its sight counts with line of sight, else its explored map) or owns its player slot; bit 29/30 weapons draw nuclogo [the slot's colour] when seen or when the viewed player owns the shooter (a shooterless nuke faults in the original). **On screen**: the copy at the origin, then the view rectangle in colour 14: x0 = w·scrollx / extent-width + origin, x1 = w·(view width rounded down to 16) / extent-width − 1 + x0 (likewise y), four lines over the whole screen. Engine: `client/src/minimap.cpp` (`minimap_box`, picture / composite / redraw, the step hooks `minimap_player_stage`, `minimap_sensor_turn`, `minimap_step_end` called from the client world's battle outside); importer: the source picture and the grey table in `client_content.cpp`.
- **Minimap over time (exact)** (2026-10-17; checked with frame_harness `--minimap-steps N`: after each scene N more battle steps, the original's marks, fog-picture and blink routines called at the client's own hooks inside each step, 0-2 frames per step with view moves, the original's blit onto a screen kept from frame to frame against the client's frame: 5 runs — Moon Quartet, Coast To Coast, Great Divide, Painted Desert, Ice Scream; 640×480 … 1920×1080; mapping, no mapping, no line of sight, dithered fog — 1,102 frames over 1,080 steps, 0 differing pixels, scroll equal). Two surfaces made at the battle load: RADAR_MAPPED (the fog picture) and RADAR_FINAL (fog picture + marks). Per step, in the players' stage, for the viewed player: after its sight updates the marks are drawn onto a fresh copy of RADAR_MAPPED into RADAR_FINAL (so marks never accumulate; a selected unit's circles are in every step's picture); later in the same player's pass, in its 30-step turn, RADAR_MAPPED is recomposed — only when minimap flag 4 (GS+0x142F1) is set, which the sight-count increments/decrements and explored-map reveals set (any change) and the loading sets; so the step's marks use the fog picture of before the turn, the new one shows from the next step. A whole sight rebuild (`0x4816A0`: option switches LOS / Mapping / LOSType / NowISee, view changes through it) sets flag 4, composes and draws the marks at once. The blink counter (`0x466580`, at the end of the step after the effects): counts 7 → 0, then toggles flag bit 1 and restarts at 7 (loaded with 7, bit 1 clear): the bit flips every 8 steps; it only gates the dots of units with a running damage timer (and the dashed interceptor rings' phase). **On screen**: the frame (`Render_DrawBattleFrame`) blits RADAR_FINAL and draws the view rectangle only when flag bit 2 is set (and clears it): bit 2 is set by the marks pass, by the fog composition and by every scrolling routine (arrow / edge scroll, drag, minimap jump, tracking, view recall); the screen surface keeps the last blit otherwise. Since the marks surface only changes in the marks pass, drawing it every frame (the client) shows the same pixels. The **flicker** the user saw was the client's GPU path, not the minimap: the per-frame minimap sprite was marked as lasting, so the GPU backend cached its texture by address and showed old minimaps (old circle positions) whenever a later frame's sprite reused the memory (headless `CLIENT_PROFILE` run with `--gpu-check`: 48–78 pixels per frame differed from the CPU frame, all in the minimap; 0 after).

**Sound.** The sound bank (GS+0x33A0F count, names at +0x33E13 × 32, samples at +0x33A13) holds allsound.tdf's sections (UI sounds: IMMEDIATEORDERS, ORDERSBUTTON, BUILDBUTTON, NEXTBUILDMENU, ADDBUILD, SUBBUILD, OKTOBUILD, NOTOKTOBUILD, SETFIREORDERS, SETMOVEORDERS, SPECIALORDERS, SELECTSQUAD, CREATESQUAD, SelectMultipleUnits, MessageArrived, TREEBURN, ...), then the weapons' soundstart/soundhit/soundwater names (`sounds\<name>.wav`). World sounds (`0x0047F300`, the projectile and feature hooks): played only when the viewed player sees the cell (sight counts with the line-of-sight option, else the explored bit); positioned only in 3D mode. Unit notices (`0x0047F850` → speech queue `0x0047FAD0`, for live, not dying units of the viewed player): the speech table at `0x005086DC` (24-byte entries: priority, minimum tick gap, text key, console text) — 1 select, 2 underattack ("Under Attack"), 3 activate, 4 deactivate, 5 ok, 6 arrived ("Arrived"), 7 cant ("Cannot Comply"), 8 unitcomplete ("Nanolathe Complete"), 9 build, 10 repair, 11 working, 12 load, 13 unload, 14 cloak ("Cloaked"), 15 uncloak ("Visible"), 16 capture, 17–22 count5..count0; the queue holds 8 by priority. The key names a sound in the unit's `gamedata\sound.tdf` category (soundcategory = section index; keys select1, ok1, arrived1, cant1, underattack, build, repair, working, count5..0, ...). Under-attack notices are skipped for units on screen. The client runs the notice queue and the world sound rules below ("Unit notices", "World sounds").

**Shots** by weapon render type: 0 line in interface colour [color] (second line [color2]), 1/3/6 the weapon's 3DO model, 4 FX.GAF cannonshell (plasma sizes by damage: not built), 5 flamestream, 2 lens, 7 lightning (drawn as a line here).

**Engine notes.** The client sets unit flag bits exactly as the original's interface (they live in the shared unit flags; the simulation reads 0x10 only to drop the selection of units that stop being selectable). Commands go through `client/commands.cpp` (adapter: `Battle::order`, `order_build_count`, `order_mobile_build`, `targeting()` for resolution) until the game-flow command layer exists; the step is `client::step_battle` (adapter for `Battle::step`). Both adapters switch to the game-flow agent's `sim/commands.h` (`issue_to_selected`, `issue_or_cancel`) and `Battle::step` / `end_frame` by `__has_include` once that work is merged; the set-up adapter loads the AI agent's computer player (`ta/ai_profile.h`: `to_ai_type`, `load_ai_profile`, `collect_ai_metal_sites`) the same way. A trial merge of both branches built and ran the same scripted game (the computer player built extractors and a solar collector). Fixed while integrating: `Battle`'s motion hooks gave goal objects handle 0, so an order's goal counted as absent and was never detached when the order was destroyed (use after free when a move order replaced a build order); goals now get nonzero stand-in handles.

**Minimap orders** (`0x00498D00`): a command given on the minimap goes to the map point (scroll extent × (pixel − origin) / size, 16-bit whole units) at the terrain's interpolated height there, with no target unit; a left click there in the default mode moves the view.

**Open / not built:** the statistics' frame 1 (the first pass after the first column comes) shows the focus ring around Main Menu applied twice in the original (1,727 pixels; the GUI pass's redraw rule that causes it is not read); (the dimmed game menu under the exit menu differed in 446 pixels: explained and fixed 2026-10-17 — the dimming routine indexes the shade row with the pixel as a signed byte, see "Chat line (exact)"; not re-run with gui_harness); the restart dialog's mission name stays empty in gui_harness (the client shows it, as read); campaign Restart (the client's mission sessions offer no restart launcher: the button closes the dialog) and the campaign end screen's other buttons; network games' end state 0 notice and present flags; the RELOAD bars, TOTALUNITS/TOTALTIME regions, lightning/plasma shot looks, the unit info screen (F1), movies (intro, credits), the TABMENU's own screens (allies, share, control: the multiplayer part's), the game menu's Help and Briefing pages, Ctrl+D's cancel of a pending self-destruct, slider art (drawn as plain boxes). Loading screen: other machines' loading progress and "has loaded" flags are not carried to the client's loading screen (their bars stay empty; the network part would feed `LoadingState::players`), and the client's loading runs on the main thread (the screen only redraws at its progress reports, at most every 200 ms; the window does not take input while loading). Pointer: the Ctrl + right-button view drag does not warp the pointer to the centre and back nor drop the remainder per pass as the original does (`0x0041CC60` / `0x0041CD50`); the hold and focus changes were not tried with a real mouse.

**Front end** (implementation: `client/front_end.h` entry points and runner, `client/menu.h` layout screens, `client/music.h`, `client/src/settings.cpp`; importer `ta_compat/src/client_menus.cpp` (art, sounds, fonts, maps, the battle session) and `client_screens.cpp` (the screens); `ta_play` starts with the menus unless battle options or `--direct` are given). Smoke-tested headless: main menu → single player → skirmish → loading → battle → Tab menu → Exit → Exit to Menu → statistics → main menu (2026-10-17: Exit to Menu now asks first and goes straight to the main menu; Exit → Restart restarts; a lost battle fades into the statistics, see "End of a battle"); options → visuals → screen size 1024 × 768 → battle at 1024 × 768; CPU = GPU (Direct3D 12) on every screenshot; a window run with sound and music. Not compared against screenshots of the original (the attempts to drive the windowed original were interrupted).
- **State machine** (`0x00426E80`, GS+0x2BBE top state, +0x2BBF/+0x2BC0 sub-state; re-read 2026-10-20): 0 start (on a full screen, display +0xF0 bit 1: PlayMovie set → `1.zrb`, state 1 = `2.zrb`, PlayMovie 0 saved; else `1.zrb` unless a network switch started the program, GS+0x39245; not full screen: no movie), 1 `2.zrb`, 2 main menu (sub-states 0 set-up `0x4263B0`, 1 run, 5 → 7 single player, 6 → 0xF multiplayer, 7 → 0 intro again, 8 the black frame and quit, 9 → 3 credits), 3 `5.zrb`, 4 / 5 the campaigns' endings (`3.zrb` / `4.zrb` + `5.zrb`), 7 single player (sub-states 0 set-up `0x4777A0`, 3 → main menu, 10 / 14 new campaign → 8, 0xB skirmish → 9, 0xD options → 10), 8 campaign screens, 9 skirmish, 10 options, 0xB–0xE the campaign's briefing / load / end states, 0xF the multiplayer connection, 0x10 the game list and battle start, 0x11 battle, 0x14 lobby launches. Every transition goes through `0x4256D0` first, which only runs the code checksum `0x428BC0` — a stub returning 0 in 3.1 — so the "Code segment checksum error found when switching FE states" box never shows: the call does nothing. The states are the original's bookkeeping; the client keeps a stack of screens whose transitions follow the same observable order (each state's rows in docs/parity/FRONTEND.md).
- **GUI loading** (`0x004A81E0`, gadget reader `0x004AD350`, 0x15B-byte gadget records): COMMON id, assoc, name, xpos/ypos/width/height (relative to the header gadget), attribs, colorf/colorb, texturenumber, fontnumber (0 hattfont12, 1 hattfont11), active, commonattribs, gaffile; per kind: buttons status, text (stages separated by `|`), quickkey (ASCII), grayedout, stages; labels link; the header crdefault / escdefault / defaultfocus. Button art: the gadget's name in `anims\<GUI>.GAF`, else in commongui; else (attribs bit 0x80 clear, gaffile bit 0 clear, no 0x1800 bits) switches take `stagebuttn<stages>` and plain buttons the stock `BUTTONS0` sequence (groups of four frames by size: 16×16, 321×20, 120×20, 96×20, 112×20, 80×20, 96×31). Kinds: 0 header, 1 button, 2 list box, 3 text field, 4 scroll bar / slider, 5 label, 6 surface, 7 font, 12 picture.
- **Gadget drawing** (`0x004A5F40`): plain buttons frame 0, 1 pressed; switches their stage, `count − 2` pressed; grayed: attribs 0x100 → last frame, else frame `min(pressed + 2, count − 1)` dimmed unless attribs 0x80: the gadget's whole box goes first through the display's grey table (`0x4BFE10`, only with display flag 0x100 — the game sets it: WinMain's display word +0x202 has 0x80, which `0x4B5980` turns into flag 0x100; the table is built once by the front end's set-up `0x491200` from the game palette, `0x4BAD30`), then through shade row 12 (`0x4BF4D0` with −20; the row is indexed with the pixel as a signed byte, so pixels 0x80–0xFF take row 11's entries). A gadget with art takes its art's first (base) frame's width and height as its size (`0x4A81E0`), not the description's (ARMPREV 44 × 16 → 45 × 17). Without art a raised/sunken box. Caption: trimmed from the end until it fits width − 6; vertical centre `top + ((bottom − text height) − top) / 2 + pressed`; attribs 1 left (x + 3 + pressed), 4 right, 2 centred (`(w − text width)/2 + pressed + 1`, the quick key's letter underlined), 0x20 centred at the bottom. Text height of the picture fonts = the 'I' frame's height + 2; their glyphs keep their own colours (frame n = character n, offsets = baseline).
- **Interface colours** (`0x004AC7D0`, the table at GUI +0x8B2 = GS+0xDCB, GUIPAL kept at GUI +0xB2): each GUIPAL entry's nearest colour (|Δr|+|Δg|+|Δb|, first of equals) in a palette; gadget colorf indexes this table. It is **not** made per screen: only the main menu (`0x004263B0`, GUIPAL loaded from `palettes\guipal`), the connection screen (`0x00444580`, from the palette then on the display, read by `0x004BA200` — the game palette), the mission briefing (`0x00478E80`) and the battle loading (`0x00497F40`) make it. The battle room (`0x00449BB0`) puts its picture's palette on the display but keeps the connection screen's colours: its chat line's insertion point (colour 9) is index 9 (in the room's palette), not the room palette's nearest colour (index 102), *2026-10-20* gui_harness `main selprov lounge:` (without `main` the harness's GUIPAL is all zeros and every interface colour is 0: the "no underlines, no insertion point" of the first room comparison).
- **Backgrounds** (`0x004288D0`, a ten-entry most-recent cache of `bitmaps\<name>.PCX` with their palettes, the palette applied with the picture): MAINMENU FrontendX, SINGLE singlebg, SKIRMISH Skirmsetup4x, SELMAP DSELECTMAP2, STARTOPT options4x, SOUNDS optsound4x, MUSIC optmusic4x, VISUALS optvisual4x, SPEEDS optinterface4x, ENDMSN outcome0 (campaign outcome1), HELP dhelp, loading loadgame2bg, statistics report scorebg.
- **Main menu** (`0x004263B0`, handler `0x00425D80`): SINGLE → single player (sound BigButton), MULTI → multiplayer (needs the multiplayer data), INTRO and Credits → movies, EXIT → quit; the music mood becomes 4 (silent). **Sparks** (`0x00425B80`, the main menu's per-pass routine; see "Main menu sparks (exact)" below): 100 sparks; an idle one draws a random spot (rand() % 640, rand() % 220) and starts when the picture's index there has low nibble > 12 (a circuit trace): life = rand() + 1 (byte), turn count = (rand() & 31) + 1, horizontal (±3 by x parity) when the life is even, else vertical (±3 by y parity); each step it restores the picture under it, moves, stops when off screen, out of life or off the trace (low nibble < 13), else draws index 0xAA; when the turn count runs out it switches axis (new direction by the parity of the other coordinate) with a new count (rand() & 15) + 1. The client draws these from the menus' rand() stream, whose state becomes the simulation thread's rand() state at the battle start.
- **Single player** (`0x004777A0`, SINGLE.GUI): New Campaign, Load Game, Skirmish, Options, Previous Menu (AnyMsn when all missions are unlocked).
- **Skirmish** (`0x0047BBB0`, rows `0x00479C50`, handler `0x0047AE60`, start `0x0047A760`): N rows (registry NumSkirmishPlayers, default 4; typing III…X roman numerals in the screen sets 3–10), spaced 200/N, first at (180 − (N−1)·spacing)/2 + 79; per row: Player name button x 45 (`skirmname` art), Side x 163 (`SIDEx`, frame = side), Color x 214 20×20 (`textures\logos.gaf` 32xlogos frame = colour), Allies x 241 40×20 (`TEAMICONSx`: frame 10 no group, 2g+1 a group of one, 2g a group of several), Metal x 286 and Energy x 337 (`skirmmet`). Kinds: open → computer; computer → player when there is no player, else open; player → open. Side: (side + 1) % sides. Allies: (group + 1) % 6, 5 = none. Colour: ± 1 (right click back) skipping colours other open rows use. Metal/energy: left +500 (cap 10000; 700 becomes 500), right −500 (min 200). Options: Commander (game ends / continues), Location (fixed / random), Mapping, Line of Sight (Permanent = off, True = type 1, Circular = type 0), Difficulty (Easy/Medium/Hard). Start refuses with a message when there is no player or no computer, too many players for the map, or every row is in one alliance; rows become player slots (controller 1/2, side, colour, allied with rows of the same group). Defaults (registry): rows open (a fresh set-up gets one player and one computer), side = row & 1, colour = row, group 5, 1000/1000; SkirmishCommanderDeath/Mapping/LineOfSight/LOSType/Location 1, SkirmishDifficulty 1, SkirmishMap = the first map of the list. (2026-10-20) The map: the screen (`0x47BBB0`) looks the setting up with `0x435A20` (map info mode 2/3 → `0x435DA0`: Maps\<name>.ota loaded, [GlobalHeader], a schema for the mode (`0x436860`); other languages also try the untranslated name `0x4C5840`); on failure `0x435D30(0)` takes the first name of `0x434BF0`'s list — Maps\*.ota in enumeration order with a network schema, **not sorted** (only the map choice `0x47AAF0` sorts its copy, `0x4AEFA0`); the list starts as one empty string, so with no maps the name is "". The saved spelling is kept when found. `0x42F9A0` does the same when the registry has no SkirmishMap and writes it back. **Player names at a single-player start** (`0x464290(slot, controller)`, from the skirmish start `0x47A760` per row and the mission start `0x496DB0`: count 2, slot 0 controller 1, slot 1 controller 2): in map info modes 1 (missions) and 2 (skirmish) the record name (+0x2B) and short name (+0x49) become "Player" (translated) for controller 1, and for controller 2 "Arm" when the side record's +0x95 is 0, else "Core" (translated in mode 2 only); other modes (network) leave them. +0x95: the skirmish row's side (`0x47A760`), in campaigns the player's side and the other one for slot 1 (`0x477360` / `0x477AB0`, the load `0x492360`). The statistics (`0x41DC20`) show these names.
- **Select map** (`0x0047AAF0`, SELMAP.GUI over DSELECTMAP2): MAPNAMES list (the client lists maps whose OTA has a network schema), SLIDER, MAPPIC (the TNT's minimap picture), DESCRIPTION, SIZE; LOAD or a double click choose.
- **Options** (`0x00460160`; STARTOPT.GUI in the menus, PREFS.GUI in a battle with the `…RT` pages at 128,128): pages SOUND (SOUNDS/SOUNDSRT: FXVOL 0–64, MODE Off/Mono/3D, SPEECH unitchat/5, TEST), MUSIC (NOTRAK, MUSICVOL 0–64, TRACKMODE = cdmode − 1, TRACKTYPE of the shown track, CDPREV/STOP/PLAY/NEXT, TRACKNUM), INTERFACE (SPEEDS: GAME speed, SCREEN scroll 0–65, TXTSCROL 0–20, MAXLINES 0–30, LEFTCLICK interface type, UNITCHAT unitchattext/5), VISUALS (GAMMA 0–20, SHADING, ANTI, BSHADOWS, VIDSLDR/VIDVAL screen size); OK keeps, Cancel restores what was there when the menu opened, Restore Defaults / Undo Changes. In a battle the VID… gadgets are hidden. Values are those of the registry (below).
- **Screen size** (`0x004B5370`, `0x0045E4C0`, slider `0x0045BBF0`): the windowed list is 640×480, 800×600, 1024×768, plus 1280×1024 when the desktop is at least 1280×1024 and 1600×1200 when at least 1600×1200 (the full-screen path enumerates the display modes); sorted by width then height, modes below 640×480 dropped; the slider picks DisplaymodeWidth/Height ("%d X %d"); Restore Defaults sets 640×480 outside a battle. The client lists the display's own modes when the platform gives them (as the full-screen path does; a player's registry with 1920 × 1080 shows that path in use), else the windowed list. The menus run at 640×480; the battle loading switches to the chosen mode and the interface lays out for it (battle view (w − 128) × (h − 64)).
- **Loading screen (exact)** (2026-10-17; checked with gui_harness `loadscreen` / `loadframe:` / `loadplayer:` — the original's routine `0x00497F40` itself, its set-up branch and then one call per frame with the six progress bytes set and the interface clock held — against ta_play `--loading-test` on the same states: skirmish (8 frames: 0 %, partial rows, rows reaching 100 % on and off clock ticks, a 255 % row, the highlights fading), mission (no map line, 2 frames) and network (4 players: two of this machine — its human and a computer player it runs — two remote, 6 frames: remote progress and "loaded" flags, 1 and 2 ready, synchronised): 16 frames, 0 differing pixels, same palette). **When**: the main loop calls it once per pass while a battle loads; each call draws one frame, presents it and sleeps 200 ms, so the screen changes at most five times a second (the client redraws at a progress report at most every 200 ms: its loading runs on the main thread). **Set-up** (first call, loading flag GS+0x38D75 bit 0 clear): every open layout closed; the screen goes to **640 × 480 whatever the battle's display mode** (and back to the battle's mode at the end) — the client draws it on the menus' 640 × 480 frame; the game palette (GS+0x143A7) on the display and GUIPAL's interface colours made from it; `bitmaps\loadgame2bg.pcx` loaded **without its own palette** (raw indices on the game palette), the same picture for every side, map and game kind; the six highlight counters and the six "last seen" values cleared; the loading thread started; the registry settings saved outside skirmishes. **Each frame**: (network games first pump the session and, once the thread has said it is done (flag 4), ask whether everybody has loaded (`0x4568C0`): then flag 4 → 8, "synchronised"); the picture copied to (0, 0); when the interface clock (30 ticks a second) is past the one kept, every non-zero highlight counter loses 2; **map line** (not for missions, game type 1): "%s: %s" of the translated "Map" and the map's name (translated in other languages) in the interface's picture font (hattfont12, glyph colours), x = w/2 − text width/2, y = whole(h − 1.5 × the console font's height) (COMIX.FNT's first byte; 480 − 1.5 × h); **six rows** at y 135, 177, 218, 262, 304, 347 (labels Textures, Terrain, Units, Animation, 3D Data, Explosions, translated, at x 90): a row whose byte is 100 while its last seen value was not starts its highlight at 30; the label is drawn through light-table row = its highlight (0: the glyphs' own colours) — the label flashes bright when its row completes and fades by 2 per frame on a new tick (about 3 s); a filled rectangle x 205 … 205 + p·7/2 (inclusive; at 0 % one column), y … y + 20, interface colour 12 below 100 %, 10 at 100 % (values over 100 widen it, clipped at the screen); the LIGHTBAR frame (commongui; its offsets zeroed, top-left at (205, y)) over it (the 3D Data row's frame twice, harmless). **Network games** (type 3) then: every player in use with controller 1–3 and a record index other than 10 gets a bar: width share = 620 / count, from x 11, rectangle share − 2 wide, y 420 … 435 in interface colour 4, the loaded part (record byte +0x20 × (share − 2)/100) in colour 10, the player's name over it (clipped to share − 2); the line at (10, 400): "Waiting for other players.  %i player(s) ready" (ready: byte +0x20 = 100 and "has loaded" GS+0x29A4[k], set at the start for this machine's players, by packets for others), or once synchronised only "Synchronization complete" (no bars). After the frame this machine's players' byte becomes the average of the six rows (sent to the others) — their own bars show it one frame late. **Progress values** (loading thread order, each a row of its own, one after the other): Textures — per textures\*.gaf file k = 0, 1 … of the n found (enumeration order; logos.gaf is not loaded but counts): 100k/(n − 1), then 100; Terrain — the TNT read in ten parts: 9, 18 … 90, then 100 at the end of the map load; Units — per unit definition k = 1 … n − 1 (n counting the blank one): 100k/n (unit files and models), then 100; Animation — per feature definition: 100(k + 1)/n of the count read at each step (features added on the way lower it again; no final 100 — it reaches 100 at the last feature); 3D Data — per movement class with a name: 100k/n, then 100; Explosions — 20, 50, 100 (the explosion tables). **Sound**: nothing of its own: the menus' looping BGM goes on while loading; at the end every sound stops and the CD music category goes to building (`0x47F750`, `0x4CE690`). No pointer is drawn while loading (only the interface pass and the battle frame show it). Client: `ta::draw_loading_screen` (`ta_compat/src/client_loading.cpp`), the state `client::LoadingState` (`client/front_end.h`; reports "row:K" / "map:NAME" through `LoadProgress`); the client's loader reports the rows in this order where its own work for them is done (Textures after the texture library, Terrain after the map, Units model by model with the original's 100k/n, then Animation, 3D Data, Explosions at 100 — their work is done inside the battle loader before the models), so each frame is a state the original could show; a network game's launcher lists its players (`net_menu.cpp`); other machines' progress is not carried yet (their bars stay empty, see Open).
- **In-game menus**: F2 opens ARMOPT.GUI (the side panel at 0,128: Load Game, Save Game, Options → PREFS, Briefing, Help, Exit, Resume); Tab does the same outside network games and opens TABMENU.GUI (Options Menu, Allies, Share, Control; Tab again closes it) in network games (`0x00495E90`, `0x00495010`, `0x00460CC0`). Esc closes an open menu, else the first press cancels the order mode, the second deselects. The client pauses the battle while a menu is open (assumption).
- **Which keys reach which in-battle menu** (2026-10-17, read in the original): the battle pass (`0x00496790`) calls the battle's key handler (`0x00495E90`, one key from the key queue a pass) only while the game-menu bit (GS+0x37EBE bit 0, set when ARMOPT opens: F2, Tab outside network games, the tab bar's Options Menu) is clear; while it is set the open screens' own key handling (quick keys, crdefault / escdefault, Tab moving the focus) gets every key and Tab / F2 do nothing more. The network tab bar is *not* the game menu: with only the bar open the battle's keys go on — Tab (`0x00495010`: "SmallButton"; with any of GS+0x2BEE bits 0x20/0x40/0x80 set it clears the three and closes TABMENU when it is the top screen, else it opens the bar and sets 0x20) closes it, F2 opens ARMOPT over it (bit 0 clear), Esc cancels the order mode / deselects, Enter opens the chat line; the bar's quick keys (O, A, S, Backspace for its hidden CANCEL) and its escdefault never see a key. The tab bar's buttons (`0x00494740`): Options Menu sets bit 0 and opens ARMOPT over the bar (the bar stays; Resume returns to it), Share / Control / Allies open their screens over it, CANCEL closes it. Allies / Share / Control are grayed unless the player is in a network game and not a watcher, with at least one other player in the game (Control also needs `0x00457A50` and GS+0x2C74 bit 0 clear). A battle is left only by Exit to Menu → Yes (or Exit Game → Yes), the end of the game, or a lost connection — closing a menu never leaves it. Client: the screen under a battle (the skirmish set-up, the battle room) is not entered again while the battle runs (the room took being entered as "back from the game" and closed itself, ending network battles whenever a menu over them closed); the tab bar passes keys to the battle (`Screen::battle_keys`); regression `tools/battle_menus_check.ps1` (CTest `battle_menus_keep_battle`).
- **Exit menu (exact)** (`0x004608B0`, handler `0x00460800`; checked with gui_harness, the original's Exit pressed through its own interface pass, against ta_play headless: the exit panel 0 of 23,250 pixels differ): EXITMENU.GUI opened with flags 0x1800 — 0x1000 places it centred right of the side panel ((w − 128 − 150)/2 + 128, (h − 155)/2), whatever its file says; 0x800 dims the screen under it once (the game menu's box through shade level 8, `0x004BF4D0` with −24) and redraws it when the menu closes. A screen whose description names no panel picture gets the stock panel (`0x004B0230`): commongui's BackTile, its frames' offsets cleared, nine frames tiling the box — rows of frame 0's height (top row frames 0-2, middle rows 3-5, the last row 6-8 flush with the bottom), columns of its width (left, middle repeated, the right one flush with the right edge). Exit to Menu, Exit Game, Cancel, and in skirmishes and campaigns (game types 1, 2) RESTART switched on with the caption "Restart" (it takes a new quick key, the R). The focus ring starts on Exit to Menu; the game menu under it keeps its own focus (pressing Exit does not move it). A press plays "Options". Exit to Menu and Exit Game close the menu and open **YESORNO.GUI** (`0x00460680`, flags 0x1000: the stock panel, CHOICE1 "Yes", CHOICE2 "No" focused, TITLE "Surrender this battle and return to main menu?" or "Surrender this battle and exit to Windows?" — "Exit the Battle" when launched from a lobby; handler `0x004605C0`, checked: 0 of 40,000 pixels differ). Yes to Exit to Menu: the battle's statistics are taken (`0x00491B60`) and the program returns to the main menu — no statistics screen; Yes to Exit Game: end flag 4 and the program ends (`0x00491C60`); No closes the question (a button press its handler leaves unconsumed closes the screen). Restart closes the menu and opens **RESTART.GUI** (`0x004604A0`, flags 0x1000; DRESTART loaded without putting its palette on the display, its top-left part the box's size drawn in the box; MISSIONNAME / MISSIONNAME1 the map's [GlobalHeader] missionname word-wrapped to the label's width (`0x004AC4C0`: at the end of a word, when the line is at least as wide, it breaks at the last space or hyphen, which the break replaces); the Difficulty switch at the battle's difficulty; checked: 0 of 37,800 pixels differ below the name labels). Its handler (`0x00460340`): RESTART (after the CD check, which the Steam version passes) sets the difficulty from the switch and GS+0x39249, and the battle loop starts the same battle again; CANCEL closes it; the switch steps. The client restarts with the same request, the clocks' inputs taken anew and the C runtime's rand() going on from the battle's state (the original has one rand() for the whole program).
- **End of a battle** — the end state machine `0x0041F7F0` (program mode 7, entered from the battle loop when end flags 0x14 are set): state 0 (network games copy the last frame and may show a notice; others go on), 2 (fade: ten steps, one tick apart after the first tick: the whole screen through shade level count + 3 = 13, 12 … 4, each on the result of the last), 3 → 4 (`0x00491A70`), 4 (campaigns: CD check, CDCHECK.GUI), 5 (the statistics screen set up: `0x0041DA60` glamour / outcome picture, `0x0041F0A0` ENDMSN.GUI and the title, `0x0041E420` the rows, `0x0041F400` the buttons; the screen is cleared), 7 (the statistics' columns, below), 8 (the interface only). Implemented as read (no harness for states 0-4).
- **Statistics (exact)** (checked with gui_harness `endmsn:` / `endrun:` — the original's `0x0041DC20` and its end state machine from state 5 driven frame by frame with a held clock — against ta_play `--end-screen` on the same tables: two tables (4 players; 10 players with a watcher, one gone, negative and out-of-range amounts, a 29-character name), 640×480 and 1024×768, frames 0-60 of the animation: the rows equal, every frame pixel-identical except frame 1, see Open). **The table** (`0x0041DC20`, when the battle is left): a slot shows when it is present (record +0x22 = 0) and either playing (in use, controller 1-3, not out (state 10), not watching (side record +0x9B bit 6)) or it ever made a unit (+0x140); name (record +0x2B, 30 bytes), kills (+0xFC) and losses (+0xFE) as signed 16-bit, energy and metal produced (+0xAC, +0xB4) and excess energy and metal (+0xCC, +0xD4) — the doubles converted by the game's conversion (`0x004E43A0`: truncated to 64 bits, the low 32 kept: 3e9 shows −1294967296, beyond 64 bits 0) — and **score** = conv((game step / 60) × map timemul) + conv(kills × map killmul) (the map's OTA [GlobalHeader] killmul / timemul, map info +0xD54 / +0xD58, floats), the sum wrapping in 32 bits, below 0 → 0. Column maxima start at 10, 10, 100, 100, 100, 100, 100. **The screen**: ENDMSN.GUI over outcome0 (its palette on the display), every gadget off except MainMenu, moved to y 416 (campaigns: Start, Load / Save Game, Missions, Difficulty instead); the title (igdefeat, or igvictory when the local player won — end flag 0x10 — and is not watching) put on the screen's surface at (screen width / 2, 28) — off-centre at larger screen sizes; per shown row from y 93, 20 apart: a picture box (16, y, 91×21) stretching the player's 32xlogos frame (texels 1 … size − 1, `0x004C7580`), a label (16, y + (20 − small font height)/2, 90 wide, centred) whose glyphs go through light level 15 (`0x004B8310`, the label's colour value), and seven bars (`0x004A4660`) at x 112, 186, 260, 334, 408, 482, 556, 67×18: a raised box (x … x + 67, y … y + 18: filled with interface colour 20, two-pixel edges 0 top/left, 17 bottom/right), inside it 2 pixels in the battle's interface colour 8, then the filled part in its colour 4 from the left over conv(shown / max × 63) + 1 columns, and the number shown centred in the small font, light level 15. **Animation**: the bars start off; each frame of state 7 that still has a bar off or growing runs the interface pass — every bar on, growing and below its value whose time has come (its first step on the first pass after it came on, then whenever the clock passed its time) adds conv(step) (step = value × (1/15 as a single) rounded to single precision, at least 1.0), capped at the value, and takes its next time one tick on — then shows the frame, then: a key not taken by the interface (not network games) switches every bar on (sound ActivateAllStatBars); if the clock passed the column time (or on that key) the next column (kills, losses, … score) comes on with sound EndGameStatBar (EndGameScore for the score), the column time becomes now + 10, the column count goes on (also past the last). With no bar off or growing the screen turns to its normal interface (the focus ring shown). MainMenu returns to the main menu. Engine: `client::end_table` (`client/src/end_table.cpp`), `BattleSession::end_table`; the screen in `client_screens.cpp`.
- **Settings** (the registry values of `0x0042F9A0`, kept in a `key = value` file): Interface Type 0, DisplaymodeWidth/Height 640/480, Difficulty 1, scrollspeed 32, textlines 10, textscroll 10, gamespeed 10, damagebars 0, Sound Mode 1 (mono), fxvol 27, musicvol 32, musicmode 1, cdmode 4, unitchat 10, unitchattext 5, speechfx 1, Shadows/FeatureShadows/Shading/Anti Alias 1, Gamma 12, clock 0, SwitchAlt 0, UnitLimit 250, NumSkirmishPlayers 4, the Skirmish… values and per row Player<n>Controller/Side/Color/AllyGroup/Metal/Energy, CDLISTS (track types).
- **Music** (`0x004CDB40` pick, `0x004CE690` mood, `0x00490FE0`; the Steam version's `win32.dll` turns "play cdaudio from N" into `music\N.mp3`): modes (cdmode) 0 stop, 1 play all (next track when one ends, wrapping), 2 random (rand() % count + 1), 3 repeat the chosen track, 4 by mood. Moods: 0 building, 1 battle, 2 victory, 3 defeat, 4 silent (menus). By mood (also for victory and defeat in any mode): r = rand() & 15; a playing track of the mood keeps playing; else walking forward from the current track (wrapping) every track of the mood counts r down and the first time r < 1 that track plays; nothing found in (r + 1) × count steps stops the music. A mood change (in mode 4, or to victory/defeat) fades the track out (every 2 ticks the volume falls by 1/18 of its level), then picks at once, or after 120 ticks when the new mood is building; from silence it picks at once. Track types per CD (CDLISTS); a fresh 16-track disc gets tracks 1–7 battle and the rest building, otherwise all building. Battle/peace judge (`0x00494E70`, once a second of wall clock in a battle): a ring of 30 one-second slots gets +1 for each damage where the attacker or the victim is the local player and +5 for each kill the local player scores (`0x00494FF0`, the simulation's stats_activity); more than 10 seconds after the last switch: building → battle when (ring total > 50 or the 5 slots before the current > 30) and the local player has more than 30 units; battle → building when the total < 10, those 5 slots are 0 and more than 60 seconds passed. The client decodes the MP3s with the platform's decoder (Windows Media Foundation) and uses its own rand() stream for the music (the original's runs on its timer thread).
- **Unit notices** (`0x0047F850`, queue `0x0047FAD0`, play `0x0047FD70`, per frame `0x0047F680`, forget `0x0047F8C0`): table (priority, gap seconds, console text) by speech id: 1 select (10,0), 2 underattack (9,20,"Under Attack"), 3 activate (4,2), 4 deactivate (4,2), 5 ok (5,1), 6 arrived (3,4,"Arrived"), 7 cant (8,1,"Cannot Comply"), 8 unitcomplete (3,3,"Nanolathe Complete"), 9 build (4,2), 10 repair (3,1), 11 working (2,1), 12 load (7,1), 13 unload (7,1), 14 cloak (7,1,"Cloaked"), 15 uncloak (7,1,"Visible"), 16 capture (4,1), 17–22 count5..count0 (10,0,"five".."zero"), 23 canceldestruct (10,0,"Self destruct terminated"). Only live units of the viewed player that are not dying. A notice is queued when the game step has reached its next-allowed step and it is not already queued; the queue holds 8 sorted by priority (a new one goes before the first of lower priority); a full queue first shows the text of its last entry and drops it. Each frame the head is taken: if the step is still within the last spoken notice's length only its text shows, else it speaks. Speaking draws rand() on the simulation thread (always, also for text only) to pick one of the type's sounds for the notice (`<key>`, `<key>1`…) as rand × count / 32768; it sounds when priority > 10 − unitchat, the speech option is on, the effect volume and sound mode are not 0, at −5.85 dB; then the notice's next-allowed step = now + gap × 30. Text shows when priority > 10 − unitchattext: "<unit name>: <text>" on the console. A killed unit's entries leave the queue.
- **World sounds** (`0x0047F300`): need effect volume ≠ 0 and sound mode ≠ 0; heard when the viewed player sees the block of (x, z − y/2) (sight counts with the line of sight option, else the explored map); mono mode plays them centred at full volume; 3D mode places them relative to the view's centre (min distance half the view's size in cells × 16, max the map's width + height × 16). The client's 3D mode approximates DirectSound's placement with a pan by direction and an inverse-distance fall-off beyond the min distance.
- **More keys** (`0x00495E90`): ',' and '.' step back/forward through the focus unit's menus (orders, build pages, sound nextbuildmenu); 't' / 'T' track the next / previous selected unit in unit order (the view follows it; Ctrl+C selects the commander's category and tracks it); tracking (GS+0x142F3, followed each step by `0x41CA10`, which also drops a unit that is no longer alive) ends when an arrow-key or screen-edge scroll actually moves the view (`0x41CE90`), on a minimap jump (`0x41D0F0`), when a mouse drag-scroll starts (`0x41CC60`) and on an F5–F8 view recall (`0x41D3F0`)); 'n' centres on the next own unit not yet visited (the on-screen marks; all marked → cleared); Ctrl+S selects the selectable own units on screen (every unit's marks cleared first); Ctrl+Z selects every unit of the selected types; Ctrl+<letter> selects the type category CTRL_<letter> (others leave the selection unless Shift); Ctrl+F5..F8 store the view, F5..F8 recall it; F1 unit info (UNITINFOx.GUI, not built), F3 jump to the last message's place, F4 option bit 0x80, F11/F12 debug; Enter opens the chat line (TALK.GUI at the bottom edge, see "Chat line (exact)"; the client sends the line through BattleSession::send_chat); `~ ! # * damage bars.
- **Unit notices (exact)** (`0x0047F850` / `0x0047F780`, queue `0x0047FAD0`, play `0x0047FD70`, per frame `0x0047F680`, forget `0x0047F8C0`; checked with sound_harness `notices`, below): table (priority, gap seconds, console text) by speech id: 1 select (10,0), 2 underattack (9,20,"Under Attack"), 3 activate (4,2), 4 deactivate (4,2), 5 ok (5,1), 6 arrived (3,4,"Arrived"), 7 cant (8,1,"Cannot Comply"), 8 unitcomplete (3,3,"Nanolathe Complete"), 9 build (4,2), 10 repair (3,1), 11 working (2,1), 12 load (7,1), 13 unload (7,1), 14 cloak (7,1,"Cloaked"), 15 uncloak (7,1,"Visible"), 16 capture (4,1), 17–22 count5..count0 (10,0,"five".."zero"), 23 canceldestruct (10,0,"Self destruct terminated"). Queued only for units of the viewed player (GS+0x2A43) with the alive flag (0x10000000) and without the dying flag (0x4000); under-attack (`0x47F850`) also only when the unit is not in the frame's visible-unit list (GS+0x1435F, `0x48BCB0`). A notice is queued when the game step has reached its next-allowed step and none of its kind is queued; the queue holds 8 sorted by priority (a new one goes before the first entry of lower priority); a full queue first takes its last entry text-only (that draws rand() too) and drops it. Each frame the head is taken: while the step is below the last spoken notice's step + 30 only its text shows, else it speaks and becomes the last spoken. The 30 is a field of the queue object set once (offset +0xA1) and never changed: the length of the sound does not count, and as the last-spoken step starts at 0, nothing speaks in a battle's first 30 steps. Taking an entry always draws rand() once (the simulation thread's) to pick one of the type's sounds for the notice (the category's list for the key: `<key>`, then `<key>1`, `<key>2`… until one is missing; each with a text from `<name>text`, empty in the shipped data) as rand × count / 32768. It sounds when priority > 10 − unitchat (GS+0x37F17), the speech switch (GS+0x37F19 bit 0x40) is on and count > 0: the file `sounds\<name>.wav` plays as a one-shot (below) at −5.85 dB when the effect volume and the sound mode are not 0, and the notice's next-allowed step = now + gap × 30 (whether or not it was heard). With the sing switch (a command) the file is "honk" in every eighth second of game time (step / 30 & 7 = 0), else "sing". Text shows when priority > 10 − unitchattext (GS+0x37F18), the entry's text is not empty and the unit still has its alive flag: "<type name>: <text>" on the console (no "MessageArrived" beep). A killed unit's entries leave the queue.
- **World sounds (exact)** (`0x0047F300`; sound_harness `world`): need effect volume (GS+0x37F0C) ≠ 0 and sound mode (GS+0x37F19 & 7) ≠ 0; the cell of (x / 2^20, z / 2^20) (rounded towards zero) must be on the map, and the viewed player must see the 32-pixel block of (x, z − y/2) (the integer parts; by the player's sight map with the line-of-sight option, GS+0x14281 bit 1, else by its bit in the explored grid). Mono mode: centred, −5.85 dB when x and z lie in the view's rectangle (scroll ≤ x ≤ scroll + view cells × 16, the same for z, height not counted), else −15.85 dB. 3D mode: placed relative to the listener at x − (view cells wide / 2) × 16 − scroll x, height 0, scroll y + (view cells high / 2) × 16 + y/2 − z (positive = towards the top of the screen), minimum distance ((view cells wide + view cells high) / 2) × 16, maximum distance (map cells wide + high) × 16, −5.85 dB. Interface sounds (`0x0047F0C0`, by name `0x0047F1A0`, looping by name `0x0047F210`): the same two settings, centred, −5.85 dB. The tree-burning sound (`Feature_Ignite` `0x4233A0`) passes a position whose height word is never written (a stale stack word; the client uses 0).
- **More keys** (`0x00495E90`): ',' and '.' step back/forward through the focus unit's menus (orders, build pages, sound nextbuildmenu); 't' / 'T' track the next / previous selected unit in unit order (the view follows it; Ctrl+C selects the commander's category and tracks it); tracking (GS+0x142F3, followed each step by `0x41CA10`, which also drops a unit that is no longer alive) ends when an arrow-key or screen-edge scroll actually moves the view (`0x41CE90`), on a minimap jump (`0x41D0F0`), when a mouse drag-scroll starts (`0x41CC60`) and on an F5–F8 view recall (`0x41D3F0`)); 'n' centres on the next own unit not yet visited (the on-screen marks; all marked → cleared); Ctrl+S selects the selectable own units on screen (every unit's marks cleared first); Ctrl+Z selects every unit of the selected types; Ctrl+<letter> selects the type category CTRL_<letter> (others leave the selection unless Shift); Ctrl+F5..F8 store the view, F5..F8 recall it; F1 unit info (UNITINFOx.GUI, not built), F3 jump to the last message's place, F4 option bit 0x80, F11/F12 debug; Enter opens the chat line (TALK.GUI over the top bar; the client sends the line through BattleSession::send_chat); `~ ! # * damage bars.
- **View drag (read 2026-10-20, built, smoke only; replaces an earlier "middle-button" note: the original has no middle-button drag)** (`0x41CC60` start, `0x41CD50` pass): started only by a right press (WM_RBUTTONDOWN in the battle input pass `0x499200` → `0x499100`) in interface type 0 with MK_CONTROL while the pointer is over the view (GS+0x2CC6 bit 1; over the minimap bit 0 starts the minimap drag `0x41D0F0` instead). Start: following ends (GS+0x142F3/F7 = 0, GS+0x1434B = 0), the pointer is hidden (`0x4C2470`), its state (6 words at GS+0x2C76) kept at GS+0x2CC7, GS+0x2CDF = 1, the view's cell (view / 16, C division) kept (GS+0x2CEB/EF), the centre (screen width / 2, height / 2) kept and the pointer put there (SetCursorPos). Each pass (instead of all other pointer handling): view = (cell + (pointer − centre) / 4, C division) × 16, the "moved" bit, clamp, the camera target = the view, the cell taken again, the pointer back to the centre (what is left of the travel is dropped); then, when the pointer state's buttons lack MK_RBUTTON, the drag ends: the pointer goes back to its kept place and shows (`0x4C2870`). Client: `GameClient::view_drag_step` (the front's pointer warp hook).
- **Pick test** (`Unit_PickUnderCursor` `0x0048CD80`, `0x0048C6A0`, inside test `0x004C1320`): in the view rectangle, every unit of the visible list: the model's bounds' four bottom corners (min.y) turned by the unit's angles and projected (x + 128, z − rot.z − (rot.y + y)/2 + 32 with the scroll) must have the pointer strictly inside every edge; among hits the smallest (size_y·0x8000 >> 16 + size_z) · size_x >> 16 of the type definition wins (first of equals). On the minimap the radar dot with squared distance under 4 (nearest).
- **Game clock** (option bit 0x40 of GS+0x37F2F, registry `clock`): "Game Time: %02d:%02d:%02d" at x 130, y = h − 34 − text height. The victory and defeat signs (IGTITLES igvictory / igdefeat) are drawn over the view centre while the end flags 0x20 / 0x40 are set.
- **Bar text rows.** The bars' text routine (`0x004C14F0`, glyphs `0x004CCF60`) starts a line at row y − raise (the font's signed byte 2) and draws it only when the whole line (x … x + width, y … y + height) lies inside the context's clip (no partial clipping). The top bar, the bottom bar and the clock pass their rows as they are (no raise added). The bars are redrawn only when the values they show change (a copy of the last values is kept), which gives the same pixels as drawing every frame.
- **Bottom bar (exact)** (`0x0046A860`; checked by frame_harness `--hud`, see "Client"). Row offset dy = screen height − baseheight (sidedata.tdf [GENERAL] `baseheight`, 480 when absent). Region names → the side record's rectangles (`0x00431A60` reads them in this order of storage: …, LOGO2, UNITNAME, DAMAGEBAR, UNITENERGYMAKE, UNITENERGYUSE, UNITMETALMAKE, UNITMETALUSE, MISSIONTEXT, UNITNAME2, DAMAGEBAR2, NAME, DESCRIPTION). What it shows, in priority:
  1. A gadget under the pointer whose name is a unit type (the build buttons), except a type named CORBUILD: "<type name>  M:<metal cost> E:<energy cost>" (two spaces; costs truncated to integers) at NAME (left-aligned, x1 / y1 + dy) and the type's description at DESCRIPTION, colour 0x53.
  2. Else the unit under the pointer. The pointer's pick runs every frame (`0x0048CD80` from the battle's per-frame handler), so with the pointer over no unit the bar shows no unit (there is no fall-back to the selected unit). A unit the viewed player does not see: "Unidentified object" centred (x1 − width/2) on UNITNAME. Otherwise: the type name centred on UNITNAME; the health bar on DAMAGEBAR (x1 … x1 + (x2 − x1)·hp/max, hp clamped to 0 … max, in [10], the rest in [4]) for own units and for types without flag 0x4000; then the owner's **colour logo** (`0x00467C00`: `textures\logos.gaf` "32xlogos", frame = the owner's colour, its corners (0,0), (w,0), (w,h), (0,h) stretched onto the box's corners by the textured-quad routine `0x004C7580`, texels copied as they are) into LOGO2. For own units: "+%.1f" metal made at UNITMETALMAKE and "+%.0f" energy made at UNITENERGYMAKE in [10], "-%.1f" metal used and "-%.0f" energy used in [12] (the unit's last settlement); for armed units (unit flag bit 31) with kills: "1 kill", "<n> kills", from 5 "<n> kills - Veteran" in [15] at DAMAGEBAR (x1, y2 + 2 + dy); and the **status line**: the display text of the head order's mission kind (mission table entry +0; "Ready" for a unit without orders) centred on MISSIONTEXT in 0x53. The display texts by kind: Ready, Activate, Airstrike, Engaging target (AirToAir, AirToGround, AirToGroundHover), Attacking (Attack_Chase, Attack_Kamikaze, Attack_NoMove, AttackUType), Annihilating (AttackSpecial), Being transported, Nanolathing (BuildingBuild, BuildWeapon, HelpBuild, MobileBuild, VTOL_HelpBuild, VTOL_MobileBuild), Capturing, Decloaking, Cloaking, Deactivate, Guarding (Follow_Ground, VTOL_Follow), Under construction, Loading, Unloading, Ready (Guard_NoMove), Unit is available, Moving (Move_Ground, VTOL_Move), Paralyzed, Parking, Patrolling, Ready with orders (QMove, QPatrol), Reclaiming, Repair patrol, Repairing (RepairUnit, RepairUnitNoMove, SelfRepair, VTOL_RepairUnit), Resurrecting, SELF DESTRUCT ENGAGED, Standby (Standby, Standby_Mine, VTOL_Standby), Acknowledged (the standing orders), Stopping, Suppressing fire, Teleporting, Evading, Under repair, Seeking to land, Landing, Seeking to attack, Seeking to guard, Waiting, Waiting for attack (texts go through the translation table; English keeps them).
