     The second line: the first secondary order with flag 0x80000 (a stockpile weapon being built) gives p = param2·100 / the weapon's reload time (slot param0); p ≠ 0 → for own units "Weapon" centred on UNITNAME2 and a bar of min(p, 100)% on DAMAGEBAR2; p = 0 → for own units the head order's target, when alive and seen: its type name centred on UNITNAME2 and its health bar (same rule) on DAMAGEBAR2.
  3. Else a feature under the pointer, unless its definition's flag 0x400: its description (definition +0x80), then for definitions without flag 0x200 a space, " M:%d" when its metal is not 0 and " E:%d" when its energy is not 0, left-aligned at NAME.
- **Selection outline (exact)** (`0x0046A530`, called by the frame for each unit with flag 0x10 in its own turn — ground units in their row, the others after the effects — just before the unit's shadow and model; any owner; option bit 2 of GS+0x37F2F) — **compared with the original** (frame_harness `--hud --no-fog`, 45 outlines in 28 scenes at three screen sizes, 0 differing outline pixels; two scenes differed elsewhere, in an effect glow). The box is the model bounds routine (`0x004CB650` / `0x004CB6A0`) called **without** its walk over the other pieces: min and max start at 0 and take in the root piece's own vertices plus the root's offset, only when the root has more than two vertices (the pick test `0x0048C6A0` uses the same box). Corners (min x, min y, min z), (max x, min y, min z), (max x, min y, max z), (min x, min y, max z) are turned by the unit's angles (+0x64, `Math_RotatePointByAngles`) and placed as x = int16((rot.x + unit x − scroll x·65536) >> 16) + 128, y = int16((unit z − scroll y·65536 − rot.z) >> 16) − (int16((rot.y + unit y) >> 16) >> 1) + 32, and joined 0-1-2-3-0 with the clipped line routine (`0x004BE950`) in interface colour [10], clipped to the view.
- **Unit notices from the interface.** Selecting units queues the "select" notice (`Unit_QueueSpeech` `0x0047F780`, as the simulation's notices); the command routine (`0x0048CF30`) and the placement click play no unit sound: a new order's "ok" comes from the order's own acknowledgement when its behaviour first runs (`MissionOrder_Acknowledge`). The client used to speak "ok" itself as well (every order sounded twice).
- **Sound engine (exact)** (sound manager at GS+0x10: start `0x004CF570`, looping start `0x004CF540`, one-shot start `0x004CF8A0`, eviction `0x004CF180`, sweep `0x004CF0B0`, stop all `0x004CF150`; sound_harness `mixer`). The manager tracks playing sounds in a 32-entry table (buffer, serial, looping flag) with a count. A start: (looping start only) nothing when the table holds a looping entry; while the count ≥ the limit ("MixingBuffers", registry, default 8) the non-looping entry with the smallest serial is stopped and removed (with only looping entries the original reads past its table and faults: a limit of 1 with the menus' loop playing crashes it); then the sound's buffer: of its four places (the loaded buffer and up to three duplicates) the first that is not playing; else a new duplicate in the LAST empty place (so the second copy is place 3, then 2, then 1); else the playing place with the largest play position (the first of equal ones) is restarted from the beginning. The buffer is set to position 0, its volume set (hundredths of a dB), placed (3D mode and a position given: position, minimum and maximum distance, normal 3D) or with 3D processing off (centred), and played (looping or not); it gets the first free table entry with the next serial (none free: not tracked). A restarted copy keeps its old entry too (counted twice until swept). The sweep (every 100 ms of the main loop's wall clock, and before every one-shot) first frees the one-shot places whose buffer stopped (stopping and releasing it and removing its first table entry), then removes every entry whose buffer is not playing; finished sounds count against the limit until then, and the eviction may "stop" one that has already ended. One-shots (unit notices, the sound test): the sweep, then one of eight places (none free: nothing plays) with a buffer of its own made from the file, started as above. Stop all (sound mode switched off, a movie, the end of a battle's loading) stops every tracked entry. Every start in the game is at −585 (−5.85 dB) except mono world sounds away from the view (−1585); there is no pan control (buffers have volume and 3D only). The effect volume setting fxvol (0..64) sets the wave output's volume to fxvol × 1024 (at most 0xFFFF), linear. World sounds (`0x0047F300`) and interface sounds (`0x0047F0C0`) also send packet 0x13 when their caller asks (network echo).
- **Sound format.** The primary buffer is asked for 11025 Hz, 16-bit stereo (`0x4CEF90`); the bank's files are 8-bit mono 11025 Hz (one 22254 Hz). Files (`0x4D02A0`): RIFF WAVE by the format chunk's rate, sample size and channels (the encoding field is not read) and the data chunk; a "DIGI" file (rate word at 0x16, 11000 read as 11025, 8-bit mono data from 0x28: sing.wav); anything else raw 8-bit mono at 11025 Hz (honk.wav). Bank entries (`SoundBank_EnsureNamed` `0x429470`): one per allsound.tdf section (sections naming the same file have separate copies), weapon sounds shared by path; file `sounds\<name>` with its extension replaced by `.wav` (so "explode.wav" works). Narration (`0x47F090` → `0x4D06C0`, a streaming thread): one streamed buffer outside the table at full volume, played whatever the sound settings; a new one replaces it, `0x4CFB40` stops it.
- **Menu sounds (exact by the handlers; the screens have no generic click).** Each screen's handler plays a bank sound by name when it handles a gadget; sliders, list picks, text fields and gadgets it does not name are silent; disabled gadgets send nothing. Main menu (`0x425D80`; opening it `0x4263B0` starts "BGM" looping, which plays on through every menu until a battle has finished loading, `0x497F40`): SINGLE, MULTI BigButton; EXIT exit; INTRO, Credits smlButton. Single player (`0x4775A0`): NewCamp, LoadGame, AnyMsn BigButton; Skirmish skirmish; Options options; PrevMenu Previous. Skirmish (`0x47AE60`): Start BigButton (also when refused); PrevMenu Previous; every player row button, CommanderDeath, StartLocation, Mapping, LineOfSight, SelectMap, Difficulty Skirmish; a typed code that unlocks ten players SkirmishCheat. Map choice (`0x47A910`): silent. Options menu (`0x45FC60`): SOUND, MUSIC, SPEEDS, VISUALS, PREV Options; CANCEL Previous; UNDO silent; in a battle opening it plays Panel (`0x460160`) and its light bar (`0x45FFB0`, from the battle frame: +21 pixels a frame from 0, stopping past 276) plays Options on the 14th frame. Option pages (sound `0x45DA90`, music `0x45D280`, speeds `0x45EAD0`, visuals `0x45E100`): every button Options; the sound test plays sounds\explode.wav as a one-shot (no Options); the mode switch to off stops all sounds, to mono outside a battle starts BGM, then Options. Game menu (`0x4609B0`): every button Options. Exit menu (`0x460800`): every gadget Options. Its confirmation (`0x4605C0`): Exit. The main menu's CD-player question (`0x426190`): SmallButton. Restart dialog (`0x460340`): every gadget Options, the difficulty switch Options twice. Tab bar (`0x495010` opens and closes it: SmallButton; `0x494740`): OPTIONS, SHARE, CONTROL, ALLIES BigButton. Statistics screen (`0x41EC50`): MainMenu, LoadGame, SaveGame, Start, Missions BigButton; Difficulty Skirmish (the bars: `0x41F7F0` EndGameStatBar per column, EndGameScore for the last, ActivateAllStatBars for a key). New campaign (`0x477AB0`): Start/Missions bigButton, PrevMenu Previous, Difficulty SmlButton, the sides SideSelect / SideSelect2. Briefing (`0x478CB0`): Start BigButton (narration stops), SHUTUP Options then SmallButton (narration toggled), PrevMenu Previous (narration stops), the text's more bar More. Load (`0x492360`): CANCEL Previous, LOAD / the list SMLBUTTON. Save (`0x492DF0`): CANCEL Previous, DELETE SmallButton, save smlbutton. Message boxes: silent.
- **Interface sounds in a battle (exact by the handlers).** Build menu (`0x41AA00`): ORDERS ordersbutton, BUILD buildbutton, a structure's button addbuild; PREV/NEXT nextbuildmenu (deferred, `0x41BDE0`/`0x41BF10`, always); a mobile type's button addbuild (count up) or subbuild (down), only for a selected focus unit of the viewed player. Order buttons (`0x419BE0`, either way round): MOVE, STOP, ATTACK, BLAST, DEFEND, PATROL, LOAD immediateorders; REPAIR, RECLAIM, CAPTURE, UNLOAD specialorders. Switches (`0x41A490`): FIREORD setfireorders, MOVEORD setmoveorders, ONOFF/STATUS and CLOAK specialorders. Placement (`0x498F70`): oktobuild / notoktobuild. Box selection (`0x48C390`): two or more selected SelectMultipleUnits, one the select notice. Keys (`0x495E90`): Enter (chat line) SmallButton; a group key SelectSquad (also for an empty group); Ctrl+group CreateSquad; view store / recall SelectSquad; Ctrl selections (categories, Ctrl+Z) silent; the digit page keys nextbuildmenu when the page exists. Console (`0x463CA0`): a message whose colour argument is not 10 beeps MessageArrived while text lines are on (other players' chat and "left" / "obliterated" messages; the simulation's and the notices' texts pass 10). Mission goals: "Victory Condition". The Space-held info panels (`0x4689C0`, `0x4948E0`) slide with Panel / Options (not built in the client). Alliances in network games: Ally (`0x453D40`, not built).
- **Top bar (exact)** (in `Render_DrawBattleFrame` `0x00468CF0`; checked by frame_harness `--hud`: random stored amounts, storages (0 included), sharing thresholds and rates covering every format, the refresh both taken and not; smoke 2026-10-17: 17 scenes at 640×480 and 1024×768, 0 differing pixels over rows 0–31). The frame keeps a 33-byte copy of what the bar last showed (GS+0x37E3F: player index, shown energy, energy made, energy used, shown metal, metal made, metal used, energy storage, metal storage) and redraws the bar only when the new copy differs (same pixels as drawing every frame).
  - Shown amounts: d = whole(real stored) − whole(shown) (C truncation of both); the step is d/8 toward zero, at least ±1 when d ≠ 0; shown = shown + step; then capped at the storage (only when the storage is smaller).
  - Rates: read again only when the player record's display timer (+0xF8) is below the game step; the timer then moves on 30 and the four rates are the record's energy made (+0x90), energy used (+0x94), metal made (+0x9C), metal used (+0xA0); otherwise the copy's rates stay. The record's timer is saved (DisplayTimer) — the original's drawing advances it; the client keeps its own copy (drawing leaves the simulation's records alone; open: whether saves should carry the drawn value).
  - Art: PANELTOP at x 129, then PANELBOT's frame repeated across wider screens (drawn right after the terrain, before the units; PANELBOT's frame is taller than 32 rows, so at wider screens it reaches into the view's first rows — there the terrain is drawn anew every frame, so those rows show the art only in frames that redrew the bar, and units, shots and effects draw over it); the viewed player's colour logo in LOGO.
  - Each resource: with the storage above 0 (NaN or ≤ 0: no bar), the bar fills x1 … whole((x2 − x1)·shown/storage + x1) (double precision, truncated), y1 … y2, in the side's colour — a shown 0 still fills column x1, an end left of x1 fills nothing; then, when the sharing threshold (energy +0xE8, metal +0xE4) is above 0 and the real stored amount is above it, a mark 3 columns wide at whole((x2 − x1)·threshold/storage + x1) in interface colour [12]. Texts in [15]: "%d" of whole(shown) at NUM, "0" at the zero box, "%d" of whole(storage) right-aligned at MAX's x1.
  - **Energy rates in whole units**: made "%d" of whole(made) when made ≤ 99 999 (NaN too), else "%dK" of whole(made)/1000; used "%d" of |whole(used)| (32-bit negate) unless used < −99 999 (or NaN), then "%dK" of whole(used)/1000. **Metal rates**: "%.1f" made, "%.1f" of |used| (no thousands form). Made in [10], used in [12]. "%.1f" is the game's runtime's: the exact value rounded half away from zero (2261393.25 prints "2261393.3"; today's runtimes print ".2").
  - Bar texts (top and bottom bars, `0x004C14F0`): a line is drawn only when its whole box (x … x + width, y … y + font height) lies inside the context's clip (the screen here); otherwise none of it (a long rate at the right edge disappears).
- **Placement outline (exact)** (`Build_UpdatePlacementGhostValidity` `0x004197D0` each pass while the pointer is over the view in placement mode, GS+0x2CC3 = 14; drawn after the fog by the drag-box code of the frame) — checked by frame_harness `--hud` (random structure types at random view points, fitting and not, 0 differing pixels). The pointer's ground point is the editor-position conversion (`0x00484B50`) of the view pixel (clamped to the view). Corner cell: cx = int16((x16.16 − footprint_x·0x80000 + 0x80000) >> 20), the same for z — the footprint centred on the point, rounding toward it (not "cell − footprint/2"). The check is the yard-map test `0x0047D2E0` with the viewing player's record: before the yard map, the footprint centre (pixel ((fx + 2cx)·8, (fz + 2cz)·8)) lifted by half the corner cell's height gives a 32-pixel block; outside the player's grid or unexplored for the viewed player → refused; in sight (counts ≠ 0 with line of sight, else explored) decides whether occupied cells (unit or marked cell) block — out of sight they do not. Fits: the height is the check's place height; else the slope-sampled place height (`0x0047D820`). Corners A = (16cx, h, 16cz), B = A + (16fx, 0, 16fz) projected (x − scroll + 128, z − h/2 − scroll + 32); the outline is drawn from A to B inclusive (16·fx + 1 pixels across) and a second outline one pixel inside it, both in [10] when it fits, else [4], clipped to the view. The pointer's look is not changed while placing over the view (it keeps the look it had when it entered the view). A placement click places only when the last pass's check fitted ("OKTOBUILD", else "NOTOKTOBUILD"): every selected own builder (type flag 0x40; fliers 0x800 VTOL_MOBILEBUILD) gets MOBILEBUILD at ((fx + 2cx)·0x80000, h << 16, (fz + 2cz)·0x80000), queued with Shift.
- **Shift-kept modes**: an order or placement given with Shift sets GS+0x2CC6 bit 0x20 and keeps the mode; each pass, when that bit is set and Shift is not held (`0x004C1B80(0xF9)`), the mode returns to the default and the order buttons' group is shown unpressed (`0x004A6A40` on the STOP gadget's group).
- **Queued orders (Shift)** (`0x0048CC30`, called by the frame after the units and effects, before the health bars and the fog, when Shift is held) — checked by frame_harness `--hud` (Shift held against not, random queued moves / patrols / build orders, 0 differing pixels among up to 2,993 changed). Units: the local player's unit range (record +0x67 … +0x6B), alive (flag 0x10000000) and not dying (0x4000). Let S = {the unit the view follows (GS+0x142F3), the unit whose menu is shown (GS+0x37E9C), the unit under the pointer (GS+0x2CBA)}; builder view = some unit of S has build options (def +0x156). A unit of S draws everything with paths; another selected unit everything without paths; otherwise, in builder view, its build boxes only (with paths); else nothing. For each order of its primary list, the mission table entry's draw bits (+0xC; below) masked by that choice are done in this order, each starting from the previous point (the unit's position for the first order); the point after the order is the last done part's result:
  - 1 **build box** (`0x00438C00`, only with a type in param0): the type's model bounds (def +0x15E min, +0x16A max) around the order's position (corners as int16 of the 16.16 sums), the minimum y's half lifting both rows; with a = min(step − order creation step, 10) (unsigned) and dx = (X1 − X0)·a/10, dz = (Y1 − Y0)·a/10, eight lines: X0 + dx − 1 and X1 − dx + 1 from Y0 − 1 to Y1 + 1, Y0 + dz − 1 and Y1 − dz + 1 from X0 − 1 to X1 + 1, in [3] (selected unit's order) or [1]; X0 + dx and X1 − dx from Y0 to Y1, Y0 + dz and Y1 − dz from X0 to X1, in [10] or [9]. The result is the order's position.
  - 2 **path** (`0x004394E0`): the marker (8, below); then with paths: d = target − previous point (16.16 per axis), length = whole(√(dx² + dy² + dz²)); from 1.0 (0x10000) on: dots of the `pathicon` look every 48 world units (0x300000), the first at ((age mod 30)·0x300000)/30 (age = step − creation step, signed), frame (age / first frame's time (≥ 1)) mod frames, the next dot the next frame; dot at previous + d·t >> 16 per axis with t = (offset << 16)/length (64-bit), drawn as a plain frame at its projection.
  - 4 ring (`0x004399F0`): used by no mission kind.
  - 8 **marker** (`0x00439740`): the target: the order's target unit's position — when the unit's owner does not see it and the order has bit 0x200000, the position kept in the order (+0x32/+0x34, whole x and z, with the unit's y); otherwise the unit's position, which the drawing then **writes into the order** (+0x32/+0x34) and sets bit 0x200000 (a draw-time write into order state; the client keeps that position per order itself) — or the order's position without a target unit. With a marker number (entry +0x10) the pointer look of that number is blended (`0x004B8500`) at the target, frame (game step / (2 × its first frame's time)) mod frames. Marker numbers: 1 attack … 20 busy as the pointer looks (CURSORS.GAF cursorattack, cursorairstrike, cursortoofar, cursorcapture, cursordefend, cursorrepair, cursorpatrol, cursorpickup, cursorteleport, cursorrevive, cursorreclamate, cursorload, cursorunload, cursormove, cursorselect, cursorfindsite, cursorred, cursorgrn, cursornormal, cursorhourglass), 21 `pathicon` (GS+0x1487F + 4n).
  - 0x10 **own ranges** (`0x004390A0`, once per unit; outside the debug view): the minimum cloak distance (def +0x208) when the unit is cloaked (activate flag 4), [15]; a kamikaze type (flag 0x10000000) with its explosion weapon (def +0x220): a ring of r = min(max(((step mod 60)·(area/2)·2)/60, 8), area/2) (area = weapon +0xD6), then the kamikaze distance (def +0x218) for units with motion, else the sight distance (+0x202), all [12]. Rings (`0x00438EA0`): n = whole(r·6.28318530717958·0.125) segments (n = 0 divides by zero), angle step 0x10000/n, points centre + (sin, cos)·(r << 16) (rounded table multiplies) at the terrain height or the centre's whole y when higher, n + 1 lines.
  - Draw bits and markers by kind (entry +0xC, +0x10): Attack_NoMove, Suppress, Attack_Chase, Attack_Kamikaze, AttackSpecial, AirToAir, AirToGround, AirToGroundHover 8/1; AirStrike 8/2; Capture 8/4; Ground_Pickup 8/12; Ground_Unload 8/13; Teleport 8/9; VTOL_Pickup 8/8; VTOL_Unload 8/9; VTOL_Landing 8/14; HelpBuild, RepairUnitNoMove 0x18/6; VTOL_HelpBuild 8/6; QMove, VTOL_Move 2/14; QPatrol, VTOL_Patrol, VTOL_RepairPatrol 2/7; VTOL_Follow 2/5; VTOL_RepairUnit 2/6; VTOL_Reclaim, VTOL_ReclaimUnit 2/11; Move_Ground 0x12/14; Follow_Ground 0x12/5; Patrol, RepairPatrol 0x12/7; RepairUnit 0x12/6; Resurrect, Reclaim, ReclaimUnit 0x12/11; MobileBuild 0x13/0; VTOL_MobileBuild 3/0; Standby, Standby_Mine 0x10/15; others 0.
- **Health bars (exact)**: drawn by the frame for the visible units after the queued orders and before the fog, only with the bar option (visual option bit 0 of GS+0x37F06); then own units get the bar and, in a group, the group digit. Without the option nothing (also not for grouped units).
- **Pointer animation** (`0x004AB5D0` in the interface pass `0x004A9FD0`; look change `0x00491C80` / `0x004AB400`): each pass dt = wall-clock ticks (`0x004B6340`: tick count × 30 / 1000) since the last pass; for a look with more than one frame its countdown (16 bits) loses dt; while it is below 1 the next frame comes (past the last: the first again when the sequence entry's loop byte (+2) is set, else the look ends — no pointer drawn — until another look is set) and its time (the frame entry's second word) is added. A new look (only when different from the current) starts at frame 0 with that frame's time. Pointer looks change only where the battle sets them (outside the view and the minimap: normal; over the view: the resolved order look, except in placement mode). Checked: frame_harness `--hud` runs the original's step (`0x004B8BF0`, start `0x004B8B30`) and the client's side by side for all 15 looks over random tick counts — 4,500 steps per run, 0 differences. The menus' pointer follows the same rule.
- **Panel buttons (exact)** (`0x004A6AE0` per button in the interface pass, gadget state +0x138; checked with gui_harness's pointer events through the original's own pass against the client's headless frames: pressed looks of build buttons with either mouse button, release off the button, page arrows held, the tabs, an order button on and off, a "+3" count — 0 differing pixels among the changed ones). A press of either button (messages 0x201/0x203 left, 0x204/0x206 right) over an enabled button grabs it and remembers which button (`0x004AB690`); the button acts when let go over it:
  - plain buttons (attribs without 0x10/0x40/8/0x100, incl. the build buttons 0x20 and the page arrows): pressed look only while held with the pointer over them; let go: unpressed, and acting when over it;
  - tabs (0x10): no look while held; let go over it: pressed (the battle then sets ORDERS/BUILD by the build-menu flag, `0x0041A120`);
  - order buttons (0x40): pressed while held over them, unpressed while held off; let go over: toggled from the state at the press (a second press ends the mode); let go off: back to that state;
  - switches (0x100): left press only, acting at once (stage + 1, wrapping);
  - a button that acts unpresses the other buttons of its group (same nonzero assoc, `0x004A0340`) — STOP ends the order mode that way.
  The build menu's handler (`0x0041AA00`) does not ask which mouse button acted except for the factory counts: PREV / NEXT set the deferred flags 0x100 / 0x80 (run next pass by `0x0041C180`: previous / next page, wrapping over the def's page count +0x22E − 1, sound nextbuildmenu) — so the **right button turns the pages too** (gui_harness: right release on NEXT sets 0x80, on PREV 0x100); ORDERS / BUILD set 0x200 / 0x400 (build-menu flag off / on, page kept); a structure's button (buildee BMcode 0) starts placement with either button (sound addbuild); a mobile type's button changes the focus unit's queue while it is selected: left +1, right −1, Shift ×5 (sounds addbuild / subbuild for the viewed player's units). FIREORD / MOVEORD step 0 → 1 → 2 → 0 (a mixed selection counts as the last) whichever button; ONOFF / CLOAK: off or mixed → on, on → off.
  - Counts: the build buttons' captions are "+n" (none at 0) of the focus unit's orders with flag 0x100 for the type (param1 summed over both lists, `0x00439D80` / `0x004199B0`); captions are laid out by the gadget's attribs (0x20: centred, `(w − 1 − text width)/2 + pressed + 1`, top row `bottom + pressed − text height − 4`) and drawn in the interface's picture font (hattfont12; glyph colours kept) whenever that font is loaded, whatever font gadget the layout names.
- **Mission table** (0x00512344, 0x19-byte entries built from static entries found by their lookup names): +0 display text, +4 behaviour, +8 the drawing routine, +0xC queued-order draw bits, +0x10 marker (pointer) number, +0x15 lookup name.
- **GUI screens are drawn into their own surface** (`0x004AB0B0` puts each open screen's surface at its header position): gadgets outside the header gadget's rectangle never show (the game menu's "GAME OPTIONS" label at 161,140 right of its 128-pixel panel). A header position of −1 centres the screen, −2 centres it in the battle view's columns; a screen that would leave the screen is centred on that axis (`0x004A81E0`). The Tab bar (TABMENU, y −33) shows at h − 33 at every screen size (gui_harness). A caption set by the program takes a new quick key (the game menu's "Settings": its "t"). Compared with gui_harness at 640×480, 1024×768 and 1920×1080: the game menu (ARMOPT) and the in-battle options menu (PREFS) are identical (0 of 45,056 pixels each). Open: the in-battle option pages (SOUNDSRT … at 128,128, merged into PREFS with flag 0x200 by `0x004AA8F0`) show one row higher in the original (top row 127) than in the client (128); the merge's placement rule is not yet read.
- **Chat line (exact)** (`0x494050` open, handler `0x493BF0`; checked with gui_harness `talk` over the client's own frame (`bg:`), the original's interface pass fed keys, against ta_play headless frames: 640×480, 1024×768, 1920×1080 — typing, Left ×3, Home, Delete / End / Backspace / Up, Enter; the field-width limit, the 63-character limit, Escape and reopening: whole frames 0 differing pixels). Enter in a battle (not while watching) opens **TALK.GUI** with flags 0x880 (TALK2.GUI with flags 0x800 when a network game asked for the recipient panel, below); interface flag 4 (GS+0x37EBE) is set while it is open (keys don't scroll). Layout: header x 128, y −33 (a negative y counts from the bottom: the box is at h − 33, 512 × 33, over the bottom bar, at every screen size); CONSOLE (picture, kind 12: the `console` sequence of `anims\TALK.GAF`, top-left at (2, 0) of the box, clipped to the box; the loader zeroes a picture's colour word so it is blitted as it is); TALK (text field at (16, 7), 350 × 19, attribs 1, maxchars 63); SENDTO (button "Options", only in network games — `0x4A0570` switches it off otherwise). Flag 0x80: no stock panel (the box keeps the screen under it as it was at the opening); flag 0x800: the layout under it (in a battle the side panel's layout, its box (0, 128)–(127, h − 1) on that layout's surface; with no layout open the whole screen) goes through the shade table once (`0x4BF4D0`, −24 → row 8) — it stays dimmed while the chat line is open, and closing redraws it. **Signed quirk**: that routine indexes the row with the pixel as a signed byte, so pixels 0x80–0xFF take row 7's entry (row 8 − 256 + p); all 45,056 panel pixels checked. **Text field drawing** (`0x4A4D70`): attribs 1 fills the field with interface colour 0 (else the panel art); the text from (field x, field y + 3), width field w − 1 (`0x4A50E0`: a glyph wider than what is left ends the line); a picture font's glyphs go through the light table row given by the gadget's colour word — the focus marking (`0x4A16F0`) clears every text field's word and sets the focused one's to 30 (a non-text gadget gets the six shaded outlines instead), so the chat text shows through light row 30; the insertion point: a line in interface colour 9 at x + width of the text before it, from y + 3 to y + 3 + font height (inclusive); no blinking (the field is only redrawn on edits). **Editing** (`0x4AB720`, all queued keys in one pass; the key queue holds 30): a character 0x20–0x7F goes in at the insertion point when the line is shorter than maxchars and its width plus the character's is at most the field's width − 4 (attribs 2: letters, digits, '_', ' ', '\'' only); Backspace (8) removes the one before the point, Delete (0xEF) the one at it, Home 0xF0 / End 0xF1 / Left 0xF4 / Right 0xF6 move the point; Ctrl+V (0xBF) and 0xEE paste the clipboard (cut to maxchars − 1 and to the field's width; not built); Up / Down: nothing (no history). Enter: focus gone, the handler sends the line and the screen closes; Escape: the line emptied, then the same (an empty line sends nothing). **Sending** (`0x493BF0`): leading spaces go; '+' runs the rest as a command line (permission bits 1, 7 with the debug switch, | 2 with cheats; a cheat that ran makes the line go to everybody); a line whose second character is ',', ':' or ';' and whose first is 'a'/'A' (allies), 'e'/'E' (enemies) loses those two characters and goes to them; a digit n there sends to player n alone ("custom"), or drops the whole line when slot n is empty; the console line is "<%s%s%s> %s" (sender name, "->" and a target name when there is one, the text), kind 4, also sent to the network (`0x453360`), and in network games logged (`0x46C810`). The line buffer (133 bytes) is cleared at the first opening and after each send. **Network**: SENDTO shown; pressing it stores its stage in GS+0x2BEE bit 8 and reopens: with the bit set TALK2.GUI (header y −164: the line at the bottom plus a recipient panel above — SENDTYPE All / Allies / Enemies / Custom and PLAYER0–9 buttons with LOGO0–9, `0x493AE0` fills them; a LIVEPLYR gadget sets Custom and toggles that player). Client: `GameClient::draw_chat` / `chat_insert` / `key_down` (`client/src/game_client.cpp`, `hud.cpp`), layout from TALK.GUI in `client_content.cpp` (`Content::chat`); typed characters come from the platform as Text events (SDL text input), Backspace / Delete / Left / Right repeat when held.
- **Main menu sparks (exact)** (2026-10-17; gui_harness `main srand:1 passes:300:…` — the original's interface pass `0x4A9FD0` run 300 times, each pass composited — against ta_play headless frames 0–299: 0 differing pixels in all 300 frames, 54,683 pixels changed pass to pass). **Speed:** the routine is the top screen's per-pass callback (screen descriptor +0x1C, set by `0x4263B0`), called once by every interface pass (`0x4A9FD0`, from the main loop's pass `0x499890`); it reads no clock. The main loop (`0x49E830`) runs passes back to back; a pass ends with the presentation (`0x4C63A0`: full screen, the surface's Flip with the wait flag, so the loop runs at the display's refresh rate; windowed, a waiting Blt). So the sparks move once per shown frame — 60 times a second on a 60 Hz display, not 30 (the client stepped them at 30 per second of wall clock: half speed). The client now steps them once per frame and its window front end presents with vsync. (The presentation in a pass comes before that pass's compositing — `0x426E80` presents, then `0x496BB0` composites — so the screen shows the previous pass's picture: one frame of lag, not modelled.) **Surface:** each pass first redraws the whole main menu (the pass starts with `0x4A81E0(…, flags | 0x40)` when GUI+0xCCA is set; the redraw ends with the focus outline `0x4A16F0`, which sets GUI+0xCCA again — so while the focus is shown, every pass): picture, gadgets, focus outline. The sparks then work on that surface: the previous pass's marks are gone (a spark's restore of its old place writes the picture's pixel, also over a gadget), the trace test sees the gadgets and this pass's earlier marks, and a mark lies over everything. (Stepping on a kept surface — the client's first version — diverged after 13 passes: old marks blocked later sparks.) **Harness note:** the C runtime keeps rand() state per thread and every harness call runs on a new thread, so gui_harness `srand:` replaces rand() (`0x4E4870`) by the same generator on one shared word.
- **Build menus (exact)** (2026-10-17; gui_harness `battle buildpages:DIR --grey` against ta_play `--dump-build-pages DIR`: every builder's pages for both viewing sides — 244 pages (122 × 2) — gadget lists (name, place, size, grayed) identical and 0 of 10,993,664 pixels differ). **Page count** (the definition's byte +0x22E, set by the battle loading `GameData_LoadAll`): with `guis\<unitname>1.GUI` … `<unitname>n.GUI` present (consecutive from 1) it is n + 1; with none, 1 when `<unitname>0.GUI` exists (flag +0x241 bit 31), else 0 (no build menu); the download loader (`UnitDef_LoadDownloadMenus` `0x42DCF0`) then raises it to every download entry's MENU for the builder. Pages shown: 1 … count − 1 (bits 23–25 of the unit's flags with the build-menu bit 22; PREV / NEXT wrap over them; with a count below 2 the PREV and NEXT gadgets are switched off). **Opening page p** (`0x41ACE0`, from the panel refresh `0x41B2E0` with the unit's name and page): the layout is `<unitname><p>.GUI`, or — when that file does not exist — `<prefix>DL.GUI` of the **viewing (local) player's** side (side record +0x1E, the nameprefix: ARMDL / CORDL, six IGPATCH gadgets). Then every download entry (in table order: `download\*.tdf` files in enumeration order, their sections in order) whose builder is the unit's type and whose MENU − 1 = p renames the layout's gadget **record BUTTON + 4** (record 0 the header, 1 the font, 2–3 PREV / NEXT, 4–9 the six slots in the file's order) to the entry's UNITNAME, clears its grayed bit, sets common attributes 4 and its record bit +0xB4 bit 0 (its art then comes only from `anims\<name>_gadget.GAF`, sequence `<name>`; its size stays the description's); a later entry for the same record wins; the layout is reloaded. So ARMMLV (download MENU 2, BUTTON 5) takes ARMVP1.GUI's sixth slot (an IGPATCH) on **page 1** (the client had it on a page 2 of its own). Then (pages from 1) every gadget with common attribute 4 gets grayed exactly when its name is no unit type (`UnitDef_FindIdByName`, a binary search over the name-sorted definitions); the description's grayedout stays on the others (IGPATCH: grayedout = 1, one frame in commongui, drawn grayed — frame 0, grey table, shade row 12). The tabs and switches follow the selection (`0x41A120`; gadgets are found by **substring** of their names, `0x49FE60`), and with the loading ability the BLAST gadget is hidden (LOAD and BLAST share a place). **Names:** the interface's own roles come before unit names: CORE's tab "CORBUILD" is also a unit type's name (the client had made it a build button). Gadgets with no role and not grayed (the stockpile buttons ARMMAKENUKE, …) are drawn enabled. In a battle the focus outline is off (the loading `0x497F40` calls `0x49FA70`). Importer: `UnitDefTable::download_menus` (the table in the game's order), `client_content.cpp` (pages per viewing side, `UnitTypeLook::pages_for`); client: `compose_panel` / `button_dims` (`client/src/game_client.cpp`).
- **Console (exact)** (2026-10-17; frame_harness `--hud`: per scene random lines added through the original's `0x463CA0` and the client's `GameClient::message` — kinds 1, 2, 4, 8, senders 0–9 or none, characters 1–255, lengths up to 90 — random line counts 0–30, text scroll 0–20 and screen chat, expiries `0x463EF0` at random later steps; the rings compared, then the pixels the console changes in either frame: 12 scenes at 640 × 480 and 1024 × 768, rings equal, 0 of 145,991 changed pixels differ). **Ring** (GS+0x12EF, 30 entries of 0x48 bytes: text 64, tick +0x40, parameter +0x44 (a unit id for notices), sender +0x46 (a player record, 10 none), kind byte +0x47 (low four bits the kind: 1 unit notice, 2 the game's own notes, 4 chat, 8 other players' notes; bit 0x20 "marked"); next GS+0x2A3E, oldest GS+0x2A40). **Add** (`0x463CA0`): nothing for an empty text or with textlines (GS+0x37F27) 0; when (next + 1) **modulo textlines** (not 30) equals the oldest, the oldest moves on (wrapping at 30) — so with textlines below 30 the ring can keep more lines than are shown, and fewer at times; the text is copied to 63 characters; the kind byte's upper bits stay what the slot had; a line with a sender beeps "MessageArrived". Own chat lines go through `0x463E50` ("<%s%s%s> %s", kind 4, no sender); a network chat line names its sender (logo). **Expiry** (`0x463EF0`, once at the end of each pass that ran steps, inside the step routine): the oldest goes when its tick + (textscroll + 1) × 30 is below the game step (unsigned). **Drawing** (`0x464060`, in the battle frame): with textlines 0 nothing; the first line shown is found by walking back from next at most textlines − 1 times, stopping at the oldest — so at most textlines − 1 lines show; from y 52, x 138; the filter (GS+0x37EFE, always 3): with screen chat (registry "screenchat", default 1) every line, else only kinds 1, 4 and 8; the colour is interface colour 15 (10 when marked); a line with a sender first gets the sender's colour logo (`0x467C00`: 32xlogos frame of the player's colour stretched onto the square (138, y) … (138 + s, y + s), s = whole(console font height × 0.8)) and its text starts at whole(138 + 1.5 s); the text is drawn by `0x4A50E0`: with the GUI's current picture font loaded (GUI+0x14: hattfont12, set by the front end's set-up) its glyphs — characters from 0x20 that have a frame, the frame drawn with its own colours at its offsets (the font loader `0x4AEDD0` lowered every frame's y offset by the 'I' frame's height), a space only moving on, no width limit — else the console font (`fonts\COMIX.FNT`, GS+0x391F9, `0x42A320`) in the colour with background 0xFE (transparent). Each shown line moves y on by the **console font's** height. (The client drew every line in the side font in plain colour 15 and added an invented "Skirmish on <map>" line; both gone.)
- **Open after the build menu / console / sparks round (2026-10-17):** the menus' grayed gadgets: the game greys them through the grey table (built from the game palette) before the shade, the client's menus only shade, and gui_harness's menu checks ran without display flag 0x100 (use `--grey`; not re-checked); the one-pass lag of the presentation (above) is not modelled; the own chat line's "<name> " prefix (`0x463E50`, the local record's name at +0x49) is not built (the client's battles keep no player names), and the client's network chat text ("Player n: …") is its own; a download entry naming an unknown builder leaves its slot's unit word as the allocator left it (the importer skips such entries; the shipped data has none).
- **Pointer confinement** (2026-10-17): the original never calls ClipCursor (its imports have GetCursorPos / SetCursorPos only): it runs full screen through DirectDraw's exclusive mode, so the pointer cannot leave the picture, menus and battles alike; Alt+Tab and the like leave the exclusive mode. The client's SDL3 front ends (menus and direct battles) hold the pointer in the window while it has the keyboard focus and release it when the focus goes (Alt+Tab, Windows key, Ctrl+Esc, Ctrl+Alt+Del, another window) or the window is minimised / hidden (`client::confine_pointer`, `front.cpp`); since the pointer-and-focus entry below, it is held again only once the system pointer is inside the window.
- **Pointer and focus** (2026-10-17; read in the original, not run). **The window procedure** (`0x004B5CC0`, same bytes as the reference program's `Env_DisplayWndProc`): WM_ACTIVATE only stores "active" (display +0xE0: 0 when the low word of wParam is 0, else 1) — a flag nothing else in the program reads (no other access to that offset near a display-context load); WM_ACTIVATEAPP, WM_SETFOCUS, WM_KILLFOCUS and WM_SIZE are not handled (default processing); WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_MOVE is swallowed; WM_MOUSEMOVE stores its client position (lParam) with the key flags in the pointer record (display +0x196 x, +0x19A y; `0x004C2360`); button messages are queued (32 events) with the tick; WM_PALETTECHANGED / WM_QUERYNEWPALETTE re-select the palette. No DirectInput (no import). **The pointer is the system pointer**: the routine that shows the software pointer (`0x004C2870`, at the end of every interface pass and every battle frame, and when the loading ends) first reads GetCursorPos (screen coordinates — in full screen the picture's) into the same record; the drawing pass (`0x004C25E0`) does too. So it keeps no position of its own across a focus change: on return it is wherever the system pointer is — nothing restores, re-centres or warps it. Ways it moves the system pointer: **only** the Ctrl + right-button view drag (`0x0041CC60` start: pointer hidden, its position kept, SetCursorPos to the screen centre; each battle pass `0x0041CD50`: the view's cell += (pointer − centre)/4 (rounded toward zero; the rest is lost), ×16, clamped, back to the cell, SetCursorPos to the centre again; when the record's key flags no longer have the right button: SetCursorPos back to the kept position, pointer shown). (The client's drag keeps its pointer and carries the remainder; not changed here, see Open.) **Screen-edge scrolling** (`0x0041CE90`): in full screen (display flag 2) from the record (x ≥ w or y ≥ h count as the last column/row); in the windowed DIB mode from GetCursorPos, and only while GetFocus() is the game's window, also taking a pointer up to 100 pixels past the right/bottom edge as on it. **Display on return**: nothing of its own; DirectDraw restores the exclusive mode, and lost surfaces are restored and redrawn when next used (`0x004C62C0`). The game keeps running while it has no focus. **Client** (windowed or borderless instead of exclusive): the pointer follows the system pointer at all times — motion events inside the window place it, focused or not; the platform's motion at a focus change places it where the system pointer is then (`client::pointer_motion` / `pointer_event`, `front.cpp`); outside the window (only possible while not held) it is **off the picture** (`InputEvent::PointerLeave`: not drawn, at no edge — no edge scrolling — nothing under it) instead of being clamped onto the nearest edge; the hold stands in for full screen (`client::pointer_held`: focused, shown, and the system pointer inside the window) so a refocus never pulls the pointer in from where the user left it — it is held from the moment it comes back into the window (or the window is clicked). Before: on refocus SDL's ClipCursor pulled the system pointer onto the window's edge and the client clamped outside positions onto the edge, so the view scrolled while the user was elsewhere. Checked: ta_play `--pointer-selftest` (16 checks over a focus/minimise/move sequence and SDL events converted as the loops do) and headless battle scripts (`wmove` / `leave`): a pointer that moves out past the left edge leaves the view where it was (scroll 4656 as without it), a pointer on the left column scrolls 32 pixels a frame until `leave` (480 = 15 frames) — not tested live: the real hold (ClipCursor) and focus changes with a real mouse and Alt+Tab (no window may be shown on the user's screen).
- **Menu keys (exact on the multiplayer screens)** (2026-10-17; the original: gui_harness `press:KEY[:MODS]` / `frame` / `report` / `sweep:KEYS:MODS`; the client: ta_play `--key-sweep KEYS:MODS[:X,Y[:TEXT]]` with `--net-screen`; implementation `client::menu_key_codes`, `client::edit_line`, `LayoutScreen::key_code` / `move_focus` in `client/src/menu.cpp`). **How keys arrive.** The window procedure hands WM_KEYDOWN to `0x4C1D50` (virtual key, 0), WM_SYSKEYDOWN (Alt held, or F10) to `0x4C1D50` (virtual key, 1), and WM_CHAR (made by the main loop's TranslateMessage) straight to the 30-entry key queue (`0x4C1B20`); WM_SYSCHAR goes to the default procedure. `0x4C1D50` queues on its own only the arrows (0xF4 left, 0xF5 up, 0xF6 right, 0xF7 down), Home 0xF0, End 0xF1, PgUp 0xF2, PgDn 0xF3, Insert 0xEE, Delete 0xEF, Pause 0xF8 and F1..F12 0xE2..0xED (20 lower with Ctrl); letters, digits and the sign keys only with Ctrl (letters 0xAA..0xC3, digits 0xC4..0xCD) or as a system key (letters lower case, digits as they are, signs through a table). So plain characters, Enter 13, Escape 27, Tab 9, Backspace 8 and Space 32 come only as typed characters (case as typed); with Alt a letter is its lower-case letter and Enter, Escape, Tab, Backspace and Space give nothing; Ctrl+letter gives 0xAA.. and then its control character (so Ctrl+M is an Enter, Ctrl+H a Backspace, Ctrl+I a Tab). Shift, Ctrl and Alt are read with GetAsyncKeyState when a key is handled (`0x4C1B80`). **One key per frame.** The main loop's frame (`0x499890`) peeks the queue for its own keys, runs the interface pass (`0x4A9FD0`) on the top layout, which takes one key (a layout whose key mode, layout +0x18, is 0 only peeks it and skips the special keys; every menu screen sets mode 1 with `0x49FB10`), then the state routine: the connection, game list and menu states take one more key off the queue first thing (`0x426E80`), the provider settings' and the battle room's states do not (the room's runs the room's frame `0x44A680`). **Special keys** (`0x4A9B90`; the layout's focus is layout +0x20, the field taking keys GUI +0x64): Tab moves the focus forward, Shift+Tab back; Enter, unless a text field takes keys, presses the Enter default (header crdefault; a screen's set-up may name it: SELPROV's is SELECT, the battle room's the local player's READYn) when it is shown and not a grayed button, else acts as Space; Escape presses the Escape default when it is shown (grayed or not), else goes on; Space presses the focused gadget when it is a shown button (not grayed; a switch steps to its next stage), list or screen-drawn area, else goes on (a focused text field gets it as a character); Left / Right go on when the focus is a text field, step a slider at least as wide as tall (its value ∓ 1 within 0..count − 1, `0x4A96D0` / `0x4A9780`), else move the focus; Up / Down move a focused list's selection (`0x4A9830` / `0x4A99C0`, then the list's callback), else move the focus — from a text field too. **Other keys** go to the shown gadgets in description order; the first that takes one is pressed: a button by its quick key (the key equals the quick key in either case; not grayed; not while a text field takes keys unless Alt is held; not unless GUI +0xCC6 is 1 — only the battle's chat line clears it); a label by its quick key (+0x147, same conditions), acting on the gadget its link names (a button steps to its next stage and is pressed; another gadget takes the focus — a text field also the keys — and is pressed; no such gadget: the label itself; one that is hidden, a grayed button or a disabled slider: the key goes on to later gadgets); the text field taking keys edits its line (`0x4AB720`; it gets 0 while Alt is held): characters 0x20..0x7F go in at the insertion point while the line is shorter than its limit and its width plus the character's is at most the field's width − 4 (attribute 2: letters, digits, '_', ' ', '\'' only), Backspace removes the character before the point, Delete the one at it, Home / End / Left / Right move the point, Insert and Ctrl+V paste the clipboard (cut to the limit and the width; the point stays), Enter and Escape end the editing and the field is pressed (Escape empties it first); the editor also takes every key still queued. A pressed gadget (key or pointer) becomes the focus, the field taking keys is cleared (a pressed text field takes them again, its point at its end), the layout's handler runs, and a press the handler leaves unconsumed closes the layout. A quick-key press and Enter / Escape do not step a switch's stage themselves (only a click, Space and a label do); the handlers seen (skirmish, battle room) toggle their setting and show the stage from it, so the client steps the stage on every press. **Focus moves** (`0x4A7960`): each gadget's column is the x of the first earlier gadget (description order) within 9 pixels of its own x, else its own (the search stops at a column of 0; the column table is cleared for 50 entries only, so gadgets past the 49th also search uninitialised stack words — not emulated); left, right and Tab order gadgets by y × 5000 + x, up and down by column × 5000 + y; left, up and Shift+Tab take the largest key below the focus's (none: the largest of all), right, down and Tab the smallest above it (none: the smallest), ties to the earlier gadget; candidates are shown gadgets without attribute 0x400 that are buttons (not grayed), lists (not attribute 0x100), text fields, screen-drawn areas, or sliders at least as wide as tall and not disabled (+0x157 = 0; the room sets it on a joining player's resource and unit-limit sliders). A move clears the field taking keys; a text field reached takes them, its point at its end. **Outcomes on the multiplayer screens** (each letter, digit, Enter, Escape, Tab, Space, Backspace and arrow, plain, with Shift and with Alt; 135 trials per screen or situation, 12 of them, 1,620 trials): SELPROV (focus DPLAY): O Settings, P / Escape Previous, S / Enter Select, Space acts on the picked provider, Tab / Right → SELECT, Shift+Tab / Left → PREVMENU; TCP (ADDRESS takes keys): characters and Space type, Backspace erases, Enter presses ADDRESS (the handler's OK), Escape PREV, Alt+C PREV, Alt+O OK, Tab / Down → OK and Shift+Tab / Up → PREV (each clears the field); SERIAL (focus HOST): C / Escape PREV, H / Enter / Space HOST, J JOIN, Tab / Right / Down → JOIN, Left / Up → SPEEDS; MODEM (focus HOST; no field takes keys at first): A / Enter / Space HOST, C / Escape PREV, D JOIN, Left → NAME (which then takes keys), Up → MODEMS; SELGAME (focus GAMENAME, no field takes keys): J Join, N / Enter Start New, P / Escape Previous, U Update, Space joins the picked game, Tab / Right → STARTNEW, Shift+Tab / Left → PREVMENU — with Shift or Alt the same; with the name field clicked: letters, digits and Space type into it, Enter presses it (the handler does nothing), Escape Previous, Tab → UPDATE, Up → GAMENAME, Down → STARTNEW, and only Alt+J / Alt+N / Alt+P / Alt+U press buttons; NEWMULTI (focus OK, no field takes keys): N / Enter / Space OK, P / Escape Cancel, Tab / Right / Down → CANCEL, Left / Up / Shift+Tab → PASSWORD (which then takes keys); message box: O / Enter / Escape / Space OK; LOUNGE2 (MESSAGE takes keys): characters type into the chat line, Enter sends it, Escape leaves, Tab / arrows move the focus, Alt + a gadget's key presses it (Alt+R the local player's Ready). **Why N started a new game in the client but not in the original**: on the game list the original's N also presses Start New while no field takes keys — but once the name (or password) field takes keys, N types an 'n'. The client's multiplayer screens kept their own field editing: after the platform's first Text event a letter's key-down no longer counted as a character there and fell through to the quick keys, so typing an N in the name field pressed Start New (the action Enter has), J joined, P left, U updated, and before that first Text event every letter went in twice. All screens now share one key path built on these rules (the key-down of a character key carries `InputEvent::text_follows` and gives no code; the character comes with the Text event). Checked (smoke): the client's outcome table now equals the original's in 1,612 of the 1,620 trials (every trial on the connection, address, serial, modem, game list — also with the name field clicked and typed into —, new game and message screens); the battle room differs in 4 trials each as host and as joiner, all effects of the room's handler the client's room does not have (the restrictions dialog, the slot question, the map choice / view, the host's Ready giving the chat line the focus back). Before the change 889 of the 1,620 trials differed. The main menu (same key path) differs only where its handlers do (Exit, Intro: 6 of 135; before 24). **Not done / open:** the key queue itself (the client handles each key at once instead of one per frame, so two keys within one frame are not lost as in the original's game list); the clipboard (Insert, Ctrl+V); auto-repeat of the arrows and Enter (the platform layer passes repeats only for Backspace, Delete, Left and Right); PgUp / PgDn / Insert keys (no client key); the 50-entry column table quirk; gui_harness: the room's Enter (chat send) faults in the harness at `0x44A791` and Alt+A (unit limit) at `0x44CD27` (the network sends are stubbed), the modem screen is swept without modems (the address query fails), and the skirmish's settings carry over between sweep trials.
- **Insertion point, quick-key underlines and the focus marking's pass (exact)** (*2026-10-20*; read in `0x004A9FD0`, `0x004A81E0`, `0x004A16F0`, `0x004A4D70`, `0x004A56B0`, `0x004A5F40`, `0x004A7960`, `0x0049FC50`, `0x004A3780`, `0x004AB720`; checked with gui_harness against ta_play headless, every frame 0 differing pixels: the battle room (`room_start_glow.txt`, 9 frames, and typing / Tab in its chat line), the main menu, single player, options, skirmish (a quick key moving the focus), NEWMULTI (Left onto the password, typing, Tab away, Left back; pointer presses on the password and the game name field), SELGAME (`selgame_two_games.txt`: pointer presses on both fields, typing, a press on a game row, Tab)). **Underlines**: a button's caption (`0x004A5F40`, centred captions) and a label (`0x004A56B0`) underline the first occurrence (exact case, `strchr`) of their quick key (+0x13A / +0x147, set by the loader `0x004A05E0`) in interface colour 2, always — no GUI flag, no Alt, no focus condition; a label's underline starts at the label's x (not its aligned text), on the row `y + text height − 1`; a grayed label (+0x148 bit 0) draws none (it is dimmed instead). **Insertion point**: drawn by the text field's drawing (`0x004A4D70`) when the field is the one taking keys (GUI +0x64) at that drawing, a line in interface colour 9 at x + width of the text before the point, y + 3 … y + 3 + text height; no blinking, no window-active or timer condition. **When gadgets are drawn**: the interface pass (`0x004A9FD0`) first runs the special keys (`0x004A9B90`: Tab, arrows, Enter / Escape defaults, Space — one key a pass), then, when GUI +0xCCA is set, the layout's full redraw (`0x004A81E0` flag 0x40), which ends with the focus marking (`0x004A16F0`: clears every text field's colour word, sets the focused one's to light row 30 — or draws the six outlines around a non-field focus — and sets +0xCCA again; only while GUI +0xA2 shows outlines): so while the outlines are shown the layout is redrawn every pass. Then the pointer and the remaining keys reach the gadgets (presses `0x004A6AE0`, lists `0x004A3780`, quick keys, editing `0x004AB720`), each redrawing only its own gadget. Consequences, all reproduced: a text field's lighting is the previous pass's marking (a field gaining the focus lights one pass later, one losing it stays lit one pass); the outlines show the focus as it was at the pass's redraw (a quick key or a pointer press moves them one pass later; Tab / arrows at once); a field that loses the keys to a press after the redraw keeps its insertion point until the next pass, the field gaining them shows its point at once (the press redraws it, `0x004A7290` → `0x004AB6C0`); a press on a list row redraws that list (its own box x … x + w − 1, so the column x + w keeps the old pick's shading for the pass) while the lists of its association only take the pick and show it at the next pass. Client: `LayoutScreen::note_pass_focus` / `pass_focus_` / `pass_field_` / `field_mark_` (`client/src/menu.cpp`), `NetScreen::pass_redrawn` / `ListState::held` (`ta_compat/src/client_net_screens.cpp`).
- **Game list Join / Watch and the menu loop's message** (2026-10-20; `0x4437C0`, `0x426E80`, `0x4517B0`, `0x4C9FD0`, `0x451220`): WATCH, JOINGAME or a press on a list gadget (kind 2) copies the picked description (0x54 bytes from GS+0x2AA7) to GS+0x2B6A. Version byte (GS+0x2B7A >> 16) other than GS+1: `0x425730` copies "You do not have a compatible version for this game." into the buffer `0x511FB8` (249 characters) and the press ends (`0x4AB0A0`), no sound; the front-end loop (state 0x10 sub-states 0 and 1, and 0x0F through the fall-through) shows a non-empty buffer in MSGBOX as wide as the text + 20 (`0x4A5030`, not translated) and clears it. Flags GS+0x2B6E bit 31 or bit 20: only "Previous". Then the empty name ("You must enter your name", 200, the name field takes the keys), the password into GS+0x2BE3 and the player record +0x80 (10 characters), Watch sets record +0x9B bit 0x40 and plays "Multi" (state 0x13), Join clears it and plays "BigButton" (state 0x12). State 0x12/0x13: `0x4517B0` → `0x4C9FD0` (the DirectPlay Open as a joiner); on failure nothing is shown: the sub-state goes to 0 and the game list is built anew (`0x443CB0`, the sessions enumerated again). The only DirectPlay-level texts: `0x450D80` failing (the service with its address) → "An error occurred trying to use this service" through the same buffer, back to the provider list; `0x451220` (the session player) failing → the record is set back to controller 0 (`0x463C60`) first, then the first record 0-9 with a controller and the host flag (+0x97 bit 0) is looked for — none: index 10, the record after the table (cleared by `0x41D920`, never seated); when that record is in use (+0) with controller 1 or 2: "Direct Play failed to add new player.\n\nRecommended you go to previous screen and re-create the game session.\n", else "... re-join the game session.\n", 500 wide (corrected 2026-10-20: the failed record itself never counts, so without records left from an earlier session the text is the re-join one, also on the host). The game's own password is checked in the room ("You did not have the correct password", out reason 4). `0x451540` (hosting) does not check the open's result. **Host failure sequence** (2026-10-20, `0x426E80` state 0x10 sub-state 0x11): `0x451540` (host flag on this machine's side record, GS+0x2A3C = 0, session open via `0x4C9920`, result ignored) → `0x451220(local, 1)`; on success `0x450A10` seats the player; either way the code checksum check, state 0x11, sub-state 0. MSGBOX (`0x4ABD90`) is only pushed onto the layout stack (`0x4AA8F0` puts a new layout on top; not modal), so the next pass's room set-up (`0x449BB0`, LOUNGE2 with flags 0) opens over it: a broken room — no record in use, the host's controls (the flag stayed), the map list's first map (`0x435D30(1)`), sends to a session that is not open; the message box is the top screen again once the room closes. **Message width** `0x4A5030`: with the GUI's picture font (GUI +0x14; `0x491200` sets it to hattfont12 (GUI +0x8, `0x4AEDD0` index 0), `0x4689C0` switches it in battles) the sum of the font's frame widths (`0x4B7F30` frame = byte) over every byte to the end, else the current bit font's width (`0x4C1480`).
- **Battle texts** (2026-10-20): the game clock option (GS+0x37F2F bit 6, `Render_DrawBattleFrame` `0x46A1D0`) is "%s : %02d:%02d:%02d" (`0x507794`) of "Game Time" with GS+0x38A47 split as hours = t / 108000 (unsigned), minutes = rest / 1800, seconds = (rest − minutes·1800) / 30 (signed), at x 130, y = height − 34 − font height, colour entry 15. The speed line (`0x490DF0`) only when the clamped speed differs from GS+0x38A4B: "Game Speed Normal", else "%s  %c%d\n" (`0x5091E0`) of "Game Speed", '+' when above 10 else ' ', and speed − 10 (kind 2, sender 10). Kill line (`0x46A860`): "%d %s" with "kill" / "kills", from 5 kills "%d %s - %s" with "Veteran". Screenshot folder fallback "user_images" (`0x42F9A0`). Map choice SIZE "%s  %s: %s" of memory, "Players", numplayers (`0x444A20`). Reject title "%s %s?" (`0x446080`). Room: "[%s]" of "BLOCKED"; "<%s%s%s> %s" chat lines (`0x463E50`).
**Sound comparison (2026-10-17, smoke).** `tools/orig_harness/sound_harness` runs the original's sound routines in the suspended TotalA.exe with its platform mixer replaced by a stand-in that logs every buffer call (duplicate, status, play position, set position, volume, 3D place / distances / mode, play, stop, release; stand-in buffers report "playing" by the harness clock and each sound's length), with rand(), the console, the path builder, the file sound and the allocator stubbed, and compares what the output was told after every operation with the client (`client/sound_mixer.h`, `client/sound_rules.h`): `mixer` (starts of 2–6 bank sounds with random lengths, looping starts, one-shots, sweeps, stop all, limits 2–8, 3D on/off with random distances), `world` (0x47F300 and 0x47F0C0 on random settings, sight / explored grids, maps, views and positions on and off the map), `notices` (queue, frame, forget with random settings, units, categories and steps). **Smoke: 12 scenarios × 450 operations (5,400): 1,369 starts (14 restarts, 313 placed), 502 cut-offs, 1,442 world and 358 interface calls, 43 notice sounds and 175 notice texts — 0 mismatches.** Not compared offscreen: what the platform mixer makes of a placed sound (below), the streaming narration, the menus' handlers themselves (their sounds are read from the handlers; ta_play's headless sound log shows them in a menu walk: `frame N: sound NAME vol V copy K [loop|restart|pan P dist D]` and `stop NAME copy K (cut off)`).
**The client's output (not exact, documented).** The client mixes on SDL3 at the output device's own rate (48000 Hz when the device doesn't report one). The original asks for an 11025 Hz primary buffer, which only old platform mixers honoured; today's systems convert every sound buffer to the device rate, interpolating between samples. The client's mixer does the same, reading every sound and the music with linear interpolation between neighbouring frames. Its first version mixed at 22050 Hz with nearest-frame reads, which made the 11 kHz effects grainy and the music aliased. volumes as amplitude 10^(v/2000), the effect volume as a linear factor. A placed sound is attenuated as the platform's distance model does (full within the minimum distance, × minimum / distance up to the maximum distance, no further beyond) and panned by its direction from the listener: the angle between the listener's left and the sound maps linearly to −100..+100 dB taken off the far channel (the platform's software 3D without virtualisation; its exact pan law is not visible offscreen).
- **Battle room, live (LOUNGE2 on a session)** (2026-10-17; checked: two- and three-machine headless games through the menus, the rooms' states against the original's own room drawing). The 1:1 room screen (`ta_compat/src/client_net_screens.cpp`, `Lounge`) now runs every live game (the original's session protocol over TCP/IP, serial or modem; `enter_room` hands the session to it): each frame it runs the room's network part (`net::BattleRoom::frame`) and shows the records as the room's rows; the old plain room (`client/src/net_menu.cpp`, `RoomScreen`) is kept for front ends without the importer's screens. What the room does, from its handler `0x447B10`, frame `0x44A680` and refresh `0x448C70`:
  - **Rows**: slot order of the host's slot table (the original's own machine keeps its own record order; see Open). A row's controller: 1 this machine's player, 2 a computer player it runs (the host's), 3 another machine's, 4 a blocked slot. A joining machine shows its own record in row 0 until the host's slot table seats it, then the rows are made again for its slot (the local row's PLAYER becomes a label). The players are kept together: each room frame that makes the rows moves a player into a free slot (neither used nor blocked) before it, record and all ( x44A680; the engine's host does it in BattleRoom::frame).
  - **Captions and quick keys**: every refresh sets each row's PLAYER caption (the whole name) and the local RES caption anew (`0x4A0BF0`): a plain button finds its quick key in the whole caption (the first character no other gadget has, `0x4A05E0`), and only then is the caption cut to the button's width − 6 (`0x4A5D50`, the key kept). So keys move as players come and go.
  - **LOGOn** (a row of this machine): the local player asks the host for that row's logo + 1 (`0x4526C0` always asks for the local player). **PLAYERn** (another row): on the host an open slot becomes blocked; a blocked one opens and, unless the game is closed ("Can't add another player when game is closed.", width 500) or the commander rule is "deathmatch" (0x1000) or a computer player exists already, gets a computer player with the first logo nobody plays (`0x451220`; its session name "AI:" + this machine's player's name, 16 characters); a computer player that has been there 31 clock ticks leaves (status 1); another machine's human brings the kick question (YESORNO, flags 0x100: TITLE "Reject <name>?", CHOICE1 Yes → status 1 "You were rejected from the game", CHOICE2 No). (The original lets a joining machine add a computer player too; the engine's room only seats the host's.) **SIDEn** (this machine's row): the next side; past the last, side 0 and — a human, with the host's WATCHING switch on — the watch bit; a watcher's press clears it. **ALLYn**: the local player's alliance with that player toggles (0x23; sound Ally / Multi); being in the same ally team leaves the team (`0x446E90` breaks the alliances with its members); the line "<name>  allied with X" / "broke alliance with X" goes to everybody. **TEAMICONSn**: (team + 1) mod 6 (0x24); players of one team count as allied both ways in the ALLY column (`0x446C70`). **RESn** (the local row): the next display mode (the right button the one before), sent with the record and kept as the battle's. **READYn**: refused without the host's map; the host's press makes START the Enter default and gives it the focus (the chat line loses the keys; gui_harness: focus MESSAGE→START, field none). **The host's switches**: COMMANDER cycles 0x800 steps (continues, game ends, deathmatch), LOSTYPE true (0x600) → circular (0x200) → permanent (0), WATCHING 0x80 (off also clears the host's own watch bit), CHEATING 0x2000, FIXEDLOC 0x4000, MAPPING 0x100, GAMEOPEN 0x8000; each change goes out with the records and the session's description. The host's sliders (metal, energy, unit limit) go into its records each frame they move. **MESSAGE** (Enter): "<name> text" on the console and to everybody (`0x463E50`); the field keeps the keys. **PREVMENU**: status 2 for this machine's players, back to the game list (the session is closed; Update / Join open the provider again).
  - **The host's rules each frame** (`0x448C70`): in the deathmatch commander mode its computer players leave (status 11); without WATCHING its watchers play again (side 0). **A joining machine** follows the host's map, sliders and switches; a new map it lacks (or whose checksum differs) brings "<name> does not have this map" (sent to everybody) and its ready bit off; its MAP button (View Map) is grayed then, and its START always.
  - **START** (host, once everybody is ready — the start grill takes the pointer until then, then never again: its "hot" bit, gadget +0xC8): enough game CDs (every human must report one: 1 for up to 3 humans, 2 up to 6, else 3; "There are not enough game CDs present to play"), not all players in one ally team ("Can not start game with all players on the same team."), a map (else the map choice opens), remote watchers sent away when watching is off (status 9); then the option word gets 0x10 (playing: the session closes to newcomers) and 0x08 goes out. **Out of the room**: a status naming this machine's player (kick 1, closed 3, password 4, full 5, lost 6, unit 7, version 8, watching 9, creator gone 10) closes the room; the game list shows the reason (width 320; nothing for one's own leaving, 2).
  - **The map choice** (host, MAP or MAPNAME: SELMAP opened with flags 0x980, centred; handler `0x444CB0`): a pick or LOAD plays Multi and sets the map (with its checksum) in the host's records; the other machines' players count as not ready until their next records. **The map view** (a joining player, VIEWMAP.GUI over DVIEWMAP, flags 0x900): MAPNAME, SIZE "<memory>  Players: <numplayers>", MAPPIC, DESCRIPTION of the host's map, shown again when the host's map changes (`0x44A680`); OK plays Multi.
  - **Lists and fields take no focus ring** (the multiplayer screens' rule was not consulted after the menu key rework; fixed in `LayoutScreen::draw_focus_ring`).
- **Build restrictions (exact)** (`0x44C7E0`, handler `0x44C420`, its frame `0x44C220`; checked with gui_harness `unitdefs` / `restrict` / `restrictframe` — the original's own unit enumeration, room and dialog, its picture loading run tick by tick — against ta_play `--net-screen lounge:FILE` with the room's Build Restrictions pressed: 0 differing pixels): RESTRICT2.GUI opened with flags 0x880 over the room, UnitRestrict5x behind it on the room's palette and interface colours. The list: every unit definition but the "norestrict" ones, each row "<name>\r<side> <metal>M  <energy>E" (the costs cut to whole numbers; side the definition's own field +0xA0), sorted by the row's bytes; DESCLIST draws two-line rows 32 pixels high, no picked row; PICLIST the pictures `unitpics\<unitname>.PCX`, one more loaded each time the clock passes the last load + 2, each mapped onto its row's box (x + 2 .. x + w − 1 by 32, texels 1 .. size − 1). Twelve rows in view, each with SLIDERn (0..101) and COUNTn ("No Limit" at 101). A row's flags: 1 its definition not yet compared (the exchange entry's verdict +0x1A is 0) — its text shaded four times (−19..−22, the lists' "&G" rule) and its picture once (−20), its slider grayed; 2 a count of 0 — a red cross over the picture (four lines in interface colour 12). Every refresh of the rows in view (`0x44BFD0`: at opening twice, on scrolling) puts each row's count through its slider and back (`0x44BE70`), which on the host answers every peer for those definitions. The scroll slider is sized by the first list of its group — the picture list: knob = whole rows shown × slider height / rows, travel = height − knob (`0x4A3EF0`). OK: Options sound, closes; Cancel: Previous, every count back to its value at opening (101 where there was none), each answered; Reset List: 100 each ("wacky" definitions 0), the changed ones answered; Load / Save List: Options, then their dialogs (next item); closing on the host enables every listed definition again, or disables it with a count of 0, each answered. Only the host's sliders move; a joining player sees the host's counts. **Note**: before any peer has compared its definitions every row is locked, on the host too (the entries start unconfirmed, see "Network and sync").
- **Restriction list files** (2026-10-20; read from the code: `0x44BC10` Load List, `0x44B990` Save List, handlers `0x44B3C0` / `0x44B690` (no function in the database: read from the listing), list callback `0x44B600`, listing `0x44B4E0`, writer `0x44B230`, reader `0x44B140`). **Files**: `SAVEGAME\*.LST` (pattern from `Hapi_BuildVirtualPath("SAVEGAME", "*", "LST")`, counted by `Hapi_CountMatchingFiles`, listed by `0x4AF320(..., sort 1)` — the same order as the saved games: directory order, then newest write time first); the list shows each name up to its last '.' (`SAVEGAME_NAMES` / `SAVEGAME_DESCS` buffers, 256 bytes a name). **Format** (C stdio, "wb+" / "rb", little-endian int32s): `n` = definition table size (GS+0x1438F) − 1 — every definition, the norestrict ones too — then for each definition index 1 .. size − 1 whose restrictions row exists (row +0x52 = index; the row table UNITSRESTRICTINFO has size rows of 0x62 bytes, unused ones index 0), in index order: key (def +0x13E, the FBI file's simple checksum) and count (row +0x5A: 0..100, −1 no limit). **Reading**: `n` pairs whatever the file holds (fread one 4-byte item each; at the end the words keep their last values, so the extra pairs repeat the last one; the pair's words start as uninitialised stack words, and an empty file leaves `n` the stale path pointer — the client takes 0 for both, no observable difference unless a definition's key matches the stale word); each key picks the first definition from index 1 with that key, then the row with that index takes the count (no row: nothing); afterwards `0x44BFD0` on the restrictions GUI (the rows in view through their sliders, answered to every peer on the host); rows out of view keep the new count unanswered until shown or until closing (enable/disable by count). **Load List**: "Options" (the restrictions' handler), LOADLIST.GUI opened with flags 0x981 (= 0x980, `0x4AA8F0` ORs 1 in anyway: centred, screen under it dimmed), background DLoadList (`0x4288D0`); no file: the GUI closed again (`0x4A9660`) and the message box "There are no saved lists to choose from" (320 wide); else GAMES the names, DELETE and GAMENAME off ("SaveGame" named but not in the GUI), GAMENAME gets the selected name; GS+0x38A51 bit 0 set (never cleared by this dialog). Handler: CANCEL "Previous"; LOAD (OK, the Enter default) or GAMES (double click / Enter): "Options", `sprintf("%s\%s", "SAVEGAME", file name)` into GS+0x38C6B, read, the restrictions refreshed; the GUI closes the dialog (commonattribs). **Save List**: SAVELIST.GUI with flags 0x880 at its own position (81, 27), DSaveList; `0x4BCF00("SAVEGAME")` makes the folder; TITLE "Save Game" set (no such gadget); DELETE ("Delete List") off only when there is no file; GAMENAME attribs |= 2 (letters, digits, '_', ' ', '\'', 20 at most), the focus (`0x4A7190`), the selected name in it ("" with no file — the GUI's "Name Of List To Save" never shows). Handler: closing clears GS+0x38A51 bit 0 and frees the buffers; CANCEL "Previous"; DELETE: "SMLBUTTON", `DeleteFileA("SAVEGAME\<file name>")` of the selected row, the list again (with no file left the old buffers stay), GAMENAME the new selection; GAMES / LOAD / GAMENAME (Enter): "Options", then if GAMENAME is not empty `Hapi_BuildVirtualPath("SAVEGAME", text, "LST")` (SAVEGAME-<language>\ when that opens; the extension rule cuts at the path's last '.') and `0x44B230`. A selection of −1 would make `0x4B6AF0` walk memory (not reachable: a filled list always has a row selected). Client: `ta/restrict_list.h` (format, listing order), `RestrictListFiles` in `client_net_screens.cpp`; folder `<game>\SAVEGAME` (ta_play `--saves` moves it). Dialog pixels not compared with the original (no gui_harness command).
- **Chat lines with the sender's name** (`0x463E50`, the room's MESSAGE and the battle's talk line `0x493BF0`): a line is made "<%s%s%s> %s": the sender's name, then "->" and the receivers' name when the line names them, then the text; the receivers show it as it came (colour 8, with the sender's record: MessageArrived), the sender in colour 4 without one. In battle a line whose second character is ',', ':' or ';' names its receivers by its first: a digit that player (its name; an unused record has none, nobody gets it), 'a'/'A' the allies ("Allies"), 'e'/'E' the enemies ("Enemies"); the two characters go. (The '+' rule of `0x453360` looks at the made line, which starts with '<', so it never applies to chat lines.) Client: `client::chat_line`, `NetBattleSession::send_chat`.
- **Loading screen, network bars fed** (the original's order kept): the host's 0x08 brings every machine to the loading screen at once; at each redraw (at most every 200 ms) this machine's players' progress (the average of the six rows) goes to everybody as 0x2A with a probe 0x06 (`0x456DE0`) and the others' 0x2A fill their bars; once this machine has loaded (the client loads its models before the battle start for this), the handshake runs (start positions, "loaded"), the line becomes "Synchronization complete" and the battle starts. (`client::launch_network_battle`; log "loading bars:" lines.)
- **Battle keys (2026-10-20, read in `0x495E90` case by case; checklist docs/parity/CLIENT.md).** Plain characters reach the handler only as typed characters (WM_CHAR): Shift+1 is '!' (damage bars), 'T' (Shift) follows the previous selected unit, 't' the next. **Digits**: with the SwitchAlt setting off (the default) a plain digit picks the focus unit's build page (`0x41C060`: page d − 1, 0 = the orders, when d − 1 < the definition's page count; sound nextbuildmenu, panel refresh) and Alt+digit selects the group; SwitchAlt swaps the two (the client had them the wrong way round). Ctrl+digit makes the group. **Group select** (`0x48D9A0`): over the local player's selectable units (flag 0x20, built, no transfer hold-off, carrier rule); when the group holds a unit of category CTRL_F and any unit with flag bit 31 (armed), its CTRL_F units are deselected; others leave the selection unless Shift. Every selection key (group, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+letter categories) ends with `0x495860`: the order mode back to the default (1), the Shift-kept bit 0x20 cleared, the STOP group unpressed; then the focus unit 0 and a panel refresh (Ctrl+S only when it selected something). **Ctrl+C**: CTRL_C category, then the view follows the **last** unit of the local player's range in the "Commander" category (`0x41C310`). **Ctrl+D**: the selected units of the local range (flag 0x10 only, `0x48CA20`) lose the first order of kind SELFDESTRUCT in the list its default flags name (`0x439E30`, `0x439F80`); only when none had one the selection is ordered to self-destruct. **F1** unit information (below). **F2** game menu (Shift+F2: a debug unit pick, GS+0x391B9). **F3** (`0x464000`): every console line's mark 0x20 cleared, then from the oldest line the first with a unit parameter, alive, without the visited mark 0x10 gets 0x30 and the camera's target becomes the unit's whole x and z (height not counted) less half the view (`0x41C7C0`, eased); none: every visited mark cleared and the search runs once more. **F4** toggles visual bit 0x80 (the kills panel kept out). **F12** empties the console (next = oldest = 0). **h** (network games, not watching) opens SHARE.GUI. **Ctrl+F10** (debug switch) starts or stops the film (`<image folder>\MOVIEnnn\FRAMnnnn.pcx` at FilmSpeed frames a second of game time; not built). **Speed keys** are refused in a network game for a watcher and in the F11 debug view. Damage bars toggled by `~ ! # * are saved at once (`0x430F00`). The original's queue repeats every key the system repeats (all keys, also Enter, Esc, F keys and Pause).
- **Camera stage (exact)** (`0x41CA10`, once per battle step inside the step; frame_harness `--camera`: 600 random states on two maps and screen sizes, view, target and following identical). The view (GS+0x1431F/23) eases toward a target (GS+0x14327/2B): a followed unit (GS+0x142F3) without its alive flag ends the following, else its position (x, z − y/2 of the 16.16 sums, whole parts) less half the view is the target, clamped to 0 .. scroll extent − view; then each axis moves by half the difference (C division: a difference of 1 stays), at most 320 pixels; then the shake (`0x41C6F0`) adds its offsets; then the clamp of the view only. Scrolls that move the view at once (arrows, edges, drags, the minimap, F5–F8) also set the target; 'n' (`0x48D4D0`) and F3 only set the target (eased), and 'n' leaves a followed unit followed. (The client re-centred on a tracked unit every frame, never shook, and 'n' jumped.)
- **Screenshot key (exact writer)** (Ctrl+F9 = queue code 0xD6, checked by the main frame `0x499890` in every program state, before anything is drawn: the picture is the frame shown last, pointer included). Folder: the image output folder (registry "Image Output Directory"; default "%s\%s" of the program's folder and the Windows user name, "user images" without one, `0x42F9A0`) + "\screenshots", made when missing; name "%s%s%s%04i.pcx" (folder, a backslash unless the folder ends with one, "SHOT", one more than the largest atoi of the text after the prefix among the folder's SHOT*.pcx, `0x4CB170`). The file (`0x4CAC40`): PCX version 5, encoding 1, 8 bits, window 0,0..w−1,h−1, the resolution words w and h, the first 48 palette bytes in the header, one plane, w bytes a line, palette info 0; each row coded alone: runs of equal bytes, and single bytes of 0xC0 and up, as (0xC0 | n, byte) pieces of at most 63, other single bytes as they are; then 0x0C and the display palette's 768 RGB bytes. frame_harness `--pcx 30`: the original's writer (its file routines collected) and the client's identical on 30 random pictures. MakePoster ("BIGSHOT", a frame of up to 3200 × 2400 around the view drawn in parts, `0x495A30`) is not built.
- **Space-held panels (exact)** (frame_harness `--hud` panels check: 11 scenes, 640 × 480 and 1024 × 768, random slides, flashes, ranks with gaps, kills, losses, counts, speeds, commander modes, watchers and players out, both game types: 0 differing pixels, ranks equal after the drawing).
