Both are part of the battle frame. **Game information** (`0x4689C0`, every game type, clipped to the view): slide 0 (in) .. −31 (out), moved once 15 ms of wall clock have passed (GetTickCount): with Space held and no text field taking the keys, out by (slide + 31)/3 (at least 1), "Panel" when it starts from 0, "Options" on reaching −31; otherwise back by −slide/3 (at least 1), "Panel" when it starts from −31, "Options" on reaching 0. Drawn while not 0: commongui's LIGHTBAR frame 1 (offsets cleared at the loading, `0x4679A0`) with its top-left at (view x0, view bottom + slide), then in the interface's second picture font (hattfont11, GUI+0xC, glyph colours) at y + 10: "Game Time : %02d:%02d:%02d" (game steps: hours step / 108000, minutes and seconds of the rest by 1800 and 30) at x + 25, "Total Units : %d  (Max %d)" (the local record's live units +0x144, the unit limit GS+0x37EE6) at x + 190, "Game Speed Normal" or "Game Speed %+d" (the effective speed GS+0x38A4D less 10), followed by " (%+d)" of the set speed GS+0x38A4B less 10 when they differ, at x + 380. **Kills and losses** (`0x4948E0`, skirmish and network games, whole-screen clip): flashes fade by 2 once the interface clock (1/30 s) has passed the time kept, which becomes the clock + 1; out while F4 (visual bit 0x80) or Space (no text field), by (125 − slide)/4 (at least 1) up to 125, else back by slide/4 (at least 1) down to 0 (sounds as above); drawn while at least 1: the box x0 = screen width − slide .. x0 + 125, y 32 .. players in the game (GS+0x2A3C) × 40 + 46 through shade level 8 (`0x4BF4D0` −24: the row indexed with the pixel as a signed byte), "Kills" at (x0 + 2, 32) and "Losses" right-aligned at x1 − width − 2 (width limit 119, the current picture font hattfont12); then for each rank 0 .. count − 1 the listed player (in use, controller 1–3, record index ≠ 10, live units or never any, not watching) holding it: the local player's row (x0 + 4, y − 1) .. (x1 − 4, y + 38) through light levels 31 then 20 (positive arguments of `0x4BF4D0`: the light table, the same signed index), the colour logo (32xlogos, frame = the side record's colour) stretched over (x0 + 7, y + 1) .. (x0 + 119, y + 37) from texels 1 .. size − 1, the name (record +0x2B) at (x0 + 9, y + 6), the kills at (x0 + 9, y + 21) through light level = the kill flash, the losses right-aligned at x0 + 119 − width − 2 through the loss flash (kills / losses: commander kills / losses +0x104/+0x106 when the commander mode GS+0x37EF6 is 2, else +0xFC/+0xFE, signed 16 bits); y advances 40 per drawn row. A rank nobody listed holds moves every listed player below it up one (the drawing writes the records' ranks). **Ranks** (record +0x148, set to the record index when the record is made, `0x464290`) change in the kill handler (`0x4866D0`'s end, skirmish and network games, an attacker in use with controller 1–3, record index ≠ 10 and rank ≠ 0): the best rank among players with a controller, not watching, with fewer kills (the same choice of counters) and a better rank becomes the attacker's, the ranks from there to its old one move down one, and reaching rank 0 adds "%s has taken the lead with %d kills" (kind 2) to the console; with F4 on, `0x4948B0` sets the attacker's kill flash and the victim's player's loss flash to 30. Client: `client/src/panels.cpp` (the battle's kill hook `kill_counted` → `GameClient::on_kill_counted`), a table blit op in the draw list (`DrawOp::BlitTable`, CPU and GPU) for the shade and light boxes.
- **Unit information screen (exact)** (F1, `0x4942E0`; gui_harness `battle unitinfo:NAME[:BT:V:A:T:BM]` — the original's routine for a unit under the pointer — against ta_play headless frames with F1 over the commander and six build buttons: 7 types, 0 of 61,750 pixels each). The type: the gadget under the pointer in the top layout (GS+0x581) names it (no unit type of that name: nothing), else the unit under the pointer when the viewed player sees it. UNITINFOX.GUI opened with flags 0x1000 (centred right of the side panel; no panel picture: the stock panel), a new screen on every press (the "open" bit 0x800 is only ever cleared). HOTR gets a draw routine (`0x494290`) blitting `unitpics\<unitname>.PCX` (its own indices, on the game palette) at the gadget; NAME takes the definition's name; labels "TEXT" are added (`0x4AB1B0`: kind 5, the given x, y, width = header width − x − 5, height 15, colour word 15, attributes 0x411): "Cost" (130, 32), "Energy", "Metal", "Build Time" (140, 47 / 62 / 77), "Statistics" (130, 92), "Max Velocity", "Acceleration", "Turn Rate" (140, 107 / 122 / 137), and the values of `0x489280` at (240, 32 + 15k): a line break, ftol(energy cost +0x186), ftol(metal cost +0x18A), build time +0x1EA, a line break, then for BMcode 0 "N/A" three times, else "%.1f %s " of 30 × (speed +0x192 × 2^−16 as a float) × 0.4 "m/s", "%.2f %s" of 30 × (acceleration +0x19E × 2^−16) × 0.4 "m/s/s", "%.0f %s" of 30 × turn rate (+0x1BA) × 360/65536 "deg/s" (30: the display's tick rate +0xE8). Labels made at run time draw their glyphs through light level 15 (their colour word; labels from the file keep 0). OK (DONE, also the Enter / Esc default) closes it with "smlbutton". The battle runs on under it and keeps its keys; pointer events outside the box reach the battle.
- **In-battle network screens (2026-10-20, read; built in `ta_compat/src/client_screens.cpp`, smoke-tested with two headless loopback clients, not pixel-compared).** The **tab bar** (TABMENU.GUI, Tab, "SmallButton"): ALLIES and SHARE live in a network game for a player not watching (`0x495010`), CONTROL also needs the host's rights. **Share** (SHARE.GUI, `0x4936F0`, 'h' or the bar; flags 0x800): PLYRLIST lists players one may give to (in use, controller 2 or 3, record index ≠ 10, live units or never any, not watching; none: closed at once); METAL / ENERGY sliders over the stored amounts (knob as high as the slider, travel width − height, amount = ftol(knob / (travel − 1) × stored), shown in METAL# / ENERGY# "%d"); SHARUNIT / MAPINFO switches; OK ("Options") sends the amounts, gives the selected units that are not commanders, carried or flying (`0x4933E0`), shares the explored map (`0x4571C0`); CANCEL "Previous". **Allies** (ALLIES.GUI, `0x4478B0`): rows by `0x447380` (PLAYERk name, LOGOk colour logo stretched from texels 1 .. size − 1, TEAMICONSk the team symbol: 10 none, 2t + 1 alone, 2t several, grayed in a battle; ALLYk for another machine's human: stage = (the other's answer << 1) | the local alliance byte, `0x446FB0`); an ALLY press ("Options") toggles the alliance (packet 0x23, `0x452960`) and says "<name> allied with <name>" / "broke alliance with" as an own chat line; VICTORY shows the side record's allied-victory bit (cd bit 1), grayed in a team of two or more or for a watcher; OK takes it and announces a change. **Control** (CONTROL.GUI, `0x4466B0`, not for a watcher; flags 0x800): rows by `0x447380(1)` (the local player left out; PLAYERk is the fixed slot k, renamed LIVEPLYRk); WATCHING shows bit 0x80 of the local side record's options word +0x9B, GAMEOPEN bit 0x8000 inverted (display only). Handler `0x4464D0`: LIVEPLYRk → `0x446080` (YESORNO.GUI flags 0x100: CHOICE1 "Yes", CHOICE2 "No", TITLE "Reject %s?"; Yes sends the player away with status 1); WATCHING toggles bit 0x80, "Options", re-shows both switches, announces the record (`0x450F90`); OK ("Options", `0x451180`): with watching off every remote player (controller 3) that is watching is sent away with status 9. **Time-out** (TIMEOUT.GUI, `0x453A50`, flags 0x800): opened while a remote player has been silent past the time-out (`0x453C20`, 30 s default), closed ("Previous") when nobody is; NAME, OUTPUT the console's last lines (as many as its rows, oldest first), TALK a chat line, TIMETEXT "will be rejected in %d seconds" (time-out + 120 less the seconds since last heard), at 0 the player is sent away (status 6); REJECT sends it away at once (`0x4538F0`). Network API used (added only): `NetBattle::reject` (status packet 0x1B), `last_heard`, `timeout_seconds`, `set_local_record`; `BattleSession` host / local_options / set_local_options / alliances / explored map / names.
- **+bps display (exact)** (`0x468380`, drawn by the battle frame after the game information panel when GS+0x391C3 is set, clipped to the view; frame_harness `--hud` bps check: 12 scenes at 640 × 480 and 1024 × 768, 0 differing pixels). The rates (`0x416150`): the 1/30 s clock (`0x4B6340`: GetTickCount × the display's tick rate / 1000, 32 bits) — once more than 30 ticks have passed since the last update, rate = (total × 30 − total then × 30) / ticks (unsigned 32 bits) for the bytes sent and received (`0x415F40` totals: the condensed packets' length + 3 and the direct packets on sending, the received messages' lengths; reset when a battle's network state is made, `0x417570`). The drawing uses the text font and colours the frame set before (the viewed player's side font GS+0x3816B + side × 0x232, colour GS+0xDDA = the interface colour 15): "Send - %1.1f K/s" (rate × 0.001) at (129, height − 95) by `0x4C14F0` (nothing when the line does not fit the frame); one font height lower a box x 129 .. 193, 8 rows high, outlined (`0x4BF8C0`) and, for p = rate × 100 / 5600 (unsigned; at most 100) above 0, filled (`0x4BF6F0`) to x = 64 × p / 100 + 129; then "Receive - %1.1f K/s" one row under the box and its box the same way. Client: `GameClient::draw_bps` (panels.cpp), totals from `NetBattle::counters()` bytes_sent / bytes_received.
- **Waiting sign (exact)** (battle frame `0x46A2A8`; frame_harness `--hud` waiting check, 0 differing pixels): with GS+0x38A51 bit 1 — set by the step budget (`0x495230`) while the slowest remote player with live units is 900 steps or more behind, cleared below — the first frame of the pointer sequence "cursorhourglass" (GS+0x148CF, `anims\cursors.gaf`) is drawn at (width − 16, height − 80), after the victory / defeat signs and the game clock, before the GUI layer. (Bit 0 is the pause, bit 2 "effective speed below the set speed".)
- **Film (read 2026-10-20; built, smoke only).** GS+0x38A53 (256 bytes) is the image output folder (registry "Image Output Directory", default "%s\%s" of the program folder and the user name, `0x42F9A0`; the screenshots go to its "\screenshots"); GS+0x38C57 the film rate (registry "Movie Output Rate", 10 when missing). **+Film DIR** (`0x417330`, debug): word 1 copied there, one trailing '\' or '/' dropped, GS+0x38C5F = 1 → saved by `0x430F00`. **+FilmSpeed N** (`0x4173E0`): N > 0 → GS+0x38C57, saved (GS+0x38C63). **Ctrl+F10** (queue 0xD7, debug switch GS+0x37F2F bit 1): GS+0x38C53 (the film number) is taken and cleared; when it was 0 a film starts: the largest atoi of the text after "MOVIE" over the finder's matches of "<folder>\MOVIE*" + 1 becomes the number, GS+0x38B53 = "<folder>\MOVIE%03i" is made (`0x4BCF00`), the battle frame is drawn with param 0 (the console and the text drawn under GS+0x3923B bit 1 left out; the client leaves out the console) and written by the numbered writer `0x4CB170` with prefix "FRAM", and the next film tick GS+0x38C5B = the game tick. The battle loop (`0x496790`), after its frame: when the number is above 0 and the next film tick ≤ the game tick (unsigned), the frame is written the same way, the next tick += 30 / rate (64-bit division: a rate of 0 would fault), and the step budget's clock GS+0x38A37 is set to the clock now (the writing's time is not caught up). Client: `GameClient::toggle_film` / `film_due` / `film_written` (screenshot.cpp), the runners write the frame they drew.
- **Display gamma (read 2026-10-20; built, not compared).** `0x4BA590(float g)` keeps g at display+0x614 and re-sets the whole palette through `0x4BA200`: the display palette (display+0x214, what screenshots and films write) takes the colours as they are; the entries handed to DirectDraw (or the DIB colour table) are each channel × g (FILD byte × float g, above 255 → 255, then the truncating ftol). g is 0.5 − n × (float)−0.041666668 (stored as a float; n = the Gamma setting GS+0x37F08: 12 gives 1.0) at the battle load (`0x491200` → `0x45BCC0`) and in the options screens' slider handlers (`0x45BEA0` and the others), and n × 0.1f from "+gamma n" (`0x417290`, which also keeps n as GS+0x37F08 and saves it). Until one of those runs the display factor is the display's own (1). The setting reads 12 when missing or 10 (`0x42F9A0`). Client: `gamma_palette` / `gamma_from_setting` / `gamma_from_command` (screenshot.cpp), the front end's display palette at presentation.
- **Paste into text fields (read 2026-10-20; built, smoke).** The field editor `0x4AB720` takes key codes 0xBF (Ctrl+V: the window procedure queues Ctrl+letter as 0xAA + letter) and 0xEE (VK_INSERT, `0x4C1D50`): OpenClipboard, GetClipboardData(CF_TEXT); with data of a size above 0 the field's 128 bytes are zeroed and min(GlobalSize, the field's maximum − 1) bytes copied as they are (no character filter), then while the text's width (`0x4A5030`) is above the field's width (+0x17, no 4-pixel margin as for typed characters) and the text is not empty its last character goes. The insertion point (+0x74) is not moved. Client: `paste_line` (menu.cpp) for the menu fields and the chat line; the clipboard from the platform (`use_system_clipboard`: UTF-8 to 8 bits, '?' above 255).
- **Game menu Help and Settings pages (exact)** (`0x4609B0` HELP / MISSION; gui_harness `battle help:P` and `battle missionname:NAME gamesettings:TYPE:MODE:FIXED:FLAGS:DIFF:METAL:ENERGY:UNITS:HOSTOPT` — the original's own set-up routines — against ta_play headless F2 → Help / Settings: help page 1 and two skirmish settings pages 0 differing pixels; pages 2 and 3 differ only in the Page button's stage, which the harness does not press). **Help**: HELP.GUI opened with flags 0x1881 over the game menu (centred right of the side panel, the game menu's box dimmed), handler `0x45FAC0`, picture "dhelp"; the gadget count after loading is kept (0x512EF0) and each page (`0x45F8C0(gui, page, 17)`) first cuts the gadgets back to it, then reads gamedata\help.tdf [Help] "Line<n>" for n = page × 17 .. page × 17 + 16 into a 128-byte buffer (strncpy: zero-padded): a line starting with '|' becomes " " (its text: the padding, empty), else the first '|' is cut to a NUL; two run-time labels (`0x4AB1B0`, attribute 2, then the record's attribute word set to 1) at (40, y) width 78 and (125, y) width 300, translated; y from 50, + 18 per line found. The file's own TEXT label ("Keyboard Commands", active 0) is not drawn. "Page" (Page 1|Page 2|Page 3) shows its stage's page, OK closes, both "Options". **Settings** (MISSION outside campaigns, `0x45F1D0`): GAMEOPTIONS.GUI flags 0x1881, picture "GameSettings", handler `0x45F190` (OK, "Options"); rows of a name label at (18, y) width 110 and a value label at (140, y) width 120 (attribute 2, all set to 1 at the end): Commander Death (90: Game Continues / Game Ends / Deathmatch by GS+0x37EF6), Starting Locations (108: skirmish GS+0x29A0 set-up +0x118, other games the host's side record option bit 0x4000: Random / Fixed; host = the first record with a controller whose side record +0x97 bit 0 is set, `0x456850`), Mapping Mode (126: GS+0x14281 bit 0 Unmapped, else Mapped), Line of Sight (144: bit 1 clear Permanent, else bit 2 True / Circular), network games Cheat Codes (162: host option 0x2000) and Watching (180: 0x80) Allowed / Disallowed, other games Difficulty (162: GS+0x37EEE Easy / Medium / Hard); then Map (the map info's name, +0xA14), Starting Metal, Starting Energy (network: the host record's words +0xA3 / +0xA1 × 100; else the set-up's local player entry +0xC / +0x10, 0x18 bytes each) and Max Units (GS+0x37EE6), 18 apart, numbers by `_itoa` base 10. In campaigns MISSION opens BRIEFING.GUI (MOREBAR and TextRegion switched on, picture "igmbrief", the mission's brief pages `0x476D80` as the briefing screen, its narration unless the sound mode is 6): not built.
### Computer player — smoke-tested: 24 scenarios × 60 steps (1,440 operations: 353 controller steps, 187 direct role ticks, 65 regroups, 120 refreshes, 71 rescores, 53 picks, 53 site searches, 44 nearest-enemy calls, 858 profile command lines incl. 54 texts through the original dispatcher; 1,227 orders (intents 2:694, 3:29, 9:327, 14:180), 23 build menu commands, 82 activations, 1,131 placement checks), 0 differences; plus 1 predicted original crash confirmed in an earlier smoke run (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/ai.cpp` (`sim/ai.h`), wired into `sim::Battle` (players' stage, unit table refresh, creation and death group changes, `start_players`); importer `ta_compat/src/ai_profile.cpp` (`ta/ai_profile.h`: `to_ai_type`, `ai_profile_path`, `run_ai_commands`, `load_ai_profile`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/ai_harness`: inside the suspended exe the real unit and weapon definition tables, three players (two computer players), units of random real types, a 64×64 map with metal and metal-spot features, fog grids; groups, controllers and tables made by the original routines; the original's controller step, role ticks (through the role objects' vtables), group assignment, table refresh and rescoring, pick, score, site search, nearest enemy, group changes, profile commands (direct and as text through the original's command dispatcher with the real category masks registered) run for real. Stubs answer from shared queues and log their arguments: mission resolution `0x43F0E0`, the order routine `0x43ADC0`, the build menu command `0x419B00`, activation `0x48B090`, the placement checks `0x47DB70`/`0x47D2E0` (plus the metal sum they leave at `0x51E688`), the range check `0x49AA80`, the explored test `0x408090`, the line-of-sight test `0x465AC0`; the weapon-target pass at the end of the controller step is silenced. Compared after every operation: stub logs, battle RNG, every table vector, builders, air bases, base centre, metal spots, search radius, site grids, controllers, every role's fields, all group lists and the units' flags and group fields.

**Where it runs.** Players' stage, per player with a controller: for an active controller-2 player the controller step (`0x408C40`) before its weapon-target pass. Table refresh (`0x40AA40`, every 30 steps per table) feeds the AI's counts, builder count, builder air bases and base centre; one time in 30 after a refresh the type scores are rebuilt (`0x409730`). Battle set-up (`0x464700`, per player with a nonzero controller byte, in slot order): the player's ten group lists emptied (`0x480190`); unless an active watched player, a controller (`0x408CB0`) and a table under the player's state index (`0x40B320`: site grids = **8 battle RNG draws**, then the scores). After all players (`0x464990`): the profile (`0x4648E0`). After the first players' stage of the battle (`0x497C4A`): metal spots per table (`0x4649D0`). Engine: `Battle::start_players()` makes controllers/tables for players with a controller byte; `Battle::ai()` gives the context for `ta::load_ai_profile`; `Battle::collect_ai_metal_sites()`.

**Groups.** Each player record has ten unit lists (the human control groups 1–9 share them); `Unit::group` (+0xAC) is the unit's group, −1 none. Changing it (`0x480250`) removes the unit from its old group of its *current* owner (search, then the last entry moves into its place) and appends it to the new group. New units join group 0 (creation); the death handler sets −1.

**Controller** (per computer player): regroup countdown (30), hold tick (the AI capturer hold, `PlayerController::ai_hold_tick`), roles for groups 1–9, each with a timer that runs it when not after the game tick:
- **Group assignment** (countdown, every 30 steps): every unit of the player's range with flag 0x20 (live units) gets standing orders (capture-able: move bits 01, else 10; fire at will); a group-0 unit goes to 1 (structure, unarmed), 5 (structure, armed: flag bit 31), 4 (builder type flag 0x40), 8 (flier 0x800), 7 (min water depth > 0), 3 (armed), else stays.
- **1 factories** (30 steps): each live finished structure: metal makers switch (off unless 2·metal < energy, NaN goes on; then on when net energy > 0 and RandMod(5) ≠ 0 — the same rule as the unused `0x408670`); idle ones with a build list (count ≠ 0, no orders) get the weighted pick through the build menu command (+1; names containing MAKENUKE/MAKEANTI would take the stockpile route).
- **2/6 strike groups** (300 steps; partner reserve 3/7, limit 20000/50000, sizes 3–6): rebalance (an empty group takes the reserve's first unit; while more than one unit, the farthest unit by squared whole-unit distance from the C-divided centre goes to the reserve when that distance ≥ limit × size — then the centre sums lose the coordinates of whatever the vacated list entry now holds, i.e. the unit swapped in (a quirk); reserve units within limit × size join afterwards). Empty → nothing. Size ≤ min, or not attacking and size < max: move to the base centre (armed structures, else unarmed structures, else constructors; group means) with **param0 0xA0**, attacking off; without a base: attacking on, attack order (intent 3) on the nearest enemy of the group centre.
- **3/7 reserves** (150 steps): move to the strike group's centre when both are non-empty.
- **4 constructors** (90 steps). Pass 1, units with a build list: capture-able ones only when the table has fewer than 5 builders and the hold tick is not after the step; head order flag 8 skips; pick a type; site search from the unit's position; for capture-able builders the site must be within ((world W − 32) + (world H − 128))/3 whole units of the base centre (flat, y ignored); build order (intent 14, replace, param0 type, param1 1) at the site. The site's **height is never written** (stack contents, often a heap address: input `AiEnvironment::stale_site_height`), and a capture-able builder's check copies it into the base centre's height used by pass 2. Pass 2, idle units (no order or head flag 0x4000; capture-able only with ≥ 5 builders): capture-able: base centre height := own height; offset to the base centre (y 0), replaced by a random 0x2800000 one beyond 0x2800000; move to centre + offset (= the mirror point), queued patrol to the centre. Others: distance d to the centre (x, y, z); d ≥ 0x1400000 → patrol to the centre; d < 0x100000 → patrol to a random point 0x1400000 away; else to 0x1400000 along the offset (scale = (0x140·2³²)/d, products >> 16).
- **5 armed structures**: nothing (its timer stays 0: runs every step).
- **8 aircraft** (30 + RandMod(900) steps, drawn first): under 5 units and a base centre: 2 + RandMod(2) orders to random points within ±(world/8)/2 of it (first a move, then queued patrols); without a base centre: queued patrol to the nearest enemy of the group centre (none → the original reads position 0x6A: crash, predicted and confirmed); 5 or more: patrol to a random point on a random map edge.
- **9 hunter** (30 + RandMod(150) steps; nobody is ever assigned group 9, but the draws happen): RandMod(10) = 0 → probe := best spot, random step 0x1400000; probe += step; seen by the player (fog: sight counts; else the explored map) → threat sum s within 160 of the probe; RandMod(best) < RandMod(s) → best := probe; attack-capable units (in weapon range when without motion) get an attack order when the intent resolves.

**Orders.** The group routine (`0x480460`): every unit of the player's range with a type and the group, in table order: the intent resolved for the unit, then the general order routine with the resolved kind (Ready included). No command layer and no network: the AI orders its own units directly.

**Table (AI part).** Per type: counts (finished own units, last refresh), threat, three build scores, base-centre weight (40 for build code 0 + 20 with a build list), weight % (100), limit (−1) and their "fixed" flags; builder count; builder air bases (builder + air base + activated); base centre: weighted float sums (sum −= pos·w·(−2⁻¹⁶), stored as floats each unit), divided by the weight sum when nonzero, ftol(·65536); metal spots; search radius; land/water site grids (margin 3: W = 8 + RandMod(10) + 3, H = 8 + RandMod(3) + 3, offsets RandMod(size) − size/2; water margin 6 with RandMod(20)).

**Scores** (`0x409730`, per type from 1): energy rate = energy use (if ≠ 0), else −wind fraction × wind output, else −tidal strength × tidal output. Threat = clamp(c + clamp(arms)) with c = ftol(ftol(base + metal cost × 0.01) + energy cost × 0.002) (base 1, 11 for extractors, +10 makers, +10 negative rate) and arms = 1 (11 attack-capable) + Σ weapons with an id: range/100 + damage/40 + 5. General = ftol((1 or 21) + 30 builder type with < 3 owned + 50 negative rate + 50 extractor + 25 maker + 40 flier + 15 sonar + 5 radar + min(energy make, 30)); ×4 with none owned, ×2 with one; ×3 water types; + threat/2 when the player's live units exceed half the unit limit; 0 for "can't build" (can-do bit 8), type flag bit 24, wind generators when max wind < denominator/2; capped at 100. Energy = clamp(float(energy cost × −0.0025) − rate × 5, 0, 100). Metal = clamp((extractor ? 100 : 0) + float(metal cost × −0.02 + (maker ? 25 : 0)), 0, 100).

**Build choice.** Score (`0x40BB00`, side = the controller's state index): 0 unless stored energy ≥ 50 and metal ≥ 25, 0 for downloadable types when the map info's provider is 1; energy need = ftol((min(ftol(capacity), 1000) − stored) × 0.125) ≥ 0 (+20 net < 1, +100 income < 50 else +10 < 200), metal need likewise with 500 and 0.25 (+20 net < 1, +100 income < 3 else +20 < 5); m = clamp(metal need, 0, 100), e = clamp(energy need − m, 0, 100), g = max(100 − m − e, 0); 0 at the profile limit; (g·general + m·metal + e·energy) × weight / 10000. Pick (`0x40BDB0`): over the build list, each positive score adds to a running total and replaces the choice when RandMod(total) < score; a choice of another side string is dropped.

**Site search** (`0x40BFE0`): radius += 160 while below the map's larger world side; centre = base centre, or (when the base is farther than the radius) the point at the radius towards it (scale = (r·2¹⁶ << 16)/d); extractors: RandMod(255) > surface metal → metal spots (`0x40A260`: spots within 4 × radius cells, score = −distance² in an STL heap, yard checks in heap order until 160 past the first fit, richest summed metal wins), else 30 random tries (`0x40A5D0`: random point within the radius, its corner cell snapped to the table's land or water grid plus random offsets, general placement check and summed metal ≤ surface metal × footprint × 2 — the sum is the last structure check's, stale for mobile types). Success: x/z footprint-centred, radius 0.

**Profile.** `ai\<aiprofile>.txt` from the OTA schema (else `ai\default.txt`), run through the console command dispatcher (lines split at `\n`, words at white space, `#` ends a line, 20 words, a 126-byte word buffer — longer lines never finish; first word looked up case-insensitively; unknown words go to the default handler: unit names matching it as a wildcard are created at the cursor, else `debugdat\<word>.txt` is run). "plan": the gate (a process-wide value, on at start) is on when a word names the difficulty (easy/medium/hard; "any" only counts as the first word). "weight X f": when gated, for every player record with a controller, types of X (a unit name → its bit and fixed; else the category mask) not fixed get weight = clamp(ftol(weight × float(atof f)), 0, 100). "limit X n": the same for active computer players with atoi. Then per active computer player, twice (weight-fixed, then limit-fixed types skipped), every downloadable type's `ai_weight` text runs with the gate forced on (the second pass runs the weight text again, not `ai_limit`).

**Engine notes.** The AI needs the battle's type indices to equal the definition indices (build lists and category bits are definition indices). `AiSystems` carries orders (`resolve_mission` + `enqueue_order`), the build menu (`adjust_build_count`), activation, placement (`can_place_footprint`, `structure_fits` with the new `ConstructionEnvironment::last_place_metal`), sight (`point_in_sight`) and weapon range. Faults instead of crashes: missing tables, groups outside 0–9, the aircraft order at no enemy, zero-size site grids, profile lines past the buffer. Dead code: `0x408920`, `0x408BF0`, `0x408620`, `0x4073B0`, `0x4074A0`, `0x409520`, `0x4095D0`, `0x40AD70`, `0x40BAB0`, `0x40C200`, `0x40C4F0`, `0x40C250` (weight report). Draw-time: `Hud_DrawUnitBuilderProbe` calls the score (no state change); the build cursor preview runs the structure check and so changes the metal sum that a mobile-type random site reads.

**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name + plate comment, smoke-verified): `0x408C40` Ai_ControllerStep, `0x408CB0` Ai_ControllerCreate, `0x408830` Ai_AssignGroups, `0x4086D0` Ai_RoleFactoriesTick, `0x4077E0` Ai_RoleStrikeTick, `0x407560` Ai_StrikeRebalance, `0x4079F0` Ai_RoleReserveTick, `0x408100` Ai_RoleConstructorsTick, `0x407AE0` Ai_RoleAircraftTick, `0x407E90` Ai_RoleHunterTick, `0x407D40` Ai_HunterCreate, `0x407410` Ai_GroupCentre, `0x4071F0` Ai_NearestEnemy, `0x408670` Ai_SwitchByEconomy, `0x40BDB0` Ai_PickBuildType, `0x40BB00` Ai_BuildScore, `0x40BFE0` Ai_FindSite, `0x40A260` Ai_BestMetalSite, `0x40A5D0` Ai_RandomSite, `0x40A150` Ai_MakeSiteGrids, `0x40A7B0` Ai_CollectMetalSites, `0x40B370` Ai_CollectMetalSitesForTable, `0x409160` UnitTable_Init, `0x409470` Ai_ResetProfile, `0x40B320` UnitTable_Create, `0x409730` Ai_Rescore, `0x40B1C0` Ai_ThreatNear, `0x40BA80` Ai_BaseCentre, `0x40C230` Ai_BuilderCount, `0x406C90` AiProfile_Plan, `0x406DB0` AiProfile_Weight, `0x406E40` AiProfile_Limit, `0x406F00` AiProfile_Register, `0x409DC0` AiProfile_ApplyWeight, `0x409E90` AiProfile_ApplyLimit, `0x409F20` AiProfile_BelowLimit, `0x406EE0` AiProfile_BelowLimitSide, `0x480250` Unit_SetGroup, `0x480190` Player_InitGroups, `0x480460` Player_OrderGroup, `0x4B7A30` Cmd_RunText, `0x4B7440` Cmd_Tokenize, `0x4B7900` Cmd_Dispatch, `0x488C50` Category_LookupOrCreate, `0x40D620`/`0x40D670`/`0x40D700`/`0x40D740` ScoreHeap_Make/Adjust/Pop/PushUp. Read, not tested on their own: `0x4648E0` (profile load), `0x464990`, `0x4649D0`, `0x40B530` (builder air bases in a radius, for the aircraft behaviours; implemented as `ai_air_bases_in_radius`), `0x417890` (default command handler), `0x4BC370` (wildcard match), `0x435430` (profile path).

**Open:** the site height word in a real game (what the players' stage leaves at that stack slot: a run-varying heap address after the factories' role, so an input); other console commands in a profile (the importer only knows plan/weight/limit and the default handler); the gate's value carried over from a previous battle in the same process; the viewer's sample run showing commanders stalling on MobileBuild step 1 (wait 0xE0) was the route search, not the AI: its passability build faulted at step 0 (feature blocking flags never imported), so route goals that wanted a route were never served (fixed, see "Fixed in whole-battle runs"); with it fixed both computer players keep building (battle_view `--ai 2`, 18,000 steps: 64 units, no faults). The site height word does not enter the build order's reach test (x and z only); it only reaches pass 2's base-centre distance through a capture-able builder.

**Large run should cover:** `ai_harness <exe> <game> N 60 <first>` in shards (thousands of scenarios): army scenarios reaching strike attacks and reserve hand-overs with large groups; capture-able builders with ≥ 5 builders and hold ticks; the hunter with populated group 9 in both sight modes; extractors on maps with high surface metal (metal-spot search with ties); difficulties and providers; profile texts with every stock `ai\*.txt` file; long runs of the controller step alone (timers and RNG over thousands of steps); and the battle end to end against a captured skirmish with computer players.
### Battle start — the computer-player-free part checked against a capture of the original (battle generator identical at step 1); set-up routines read, not run in a harness
Implementation: `sim::start_battle` (`engine/sim/src/battle_start.cpp`, `sim/battle_start.h`), the importer `ta::load_battle` (`ta_compat/src/battle_loader.cpp`, `ta/battle_loader.h`). `battle_view --battle` builds its battle through them.
**`Game_InitializeBattle` `0x497180`** (runs on the battle loading thread):
1. `SeedBattleRng(QPC low + high)`, then `srand(time())` — the **loading thread's** `rand()`; the simulation thread's `rand()` is whatever the menus left (both inputs: `BattleStart::battle_rng_seed`, `crt_rand_state`). Game tick 0.
2. Options by provider (map info provider: 1 campaign, 2 skirmish, 3 network): skirmish takes commander death, line-of-sight type (map flags bit 2), mapping (bit 0) and line of sight (bit 1) from the skirmish settings and the unit limit from the ini; network games take them from the host side record's option word (bit 13 non-campaign, bits 11-12 commander death, high byte bits 0-2 map flags, max units), after waiting for the side records.
3. `Game_LoadMapTnt` `0x4917D0`: chat/order UI reset, textures, features, weapons, `Map_LoadTnt` (features placed: vents start their steam emitters here, on the loading thread's `rand()`), camera reset, game data, unit definitions, passability, **wind**: denominator 5000, next change 0, one wind step at tick 0 (which changes nothing: the change needs next < tick), tick 0, **meteor schedule** (`0x437CD0`), minimap, **every player record with a controller is set up** (`0x464990` → `0x464700` in slot order: economy, sight counts; unless active with controller 3, its computer-player controller and unit table — the latter draws the battle generator **8 times** (`Ai_InitRandomPlacementOffsets` `0x40A150`), for the human player too), the AI profile, the explosion tables (`0x4207xx`, ~400,000 `rand()` draws on the loading thread), end-game flags cleared. The end countdown is set to −1 by the front end's loading state (`0x498199`).
4. Skirmish: start positions. Without fixed locations the eligible players' slot numbers (active, controller 1/2/3, state ≠ 10, in slot order) are shuffled with the **loading thread's `rand()`**: fewer than three players → one draw decides (r×2/0x8000 ≠ 0) whether to shuffle at all; the shuffle swaps position p (p = 1..n−1) with position (r & 0x7FFF) mod p (never with itself: a Sattolo-style cycle; three or more players always shuffle). Then per eligible player in slot order `Map_SpawnCommanderAtStartPos` `0x496EE0`: side and colour bytes; storage bonuses = max(start energy/metal, 200) and player flag bit 0; the commander (side's commander name → type) at the start position with that id (y 0), fully built, layer 1. Start position ids come from the schema's specials: `StartPosN` → id N−1, a StartPos without digits → a running count − 1 (`0x437320`). Then the stored amounts (`0x465E30`: skirmish = the player's settings as floats, for all ten records).
   Network: per player with controller 1/2, **two battle generator draws** for a random spot (RandMod(width·16 − 160) + 80, the same for z), replaced by the assigned start position if found; no commander for a watcher; bonuses from the **local** side record's start values × 100 (≥ 200). Stored amounts: every record gets the **local** side's start × 100.
5. `Los_RebuildAll(1)`; campaigns: map-schema units.
6. HUD, `Lobby_BroadcastPlayerSlots`, **one players' stage at tick 0** (`0x464F80`: AI controller steps draw the battle generator; the economy's first turn), **the stored amounts again** (`0x465E30`), the computer players' metal-site survey (`0x4649D0`).
**Schema choice** (`Mission_SelectOtaSchema` `0x436860`, skirmish/network): types "Network 1".."Network 4" in that order, schemas "Schema N" from 0 until one is missing; a candidate needs StartPos specials; it replaces the current choice when its count equals the player count (highest filled slot + 1), the player count is 0, or it has more start positions than the best so far and the best is not the player count. Campaigns: Easy/Medium/Hard by difficulty. The schema also gives HumanMetal/Energy, ComputerMetal/Energy (campaign start amounts), SurfaceMetal, the AI profile and the meteor keys.
**Engine:** `start_battle` does steps 1-6 on a battle whose static data the importer loaded (types, map, features, economy, sight tables, meteor configuration); the computer-player parts go through `BattleOutside::ai_player_setup(player)` (in the set-up loop) and `ai_battle_ready()` (the survey). `BattleStart::loading_rand_seed` is the loading thread's state **at the shuffle**.
**Checked** (`game_flow <game> start golden_skirmish_moonquartet_300.jsonl`): with the capture's 38 computer-player draws replayed through those hooks, the engine's own draws (4 commander creations) and order give the battle generator state the original had at step 1 (`04deacf1`): **identical**.
**Open:** the network handshake (start position assignment `0x4568C0`, side records), the exact count of explosion-table draws (needed to derive the shuffle state from `time()`).

### Victory, defeat and the end of the game — read, implemented, not differential-tested
Implementation: `Battle::victory_turn`, `victory_stage_end`, `kill_team`, `deathmatch_respawn` (`engine/sim/src/victory.cpp`, state `sim/victory.h`, `Battle::victory`, `Battle::end_game`).
- Each machine judges **only its local player**, in that player's 30-step turn of the players' stage, before the settlement (`0x464F80`). Campaign: win conditions all true (`0x490230`, default "destroy all units"), else defeat conditions (any true, default "all units killed") — built and smoke-verified, see "Campaign". Skirmish/network: defeated (`0x490360`) when the local player has no live units (and, when the copy-protection check failed, at a forced step 9000 + r×9000/0x8000 — one `rand()` draw); only when not defeated: skirmish win when every other player that is not the local player's ally has no live units (inactive slots count as none); network win (`0x490080`, never in deathmatch) when every other playing non-watching player has made units and either has none left or is a mutual "allied victory" ally that is allied with every remaining player.
- A decision runs the **end countdown** (GS+0x39239, signed, −1 at the start): first 4, then −1 per turn; when it goes negative: win → end flags |= 4 | 0x10 | 0x20; defeat → deathmatch (commander mode 2) respawn, or a network watch mode (side option 0x40, mapping and line of sight off for this machine), or end flags |= 4, &= ~0x10, |= 0x40 (unless the lobby status is set). While the countdown runs (≥ 0) or the end flag (4) is set **no player's economy is settled** (sim/economy.h `settlement_allowed`). After the players' loop a network game (not deathmatch) without any human combat player left counts down too (`0x464F80` tail).
- **Deathmatch respawn:** up to 9999 tries: x = RandMod(W − 2·(W/10)) + W/10 (W = map width in world units), z alike (two battle generator draws); fits when the commander's footprint can be placed (`0x47DB70`, layer 1) at all nine probe cells (x, z ± the map's size in **cells used as world units**, a quirk), no feature at the spot, and on lava maps the ground is above the sea; then the commander, storage bonuses = side start × 100, and the start amounts credited as the commander's production (credit rule).
- **Commander death** (`Unit_BroadcastKilledPacket` `0x4864B0`, commander mode ≠ 0, playing owner): `Gui_PopUntilNamedLayout` (UI), then `Player_KillAllUnitsOnTeam` `0x486F10`: every live, not dying unit of the player: playing owner → 30000 damage of kind 3 from itself (`0x489BB0`), else explode as self-destruct (`0x49B000`), dying flag, killed packet kind 3.
- **The goals switch** (2026-10-20): the victory object at GS+0x391ED has +0x88 (set at the start; cleared by `0x4904B0`, which the "+kill" console command calls and a mission without placed units leaves off). Both the victory check `0x490230` and the defeat check `0x490360` return false at once while it is 0, **in every kind of game** (the defeat check's copy-protection draw included). The engine had the gate for missions only; `Battle::victory_met` and the defeat lambda of `victory_turn` now test `mission_goals.active` first (sim_tests `test_victory_goals_switch`).
**Engine API:** `Battle::victory` (`outcome` per slot, `over`, `decided_tick`, per-side network flags), `BattleSettings::provider` and `cd_mismatch`; `BattleOutside::player_victory_turn` still follows for the user interface.

### Commands (user interface and computer player routes) — the user interface's order routine differential-tested
Implementation: `sim/commands.h` (`engine/sim/src/commands.cpp`, `commands_core.cpp`).
- **There are no command packets.** The user interface orders the local player's **selected** units directly (unit flags bit 4); the computer player orders single units directly (`MissionOrder_EnqueueOnUnit` `0x43ADC0`, `Battle::order`); both only ever order their own machine's units (see "Network and sync").
- **`Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected` `0x48CF30`** (left/right click, order buttons, hotkeys, standing-order buttons): queued = the click's key flags bit 2 (shift). The unit under the cursor (GS+0x2CBA) is the target, and left out, when the intent takes one (every intent but unload 5, stop 10 and build 14; `0x43E470`) or, without an intent, when the mission's table flags have bit 9. Centre = the mean of the other selected units' whole-unit x and z (int division, ×65536 via `ftol`). Per selected unit in table order: the intent's mission (`Unit_ResolveMissionKind` `0x43F0E0`, with the **cursor's** world position GS+0x2CAA, not the given position); mission 0 → nothing; StandingFireOrder needs can-do bit 1, StandingMoveOrder bit 0; a point order (table flags bit 1) for a unit whose squared distance to the centre ((dx²)>>32 + (dz²)>>32, 64-bit) is at most 3000 × the count keeps its offset: position = given + unit − centre (y as given). Then `MissionOrder_IssueOrCancelMatch` `0x43AFC0`: queued and an order in the **primary** list of the same kind, the same target (when one is given) and within 0x100000 on x and z (when a position is given) → that order is unlinked from the list its secondary flag names (nothing if not found there), marked not-current unless it was the primary head, destroyed and freed — a cancel; otherwise the order is given (`0x43ADC0`).
- `Player_IssueOrderToMatchingUnits` `0x480460`: every unit (non-empty) of a player in group g: resolved mission, `0x43ADC0` directly (no cancel).
- Selection (`0x48C7F0` and family): own unit, selectable (0x20), complete, no transfer hold-off, not carried unless the carrier allows (0x40000000); a plain click clears bits 0x10|0x40|0x80 of **every** unit first; shift toggles.
- Engine `Command` (plain data, encodable) kinds: Select/Deselect/ClearSelection, IssueIntent/IssueMission (the routine above), OrderUnits (computer route), BuildCount (`0x43B0B0`), MobileBuild, AssignGroup/SelectGroup, TransferEnergy/Metal (`0x464B30`/`0x464C60`). `command::apply` between steps.
- **Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/cmd_harness` (the original routine with the resolution, table lookups and order call stubbed from shared tables): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 1,200 calls / 7,879 resolutions / 9,957 orders identical.** `issue_or_cancel` itself is read, not run in a harness.

### Console commands (typed '+' lines) — smoke-tested: console_harness 14 scenarios (1,320 random lines: 650 commands ran, 213 through the default command; 84 unit rows with 187 units and 39 include files; 56 meteor starts; 364 switch handlers; 168 resource commands), 0 differences; dithered fog compared with the original (frame_harness `--dither --no-mapping`: 11 of 14 scenes identical, the other 3 differ exactly as without `--dither`)
Implementation: engine `sim/console.h` (`engine/sim/src/console.cpp`: words, registry, the default command's unit row `place_unit_row`, `start_meteor_shower`); the table and its effects in `ta_compat/src/console_commands.cpp` + `console_table.cpp` (`ta/console_commands.h`: `install_console_commands`, `console_table`); the chat line in `client/src/game_client.cpp` (`GameClient::enter_chat_line`, `on_command`, `cursor_world`, the '\' key); dithered fog in `render/src/battle_view.cpp` (`BattleScene::dithered_fog`); the effects switch `EffectLists::spawning_off`. Checked by `tools/orig_harness/console_harness` (below) and `frame_harness --dither`.

**The talk line** (`0x493BF0`, Enter on TALK.GUI's text field): spaces at the front are dropped. A line that then starts with '+' is a command: the text after the '+' runs with the allowed permission bits 1, plus 2 when cheats are allowed (`0x5091CC`), or 7 (and 2 as before) while the debug switch (GS+0x37F2F bit 1) is on, through `0x417B50` (keeps up to 79 characters of it at `0x511BD0` for the repeat key, splits it into words, dispatches). When the bits of the command that ran include 2 (a cheat), the line's recipients become everyone. Then, whatever it was, a non-empty line is sent and shown as chat ("<name> text", `0x463E50`), the '+' included — so other players see every typed command, and cheats always reach all of them. With the debug switch on, the '\' key (code 0x5C in `0x495E90`) runs the kept line again with every bit allowed.

**Cheat permission** (battle start `0x497180`): campaign off, skirmish on, network games from the host's side record option word bit 13 (+0x9B 0x2000: the battle room's CHEATING switch, `0x445ED0`/`0x447B10`; the room's summary prints it after "Cheat Codes:"). (Correction to "Network and sync": bit 7 of that word is the room's WATCHING switch, not cheats; bit 13 is the cheat switch, not "non-campaign".)

**Debug switch**: "+Now Film Chris Include Reload Assert" — exactly six words, the five after "Now" compared case-sensitively — sets GS+0x37F2F bit 1; any other "+Now" line clears it.

**Words** (`0x4B7440`, as in "Computer player"): C-locale white space separates, '#' ends the line, 20 words kept, 126 characters of words (each with its terminator in a 128-byte buffer); a longer word never finishes (the original writes terminators past the buffer forever). Word access: missing words give the caller's default; numbers are the runtime's atol (`0x4B73E0`), a few take atof (`0x4B7410`). Command text from a file (`0x4B7A30`) replaces words "%N" by word N of the calling line when it has one.

**Registry** (vector at `0x51FC99`, 12-byte entries {name, handler, bits}, kept sorted by the runtime's case-insensitive compare): registering a name again replaces handler and bits (`0x4B7620` one, `0x4B7760` a null-terminated list). Dispatch (`0x4B7900`): no words → nothing (0); the first entry not below the first word, if it equals it (case-insensitively) and its bits meet the allowed bits, runs (handlers are stdcall with the words); otherwise the default handler (`0x51FC90`, bits `0x51FC94`) runs when its bits meet them. Returns the bits of what ran (0: nothing). At battle load (`0x419560` from `Loading_LoadBattleData`): three lists — options `0x501D38` (bits 1), cheats `0x501F48` (bits 2), debug `0x501FD0` (bits 4) — and the default handler `0x417890` with bits 4. The profile loader adds plan / weight / limit with bits 8 (`0x406F00`), so in a game they only run through the repeat key.

**The table** (handler; what it changes; engine side):
- Options (bits 1, always): NoShake `0x416E60` toggles GS+0x37F2F 0x10 (`settings.shake_disabled`); Contour `0x416DB0` two numbers (ftol of atof words 1, 2; defaults 0, 0.75) for the contour display (not drawn); ScrollSpeed `0x416CF0` (byte, word 1) and IFace `0x416D20` (interface type, word 1), both saved; Give `0x416BD0` "<player> <amount> metal|energy": when word 1 names a player in the game, a transfer of float(atol(word 2)) from the local player (GS+0x2A42) to it (`0x464C60` / `0x464B30`, sending; word 3 compared case-insensitively, both may match neither); CDPlay/CDStop/MusicMode/Sound3D (music and the 3D sound switch); Shading 0x20, AntiAlias 0x02, Shadow 0x04, TShadow 0x08 (VehicleShadows), FShadow 0x10, **Dither 0x40** of GS+0x37F06, then the drawing cache is rebuilt (`0x437C80`, not for Dither/TShadow/FShadow) and the settings saved (`0x430F00`, not for TShadow/FShadow); SwitchAlt `0x4165C0` toggles 0x100 (with a word: sets it to word 1 & 1, not saved); LOSType `0x416690` toggles map flag 4 and rebuilds sight keeping the explored map (`Los_RebuildAll(0)`; `settings.true_sight`); Light (light direction, three words), RCache (cache rebuild only); Selectable `0x416460` sets flag 0x20 on every unit with the alive flag; Logo `0x4168D0` "<colour> <player>": colour below the logo count and a player in the game → the player's side record logo (team colour), else console "Invalid logo setting"; ScreenChat toggles GS+0x37F02; Gamma (word 1 × 0.1, saved); Clock toggles 0x40 of GS+0x37F2F; NetStats resets the network counters; Sing toggles `0x51E698` (unit notices then play "honk" in every 8th second of the game clock, else "sing"); NoMetal `0x417150` / NoEnergy `0x4171F0`: the player (the local one when the line has one word, else byte(word 1)) if in the game gets stored metal / energy = float(atol(word 2)) (default 0); BigBrother toggles GS+0x14373 bit 1 (the view follows; off resets the tracking); Now (above); Drop `0x4177A0` sets GS+0x37F2F bit 0 to (word 1 == 0) (no time-out drops, see "Network and sync"); **ShootAll** `0x418CA0` toggles 0x400 of GS+0x37F2F (the targeting's "target everything", `settings.target_everything`); ShareMetal 2 / ShareEnergy 4 / ShareMapping 0x20 / ShareRadar 0x40 (network games only, GS+0x2A44 bit 0): toggle the local player's side record flag word +0x97, console "Toggled ShareMetal to: ON|OFF" (the new state; likewise ShareEnergy, ShareMapping, ShareRadar), announce the side record (`0x450F90`); ShareAll does the four in that order; ShowRanges toggles GS+0x391BF; SetShareMetal / SetShareEnergy (network only): threshold +0xE4 / +0xE8 = float(atol(word 1)) unless that is above the metal / energy storage (then the storage), console "OK.  Will share metal if above %d" with atol(word 1); Compression (network only) toggles outgoing compression, "Ok.  Outgoing packet compression turned ON|OFF"; BPS toggles a display; **SFX** `0x419550` toggles `0x51E608`, the effects switch: every effect spawner and the nano stream spawns make nothing while it is on (checked first; burning debris draws its four numbers before the check) — simulation state (`EffectLists::spawning_off`).
- Cheats (bits 2): Radar toggles GS+0x37F2F 0x200 (the minimap shows every unit; client `radar_everything`); **ATM** `0x4170C0`: the viewed player (GS+0x2A43) stored energy and metal each = float(stored − (−1000.0)) in x87 double; View "<player>": a player in the game becomes the viewed player (GS+0x2A43; `settings.local_view`, client `view`); **LOS** `0x416D50` toggles map flag 2, saves, rebuilds sight keeping the explored map (`settings.fog_of_war`); Mapping `0x416D80` toggles map flag 1, saves, rebuilds clearing it (`settings.explore`); DoubleShot 0x80 / HalfShot 0x100 of GS+0x37F2F (weapon damage doubled / halved, `settings.damage_options`); NowISee `0x417540` clears map flags 1 and 2 and rebuilds clearing; Meteor `0x417760`: without a word a shower starts now (`0x438070`: active, end = now + duration, next schedule = end + gap, next meteor now, origin and aim from four rand() draws exactly as the scheduled start — but the map's switch is not consulted, so meteors fall on maps without showers), with a word the map's shower switch = (word 1 ≠ 0); MakePoster (a large screenshot).
- Debug (bits 4): AI "<player>" (default the local one): a player in the game whose record is local (controller 1) becomes a computer player (2), any other becomes local (1) (`0x463C60` also writes the side record kind unless 3); Control "<player>": local and viewed player both become it; Kill: one word — every unit with a type goes through the kill routine with kind 8 (`0x4864B0`; its wreck word is a stack input), with a player — that player's team kill (`0x486F10`); IWin / ILose: the team kill of team (local side ≠ 1) / team (local side), then the end flags (win 0x10|0x20|4, lose clears 0x10 and sets 0x40|4); Film / FilmSpeed (frame capture), Assert / DPrint / Mem / ZBuffer (nothing), MemDump (creates memdump.txt), DebugBreak (needs a second debug switch), Assign (an order by mission name to the selection), BurnAll / BurnOne (features burn), Edge (scroll limits), Include (runs debugdat\include.txt — the file named after its own first word), Move (moves the pointer's map point by 16 × words 1, 2), PrintWeights, Profile, Reload / ReloadAIProfiles, Save "<name>" (savegame\<name>.sav), SeaLevel (the sea level byte only), Search (route search limits), SelBoxes (selection outlines, 0x04), Senderror (0..100 %), TreeDeath (0x08: weapons damage features), Feature "<name>" (places a feature at the pointer).
- **The default command** (`0x417890`, bits 4: unknown first words once the debug switch is on — "+<unit name>"): for every unit definition 1..count−1 in table order whose name matches the first word as a wildcard ("ARM*" makes every ARM unit), a fully built unit (layer 1, any id) for player atol(word 2) (default 0 — the first player record, not the local one) through `Unit_CreateFromDefId` with the engine's creation (RNG draws, ids, packet 0x09 in network games). Placement: a row from the pointer's map point (GS+0x2CAA, a copy): every unit after the first moves the point right by its definition's left model extent (x − min x), structures snap to their footprint (`0x47DDC0`), and after each unit (made or refused) the point moves right by 32 units plus its right extent; when x reaches the scroll width (map width × 16 − 32 units, GS+0x1422B) the row restarts at x = 160 units, 160 units further down. No definition matches → `debugdat\<word>.txt` runs as command text with every bit (its "%N" words from the line), the pointer's point saved around it.
- **The pointer's map point** (`0x498DA0` on pointer moves): over the minimap, (pointer − minimap origin) × (map width × 16 − 32) / minimap width (likewise height with − 128); elsewhere the pointer clamped to the battle view, plus the scroll; then the editor-position-to-terrain conversion (`0x484B50`, `sim::screen_to_terrain`).

**Dithered fog** (`Map_DrawLosFogTiles` `0x4848E0` with GS+0x37F06 bit 0x40, registry "DitheredFog", default off): unexplored cells as usual; a wholly unseen cell (`0x4BFF20`) instead of the grey remap sets to 0 every screen pixel of the cell (clipped) whose x + y + p is even, p = (scroll x + scroll y) & 1 — the start up to a multiple of 4 pixel by pixel, the middle as 32-bit masks up to the clipped end rounded up to 4 (so a clipped end x of 4k+1 or 4k+2 also clears one or two pixels past the cell), the rest pixel by pixel; a partly unseen cell (`0x4B88D0`) sets to 0 the screen pixels under the grey edge frame's non-key pixels with x + y + p even. The black edge frames follow as usual. Client: `ClientOptions::dithered_fog`, `BattleScene::dithered_fog`; CPU = GPU (Direct3D 12) on the smoke screenshots.

**Network.** Every effect is local to the machine that typed it; nothing is sent besides the chat line itself, what the engine's own routines send (unit creation 0x09, transfers 0x16, side records), and nothing refuses a cheat beyond the permission bits. Packet 0x25 is not a cheat warning: it is the "has modified his executable" notice (shown 12 times; the room's checksum check), see "Network and sync".

**console_harness** (`console_harness <TotalA.exe> [scenarios] [lines] [first seed]`, 32-bit): inside the suspended exe the original registers its tables (`0x419560`, `0x406F00`; bump heap). (1) Every registered name and its bits against `ta::console_table()`. (2) Every handler replaced by a stub copying its words; random lines (names in random case, unit-like patterns, numbers, junk, every white-space kind, '#', up to 24 words) with random allowed bits through `0x417B50` against `split` + `Registry::dispatch`: bits returned, the command (or default) that ran, its words. (3) The default command on random definitions (names, extents), scroll widths, pointer points and patterns, with snap / creation / file-open stubs, against `place_unit_row` (every snap and creation argument, the include file name). (4) `0x438070` against `start_meteor_shower` (all shower fields and the rand() state). (5) Switch handlers run for real (the word and bit each flips, the sight rebuild argument; NowISee; SFX) and ATM / NoMetal / NoEnergy / Give on random player records (the floats bit for bit, the transfer calls). **frame_harness `--dither`** (with `--no-mapping` so views hold explored, unseen cells): the original's frame with the dither bit against the client's. Smoke: 14 scenes (seeds 7 and 21, 640×480): 11 identical (dithered views, some with map edges); 3 differ in the same scenes and the same way as without `--dither` (the harness's own sight state for scenes with units in view: the original draws those views as unseen).

**Smoke runs:** console_harness 2 × 60 and 12 × 100 lines: 0 differences. Headless client (`ta_play --headless --script`, the new script event `type TEXT`): +dither, +shootall, +atm, +armpw refused before the debug switch, +now Film Chris Include Reload Assert, +armpw and +CORAK 1 created at the pointer, +doubleshot, +noshake, +los, +meteor, +radar, +give 1 300 metal, a plain chat line — the final options and units as expected; dithered fog screenshot CPU = GPU.

**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name + plate comment; smoke-verified by console_harness): `0x417B50` `Cmd_RunTypedLine`, `0x419560` `Cmd_RegisterGameCommands`, `0x417890` `Cmd_DefaultCreateUnitsByName`, `0x438070` `Meteor_StartShowerNow`, handlers `Cmd_ShootAll`, `Cmd_LOS`, `Cmd_Mapping`, `Cmd_NowISee`, `Cmd_LOSType`, `Cmd_NoShake`, `Cmd_DoubleShot`, `Cmd_HalfShot`, `Cmd_TreeDeath`, `Cmd_Radar`, `Cmd_Clock`, `Cmd_SelBoxes`, `Cmd_Dither`, `Cmd_Shading`, `Cmd_AntiAlias`, `Cmd_Shadow`, `Cmd_TShadow`, `Cmd_FShadow`, `Cmd_SFX`, `Cmd_ATM`, `Cmd_NoMetal`, `Cmd_NoEnergy`, `Cmd_Give`. Read and implemented, not named: the talk line `0x493BF0`, the dither blits `0x4BFF20` / `0x4B88D0` (compared through frame_harness), the other handlers (addresses in the three lists).

**Debug commands with a simulation effect (built 2026-10-20, smoke: ta_play headless lines, see docs/parity/SIM.md 7.1):** BurnAll (`0x416390` → `0x424840`: every cell whose feature is below 0xFFFB or a part 0xFFFE goes through `Feature_RemoveFromCell` with force, in cell order); BurnOne (`0x4163A0`: when the pointer's feature, GS+0x2CBC from `0x421E60` at the last pointer move — a part resolved to its anchor —, is below 0xFFFB, the pointer's cell GS+0x2C8E/0x2C90 the same way); Feature (`0x4163D0`: the first type of the battle's feature table named word 1, `0x422DD0` stricmp, placed on the pointer's cell by `Feature_PlaceOnGrid` with owner 10; nothing is loaded); Assign (`0x416310`: `MissionOrder_FindByName`(word 1), if nonzero `0x48CF30`(pointer record GS+0x2C76, intent 0, that kind, no position, atol(word 1), atol(word 2)) — the first parameter is atol of the mission's own name); Search (`0x416780`: route search +0x48 = atol(word 1) if nonzero; exactly three words: +0x54 = ftol(atof(word 2))); Reload (`0x417490`: word count > 1 and a known type: `0x486E80` sends the kill packet kind 8 for every table entry of the type, then `0x42D1F0` when the definition word has bit 23: `units\<name>.FBI` read again over the record by `0x42BF40` and the script reloaded — the old script is freed while the dying units still use it; the engine keeps it; a corpse the battle did not load reaches the feature loader with no parsers: "Record missing" and a crash, a feature fault in the engine); ReloadAIProfiles (`0x4174D0` → `0x40A100`: `Ai_ResetProfile` of the table at `0x5119C0`[slot] for every active controller-2 record, then `0x4648E0`, the battle's whole profile load). **Kill** (`0x4164B0`) ends, in both forms, with `0x4904B0` on GS+0x391ED: the map's goal object's +0x88 = 0, so a mission never ends by its goals afterwards (engine `MissionGoals::active`). **Not built / open:** DebugBreak (`0x417A60`) needs, besides the debug switch, the debug-mode key's bit (GS+0x3923B bit 1, toggled by key code 0xEC in `0x495E90`, which the client does not have): with both it ends the program on purpose (word 1 = 1 or 2 runs out of memory, 3 divides by zero, else `DebugBreak()`), so here it does nothing; the room's default for the cheat switch (the engine's room sets bit 13 by default, so its network games allow cheats); the pointer point is taken when the line runs (the original keeps the last pointer move's); the chat recipients of a non-cheat line (the client sends every line to everyone); a profile file's unit names would create units in the original (the importer only notes them); the kill routine's stack word for "+kill" is an input (0 here).

**Console commands, the rest (read and built 2026-10-20; all 83 names of the three lists `0x501D38` / `0x501F48` / `0x501FD0` are registered; smoke: ta_play headless lines and screenshots, not compared with the original):**
- **Settings writer** `0x430F00` (the whole registry block: Interface Type, scrollspeed, screenchat, damagebars, Sound Mode, Anti-Alias, Shadows, FeatureShadows, VehicleShadows, Shading, DitheredFog, Gamma, SwitchAlt, clock, Image Output Directory, Movie Output Rate, textlines, textscroll, unitchat, unitchattext, gamespeed and the skirmish keys) runs after ScrollSpeed, IFace, Sound3D, Shading, AntiAlias, Shadow, Dither, SwitchAlt (the toggle form only), ScreenChat, Gamma, Clock, LOS, Mapping, Film, FilmSpeed; not after NoShake, Contour, TShadow, FShadow, LOSType, Light, Logo, Sing, DoubleShot, Radar, SelBoxes, TreeDeath, ShootAll, MusicMode (they are written by the next save). Client: `GameClient::save_settings`.
- **Light** `0x4166C0`: `0x4597F0`(word 1, 2, 3) sets the lit path's light vector (`0x5065F8` .. `0x506600`, default (−0.8, 1.0, 0.25)) to float(n) × 0.01 each, read by the vertex light routine `0x459C70` (dot product × 5, as before); then the drawing caches (`0x437C80`). RCache only rebuilds the caches.
- **Contour** `0x416DB0` sets `0x511DD0` (interval) and `0x511DD4` (offset). `0x418310`, called right after the terrain tiles, draws when the interval is not 0 (or a debug view of GS+0x14280 is on): from cell (scroll / 16 toward zero) across the view's width in cells + 1 (at most map width − 1), row after row from scroll y / 16 while some cell of the row has its first corner above the screen's bottom (GS+0x37E23), up to map height − 1. Corners of cell (x, z): heights of (x, z), (x+1, z), (x+1, z+1), (x, z+1) at (16(x + 8) − scroll x, 16(z + 2) − h/2 − scroll y). `0x4181D0`: centre = ((sum of x) + 2) / 4 and ((sum of y) + 2) / 4 toward zero, centre height = sum of the four heights × 0x40; four triangles (corner k, corner k+1, centre) with heights × 256. `0x417F60`: the three points sorted by height a ≤ b ≤ c; level L = (c / I) × I + O, less I while above c; while L > b a line from a–c to b–c at L, then while L > a from a–c to a–b, each point (p·(d − t) + t·q) / d in integers, L −= I, colour = byte table `0x501D18`[((L >> 8) − sea level + 256) >> 4] (zeros, then 111..106, 88..80, then 255). A negative interval never ends the loops (the original hangs; the client draws nothing).
- **Edge** `0x416730`: GS+0x1422B = map width × 16 − word 1 (default 32), GS+0x1422F = map height × 16 − word 2 (default 128). Those are the scroll extent the view, the minimap, the pointer's minimap point, the default command's row wrap, MakePoster, the aircraft missions (`Mission_AirToAir` etc.: the map size) and the computer player's capturing constructors (reach = (w + h) / 3) read: the command changes the simulation of this machine's units. Engine: `Battle::set_scroll_edges`, `scroll_width/height`.
- **MakePoster** `0x417600`: size = words 1, 2 (defaults 3200, 2400) or the scroll extent for "all"; at least the view's size in whole cells (GS+0x1423B/F × 16), at most the extent; corner = view scroll − size / 2 + cells × 8, below 1 → 0, at most extent − size. `0x495A30`: the map's LOS and mapping flags (GS+0x14281 bits 0, 1) cleared and the sight rebuilt clearing the explored map (`Los_RebuildAll(1)`), the paused bit (GS+0x38A51 bit 0), the clock (GS+0x37F2F 0x40) and GS+0x37F27 cleared; square tiles of view height in cells × 16 − 1 pixels, each drawn by a whole `Render_DrawBattleFrame(1, 0)` at that scroll (the frame's side effects with it) and its view part copied; written as `<image folder>\screenshots\BIGSHOTnnnn.pcx` (`0x4CB170` naming); then everything back, the sight rebuilt clearing again (the explored map is lost), the view back, a frame drawn, and the step clock restarted (GS+0x38A37). Not compared: the exact tile loop (the decompilation of its bounds is unclear).
- **Save** `0x417430`: with a name, `savegame\<name>.sav` (folder made) through `0x4326B0` with the description "Generic Game Description" and Game ID 666 (0x29A) instead of the time.
- **MemDump** `0x416240`: memdump.txt created empty (CREATE_ALWAYS) in the working folder; nothing written.
- **PrintWeights** `0x418BB0`: exactly three words, word 1 a player in the game: word 2 opened "w+b", `0x40C250` writes "gametime: '%02d:%02d:%02d'" (tick / 108000, then / 1800 and / 30 of the rest), "player:   '%s' num: %d" (record name, word 1), "controller: HUMAN|AI|INVALID", "terrain:    'Maps\<map>.TNT'" and "profile:    'ai\<profile>.txt'" (map info path fields 1 and 7), "difficulty: 'EASY|MEDIUM|HARD'" (GS+0x37EEE), a rule, the column header, then for every definition 1 .. count − 1 the limit ("%4d", "n/a " below 0) and " - %3d : %3d : %3d = %3d - '%s\t\t:%s'" of the table's three build scores, its weight, unit name (+0x20) and name (+0); lines end in "\r\n".
- **NetStats** `0x417570`: the network counters zeroed, among them the sent / received totals the +bps rates use (`0x511BC8`, `0x511C50`) but not the totals of the last rate update, so the next update wraps (unsigned) once.
- **BigBrother** `0x4174E0`: GS+0x14373 bit 1 toggled; on, the countdown GS+0x14371 = 1; off, `0x41C390` (the followed unit and GS+0x1434B, +0x142F7 cleared). At the end of the unit stage (`0x48B02E`), with the bit and Shift not held, the countdown loses 1; below 1 it is 90 again, `0x48D790` selects the next selectable unit of the viewed player (at the first selected one every unit loses flags 0x10, 0x40, 0x80 and the next selectable after it, else the first, is selected; with none selected the first) and `0x41C2E0(0)` follows the next selected own unit.
- **Profile** `0x417520` toggles GS+0x38DD5. Every pass (`0x496790`) the nine accumulators (GS+0x38DB1, wall-clock ms by `0x46A400`) become the shown values (GS+0x38D8D) and their sum (at least 1) the total. Categories by stage: network in 0, unit stage 1, projectiles 7, effects 8, players 2, wind/meteors/camera 8, effect sweep 6, minimap 8, flush 0; the frame's terrain / units / fog 3, 4, 5, the rest 8. Drawn at the frame's end (`0x46B900` per row): a line in colour 255 from (width − 290, 38) to (639, 9h + 41) (meant as a box; drawn nine times), the name at (width − 85, nh + 40) in the text colour set last, a bar from width − 90 − 2 × percent to width − 90, h tall, colour n + 1.
- **ShowRanges** `0x4194C0` toggles GS+0x391BF, read by the queued-order view: in `0x4390A0` instead of the cloak / kamikaze rings every range of the type in colour [14] with a label (mincloak, sight, radar, sonar, radarjam, sonarjam, build distance, maneuver, kamikazedistance; the label index counts the rings drawn before), then the weapon ranges ("weapon1 range" .. "weapon3 range", indexes 0..2; slots 1 and 3 by slot 1's flag 2, slot 2 by its own) in [4] on odd steps, else [12]; in `0x439740` at an attack's target (markers 1 and 2), per weapon slot with flag 2 its area of effect ("weapon %d - area of effect", slot from 0) and coverage ("weapon %d - coverage"), then the type's attack run length ("attack length"). Labels (`0x438EA0` → `0x4C14F0`): at the end of segment 3 × index (the last segment's end when there is none), 4 rows down, whole text inside the clip, in the text colour set last.
- **Compression** `0x4194D0`: network games only; GS+0x4ED = (it was 0) (nonzero: outgoing frames not compressed), console "Ok.  Outgoing packet compression turned OFF" while set, else "ON". **Drop** sets GS+0x37F2F bit 0 = (word 1 = 0): the time-out check off. **ShareMetal / ShareEnergy / ShareMapping / ShareRadar** toggle the local side record's flag word (+0x97 bits 2, 4, 0x20, 0x40) and announce it (`0x450F90`).

### Network and sync — the original's model built (wire format differential-tested, smoke; games loop-tested, smoke); the engine's lockstep model kept as a test tool
- **The original is not lockstep.** Each machine simulates **its own** players' units (controller 1 local human, 2 computer); other machines' units (controller 3) only run their scripts and take states from the network. Packets (`Net_ProcessIncomingPackets` `0x453D40`, step stage 2; sizes from `0x451FD0`): 9 unit pose/create (0x17), 0x0A transport attach (7), 0x0B damage (9), 0x0C killed (0xB), 0x0D weapon fire (0x24), 0x0E projectile detonate (0xE), 0x0F feature damage (6), 0x10 unit script call (0x16), 0x11 activate flags (4), 0x12 builder link (5), 0x13 sound (0x12), 0x14 ownership transfer (0x18), 0x16 resource/explored-map share (0x11), 0x19 pause/speed (3), 0x28 player view state (0x3A), 0x2C unit state bits (`Net_DeserializePlayerUnitStates`, 3+), plus lobby/chat/status types 2-8, 0x15, 0x17, 0x18, 0x1A-0x27. Outgoing packets are batched per frame (`NetSendFrameState`, flushed in stage 10 when `0x506DBC` is set). The battle generator is seeded per machine from the clock, consistent with this model.
- The `Sync_*` family (`0x46CBE0`-`0x470790`) exchanges **unit definition (FBI) checksums** between players (data integrity), not simulation state.
- **Engine model** (`sim/lockstep.h`): every machine runs the whole simulation; only commands travel. A turn message = (player, tick, that player's commands for the step `tick`, its checksum of an earlier step); commands given at step t are due before step t + delay; a step runs when every player's message for it has arrived; commands apply in player order. `state_checksum` (FNV-1a over units, orders, motion, projectiles, players' resources, the `rand()` state and the tick; selection excluded) is exchanged and compared (desync = first differing tick). `CommandLog` records commands with their steps and checksums; `Replayer` replays one and checks the checksums. **All simulation settings must be the same on every machine, including `local_player` (the judged player) and `local_view`** (line of sight and EMIT_SFX depend on the viewed player, see "Draw-time side effects"); each machine's own player only sets the `player` of its commands.
- **Loop test** (`ta_compat/tools/game_flow <game> [steps] [seed]`: two battles from the same start with the same random commands; two "machines" over a reordering loopback with delay 3; the recording replayed on a third battle): **smoke: 5 seeds × 300 steps: identical, 0 desyncs, every checksum reproduced.** Patrol commands (`GAME_FLOW_PATROL=1`) no longer hang: smoke 8 seeds (300 to 3,000 steps) identical, no faults; the hang did not reproduce on base 9749015 either (seed 1, 300 steps; the use-after-free fix of retired order goals in 387e68a, made after the hang was seen, is the likely cure — not proven). game_flow now also fails on any engine fault (`Battle::first_fault()`).
- **Found with it (fixed):** use-after-free of orders and order goals: a replaced order's goal (and the route search) kept pointers to the order and goal objects, which were freed, so the "goal replaced" event landed in freed memory and runs diverged (ASan: `OrderGoal::report` after `free_order`). The engine now reuses freed orders **last freed first, with their stand-in address** (as a heap hands back the block it just took, so the event reaches the order now living there — to be confirmed against the original's allocator), and keeps retired order goals while a motion goal or the route search still points at them (`MotionContext::retired_goals`, pruned after each route search step).

#### The original's model, built (multiplayer, 2026-10-14) — wire format differential-tested (smoke: 6 scenarios / 133 compressions / 57 envelopes / 50 bit streams identical); games between engine instances loop-tested (smoke)
Implementation: `engine/net` (library `net`: `net/codec.h`, `net/protocol.h`, `net/frames.h`, `net/transport.h`, `net/net_battle.h`, `net/lobby.h`), the battle's network members (`engine/sim/src/net_units.cpp`, `sim/net_units.h`, declared in `sim/battle.h` "Network play"), bit streams `sim/bitstream.h`, game-data side `ta/net_setup.h`, client adapter `client/net_menu.h` (built when the front end's `client/front_end.h` is present), tool `ta_compat/tools/net_play`, harness `tools/orig_harness/net_harness`.

**Who simulates what.** Each machine runs the whole step for its own players (controller 1 human, 2 computer). The units of players on other machines (controller 3) only run their scripts in the unit pass (no weapons, orders, motion, healing or water damage) and follow packets. Everything random is decided once, on the owner's machine; the others copy the result. Each machine seeds its own generators from its own clocks, so the battle generators of two machines never agree, and nothing requires them to.

**Packet types** (type byte, then a fixed size per type from the table filled by `0x451FD0`; the unit state packet 0x2C carries its own size in bytes 1-2). Acceptance by game phase (table at `0x512BC0`): bit 0 menus/room, bit 1 loading (phase 5), bit 2 battle (phase 6). Sizes/phases: 2 ping 13/all, 3 3/all, 5 chat 65/all, 6 probe 1/all, 7 probe answer 1/all, 8 game start 1/all, 9 unit 23/battle, 0x0A transport 7, 0x0B damage 9, 0x0C killed 11, 0x0D fire 36, 0x0E detonate 14, 0x0F feature 6, 0x10 script call 22, 0x11 activation 4, 0x12 finished 5, 0x14 ownership 24, 0x16 share 17 (all battle), 0x13 sound 18/all, 0x15 loaded 1/loading+battle, 0x17 logo ask 2/all, 0x18 logo grant 2/all, 0x19 pause/speed 3/all, 0x1A unit sync 14/room, 0x1B status 6/all, 0x1C disconnect 5/all, 0x1D 9/never, 0x1E start position set 2/loading+battle, 0x1F its acknowledgement 5/loading+battle, 0x20 side record 186/all, 0x21 group ask 10/room, 0x22 group set 6/room, 0x23 alliance 14/all, 0x24 ally team 6/all, 0x25 5/room, 0x26 slot table 41/all, 0x27 modified executable 17/all, 0x28 view state 58/all, 0x29 3/all, 0x2A 2/all, 0x2C unit states ≥3/battle. Types 0, 1, 4, 0x2B have no size.

**Sending.** A packet "to everybody" (`Net_BroadcastGuaranteed` `0x451DF0`) leaves only from a present (+0x22 = 0) playing player (controller 1/2); a directed packet (`Net_SendPacketToPlayer` `0x451BC0`) also needs a present remote addressee (controller 3). With frame timing on (the default, global `0x506DBC`; switch `-p<n>` sets n packets per second, 2..30, `-p-` turns it off) packets wait in a channel: one for everybody and one per addressee (`0x513000`: 11 channels). A channel sends when its interval has passed (200 ms by default = 6 clock ticks of 1/30 s, `(ms·30 + 999)/1000`) or when forced, and at once when more than 0x429 bytes or 0x400 packets wait. Sending makes one frame per sending player: a 4-byte head (the everybody channel's running number: starts at −2 and counts down, back to −2 if it would rise above; −1 for addressed frames) and the packets in order. Without frame timing every packet goes out at once, unwrapped. Flushes: stage 10 of a step (not forced), and forced in many room and start routines.

**Message envelope** (`Condenser_WrapAndSend` `0x44FC10`, `Condenser_ReceiveUnwrap` `0x44F9C0`): kind byte 3 (plain) or 4 (compressed), u16 checksum, body. A frame of 13 bytes or more is compressed when compression is on (GS+0x4ED = 0) and the compressed body + 3 is shorter than the frame. Then every byte from offset 3 up to the last three bytes of the message is XORed with the low byte of its offset; the checksum is the 16-bit sum of those (scrambled) bytes. **The last three bytes are neither scrambled nor checksummed.** The receiver refuses messages under 4 bytes and bad sums, and passes other first bytes on unchanged. A debug loss rate (GS+0x37F35, normally 0) drops messages by `rand()` (no draw at 0).

**Dictionary coder** (`Squash_CompressLz77` `0x4D0F60`, `Squash_DecompressLz77` `0x4D1480`): window of 4096 bytes, zeroed, writing from position 1; up to 17 bytes look-ahead; matches of 2..17 bytes. Output: a flag byte for each eight items, lowest bit first: 0 = a literal byte, 1 = a little-endian word (window position << 4 | length − 2). A word with position 0 ends the data. The encoder keeps a binary search tree over window positions (node 0x1000 is the root, position 1 starts as its only child; positions are deleted 17 ahead of the writing point before being overwritten; position 0 is never inserted). Insertion walks from the root's larger child, comparing up to 17 bytes **as signed bytes** (the smaller side when the new string's first differing byte is less); a match at least as long as the best so far wins (ties go to the later node); a 17-byte match replaces the old node by the new one. Deletion of a node with two children moves the rightmost node of its smaller subtree into its place; links to node 0 can pick up a parent (emulated). After the end marker the encoder writes the item slot of the next flag bit from its stack buffer: an earlier literal of the same message or **uninitialised stack** (engine: an input; never read by the receiver; it can decide whether the compressed form is shorter). A "preset dictionary" path exists but its flag (`0x526FF8`) is never set.

**Receiving** (`Hapinet_ReceiveFramedPacket` `0x462F30`, split `0x463790`): per sending player a queue of packets with the step from which each may be delivered. A frame is split by the size table (stops at types < 2 or ≥ 0x2D or when the sizes run past the end; **a type with size 0 loops forever**). Its packets are stamped from the current step: one step per packet for up to `interval` packets, else spread `count·16/interval` sixteenths per packet, where interval = current step − the record's base step (never set after creation, so 0), clamped to 1..30 — after step 30 always 30. In a frame of more than 128 packets the oldest unit state packets beyond 128 are dropped. A queue holds 512. Delivery (`Net_PollReceivePackets` `0x4534E0`, one packet per call): the first sender queue (record order) whose head is due (not 1..30 steps in the future; at step 0 everything) gives it; only when none is due the next message is read; if that sender's head is still waiting, nothing more this time. Ordering: see "Frames out of order" below (built).

**Dispatch** (`Net_ProcessIncomingPackets` `0x453D40`, stage 2 and while paused): per delivered packet: phase mask; the sender must be a known, present remote player (controller 3, state ≠ 10) and the addressee a record in the game (in use, controller 1-3, state ≠ 10; else dropped).
