A sender that fails goes to `0x453010(sender, 6)` unless it is local (controller 1/2: ignored): an unknown id returns there before anything is sent (the check itself reads a "record 10" past the table, GS+0x2851, an input), an inactive record only gets its leaving byte +0x22 = 6, one with state 10 is sent away (status 0x1B with 6 to everybody) — so "unknown" never reaches the sender as such (2026-10-20); the sender's last-heard clock and packet count update; then by type: 2 ping (empty answer field: echoed with the time, else the latency is stored), 5 chat (shown when the addressee is human), 6 probe → 7, 8 start (ends the loop), 9 unit, 0x0A transport, 0x0B damage, 0x0C killed, 0x0D fire, 0x0E detonate, 0x0F feature (0xFD destroy, 0xFE ignite, 0xFF reclaimed, else weapon damage), 0x10 script call, 0x11 activation, 0x12 finished, 0x13 sound, 0x14 ownership, 0x16 share (1 energy, 2 metal transfer; 3 explored map), 0x19 pause flag / game speed (1..20), 0x1B status (player leaving), 0x23 alliance, 0x28 view state, 0x2C unit states; the room types below.

**Unit packet 9** (`0x4861D0`, also synthesised by the state packets when a unit's type differs): for the sending player (its record must have a unit range): a live unit at that id is killed first (kill packet kind 0); then the unit is set up as a **new unfinished** unit (fraction 1, no hit points — remote copies look unbuilt until a whole state or 0x12 arrives) at the packet's position: field set-up (two battle generator draws: facing and hover phase), script and pose, weapon slots, metal extraction, a motion object for mobile types (heading = the type's build angle), ground height, footprint claim, sight stamp, the player's live and made counts, the victory notice. The packet's angles are not used.

**Unit state packet 0x2C** (`Net_BroadcastPlayerUnitStates` `0x48B710`, every step for each playing player after its units' pass; bit stream `BitStream_Write` `0x415C10`: 32-bit words filled from bit 0): type 8 bits, size 16 bits (filled at the end), step 32 bits; for each live unit of the player's range whose motion goal has news (route goal: changed bit 3 or the blocked bit differs from its sent copy; flight goal: network bit 0): unit offset in the range 16 bits, type index (bits = bit length of the type count, 9 for the stock 279), the goal's state; stop after the unit that takes the packet past 511 bytes. Then 0xFFFF, a 1 bit and the **whole state of one unit**: the unit at step mod units-per-player in the range (a rolling refresh, 250 steps per round with the default limit).
- Route goal state (`0x44F4A0`): blocked bit, waypoint count (0 without a route, else min(count, 3)) in 2 bits, waypoints x16 z16. The goal's sent copy of the blocked bit is updated, its changed bit cleared.
- Flight goal state (`0x4908C0`): order goal kind in 2 bits (0 none, 1 point, 2 manoeuvre; other kinds write nothing) followed by the goal (point `0x44DDC0`: flags 8 bits; follow: piece 16, unit id 16; radius / altitude / heading 16 each when flagged; position 3×32 with flag 0x20. Manoeuvre `0x44E930`: flag bit, position 3×32, velocity 3×32, target heading 16 with the flag), then the layer in 2 bits; the network bits become the layer << 1.
- Whole state (`0x48B200`): type index; for a live type: hit points 16, build fraction 8 (0 when finished, else 1 + trunc(fraction × 254) — receivers divide by 255), activation byte, layer 2 bits, carried bit: carrier id 15 bits and transport piece 8; else position 3×32, heading, bank, pitch 16 each and, with a motion object, its speed 32.
- Receiving (`0x48B920`): the sender's step goes into its record (+0x18, the lag the step budget throttles on, ≥ 900 steps behind). Per unit: a type that differs synthesises packet 9; the remote goal reads its state (remote route goal `0x44F5C0`: blocked bit into the motion flags, the waypoints; remote flight goal `0x490A10`: the old order goal is deleted, a new one is built from the stream (constructors `0x44E080` / `0x44E9C0`; fields not sent keep fresh heap contents: the point goal's position without flag 0x20 and its formation offset — engine inputs `Battle::net_inputs`), then the layer change routine). Then **every live unit of the sender with a motion object takes one motion step and a ground settle** — remote units move only when state packets arrive (normally one per step after the spreading above). Then the whole state, when its bit is set (`0x48B3F0`): type 0 → a live unit gets the dying flag; a different type → packet 9; hit points; fraction (flag 0x2000 when it changed, compared before rounding to float); activation (set the given bits, clear the others, with their scripts); layer; carried → the transport routine with the carrier and piece, else a carried unit is dropped; position, angles; a changed cell or layer releases and reclaims the footprint and updates sight; flag 0x10000; speed.

**Other battle packets (applied):** fire 0x0D (`Net_HandleWeaponFirePacket` `0x49D270`): a meteor-type weapon (flag 0x20) launches an ownerless shot with the packet's velocity; else a live owner's slot (packet byte 35) takes the aim heading/pitch, the target is looked up, an interceptor finds the shot it chases (another player's shot with the same aim point whose owner is the packet's target), and the packet's weapon picks the routine (ballistic / vertical launch / dropped / directed) that fires the **slot's** weapon. Script call 0x10: a live unit starts the function by index with the packet's count and four arguments (not run now). Activation 0x11 (sent by every change of a playing player's unit, `0x48B090`). Finished 0x12 (`Unit_FinalizeBuiltUnit`(builder, unit)). Ownership 0x14 (`0x488570` with the packet): the new local owner gets a finished copy with the packet's hit points, **build fraction sent as an integer** (`ftol`: a half-built unit arrives finished), attitude and stockpiles; the old copy is destroyed by its owner's damage packets. Share 0x16 is addressed (`0x456EE0` energy, `0x457050` metal, `0x4571C0` map). View state 0x28 (`0x4573D0`, every fourth 30-step turn of the viewed player in a network game, to the human remote players): the player's totals (its record's kill/loss words and resource fields) for allies' displays; `0x457540` applies them unless a local player shares that player's view. Alliance 0x23 (`0x452960`): a local change sets the own alliance byte (+0x108) and, for a computer player, its answer; a remote player hears 0x23 and the receiver sets its "allied by" byte (+0x113) (+0x108 too for computer players or when forced).

**Battle room** (`net/lobby.h`): the session gives each player an id; every two seconds (60 clock ticks, `Lobby_PeriodicPlayerSyncBroadcast` `0x456310`) each playing player pings (2: its clock, 0, its id), probes (6) and sends its side record (0x20, 185 bytes from its record +0x27) and 0x24; the host also sends 0x26 (the net id of each slot; 0 empty, −1 for state 4). Side record fields read by the original (corrected 2026-10-15, see "Room and loading" below): map name at 0, map ready +0x8F, kind +0x94 (1 human, 2 computer), side +0x95, logo (team colour) +0x96 (−1 none), flags +0x97 (bit 0 host, 1 share metal, 2 share energy, 5 share map), option word +0x9B (bit 5 ready, 6 watch, 7 the room's WATCHING switch, 8-10 = the map flags +0x9C: mapping, line of sight, its type; 11-12 commander mode, 13 cheat codes (the room's CHEATING switch, see "Console commands"), 14 fixed locations, 15 closed), +0x9D bit 2 has the CD, unit limit +0xA5; start resources in hundreds (victory "side records"). The player's name is the session's (DirectPlay) name, not in the record; the engine places it at +0x40, a byte the original does not read. Start: the host's start (packet 8) after everyone human is ready, has the map and finished the definition exchange; start positions are assigned while loading (built, below). Then every machine loads with provider 3 (`sim::start_battle`): its players local, the others remote; battle generator and loading `rand()` seeded from each machine's own clock.

**Unit definition exchange** (`Sync_*` `0x46CBE0`-`0x470790`, packet 0x1A: sub byte, 4 zero bytes, key u32 at 6, value at 10): the host greets each human (sub 0); a client that heard the host sends its definition count (1), then four definitions per pass (2: key, checksum), then its count of host answers (4) every pass. The host enables a definition when every human peer sent it with the host's checksum (built 2026-10-15, below), answers everyone with sub 3 (key, enabled byte, confirmed byte, the unit limit short) and is ready when every human sent all its definitions. Key = the FBI file's simple checksum (def +0x13E); checksum (def +0x142, computed on demand by `0x42A610`) = XOR of the simple checksums of scripts\<unitname>.cob, every guis\<unitname>*.gui, download\<unitname>.tdf and the weapon section hash (+0x146). Applying (`0x46E160`): definitions the host did not confirm lose their "version compatible" bit (they cannot be built) and the confirmed ones get the room's unit limit (`ta::unit_sync_checks`; whether the original's gui enumeration names carry the extension is not verified).

**Frame loop of a network game** (`0x496790`): at most **one** step per frame (single-player runs up to 5); while the budget gives none and the game is paused: forced flush, network input and, every 60 clock ticks, a probe from the first human. The step budget slows when the slowest remote player's last state step is 900+ steps behind (`0x495230`).

**What diverges between machines, and how the original keeps it consistent.** Remote copies lag their owner by the frame interval plus the spreading (smoke: 3-8 world units for walking units, up to 30 in fights); between whole states they are extrapolated by the remote goal (waypoints or flight target) one motion step per state packet; the rolling whole state corrects position, hit points, fraction, activation and carrying every units-per-player steps. Random draws, effects, debris, wrecks and sight differ per machine; damage, kills, shots, creations, construction, ownership and transport are copied by packets from the machine that decided them, so the game outcome (who lives, who owns what) agrees. Remote copies start unbuilt with no hit points until their first whole state.

**Explosions and features across machines (2026-10-17; details in "Features and wrecks", "Features across the machines of a network game").**
- **A remote shot** is the receiver's own copy, launched by the fire packet 0x0D (start, aim, the slot's aim angles) and then flown and hit-tested by the receiver's simulation: it explodes where it meets ground, a feature or a unit *as this machine has them*, which can differ from the owner's (remote targets trail their owners; a feature already gone here or not yet). No packet reports a normal impact: 0x0E is sent only by an interceptor's area damage (the shot it set off, then itself), and its receiver detonates the first shot with that aim point and weapon index. Explosion art, sound, camera shake and smoke play on every machine where its copy explodes.
- **Damage only on the owner's machine.** The detonation skips all damage — units and features alike — when the shot's player record is a remote one (controller 3); the same holds for a remote unit's death explosion (its unit's player slot). The owner's machine sends unit damage and kills (0x0B, 0x0C); feature damage goes to the host, which alone decides it and announces destructions (0xFD) and fires (0xFE). So no damage is applied twice and none is lost, but explosions themselves need not be in the same place.
- **Per machine by design:** debris and shards (the Killed script runs on every machine with the packet's severity and draws that machine's battle generator), world effects, smoke, nano particles, burning smoke puffs (`rand()`), and every draw-time `rand()` use ("Draw-time side effects"). None of them changes features.
- **What the original guarantees for features:** the set of destroyed and burning features follows the host's decisions on every machine (guaranteed delivery), so maps converge once the packets are in, except for the races listed in "Features and wrecks" (late requests, a destroy meeting a still-burning copy, wreck cells from trailing copies, reproduction, console commands) and the damage counters, which only the host keeps.
- **Engine bug found and fixed (2026-10-17):** the battle never received its feature role in network games, so every machine applied the damage of its own shots locally and nobody announced destructions — features desynced exactly as reported ("some explosions exploded differently and caused features to desync"). Requests also went to everybody instead of the host. **Evidence** (`net_play features`, two engine machines on the loopback, Coast To Coast, 1,500 steps, a comparison every 60 steps): before the fix 2 of 26 comparisons identical, 28 cells different at the end (each machine had destroyed only the trees and rocks its own shots hit); after it, seeds 1-3 and Moon Quartet: every final map identical (the host's damage counters aside); 20-23 of 26 intermediate comparisons identical, the others only cells in flight (a fire or destruction the other machine hears of a frame interval later); three machines (seed 5): final maps identical. **With the original program** (ta_capture offscreen, a short guarded run first; menu command `dump_features` reads the original's cells, slots and feature definitions with its threads held): the original hosts (Canal Crossing, 514 × 552), our `dplay_play join --features` places six shooters by the original's commander and shoots at the features around it for 2,700 steps, then 1,500 quiet steps; the original sent 366 feature announcements (0xFD / 0xFE) and received 108 requests from us, each in an addressed frame (−1); at the end the two maps (18,921 cells holding something) were **identical**, damage counters included (all destroyed at once there). The other direction (our host, the original joining, Coast To Coast) ended identical too but proves nothing: its shooters stood out of range and fired no shot (no 0x0D / 0x0F sent) — to redo with the shooters closer. Re-checked 2026-10-20 on master 14456bf + this change: `net_play features` 900 steps seed 1: final maps identical, 14 of 16 comparisons identical (the other two a destruction in flight); three machines (seed 5, 600 steps): final maps identical. **Explosion positions follow the original's model** (re-read 2026-10-20: `0x49D270` launches the remote copy from the packet's start and aim through the slot's routine; `0x49AF90` detonates the first shot with the packet's aim and weapon index; `0x49B000` explodes a dying unit at its local copy's position): explosions are not synchronised in the original either, and with feature damage decided on the host they no longer change features differently. Regression: ctest `net_features_match` (900 steps) and `sim_tests` (`test_feature_damage_network_roles`).

**Engine API.** `net::NetBattle` is the battle's outside (wrapping the client's own): `attach(battle)` before the battle start (the start already sends packet 9 for the local commanders) and again after it (registers the local players with the transport); `set_clock` (the 1/30 s clock, an input), `send_chat`, `send_pause`, `send_speed`, `send_alliance`, `remote_lag`. `net::BattleRoom` runs the room; `battle_start()` gives the `sim::BattleStart`. Transports: `net::LoopbackHub` (in-process), `net::tcp_host` / `net::tcp_join` (a star: the host relays; framing u32 length, i32 from, i32 to; a message from −2 registers a machine's players).

**Interoperability with the original: built 2026-10-17, see "DirectPlay wire protocol" below (TCP/IP games with the original program played both ways, smoke).** The earlier note: it would need: a DirectPlay service provider speaking the original's session protocol (DirectPlay 5 TCP/IP: enumeration on UDP 47624, session messages, system messages 0x0003 add player, 0x0005 delete, 0x0102 player data, 0x0103 name, 0x0104 session description — handled at the top of `0x453D40`), DirectPlay's own guaranteed delivery on the wire, and the session description strings (`Lobby_BuildGameInfoStrings` `0x451090`). The packet formats, frames and envelope above are the original's.

**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/net_harness` (the original's coder, envelope (session send/receive replaced by stubs, import table filled with stubs) and bit streams in the suspended process): **smoke: 6 scenarios / 133 compressions (48 ending in a stale byte, compared but that byte) / 133 decompressions / 57 envelopes (24 compressed) / 57 unwraps / 50 bit streams (21,206 fields) identical.** `net_tests`: codec, frames, loopback, battle room (424 checks). Loop tests (`net_play <game> loop`, smoke): 2 machines × 300 steps; 3 machines × 900 and 1,800 steps; duel (each machine places 4 armed units near the next player: shots, damage, kills over the network) 2 × 900 and 3 × 900 steps: every owned unit present on the other machines with the same type, no stale copies of dead units at the end, positions within 3-30 world units; TCP between three processes on one host × 300 steps. **Not run in a harness:** the dispatch and the other battle packet handlers, the room and loading routines — read and implemented (named only here). The unit state packets are harness-verified (below).
**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`:** `0x4D0F60` `Squash_CompressLz77`, `0x4D0DE0` `Squash_TreeInsert`, `0x4D0C50` `Squash_TreeDelete`, `0x4D0C10` `Squash_TreeRightmostOfLeft`, `0x4D0B80` `Squash_TreeReplace`, `0x44FC10` `Condenser_WrapAndSend`, `0x44F9C0` `Condenser_ReceiveUnwrap`, `0x415B60` `BitStream_Init`, `0x415C10` `BitStream_Write`, `0x415DC0` `BitStream_Read` (created where missing: `0x460E20`, the goal network methods `0x44F480`, `0x44F4A0`, `0x4908B0`, `0x44EFC0`-`0x44EFE0`).
#### Room, loading and delivery (multiplayer, 2026-10-15) — unit state packets differential-tested (smoke); room and loading read and loop-tested (smoke)
**Side record, verified offsets** (each read site checked in the decompile): map name 0 (0x8F bytes), map ready +0x8F, kind +0x94, side +0x95, **logo +0x96** (team colour 0..9, 0xFF none; earlier notes called it the start position, wrong), flags +0x97, memory in MB +0x99 (u16, the room's MEM column), option word +0x9B (bits above; its high byte +0x9C is the map flags), +0x9D bit 2 CD, energy +0xA1 and metal +0xA3 in hundreds (read by the deathmatch start `0x464F80`), unit limit +0xA5, version major/minor +0xA7/+0xA8, **map checksum +0xA9** (u32). The map checksum (`0x4373A0`) is the XOR of: the TNT's 64-byte header, the attribute block (width × height × 4 bytes at the header's offset +0x10), the feature block (count +0x1C × 0x84 bytes at +0x20), and the OTA's `[GlobalHeader]` section checksum (stored by the TDF reader in the map info +0xC20). A client compares its own with the host's when the host's version is 3.1 or later (major > 1, or 1 and minor > 1) and sets map ready when they agree (`0x448C70`). Engine: `ta::map_sync_checksum`, `BattleRoom::map_checksum`.

**Logos** (0x17 ask → host, 0x18 grant, `0x4526C0` / `0x452570` / `0x4523E0`): a client asks the host for a logo; the host (and a host asking for itself) keeps it when nobody else present (state ≠ 0 and ≠ 4) holds it, else gives the first free logo from it upward, wrapping at 10; with every logo taken the count past the last is given (cannot happen with ten seats). The room's colour button asks for the next one.

**Ally teams** (0x24, `Lobby_BroadcastAllyTeamPacket` `0x452BD0`): the player record +0x13F, 0..4 a team, 5 none; the button cycles (team + 1) mod 6. Teams become the battle's alliance groups (5 → none).

**Machine groups** (0x21 ask / 0x22 set, `0x450530`): the host takes group 1; local computer players join their machine's group; each human machine asks the host for a new group (flag 1), or asks the host to repeat a remote player's group (flag 0). `0x450980`: when two players share a group (1..10) the game switches to **per-group directed sends** (GS+0x299C): a broadcast goes out as one directed copy to the first player of each other group. Engine: `RoomPlayer::group`, `NetBattle` (per-group copies after each network input).

**Loading handshake** (`0x4568C0`, every pass of the loading loop; `0x497180` waits first for the own logo and map ready): the host, once, assigns start positions to the eligible players (active, controller 1..3, state ≠ 10, not watching): with fixed locations (host option 0x4000) in slot order 0..n−1; otherwise a shuffle of 0..n−1 that swaps each place with an earlier one (Sattolo) using the **main thread's C runtime `rand()`** — with fewer than three players a single draw (`rand()·2 / 0x8000`) decides the order. The host sends 0x1E {position} to each remote player until it answers 0x1F {its id}; local players get theirs directly (record +0x147). Clients broadcast 0x15 (loaded) every pass; the host broadcasts 0x15 when every remote player has loaded (GS+0x29A4) and acknowledged (GS+0x29D0). A machine is done when it has heard 0x15 from every remote player. The `rand()` state is an input of the machine (the menus leave it); the engine takes it from the room's `sim::Lcg15` (`BattleRoom::frame(clock, crt_rand)`).

**Unit definition agreement** (`0x46D970`): a definition is enabled when **every human peer** sent it, with the host's checksum, and the peer's version is 3.1 or later (the engine: major ≥ 2, or 1 with minor ≥ 2). Others lose the compatible bit as before.

**Join gate** (TCP/UDP star of the engine; DirectPlay in the original): when the room is closed (option 0x8000) or the game has started, the host refuses a machine it does not know with 0x1B status 6.

**Frames out of order** (`0x462F30`): the expected running number is the last − 1 (when that is ≥ −1, −2). A frame newer than expected (a gap: head < expected) is **held** when nothing is held (nothing delivered). With a frame held: a newer arrival is deferred and the held one goes first, **unspread**, the deferred one spread later; an older arrival goes now unspread and the held one is replayed next, unspread. Without a gap but with a frame held, the held one still goes first and the new one is deferred (the original's order). The last running number becomes that of the frame processed first. When a frame's packets arrive while the sender's queue is not empty, the queue is restamped from the current step. Engine: `net::FrameReceiver` (`net/frames.h`).

**Delivery as the original expects it:** DirectPlay delivers guaranteed messages; the engine's UDP transport (`net::udp_host` / `net::udp_join`) gives the same: datagrams carry a kind (guaranteed, plain, acknowledgement, register) and a per-link sequence number; guaranteed ones are resent every 100 ms (up to 100 times) until acknowledged and duplicates are dropped by sequence; the host relays (a star like DirectPlay's host). Pings are sent unguaranteed (`0x4C9790(0)`), as in the original. A deterministic loss rate can be set for tests. Frames can then arrive out of order, which the receiver above handles.

**Other room and battle packets:** 0x23 alliance: the "allied by" bytes (+0x113) are only shown by the room screen. Watch mode: when the players' stage makes the local player a watcher (`0x464F80`) its record gets the watch bit, mapping and sight off, and is announced (`0x450F90`). Time-out (`0x453C20`): off when GS+0x37F2F bit 0 is set; while paused the baseline is the pause; a remote player silent for more than GS+0x37F31 × 30 clock ticks is dropped — unless players of several groups are silent, then nobody (`0x453A50(−1)`). Chat recipients (`0x453360`, GS+0x2BF0): 0 everybody, 1 allies, 2 enemies (by the local record's alliance byte for each record), 3 the chosen players; a message starting with '+' goes to everybody. 0x27: "has modified his executable.  Game integrity breached." shown 12 times as "%s %s" (the sender name). 0x1C: "Player %s has disconnected". Network games load with the user's client settings (screen, speeds, interface type) like skirmishes.

**Leaving players (read 2026-10-20).** *Send away* `0x453010(id, reason)`: the record by id (controller ≠ 0; none → return 0); the 6-byte status {0x1B, id, reason}, always from the first local record (controller 1/2), through `Net_BroadcastGuaranteed` (`0x451DF0`, which refuses a sender whose leaving byte +0x22 is set). Only when the record is in use and +0x22 is 0: a local human (1) — for every local record k in slot order a status with k's id, then `0x452CC0(the human's id)` (not k's: this machine's computer players' teams are not killed) and +0x22[k] = reason; since the first local record is leaving after its own turn, only the first status goes out. A computer player (2): status, `0x452CC0`. A remote record (3): status; a computer (side +0x94 ≠ 1) → `0x452CC0`, a human → for each of the 10 records whose group dword +0xC equals its group byte (in use or not; freed ones have 0): `0x452CC0` and +0x22 = reason. Finally +0x22 = reason on the record itself. Callers: the 0x1B receiver (case 0x19, the packet's reason), the session's "player destroyed" (1), the time-out dialog's Reject (6, `0x453640` / `0x4538F0`), the sender check (6), the host's checks of a joiner's session data (3 closed, 4 password, 8 version), the watch check (9), the room's kick (1, `0x446020`), "leave" (2) and watch refusals (9) in `0x447B10` / `0x4464D0`, `0x448C70` (11). **Status 7** ("You need a unit you don't have for this game", `0x452C40` / `0x450E20`) is never sent by TotalA.exe: no caller passes 7 and no other writer of +0x22 stores it; only a 0x1B from another program could carry it. *Remove* `0x452CC0(id)`: the record by id, in use, controller 1-3, state +0x146 ≠ 10; every local record's alliance bytes toward its state cleared (+0x108, +0x113); its team killed (`0x486F10`); in the room (GS+0x2A44 bit 2 clear) a local record's session player is destroyed (`0x4CA780`) and any record freed; in the battle only a remote one is freed (`0x463C60(record, 0)`: controller 0 and side kind 0; in-use 0, net id −1, group 0) and a local one stays as it is. Then GS+0x2A3C − 1, side +0x9D bit 2 cleared, its own alliance bytes +0x108..+0x112 zeroed, the menus redrawn when the screen is 3; in the battle, when its side record had the host flag (+0x97 bit 0), the record (in use, controller 3 **or** 1, so another machine's computer player counts) with the highest net id (unsigned, above 0) gets the flag too — machines can disagree (each sees its own computer players as controller 2) and the old record keeps its flag, so `0x450030` (the first flagged record) still names the old host where it comes first (−1 once freed: requests go nowhere), while the machine whose local human got the flag decides feature damage itself from then on. *0x1C received* (case 0x1A): the named record (controller ≠ 0) → "Player %s has disconnected" (its session name +0x2B, line kind 4, from the sender); `0x452CC0(id)` when it is an in-use remote record or the loading is not under way (GS+0x38D75 bit 0 clear or bit 1 set); the 0x1C passed on from the local record (GS+0x2A42) — once per machine for a remote record (freed afterwards); naming the first controller-1 record sets GS+0x3923B bit 2 and clears bit 4. Nothing in TotalA.exe starts a 0x1C except `0x452800` (same body, no caller found). The probe answer 0x07 (case 5) sets the sender record's +0x21 bit 0; no instruction reads that bit (searched: GS-relative +0x1B81..+0x1B87, record-pointer +0x21, +0x1D, −0x52), bit 1 is the local record's copy of GS+0x2B4C bit 4 (`0x426E80`; cleared by `0x451220`), bit 2 is tested by `0x497180`; the byte is not cleared when a record is freed or seated again.

**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/netstate_harness` (the original's routines in the suspended process; packet 9, activation, transport, footprint release/claim and sight replaced by logging stubs, unit links by stubs): whole-state write `0x48B200` vs `sim::write_whole_state`, whole-state apply `0x48B3F0` vs `sim::apply_whole_state` (calls in order with their arguments, every unit byte, the motion speed), point goal write/read `0x44DDC0`/`0x44E080`, manoeuvre goal write/read `0x44E930`/`0x44E9C0`, route goal write `0x44F4A0` (with the flag update) and remote read `0x44F5C0`. **Smoke: 6 scenarios / 450 operations: 191 whole states written and applied (82 cell moves, 73 remade units, 36 transport calls), 75 point goals, 94 manoeuvre goals, 90 routes identical.** Found: a negative route count (sent as its low two bits, no waypoints) — the engine looped over it. A swapped angle in the engine's apply is caught at once (mutation check). The apply also clears the object state's draw frame (+0x10, render only). `net_tests` 454 checks (frames out of order, room: logos, ally teams, groups, loading with fixed and shuffled positions, join gate). `net_play` loop (3 machines × 600 steps, duel) and UDP with 10 % loss (2 machines × 600 steps, duel: 38/45 resends, 20/16 duplicates, 9/87 frames out of order, copies consistent). **A two-player network game through the client's menus** (two headless `ta_play --realtime` processes on loopback TCP: main menu → multiplayer → host / join → ready → start → loading handshake → battle, 2,400 frames, both commanders on both machines at their assigned positions with their logos).
**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe` (harness-verified):** `0x48B200` `NetUnit_WriteWholeState`, `0x48B3F0` `NetUnit_ApplyWholeState`, `0x44DDC0` `PointGoal_WriteNetState`, `0x44E080` `PointGoal_ConstructFromNetState`, `0x44E930` `ManoeuvreGoal_WriteNetState`, `0x44E9C0` `ManoeuvreGoal_ConstructFromNetState`, `0x44F4A0` `RouteGoal_WriteNetState`, `0x44F5C0` `RemoteRouteGoal_ReadNetState`. Read only (named here): `0x4568C0` loading handshake, `0x4526C0`/`0x452570`/`0x4523E0` logos, `0x450530` groups, `0x450980` per-group sends, `0x453C20` time-out, `0x453360` chat, `0x4373A0` map checksum, `0x46D970` definition agreement, `0x462F30` frame order, `0x497180` loading wait.
**Open:** a harness of the dispatch, room and loading handlers (they need the player table and the session; read and loop-tested only); the multiplayer screens are plain engine screens, not yet the original's `SELGAME.GUI` / `LOUNGE2.GUI` layouts (1:1 to do, with the front end); DirectPlay system messages and interoperability with the original (built since, below); cheat warning 0x25; game-speed and pause keys only send the packets.

#### DirectPlay wire protocol (2026-10-17) — TCP/IP: games with the original program in both directions (smoke); serial frame checked against the original's provider both ways (smoke); serial / modem sessions between engine instances (smoke)
Our own implementation of the wire protocol the original's network layer (Microsoft DirectPlay 4, message version 14, through the system's `dplayx`) speaks; the engine links no DirectPlay code. Written from watching a real `TotalA.exe` (capture tool, below) exchange messages with probes and with our implementation, from the original's own use of the library (decompiled call sites, named here) and from the protocol's public description; for the serial frame, from the system serial provider's receive and send routines (bytes and checks only). Implementation: `engine/net` `net/dplay_wire.h` (messages), `net/dplay_session.h` (`net::dplay::Session`: a `net::Transport` with a player directory — host / find / join / players / game messages / directory events), `net/dplay_tcpip.h` (TCP/IP provider), `net/dplay_serial.h` (serial and modem provider); room hooks in `net/lobby.h` (`Transport::next_event / create_player / player_name / set_session_info`); client `ta_compat/src/client_net_screens.cpp`; tool `ta_compat/tools/dplay_play` (whole games over the protocol, `decode` of logs); tests `net_tests` (`test_dplay`: the original's messages decoded and made again byte for byte, the serial frame, a session over an in-memory link); evidence logs `ta_compat/tests/fixtures/dplay/`.

**How the original uses the library.** Imports `DPLAYX` ordinals 1 (`DirectPlayCreate`, `0x4CA5D0`/`0x4CA900`) and 4 (`DirectPlayLobbyCreateA`, `0x4CA490`); interfaces `IDirectPlay3A` {133EFE41-32DC-11D0-9CFB-00A0C90A43CB} and `IDirectPlayLobby2A` {1BB4AF80-A303-11D0-9C4F-00A0C905425E}. Application id **{99797420-F5F5-11CF-9827-00A0241496C8}** (`0x4FCFB8`). Providers listed by `EnumConnections` (`0x4CA250`, the connection screen `0x444580`); on current Windows: Serial, Modem, Internet TCP/IP (in that order; no IPX). Wrappers (session object GS+0x14): send `0x4C97B0` (guaranteed when `0x50A780` ≠ 0; `0x4C9790` clears it around the ping), receive `0x4C9840` (receive-all; sender/addressee kept at object +0x4B5/+0x4B9), host `0x4C9920` (`Open` create: description flags 0, at most GS+0x4F1 = 10 players, application id, name and four words from `0x451090`), join `0x4C9FD0` (`Open` join by instance id), enumerate `0x4C9E50` (timeout 1500 ms, flags 0x81 = available + return status; called again while it answers "connecting"; ESC aborts; the "Connecting (ESC to abort)" box), create player `0x4CA6A0` (short and long name = the nickname; **player data 21 bytes: the typed password in 17, then u16 0, u16 0x50**), destroy `0x4CA780`, names `0x4CA7C0`, description change `0x4C9890` (`0x451180`: option bit 0x10 sets the description's join-disabled flag 0x20). Address of a connection (`0x441C30`): TCP/IP the typed address string (TCP.GUI, registry TCPADDR); serial a 20-byte port block at `0x512770` (port, speed, one stop bit, no parity, RTS/DTR flow) from SERIAL.GUI (`0x442560`: PORTS COM1..COM4, SPEEDS 115200/57600/38400/19200/14400/9600, HOST, JOIN; registry SERPORT/SERBAUD); modem the number (MODEM.GUI `0x443100`: NUMBER, MODEMS, the address book ACCOUNTS / MODEMNUMBERS / MODEMACCOUNTS, HOST answers, JOIN dials).

**System messages the game handles** (top of `0x453D40`, sender id 0): create player (3) on a host whose record has the host bit: closed game (option 0x8000) → status 3; data not 21 bytes or words not 0 / 0x50 → status 8; a password set (+0x9D bit 0) and the data's string differs → status 4; else `0x450A10` seats it in the first free slot as a remote player (state 3) with the session's names (record +0x2B long, +0x49 short); destroy player (5) → the player leaves (status 1 / 10); player data (0x102) copies a 185-byte record (not seen used); names (0x103); session description (0x104) is copied to GS+0x471 on clients.

**TCP/IP provider (what goes over the wire).** Every machine listens on a TCP "stream" port (first free from 2300) and a UDP "datagram" port (first free from 2350); together they are its provider data (two 16-byte socket addresses: family 2, port big-endian, the address — zero in what a machine says about itself; the receiver fills in the address the messages came from). A host answers enumeration on **UDP and TCP 47624** (on the original's side the system's helper `dplaysvr` owns 47624 and hands requests to the game's session). A client sends its enumeration to both (to the typed address, or a broadcast when none): TCP connect, send, close. **Framing (TCP and enumeration datagrams):** u32 = message size (header included) in bits 0-19 | token 0xFAB << 20, then the sender's stream address (16 bytes: where answers go), then the payload. A machine sends over connections it opened to the other's stream port and reads whatever arrives on connections others opened (answers arrive on the host's own connection back). **Game messages** (from the game's send): guaranteed ones over TCP as header + u32 from + u32 to + bytes; the others (pings) as bare UDP datagrams to the datagram port: u32 from, u32 to, bytes (no header). A message to everybody goes with to = 0 to each other machine once; the receiving library hands it to the game with to = its local player's id. System messages start with the signature "play", u16 command, u16 version (14); offsets inside them count from the signature.

**Messages** (fields after signature/command/version; u32 unless said):
- 0x02 enumerate: application id (16), password offset (0 or 32), flags (0x81). 0x01 its answer: session description (80 bytes: size 80, flags, instance id, application id, most players, current players (game players only), two pointers of the sender's memory (zero from a host; stale heap addresses in the original's 0x1A), key, reserved, four application words), name offset (92), the name (UTF-16).
- 0x05 player id asked of the host: flags (9 = system + sender-local for a machine, 8 for a game player); 0x07 answer: id, 24-byte security description (zero), two offsets (zero), result.
- 0x13 add-forward request (the newcomer's machine): addressee 0, its system player id, group 0, offset 28 of a packed player, password offset; the packed player; the password (UTF-16, an empty one = two zero bytes); **the session key echoed** (u32). Refusal: 0x24 (result).
- **Packed player** (create, add-forward): size, flags, id, short-name bytes, long-name bytes, provider-data bytes, player-data bytes, member count, its machine's system player id, fixed size 48, version 14, parent; then short name, long name (UTF-16 with terminator), provider data, player data.
- 0x29 the host's player table for a newcomer: player count, group count, offset of the players, shortcut count, description offset (36), name offset, password offset; the description; the name; the players **super-packed**: fixed size 16, flags, id, a mask, version (system players) or their machine's system player id; then by the mask: short name (bit 0), long name (bit 1), player data (length field size in bits 4-5: 1 byte, 2, 4), provider data (bits 2-3), member ids (bits 6-7), parent (bit 8), shortcuts (bits 9-10). The newcomer first, then the host's system player (flags 0xF: system, name server, in session, sender-local), the host's game players (0xC), other machines (as stored: flags without bit 3, with bit 2).
- 0x08 create player (from its own machine to every other machine): addressee 0, id, group 0, offset 28, password offset 0, the packed player (game player: flags 8, its machine's provider data), then u16 0 and u32 0. 0x0B delete player: addressee, id, group, two zero offsets. 0x1A description changed: addressee, name offset, password offset, the description, name, password (the empty password is sent too). 0x16 / 0x17 ping / answer: sender, tick (not seen: the game's sessions have no keep-alive flag).
- **Player ids** (host): (uniqueness 1 << 16 | index) XOR the description's key (dwReserved1); index 0 the host's system player, 1 its first game player, then in asking order. Flags: 1 system player, 2 name server, 4 in the session, 8 on the sending machine.
- **Joining**: enumerate → answer; player id (9) → id; add-forward request → player table (0x29). Then per game player: player id (8) → id → create player to every machine. Existing machines hear of a newcomer from its create messages (the engine's host also sends them the newcomer's system player as a create message; the original's own way for a third machine is not observed).

**Serial / modem provider (one byte stream).** Each message gets an 8-byte head: u16 0x2BAD, u16 the whole frame's length (≤ 0xFFFF), u16 check of the message bytes, u16 check of the head's first six bytes; the check is the 16-bit cyclic code of reflected polynomial 0xA001 (CRC-16/ARC: start 0, no final inversion, check value of "123456789" 0xBB3D). A receiver whose head does not check drops one byte and looks again; a bad message check drops the frame. The message is the session message itself (system or game), no addresses (provider data empty). The original's modem provider dials and answers through the system's telephony service; the line then carries the same frames; the engine drives a Hayes modem itself (ATZ, ATDT number / wait for RING, ATA; CONNECT starts the frames).

**Game-level findings made by playing against the original (fixed in the engine):**
- Side record packet 0x20: bytes +0x91..+0x94 (record +0x90) hold the record's owner id; **the receiver finds the record by it** (`0x453D40` case 0x1E; written by `0x4517B0` and `0x456310`) — without it the original ignored our records.
- A joining machine asks the host for logo 0 at the end of its join (`0x4517B0` → `0x4526C0(0)`); the original's host grants logos only when asked. **A remote player left with logo 0xFF crashes the original's battle view** (`0x467C00` indexes the logo table with it; seen at step 78).
- The host's start condition (`0x456760`) needs at least one remote player and the ready bit (0x20) of every playing record; it does not look at +0x8F (the original's clients never set +0x8F in the room).
- **Unit ranges in network games** (`0x4854A0`): the ten player records are sorted by their session ids (unsigned, insertion sort; unused records have id −1 from `0x4644D0`, so they come last) and range i (units 1 + i × limit …) goes to the i-th; every machine numbers units alike although each seats its own player first (the original's client: itself in slot 0). Without it the original crashed on our first unit packet (`Unit_SetGroup` `0x480250`, a unit in an unused record's range). Engine: `sim::start_battle` (provider 3).
- Loading: a client counts the host's 0x15 only after its own loading finished (GS+0x38D75 bit 2, set by `0x497180` after `Loading_LoadBattleData`); the host's single 0x15 can then sit in the frame receiver until the host's next frame (the battle's frames follow at once).
- The session description's words: `0x451090` (name: 16 places of game name + 15 of map name, space padded; words = the host record bytes +0x99..+0xA8; the option word's low 4 bits = the player count).

**Capture tool additions** (`ta_compat/tools/capture`, all offscreen as before): `--menu FILE` (wait / key / type / erase / click / frame / dialog_* / until_* / threads / quit), `--net-trace` (every message the game sends (`0x4C97B0`) and receives (after the call in `0x4C9840`)), **every socket bind of the game moved to 127.0.0.1** (a breakpoint on `ws2_32!bind`; also the provider's router-discovery sockets on port 1900), a stop when a firewall question window appears, `--serial-pipe NAME` (the serial provider's port opened as the pipe `\\.\pipe\NAME`; the port set-up calls answered as a port would). Menu path: M, DOWN DOWN (TCP/IP), S, the address (blank to host), ENTER; N, N hosts (`NEWMULTI`); a client clicks the game (100,125) and J; Go? at (487,77) for the first row; Start at (565,452).

**Smoke results (2026-10-17, one machine, 127.0.0.1):**
- Engine host (`dplay_play host`) + original client: found in the original's game list (name, map, words), joined, room (records, map, logos, definition exchange, ready), start, loading handshake, battle 1,200+ steps: both commanders on both machines, our built units created on the original (packets 09/10/11/12/0C), unit states (0x2C) both ways, chat; the original did not crash. Logs: `fixtures/dplay/engine_host_original_client.log`, `original_side_engine_host.txt`.
- Original host + engine client (`dplay_play join`): listed, joined, seated, logo granted, chat shown in the original's room, start, battle 900+ steps both ways. Logs: `original_host_engine_client.log`, `original_side_original_host.txt`.
- Original host + **the engine client's own menus** (`ta_play --headless --realtime`, script: connection screen → TCP/IP → address → game list → join → room → ready): the original's game listed with its columns, joined, the original's room shows the engine player ready, both loaded the battle (`original_side_client_menus.txt`). The engine client's menus against an engine host: `engine_client_menus_join.log`.
- Two engine instances over the protocol: TCP/IP (300 steps), serial over a TCP stand-in cable (300 steps), modem through two emulated Hayes modems (dial, RING, answer, CONNECT; 300 steps): rooms, start, battles, chat, units both ways.
- Serial with the original: its serial port redirected to our pipe; the original's provider framed its enumeration (our receiver checked head and message), and its receiver took our answer frame whole (no resync: the frame checks out) — **but the original stays in "Connecting" and no game is listed**: not resolved (the port is emulated by answering the port calls; the provider's own connection state for serial was not found). `fixtures/dplay/serial_original_enum.log`.

**Not tested / open:** serial or modem sessions with the original past enumeration (no null-modem pair or modems on this machine; com0com is not installed); real modems (only emulated); IPX (no provider on current Windows; not built); three or more machines with original programs (how the original's host announces a newcomer to other machines: not observed; the engine sends a create message of the newcomer's system player); password-protected sessions against the original (the engine's host refuses wrong passwords with 0x24; the original's handling not observed); the original leaving mid-game (delete messages decoded, the engine also treats a broken link as the machine leaving); live sessions now run the 1:1 `LOUNGE2` room (see "The live battle room and the network start" below).

#### The live battle room and the network start (2026-10-17) — two- and three-machine games through the 1:1 menus (smoke); the rooms' states against the original's room drawing (smoke); the restrictions against the original's dialog
The client's room for live games is the original's LOUNGE2 screen (see "Client", "Battle room, live"); everything it changes goes out as the original's packets and everything it shows comes from them. What was found and built on the way (engine `net::BattleRoom`, client `client::launch_network_battle`):
- **Session ids have any sign** (DirectPlay ids are XORed with the session's key): a host whose own id was negative failed with "Direct Play failed to add new player", and computer players got stand-in ids the other machines' sessions did not know (their records never arrived). Only 0 and −1 mean "none".
- **Computer players' names**: the host's session player for a computer is named "AI:" and the host's player's name (16 characters, `0x451220`); the other machines show the session's names.
- **The start in the original's order**: the host's 0x08 brings every machine to the loading screen; the loading handshake (start positions 0x1E / 0x1F, "loaded" 0x15, `0x4568C0`) runs only once the machine's data is loaded (`BattleRoom::defer_loading_handshake` / `data_loaded`). Earlier the engine ran the handshake in the room and loaded afterwards (the others then started their battle while one still loaded). While the handshake runs the room stops reading the network at the packet that completes it: what follows is the battle's (a machine's first battle packets can come right behind the host's "loaded").
- **Loading progress, packet 0x2A** (2 bytes, all phases; `0x456DE0` after each loading-screen frame): for each of this machine's players the average of the six loading rows, sent from that player's id; the receiver puts it in the sender's record byte +0x20 (`0x453D40`), the bar on its loading screen. Each frame also sends a probe (0x06) from each of this machine's players. (The protocol table called 0x2A "view flag"; `packet::kLoadProgress`.)
- **Unit definition exchange entries** (the sync object `GS+0x2A30`, `0x46D040`): per definition key: the checksum, a 32-bit word whose low short is "allowed" (starts 1; the restrictions' closing sets 0 / 1, `0x46E450` / `0x46E4D0`) and whose high short is the verdict (starts 0 on every machine — checked by calling the original's lookup `0x46E330` in the suspended process: word 0x00000001 — and is set by the host's comparison with every peer, `0x46D970`), and the count (the most units of it one player may own: −1 no limit, 0 for "wacky" definitions; `0x46E550` sets it). The host's answer (0x1A sub 3, `0x46D860`): key at 6, allowed byte at 10, verdict byte at 11, the count's low 16 bits at 12 — to every peer, after each comparison and each count or allowed change (the restrictions answer the twelve rows in view at every refresh). A joining machine replaces its entry with the host's (`0x46D6C0`; the count sign-extended). (The engine sent the verdict at 10, 1 at 11 and the room's unit limit at 12.)
- **The rules at the battle start** (`0x46E160`): each definition is buildable only with both shorts set, and its "most per player" (def +0x15A) becomes the entry's count; a definition without an entry can be neither built nor owned. The engine's network battles now take these rules (`NetGameRequest::unit_rules` → the loader's `prepare_start`: the "version compatible" type bit and `UnitCreateType::max_per_player`).
- **Room rules on the host each frame**: deathmatch commander mode sends computer players away (status 11); watching off makes watchers play.

**Verified (smoke):**
- **Two machines, the original's menus on both** (two headless `ta_play --realtime` on 127.0.0.1, TCP/IP provider of the original's session protocol): connection screen → TCP/IP → address → game list → Start New / Join → room: chat both ways, a slot blocked then a computer player (its side, team), teams and an alliance, five option switches, a new map through SELMAP, the joiner's View Map, the host's restrictions (a count changed, seen on the joining machine), ready, START → loading screens with each other's bars → battle; in battle "<Alice> gg all" reached the other machine and "<Alice->Allies> hi allies" did not (not allied in battle). 2,200–3,000 frames.
- **Three machines** (a second joiner leaves the room, joins again, takes CORE): all four players (three humans, a computer player) in the battle on every machine; every loading screen showed the other machines' bars going up from their packets.
- **The rooms' states against the original** (each state the live room drew written as a room file and its frame, `TA_ROOM_DUMP`; gui_harness drew the original's own room from the same records — the main menu run first (it loads the interface colours), the room's focus, the start grill's frames at the same clocks, the unit definition exchange stubbed done or running; `focus` / `keys` / `grill` lines of the room file): last two-machine run, 26 states (host and joiner): 18 identical; 7 start-grill states identical but for the glowing START button (at most 344 pixels: its light row, not resolved); 1 a joining room's first frame before the host's record arrives (see Open). Found on the way and fixed: no focus ring on lists and fields; a joining machine's map before the host's record; quick keys found anew each refresh; **the players are kept together** (the room frame moves a player into a free slot before it: a computer player added in slot 3 with slot 2 free shows in slot 2) — the engine's host now does so.
- **Key outcomes in the live rooms' states** (gui_harness `sweep` vs ta_play `--key-sweep` on a host's and a joiner's room file: every letter, digit, Tab, Space, Backspace and arrow with none / Shift / Alt; Enter, Escape and Alt+A left out — they fault the harness, whose network sends are stubbed): host 128 of 128 identical (the restrictions, the kick question, the map choice and the host's Ready → START focus included; the keyboard sweep's 4 differences are gone), joiner 126 of 128 (Alt + an UNUSED row's key: the original's handler adds a computer player for a joining machine too — in the harness its session refuses and a message box opens; not built).
- **With the original program** (ta_capture offscreen: hidden desktop, GDI drawing into memory, stubbed imports, safety monitor; a short guarded run first; 127.0.0.1): (A) the original hosts, our client joins through its menus: each room shows the other's records, side, team, options (the original's switch to deathmatch), chat lines "<Ours> hello original" / "<PRIME_...> hello from the original" both ways, ready lights; the original's START; each loading screen shows the other's bar from its 0x2A packets (the original's own loading is slow under the debugger: about 60 s); handshake (0x1E / 0x1F / 0x15); battle on both (unit states 0x2C both ways). (B) our client hosts, the original joins: the same, the original's room showing itself in row 0 (its own record order); battle on both. Summaries: `fixtures/dplay/lounge_original_host_client_menus.txt`, `lounge_client_menus_host_original_joins.txt`. Found: the joining client sent "loaded" (0x15) every 5 ms while waiting (the original: once a loading-screen frame, 200 ms) — fixed.

**Open:** the original keeps its own record order (a joining machine sees itself in row 0; seen with the original as joiner); the engine's rooms all use the host's slot order. A joining machine adding a computer player is not built. The room's alliances (+0x108) are not carried into the battle's alliance table (the battle uses the ally teams), so "a:" lines reach only team allies. Load / Save List of the restrictions (their files) not built. In the first frame of a joining machine's room, before the host's record arrives, the original's unit limit text reads past the player table (record 10); the client shows the settings' limit. The glowing START button's light differs in up to 344 pixels from the original's in the grill states. The time-out dialog of the battle (TIMEOUT.GUI) and status 7 ("You need a unit you don't have") are not built.

#### Room alliances, the end-of-game exchange, serial findings (2026-10-20) — read; net_tests and net_play smoke
Checklist: `docs/parity/NETWORK.md`.
- **Room alliances are the battle's alliance table.** The player records go on into the battle, so their alliance bytes (+0x108, written by slot in the room) are the table the battle starts with. Every room frame (`0x446C70`, from `0x448C70` and `0x447B10`) each live record is allied with itself and with every live record of its team (team not 5), +0x108 and +0x113 both, on every machine and without packets. Before a team changes (`0x446E90`, the TEAMICONS button `0x447B10`, `0x446F50`) the player breaks, forced, with every other player of its old team: `0x452960`(its id, other id, 0, forced = 1); packet 0x23 carries the forced word at bytes 10-13 and a forced receiver clears +0x108 as well as +0x113. ALLY toggles the local +0x108 byte (`0x452960` not forced). Engine: `BattleRoom::alliance_rows()` → `BattleStart::alliances` (the battle no longer derives alliances from the teams in network games). Not built: the ALLY button on a teammate also takes a record off the team (which record is ambiguous in the decompile).
- **Final totals at the end of a network game** (`0x499200` → `0x4572A0` each frame once the end flags are set, then `Sleep(250)`): each local playing record asks every remote record for its totals (0x28 with byte 1 = 1, `0x4573D0`) until, for a remote human (side record kind 1), it acknowledged ours (+0x11E[slot]), said it had our acknowledgement (+0x134) and its own arrived (+0x129); for a remote computer player until its own arrived. Receiving 0x28 (`0x457540`): the totals are kept unless a local record already has that sender's final ones (+0x129 — earlier notes called this a shared-view test, wrong); with the ask byte every local playing record marks +0x129, answers 0x29 {1, +0x11E != 0} and, while `0x490230` (the victory check) is false and +0x11E is 0, sends its own totals with the ask byte. 0x29 (case 0x27 of the dispatch): byte 1 sets the addressee's +0x11E for the sender, byte 2 its +0x134. The regular every-fourth-turn view state goes only to remote records whose side record is human. Engine: `NetBattle::end_exchange()`, `Battle::victory_met()`; the client waits for it before the statistics. `0x41F7F0` also sends the local record's totals twice (not built: its trigger not read).
- **Probe answer 0x07** sets the sender record's +0x21 bit 0 (case 5); its reader not found (not built).
- **Serial "Connecting" stall — cause found and fixed:** the original's enumeration loops only on DPERR_CONNECTING, which the system serial provider never returns (only its TAPI path), and all its port waits time out within 5 s. The stall was the capture tool's port emulation: its `WaitCommEvent` stub slept inside the debug loop at every pass of the provider's reader thread, freezing the whole game; empty waits now become `Sleep(20)` in the game. Then the original listed our serial session. Its join came as **reliable pieces** (system message 0x30 PACKET2 data: id 16, index, size, offset, count, message size, a word, the piece at +0x30; acknowledged by 0x31 = the first 28 bytes with the command changed; the library over a provider without guaranteed delivery), which the engine now reassembles, acknowledges and handles: joined, room, ready, start (`fixtures/dplay/serial_original_join.log`); the original then exited (−1, no exception) while loading, as the tool's runs sometimes do at start-up (not resolved).

### Effect particle lists — differential-tested: 24 scenarios / 3,226 operations / 5,428 sweep steps / 1,945 spawns (46 refused by a full pool) identical; 7 predicted original crashes (zero-length flames)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/effect_lists.cpp` (`sim/effect_lists.h`), `Battle::fx_lists`; harness `tools/orig_harness/fx_harness`.
- Ten ordered lists (GS+0x38D77, 10 × {?, begin, end, cap}); emitters come from one pool of **1000** objects (`0x51E610`, `Fx_InitEventNodePool`, free stack, shared with the nano streams; none left or the blocked flag `0x51E608` → nothing spawned). A spawn sets the emitter up (which emits once), then, if the list already holds **more than 400**, deletes its oldest, then appends.
- **Sweep** (`0x471EB0`, stage 9): per list in order, per emitter: finished → deleted (order kept), else its particles step, then it emits when due.
- Kinds (vtables): **flame dots** `0x4FD588` (teleport; 1 `rand()` per emission (start frame), every 10 steps until the deadline; flight = (int16)((ftol(len)<<16)/0x50000 >> 16), 0 crashes), **flames** `0x4FD5D8` (EMIT_SFX 0/1 on list 7 with (1, 6/7); burning debris at draw time: 4 draws (jitter x/y/z by r×3/0x8000 − 1 on the whole part, duration r×3/0x8000 + 1) before the pool is asked; no draws afterwards; a zero duration divides by zero), **wakes** `0x4FD5F8` (EMIT_SFX 2-5 on list 2 (size 16/8, 4-5 reversed), 0x103 on list 7 towards the sea surface; velocity = difference × (2³² / (2·ftol(len))) >> 16; each emission (2 in all) 3 draws jittering the start ±3 whole units; a particle goes when the step passes tick + size×6 or the ground under it is not below the sea level), **smoke** `0x4FD618` (impacts with end smoke, shot trails and EMIT_SFX 0x101/0x102 (smoke 2) on list 9: one puff; muzzle smoke (start smoke) list 9: at most 3 frames, 30 steps; burning features list 5; burning wrecks (15, 900) and explosion smoke above the sea (7, 15) list 9: a puff every period for the duration), **steam** `0x4FD638` (vents at feature placement, list 4: a puff every 5 steps forever, never finished), **nano** `0x4FD5B8` (list 6, see "Construction and reclaim").
- Smoke/steam puff: frames to live = r×(frames − 2)/0x8000 + 2 (frames = the smoke sequence's count − 1, clamped for muzzle smoke); each step the hold count drops and at 0 the frame advances and the hold is redrawn: r×(hold/2)/0x8000 + hold/2 (hold 7 unless given); gone at its last frame. A smoke emitter is finished when empty and past its deadline (unsigned).
- **EMIT_SFX** (`0x480EB0`): only for units the viewed player sees; the pose is synced; from the piece's first transformed point towards its second (types without 0x100) or the piece origin (0x100 types). Engine: `Battle::emit_sfx`.
- **Draw time:** each drawn frame starts emitters for smoking (smoke, list 9) and burning (jittered flames, list 9) debris pieces (`0x421550`); engine: `BattleSettings::draw_side_effects` → `Battle::end_frame()` (off by default).
- **Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (game flow; name + plate comment; smoke-verified; the emitter methods were created): `0x437DE0` `Meteor_TickShower`, `0x41C640` `Camera_AccumulateShake`, `0x41C6F0` `Camera_ApplyShakeDecay`, `0x471EB0` `Fx_SweepEffectLists`, spawners `0x471FD0` `Fx_SpawnFlameDots`, `0x472330` `Fx_SpawnFlames`, `0x472AB0` `Fx_SpawnFlamesJittered`, `0x472430` `Fx_SpawnWakeRising`, `0x472530` `Fx_SpawnWakeFalling`, `0x472630` `Fx_SpawnSmokeEmitter`, `0x472810` `Fx_SpawnSmoke1`, `0x4728F0` `Fx_SpawnSmoke2`, `0x4729D0` `Fx_SpawnMuzzleSmoke`, `0x472C50` `Fx_SpawnVentSteam`; emitter methods `FxFlameDots_*` (`0x4736E0` setup, `0x472D50` step, `0x472E70` done, `0x4737C0` emit), `FxFlames_*` (`0x4742C0`, `0x473010`, `0x473130`, `0x4743A0`), `FxWake_*` (`0x474760`, `0x473170`, `0x473290`, `0x474880`), `FxSmoke_*` (`0x474D50`, `0x475340`, `0x474F80`, `0x474DF0`), `FxSteam_*` (`0x475150`, `0x475600`, `0x4751C0`); `0x48CF30` `Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected`. Read and implemented but not run in a harness (named only here): `0x497180` battle init, `0x4917D0` map load, `0x464990`/`0x464700` player set-up, `0x496EE0` commander spawn, `0x465E30` start amounts, `0x436860` schema choice, `0x437320` start position lookup, `0x437D60`/`0x437CD0`/`0x438320` meteor configuration, `0x490360`/`0x490230`/`0x490080` victory and defeat, `0x486F10` team kill, `0x43AFC0` issue-or-cancel, `0x480460` group orders, `0x480EB0` EMIT_SFX, `0x453D40` network input.

### Performance with many units (2026-10-17) — every change keeps every result bit for bit
Where the time went with 200-400 units moving (sampled by suspending the main thread and reading its instruction pointer, on `ta_play --headless` and `move_bench`), and what changed. No order of work, random draw or result changed.
- **Drawing: point rotations.** A unit's drawn pose turns every vertex of every piece through `sim::x87::rotate_point_2d`, whose 53-bit product rounding walked the product bit by bit and whose integer store used `fmod`: 20 ms of a frame with 240 units on screen. The rounding now works on whole 64-bit words (leading-zero count, shifts, masks) and the store's tie to even uses the integer's parity. Old against new routines: 44 million inputs (every angle × 64 points, random extended values, store inputs with ties and range edges), 0 differences, 32- and 64-bit builds.
- **Drawing: caches by unit id** (presentation only: `render::PoseDrawCache`, `render::UnitSpriteCache`, owned by `ta::ClientWorld`, optional in `BattleScene`). The drawn pose is a function of the pose state and the attitude only: when the whole pose state (every piece field and point, dirty flag, base angles, draw count, model, fault) and the attitude equal those of the last drawing, the last drawn pose is used. The plain sprite (cached pieces) is a function of the drawn pose, model, team colour, type flags, structure and depth flags, build fraction (compared bitwise), visual options and tables; it is kept while all of them are unchanged (the pose by the cache's version number). The texture cursor is not an input of the cached pieces. Idle units are drawn from both caches.
- **GPU backend.** One-frame sprites go into pooled textures of power-of-two sizes (the blit reads only the sprite's own texels), so units turning and selection outlines no longer create a texture for every new sprite size.
- **Simulation: exact x87 arithmetic.** The FPATAN emulation (`fpatan`, reached through `crt_acos` by the ballistic pitch solver) and the 64-bit square root of the C runtime hypot were most of a step with 400 units fighting. The multi-precision series multiplies on 64-bit limbs where a 128-bit product exists (stack arrays, zero limbs skipped; the 32-bit fallback is the old schoolbook on a stack array) and divides each term once by the product of its two small divisors (the same truncated quotient); FPATAN keeps its last 1,024 results by argument bits; the square root starts from a double estimate (off by far less than 2^18), finishes bit by bit and is checked against the definition (the digit-by-digit root remains the fallback). Old against new: 3.3 million fpatan / acos / hypot / sqrt / atan2 inputs plus every angle's sincos model, 0 differences (64-bit; 1 million on the 32-bit build).
- **Simulation: sight sync.** The players stage synced the sight system (a copy of every type's sight data) before each unit's sight update; a sight update changes nothing the sync copies, so it syncs once per player, before its first live unit.
- **Not bottlenecks:** the route search (its budget, above in "Route search"), the client's linear loops over the unit table (HUD, selection), motion and targeting apart from the arithmetic above.
- **Measured** (this machine, 640×480, headless; frame parts as `CLIENT_PROFILE` prints them; before → after):
  - 240 own units on screen, all selected and ordered across the map (frames 600-899): simulation 0.75 → 0.29 ms, draw list 25.2 → 1.35 ms, CPU raster 0.82 → 0.82 ms, GPU 13-19 → 7.4 ms; the 60 frames after the order: draw list 30.6 → 4.2 ms, GPU 55 → 18 ms.
  - 240 own + 160 enemy units meeting (frames 480-1439): simulation 0.95-3.0 → 0.31-0.93 ms, draw list 18.4-22.3 → 1.2-1.6 ms, GPU 12.5-15.3 → 5.6-6.0 ms.
  - `move_bench <game> 200 1400` (400 units, no drawing): 4.42 → 1.26 ms per step on average; the worst 100-step block 7.66 → 2.17 ms; the worst single step 10.7 → 4.2 ms.
- **Identity:** `move_bench` state checksums at every 100 steps identical before and after (400 units, 1,400 steps); whole-game replays g8, s2, g9 identical for all 3,000 / 4,500 / 2,759 steps; `game_flow 300 1` output identical to the build before; sim_tests, render_tests pass; `ta_play` frames of a 400-unit battle byte-identical to the build before (6 frames, also the final unit list), GPU = CPU on each; harness smoke runs after the changes: x87_check (rotation 524,288 / atan2_scaled 4,000,150 / crt_hypot 1,000,000 calls, 0 mismatches), route_harness 10 × 40, motion_harness 10 × 30, pose_harness 278 units × 50 operations, unitloop_harness 10 × 30, orders_harness 10 × 30, targeting_harness 10 × 40: 0 mismatching scenarios each (orders_harness's one engine fault is there before the changes too); frame_harness 8 scenes seed 7 with `--no-fog`: 8 of 8 identical (0 pixels), and without it the same 6 identical / 2 different as the build before (its engine frames byte-identical to that build's).
- **Tool:** `move_bench <game> [units per player] [steps] [map]`: two blocks of units (ARM kbots and tanks, CORE ones) ordered across the map so they meet; prints each 100 steps' mean step time and the state checksum.

### Simulation completeness — every hook classified (2026-10-15)
**Built in this pass** (each checked against the original as noted):
- **Spatial bucket heights** (`Map_BuildDerivedLayers` `0x482C20`, the bucket part): a bucket's ground = max(sea level, the `height_max` of its 8 × 8 cells); its record byte 1 ("top") = the highest ground over the pairs it belongs to along its row, then the same over those along its column (the last bucket of a row or column keeps only its own pair; a grid one bucket wide or high gets 0); the off-map record is all zero. Aircraft cruise at cruise altitude + top (`FlightGoal`, point goals without altitude). `sim::build_bucket_heights` from `Battle::init_buckets`. **los_harness smoke 24 × 20: 1,373 buckets + the off-map record identical** (random cell tops).
- **Teleport trail**: `fx_dots(unit position → destination, 30, list 5)` (`0x406AA0` → `0x471FD0`, decompile checked).
- **Nano streams and the shared effect pool** (`0x4720D0` / `0x472200` take from `0x51E610` like the effect lists).
- **Out-of-units message**: one `rand()` (skirmish `0x47BD70`; network `0x450380` while the local player is present).
- **Kill team's stale word** (`0x486F10`): see "Stale stack word".
- **Link release at death** (`0x489740`): now run for real in orders, combat, weapons and projectile harnesses (smokes 24 × 40 / 20 × 30 ×3: 0 differences).
- **Air base search** (`0x40B530`): ai_harness op (smoke 24 × 60: 49 searches, 0 differences); the unused duplicate in ai.cpp removed.
- **Gravity** (`Map_LoadTnt` `0x483610`): GS+0x14263, read by projectiles, ballistic aim, debris, features and the aircraft lean, is not the map's number: for TA (0x2000) maps the map info's GlobalHeader `gravity` (an int read by `0x435DA0`: 0 when the key is missing, the map info holds −1 before parsing) when ≥ 0, for 0x1020 maps the header's word 13; then ftol(g × 65536.0 × (1/900)) (two double multiplies, truncation); with neither (or 0 from a 0x1020 header), 0x1FDB. The loader used 112 for every map. AirStrike's drop lead alone reads the raw map info value (+0xD3C; 0 → the order ends): `BattleSettings::map_gravity`. **Checked against a captured game:** s2 (an ARMATLAS flies; Moon Quartet `gravity=60` → 4369) replays identical for all 4,500 steps; before, the lean's pitch differed at step 1529 (and, without the bucket heights, its height too).
- **Map info switches** (`0x435DA0`, GlobalHeader ints, 0 when missing): `lavaworld` (+0xD44: splashes, debris, map edges, deathmatch respawn, the weapons' lava explosion art), `nosealeveltrigger` (+0xD48: shots and debris ignore the sea), `waterdoesdamage` / `waterdamage` (+0xD4C / +0xD50: the unit pass's water damage, both nonzero). The loader never set them.
- **Provider**: the reclaim announcement (provider 3) and the computer player's downloadable-type rule (provider 1) read `BattleSettings::provider` (the duplicate `network_provider` was never set).

**`BattleOutside`, every virtual** (S = must stay outside because it is an input, P = presentation, D = no longer called by the battle):
- D: `structure_fits`, `unfinished_decay`, `ai_own_unit`, `ai_rescore`, `ai_controller_step`, `run_mission` (every kind is built), `attach_unit`, `drop_unit`, `detach_from_carrier`, `bucket_height` (now built).
- S (inputs): `clock_ticks` (hover bob, wall clock), `ai_site_height_word`, `reclaim_site_y` (stale stack words), `unit_visible` / `position_explored` / `update_sight` / `stamp_line_of_sight` only before `init_sight()` (the built sight system answers afterwards), `feature_flags` only for ids past the feature table (the original reads past it).
- P: `on_activate_flags_changed`, `speech`, `under_attack_notice` (the client's speech queue draws the battle's `rand()` once per played notice and per frame — frame-driven, see below), `display_changed`, `build_counts_changed`, `nano_beam`, `forget_speech`, `leave_group` (after the group change, which is built), `stats_activity`, `kill_counted`, `stockpile_used` (HUD), `camera_follow_step`, `play_sound`, `spawn_smoke` / `spawn_muzzle_smoke` / `feature_smoke` / `feature_steam` / `wreck_fire` / `explosion_smoke` (each after the simulated emitter), `camera_shake` (after the simulated shake), `feature_fire_sound`, `builder_menu`, `build_menu_refresh`, `orders_panel_changed`, `selection_changed`, `mission_goal_met`, `teleport_trail` (after the simulated dots), `release_unit` (after the built release), `emit_sfx` (after the built part), `reveal_dead_unit` / `forget_fog` (after the built part), `victory_unit_*`, `player_out_of_units` (after the `rand()` draw), `commander_died` (the menus; the team kill is built), `player_stage_rest` (minimap composite `0x466DC0`), `player_victory_turn`, `player_sensor_turn`, `players_stage_end`.
- Network (the network work's): `network_send`, `network_input`, `network_flush`, `broadcast_*`, `feature_packet`, `resources_shared`, `explored_map_shared`; set-up `ai_player_setup` / `ai_battle_ready` are replay hooks.

**Every C runtime `rand()` caller** (55 call sites of `0x4E4870`): simulated — wind, meteors, camera shake decay, the feature step (burning, reproduction), the effect lists' emitters and particles (incl. `0x474B00`), the nano streams, the defeat check's forced step (`0x490360`), the out-of-units messages; loading thread — battle init (`0x497180`), the explosion tables (`0x420D20`); frame-driven (inputs, as the original's own stream depends on the frame rate) — the speech queue (`0x47FD70`: one draw per played or dropped notice; modelled by the client's `GameClient::speech_*`, not by `Battle`), the CD audio (`0x4CDB40`), lightning drawing (`Gfx_DrawProjectiles`: three draws per segment of a visible lightning shot — camera-dependent, not modelled), the debug overlay (`0x418310`); menus and network (`0x425B80`, `0x478E80`, `0x4787xx`, `0x4568C0`, `Condenser_WrapAndSend` debug loss rate 0).

**Remaining, with reasons:** the lightning draws and the speech queue are frame/camera dependent (inputs; the client models the speech queue and, since 2026-10-20, feeds its renderer's lightning draws and drawn units' pose syncs back to the battle); `0x4861D0`'s and `0x452CC0`'s stale words are network-handler stack (inputs, network work); route goal flag bits 2–7 come from fresh heap memory (nothing reads them); nothing else in the simulation is left on a hook.

### Fixed in whole-battle runs
Found by running `battle_view --battle` (with and without `--ai 2`), `game_flow` and the client headless for thousands of steps; each fix is checked against the original as noted. `Battle::first_fault()` gives the first fault any system recorded; battle_view prints it every frame, ta_play at the end, and game_flow fails on it.
- **Route search dead from step 0** (2026-10-14): `ta::load_battle` and the client set-up never filled `Battle::feature_blocks`, so the passability build saw every part cell of a multi-cell feature pointing past the (empty) table and faulted; a faulted search stops serving units. Symptom: computer players' commanders waiting forever on MobileBuild step 1 (wait 0xE0, route goal flags 0xB, search tick 0). Fix: `ta::bind_feature_blocks` (flag 0x40, the bit `0x47DE60` reads). The route search itself was already verified (route_harness, smoke again 0 differences).
- **Links to dead units never released** (2026-10-14): the kill packet's teardown `0x489740` was an unimplemented outside hook. Orders aimed at a unit that died kept their link and never heard event 8; an AirStrike went on to build a follow goal on the empty slot (motion fault, all motion stopped). Fix: `sim::release_referrers`; orders_harness runs the original routine for real (smoke 24 × 40, 89 kills, 0 differences). Named in `/TotalA.exe`: `0x489740` `Unit_ReleaseReferrers` (plate comment, saved).
- **Patrol hang** (game_flow): not reproducible any more (see "Network and sync").
- **Aircraft cruised above height 0** (2026-10-15): the cruise height reads the unit's spatial bucket record (byte 1), which `BattleOutside::bucket_height` answered with 0 everywhere. Now `sim::build_bucket_heights` (map load `0x482C20`), compared by los_harness (see "Simulation completeness").
- Smoke after the fixes: battle_view `--ai 2` 18,000 steps (both players build up to 64 units, no faults), the battle scene 3,000 steps, the client headless 6,000 frames, game_flow 8 seeds with patrols, sim_tests, orders/motion/route/combat harness smokes: 0 differences.
### Whole-game replay — a captured skirmish of the original replayed in the engine, the whole state compared after every step: 4 passive games identical to the end of their capture (400, 496, 2,759 and 3,000 steps; one with 5 interface orders identical for 2,220 steps)
**Tools.** `ta_compat/tools/capture` (`ta_capture --game [--auto-skirmish] [--rng-calls] --max-ticks N`, 32-bit, runs the windowed copy `OTA 31 Capture` under the debug API) writes the capture; `ta_compat/tools/game_replay.cpp` (`game_replay <game_dir> <capture.jsonl> [--steps N] [--continue] [--trace-unit ID] [--ignore-cells] [--no-draw-effects] [--verbose]`) rebuilds the game with `ta::load_battle` and compares. Run unattended:
```
ta_capture --exe "...\OTA 31 Capture\TotalA.exe" --cwd "...\OTA 31 Capture" --out g.jsonl --max-ticks 3000 --game --auto-skirmish --rng-calls
build\game_replay.exe "...\OTA 31 Capture" g.jsonl
```
`--auto-skirmish` makes the DirectX version check (`0x4B5070`, tested at `0x4266A5`) succeed so its notice never shows (start-up interface only), then posts the S quick key every 3 s to the game's visible windows until the battle seed is drawn (main menu S → single player S = skirmish → skirmish S = start, with the skirmish settings the registry kept). Only one TotalA can run: a second instance exits at once with code −1 (another agent's capture blocks yours).

**Capture format** (JSON lines; with `--game` the per-draw `rand` breakpoint is armed only from the first step):
- `battle_seed` (the value passed to the seeding routine), `srand`/`thread_td` per thread, `map` (`Map_LoadTnt` `0x483632`: EAX = the TNT path), `shuffle_rand` (loading thread's `rand()` state at each start-position shuffle draw, calls `0x4979E7`/`0x497A32`; the first one is `BattleStart::loading_rand_seed`; none → fixed locations).
- `start` at the first step's breakpoint: game state and unit table addresses (→ `settings.addresses`), local player/view (GS+0x2A42/0x2A43), map flags (GS+0x14281: bit 0 mapping, 1 line of sight, 2 LOS type), unit limit (GS+0x37EE6), AI difficulty (GS+0x37EEE), game mode (GS+0x37EF6), the simulation thread's `rand()` state (`crt_rand_state`), and per player record (GS+0x1B63 + 0x14B·p): active, controller (+0x73), net id, state (+0x146), unit range, stored energy/metal and storage (+0x8C/+0x98/+0xA4/+0xA8), the commander's unit name (its side), the alliance bytes (+0x108). Unused skirmish slots are **active with controller 0** (they take no part).
- `route_state`: the route search's scheduling state after the battle start (GS+0x2A3C player count, GS+0x1434F units per player, refresh counter `0x511A38`, search object GS+0x14207: player +0x78, cursors +0x79, credits +0xA1, served unit +0x58; scales `0x5119E8`, turns `0x511A10`).
- `cells`: every map cell with a unit, feature, flags or metal at the start (unit, unit2, feature, aux, flags, metal).
- `step` (`0x4954C6`, after the tick increment: generators at the start of the step), `digest` (`0x49566C`, the end of the step), `frame` (`0x495674`: after the frame's steps, also with none; generators, viewed player, map flags).
- `digest`: tick, `rand()` and battle generator states, wind heading/speed (GS+0x37ED8/0x37EDA), per player [slot, energy, metal, energy storage, metal storage] (float bits), per unit with a type index (unit +0xA6 ≠ 0; empty entries keep a pointer to definition 0) [id, type, owner slot +0xFF, x, y, z, pitch, heading, bank, hp +0x108, build fraction left bits +0x104, activate +0x10E, script flags +0x10F, flags +0x110, health % +0xF6, kills +0xB8, reload of the three slots (+0x18/+0x34/+0x50), primary order kinds (list +0x5C, kind +4, next +0x4A), secondary kinds (+0x60), motion [speed +0x20, turn +0x24, flags +0x2E & 7, goal vtable, route count +0x5C, search tick +0x60, route flags +0x64 & 15, points +0xC…]], per projectile slot (table GS+0x141F7, 300 × 0x6B) [slot, weapon index, pos, velocity, owner id, target id], FNV-1a hashes of the cells' feature bytes (+8..+12) and occupancy (+0..+3).
- `order`: every call of `Unit_EnqueueOrder` `0x43ADC0`, the interface's per-unit order `0x43AFC0`, `Unit_AdjustBuildCount` `0x43B0B0`, the build menu command `0x419B00` and `Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected` `0x48CF30`, with tick, return address, arguments (units as ids) and whether it ran inside a step (`in_step` 0 = the interface, between steps, applies before step tick + 1).
- `stale`: stack words the original reads uninitialised, per call: `ai_site_y` (`Ai_RoleConstructorsTick` `0x408100`: the site's height = [entry ESP − 0x2C]) and `reclaim_site_y` (`Map_PickReclaimSites` `0x47EA40`: [entry ESP − 8]).

**Replay.** Players from `start` (controller 1 local, 2 computer; side from the commander's name in `sidedata.tdf`; start amounts; alliances), options from the map flags, the seeds as above, `settings.addresses` from the capture, `local_view`, `draw_side_effects` on.
