The stale words are fed per step in call order through `BattleOutside::ai_site_height_word(player, fallback)` and `reclaim_site_y(fallback)` (new input hooks; an engine call the original did not make is reported). Interface orders (`issue_or_cancel`, `build_count` with `in_step` 0) go through `sim::command::issue_or_cancel` / `Battle::order_build_count` before the next step. At each `frame`: `end_frame()`, the viewed player, and a map-flag change rebuilds sight. **Between frames the original draws `rand()` outside the simulation** (lightning drawing, the CD audio sync, the interface): at a frame's first step the engine's state is set to the captured one and the draws are counted (1 in 3,000 steps of a passive game). Compared after every step: everything in the digest, except unit flag bits only the interface writes (0x10, 0x40, 0x80, 0x3C00000) and route flag bit 2 (below).

**Found and fixed with it** (each was the first divergence of a capture):
1. **Map edge marker** (`0x4833B0`, called at the end of `Map_LoadTnt`): cells outside the visible area get feature 0xFFFD (blocks movement) where they hold no feature (0xFFFF) or a part (0xFFFE): the last two columns of every row; per column from the top while z·16 − height/2 < 0; per column from the last row upwards while z·16 − height(z)/2 > visible height (cells·16 − 128) the cell **above** (so the last row is never marked); on lava maps (map info +0xD44) every cell whose lowest corner ≤ sea level. Engine `sim::mark_map_edges`, called by the loader and the client set-up. Moon Quartet: 6,096 cells.
2. **Feature blocking was never wired**: `Battle::feature_blocks` stayed empty, so every feature blocked and every feature part raised a route-search fault — and with a fault set, `run_route_search` did one unit of work per step (its loop returns while a fault is pending): the original served the first build order's route at step 91, the engine never. The loaders now fill it from the feature types' flag bit 0x40; the targeting's feature flag byte (0x80 reclaimable) comes from the battle's feature types instead of `BattleOutside::feature_flags`.
3. Harness plumbing: unused skirmish slots, side lookup, start-position shuffle state, empty unit entries.

**Not compared:** route flag bit 2 — the route goal's constructor keeps bits 2–7 of its flag byte from fresh heap memory (here set for the CORE commanders, clear for ARM); nothing reads it. Motion goals other than route goals (flight etc.) only by speed/turn/flags.

**Results (smoke):** Moon Quartet, local ARM player passive, 3 hard computer players (ARM, CORE, CORE), 1000/1000, line of sight + true sight: captures of 400, 496, 2,759 and 3,000 steps (the 496- and 2,759-step ones ended by the process exiting with code 0 from outside — another tool's clean-up or the window closed — not a crash): **identical at every step**, generators included. A 3,000-step capture whose window received a player's right clicks (5 move orders and a solar collector build, 17 interface order calls) stayed identical for 2,220 steps; at 2,220 every computer-player unit gained flag 0x100 (radar mark set by the sensor pass, step 5 "the viewed player sees its block") in the original only — most likely a line-of-sight option toggled from the keyboard, which that capture did not record yet (map flags are now captured per frame and replayed); not reproduced since.

**Observed inputs:** the constructor role's site height word was, per computer player in slot order, 0 for the first and the **previous computer player's record address** (GS + 0x1B63 + 0x14B·(p − 1)) for the others, at every call (ticks 0, 90, 180, …) — left by the previous player's iteration of the players' stage; the replay feeds it from the capture. The battle generator and both `rand()` streams need no other input in these games.

**Open:** the air repair patrol's site address (`VTOL_RepairPatrol` `0x4152F0` stack record; not captured — no aircraft in these games), the hover bob clock (`clock_ticks`), the loading thread's `rand()` before the shuffle (vent steam at map load: none on Moon Quartet), network games, campaign starts, scripted input for the local player (the capture records interface orders; `--auto-skirmish` only starts the game), games long enough for combat (the original's computer players built 15 units in 3,000 steps), the `0x48CF30` selection path (only its per-unit calls are replayed).

### Movies — decoder checked against the game's own movie library (movie_harness: all five movies, 11,407 frames and 16.8 MB of track audio identical)
**Files and when.** `Data\1.zrb` … `5.zrb`, loose files beside the game (the path builder `0x41D7B0` makes "<CD>\Data\<n>.zrb"; on the Steam version the CD check `0x41D6A0` answers '.' at once, `0x50289C` = 1, so the game folder is the CD). `0x426780` plays one when the file exists. The front-end state machine (`0x426E80`): state 0 at start (only when the display is full screen, display flag 2): registry `PlayMovie` (default 1) set → 1.zrb, then state 1 (2.zrb), `PlayMovie` written 0 and the settings saved; else 1.zrb unless a lobby switch (`-c` / `-n`, GS+0x39245) started the program; then the main menu. Main menu INTRO (`0x425D80`, "smlButton"; full screen, else the message "Debug:  You must be in full-screen mode to play a movie."; then the CD check): state 1 = 2.zrb, with Shift held GS+0x39241 = 1 makes it repeat until a key. Credits (an invisible 80 × 40 button at 280, 440): state 3 = 5.zrb. A campaign's last mission won (end state 5, full screen): state 4 (Arm: 3.zrb then 5.zrb) or 5 (Core: 4.zrb then 5.zrb), by the local record's side byte +0x95; windowed: straight to the main menu.
**Player** (`0x47BDF0` open with tracks 1–7 and the extra buffer, flags 0xFE100; `0x47C6C0` pump; `0x47C3A0` frame): every sound stops first (`0x47F750`), the screen is filled with 0 and the pointer hidden; each frame: the palette when the frame has one (the library's 8-bit values straight to the display), `SmackToBuffer` at x 0, y (480 − height) / 2 with the library's height (480 for the interlaced 640 × 240 files: lines on every other row, the rows between stay black; 5.zrb is 640 × 304 at y 88), `SmackDoFrame`; it stops once the last frame is shown. Messages while it plays: a character message (WM_CHAR) ends it and the repeat; Alt+F4 (WM_SYSKEYDOWN F4) ends the program (PostQuitMessage); mouse buttons do nothing. After it the key queue is emptied. Sound through the game's DirectSound object at the buffer's full volume (the wave output volume — fxvol — scales it).
**Format (Smacker 2)**, as decoded (`client::SmackerFile`, `client/smacker.h` gives the full description): header "SMK2", width, height, frames, rate (> 0 ms, < 0 units of 10 µs: −3333 = 30 frames a second), flags (1 ring frame, 2 interlaced, 4 doubled), 7 audio sizes, tree block size, 4 tree sizes, 7 audio descriptions (bit 31 compressed, 30 present, 29 16-bit, 28 stereo, 24-bit rate), spare; frame sizes (low 2 bits flags), frame types (bit 0 palette, bits 1–7 tracks), the four Huffman trees (block map, block colours, full blocks, block types; each 16-bit tree built from a low and a high byte tree, with three escape codes standing for the three most recent values — cleared each frame, shifted when a new value differs from the latest), then frames: palette runs (skip / copy from the old palette / three 6-bit components widened `(c << 2) | (c >> 4)`), audio chunks (compressed: unpacked size, then DPCM with per-channel byte trees, first samples stored whole), the picture as 4 × 4 blocks (two-colour pattern, full, unchanged, filled; runs of 1–59, 128 … 2048). All five game movies: 640 × 240 interlaced (1–4) or 640 × 304 (5), 30 frames a second, track 1 22050 Hz 8-bit stereo DPCM.
**Engine:** `client/smacker.h` (decoder), `client/movie.h` (`MovieScreen`: the player's rules above; sound on the narration channel), the importer's choices in `ta_compat/src/client_screens.cpp` (start-up, INTRO, Credits; `MenuArt::movie`, `MenuArt::full_screen`: the window front end stands for the full screen, headless runs play none unless `ta_play --movies`). **Checked:** `tools/orig_harness/movie_harness` loads `smackw32.dll` (its sound, window and dialog imports stubbed) and compares each frame in the player's 640 × 480 placement, the palette and `SmackGetTrackData` of track 1 with the engine's: 1.zrb 599, 2.zrb 4,058, 3.zrb 889, 4.zrb 1,841, 5.zrb 4,020 frames, 0 differences.

### Campaign — goals smoke-verified (goal_harness: 24 scenarios / 4,800 operations identical), placed units' orders smoke-verified (order_harness: 24 scenarios / 960 strings / 3,531 orders / 68 attaches identical); the rest read and implemented
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/mission_goals.cpp` (`sim/mission_goals.h`), the provider-1 path of `sim::start_battle` (`sim/battle_start.h` `MissionStart`), `Battle::victory_turn` (provider 1); importer `ta_compat/src/campaign.cpp`, `mission_text.cpp` (`ta/campaign.h`); front end `ta_compat/src/client_campaign.cpp` (`ta/client_campaign.h`); tool `campaign_check`.
**Files.** `camps\<name>.tdf`: [HEADER] campaignside; [MISSIONn] (n = 0.. until missing) missionfile, missionname (plus language-prefixed variants). The campaign list is `camps\*.tdf` in enumeration order. A mission's map info is `Maps\<missionfile>` (an OTA); its map `Maps\<base>.TNT`. Briefing `camps\briefs\<brief>.txt`, narration and glamour sound `camps\briefs\<name>.wav`, hint `camps\hints\<name>.txt`, unit list `camps\useonly\<UseOnlyUnits>.tdf` (one section per allowed unit name). On the Steam install the original campaign's maps (`AC01`-`AC25`, `CC01`-`CC25` except `AC03`) are in `totala4.hpi`, which the start-up scan's ten-archive limit leaves unmounted; the rescan the original runs when New Campaign is pressed mounts it (see "File system and archives"). `ta::mount_cd_archives` is that rescan (`Vfs::scan`).
**Mission header** (`Mission_LoadOtaHeader` `0x435DA0`, provider 1, from [GlobalHeader]): maxunits (default 200; the unit limit), missionname, brief, narration, missionhint, glamour, glamoursound, UseOnlyUnits, mapping, lineofsight (the battle's mapping and line-of-sight options; the line-of-sight type is always the true one and commander death always off), memory, numplayers, Planet, nomovie, missiondescription (default "No description available"), wind, gravity, tide, lava, sea-level trigger, water damage, killmul, timemul, then the goal keys (below). **Schema** (`0x436860`): by difficulty the type preference is Easy, Medium, Hard (easy) / Medium, Easy, Hard (medium) / Hard, Medium, Easy (hard); the first "Schema N" (N from 0 until missing) whose Type matches the first preferred type wins, else the next type. From the schema: HumanMetal/HumanEnergy, ComputerMetal/ComputerEnergy (integers stored as floats), SurfaceMetal, aiprofile, the meteor keys, and (`0x436C30`) the placed units, specials and features.
**Placed units** (schema [units], children in order): Unitname, Ident, InitialMission (strings, 1023 characters at most), XPos/YPos/ZPos (whole units, shifted to 16.16), Angle (degrees; facing = (angle << 16, 32-bit) / 360 truncated), Player (1-based; 0 reads as 1), HealthPercentage (default 100), BuildPriority, CreationCountdown, MissionCriticalUnit, AiIgnore, AiPriorityTarget, InitialGroup, Immunity (bit 0 → unit flag 0x8000). Kills is not read.
**Battle start, provider 1** (`0x497180`): seeds as for every provider; options from the mission; the unit list (`0x431740`): when the useonly file loads, every type loses its "can be made" flag (definition word bit 23, `create_flags::kLoadable`) and the listed ones get it back — types not listed cannot be created at all (factories cannot build them, unlisted placed units are not placed). Map load, player set-up (two players: slot 0 the human on the campaign's side, slot 1 a computer player on the other side, colour 1). No start positions or commanders. After the line-of-sight rebuild the placed units (`0x488310`): each entry whose type exists — its player must be playing, else the original shows "Player number %d invalid" and exits — snaps a structure's position to its footprint, is made fully built on layer 1, then immunity, hit points = (max hit points × percentage, as unsigned) / 100, facing; after all entries, each placed unit with an InitialMission string gets its orders (below). No entries at all: the goals are switched off (the mission never ends by them). Then the camera goes to StartPos1 (presentation), the players' stage at step 0 and the start amounts (`0x465E30`): for each of the ten records energy/metal = the schema's (player 0 human, 1 computer, the others the zero fields after them), and the storage bonus = those truncated (at least 200, `0x496E90`).
**First orders** (`Mission_ParseInitialOrders` `0x487BF0`): tokens separated by commas (leading white space skipped); by the first letter (either case): **A** "x z" two numbers → attack intent at the point; else a unit name → attack-unit-type of that type (if known). **B** "name count x z" → structures queue `count` of the type (BUILDINGBUILD), mobile units build it at the point (MOBILEBUILD); **BW** "count" → BUILDWEAPON (no position). **D** → SELFDESTRUCTFG (parameter 1). **G** name → guard intent on that placed unit. **I** name → the unit is put into that transport (attach, piece 0xFF, layer 0). **M** / **U** "x z" → move / unload intent. **O** "m f" → move and fire standing orders (missing values keep the unit's). **P** "x z t" → patrol intent with parameter t × 30. **S** → MAKESELECTABLE. **WA** [name] → WAITFORATTACK on that unit (no name read: the unit itself). **W** "t n" → WAIT (t × 30, n). Positions are floats × 65536 truncated; y = 0. Every order is queued. Unit names match a placed unit's Ident, else its Unitname (case-insensitive, first in placement order, only units that were placed); a name that fails to parse leaves the token's own text as the name. Any order but BW leaves the unit unselectable (flag 0x20 cleared) and, unless A-at-a-point, D, P or S was among them, a closing MAKESELECTABLE is queued. **Stale values:** the two position floats are stack slots shared by all tokens and all units (a token whose numbers do not parse uses the previous ones); the importer keeps them across the units in placement order (`MissionParseState`; their value before the first parse is an input).
**Goals** (`VictoryConditions_LoadFromTdf` `0x48E010`, keys of [GlobalHeader]; at most 16 win and 16 loss goals, in this order): win — KillEnemyCommander, DestroyAllUnits, KillAllMobileUnits, BuildUnitType (name), CaptureUnitType (name), KillAllOfType (name), KillUnitType ("NAME,count"), MoveUnitToRadius ("NAME,x,y,radius"; ANYTYPE = any), UnitTypePassesX / UnitTypePassesZ ("NAME,v": column / row v >> 4), VictoryTimerRunsOut (seconds); loss — CommanderKilled, AllUnitsKilled, AllUnitsKilledOfType (name), UnitTypeKilled ("NAME,count"), DeathTimerRunsOut, AnyUnitPassesX / AnyUnitPassesZ (v >> 4). No win goal: DestroyAllUnits; no loss goal: AllUnitsKilled. The "NAME,..." keys are read with the runtime's scanner (letters only, then comma-separated integers in any base); a part that does not parse keeps what the parser's stack slots held — values left there by earlier keys, including heap addresses of earlier goal records (inputs, `MissionParseState`).
**Goal behaviour** (slot 0 = the player, slot 1 = the opponent; "walk" = a player's table entries with a type, in order, stopping when the filter says so): each goal has satisfied and cue flags; meeting it the first time asks for the "Victory_Condition" sound (presentation, `BattleOutside::mission_goal_met`). Polled: DestroyAllUnits (opponent's live count 0; flag not set), BuildUnitType (resolves the type once; a finished unit of it), MoveUnitToRadius (first check converts (x, y) with `Map_EditorPosToTerrain` `0x484B50`; then any usable player unit of the type in the circle, `0x47E890`), UnitTypeNearX/Z (any player unit of the type with |cell − line| < 3 — no other test), timers (tick ≥ deadline), AllUnitsKilled (set, then any usable player unit clears it), AnyUnitNearX/Z (an opponent unit within 2 cells of the line). Events (`0x4904C0` destroyed, `0x490520` transferred; every win goal then every loss goal; creation has no handler): KillEnemyCommander (opponent unit named as its owner side's commander), KillAllMobileUnits (opponent mobile unit; count mobile units, stopping at 2; fewer than 2), KillAllOfType (opponent unit of the type; same count), KillUnitType (count left > 0, opponent unit of the type: −1, below 1 met), CaptureUnitType (opponent unit of the type changes hands — called before the owner changes), CommanderKilled (player's commander), UnitTypeKilled (any owner; −1 without a lower guard), AllUnitsKilledOfType (any owner; counts the player's then the opponent's table, each walk stopping after reaching 2). "Usable" = selectable (0x20), finished, no hand-over hold-off, not carried or carried by a carrier with flag 0x40000000.
**Judging** (the local player's turn of the players' stage, `0x464F80`): when every win goal holds (in order, stopping at the first that does not; inactive goals: never) the end countdown runs as a win; otherwise, with active goals, the copy-protection draw (as in skirmish), then any loss goal (in order, stopping at the first that holds) runs it as a loss (end flags |4, &~0x10, |0x40).
**Editor position to terrain** (`0x484B50`): x and y clamped to the map (world units); from row (y & ~15) + 128 upwards in steps of 16 (at most nine), the terrain's screen row = z − height/2 (height = sample, at least the sea level); the first at or above y, then linear interpolation towards the point 16 lower; the y of the result is the terrain there. The two rows equal → division by zero (engine: `MissionGoals::faults`).
**Progression and front end:** a won mission sets the campaign's progress (missions completed) and opens the next mission's briefing; the menu offers the missions reached. The engine keeps progress in the front end's settings (key "Campaign <name>"); the original keeps a progress string in the registry (`Prefs_SaveCampProgressString` `0x432B00`, not reverse engineered). Briefing art, glamour and the planet animation are presentation (not built: the briefing screen shows the text and plays the narration).
**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/goal_harness` (the original's goal objects with their real vtables on a synthetic unit table; the sound stubbed; plus `0x484B50` on random heightmaps): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 4,800 operations: 2,385 checks (1,422 met), 930 destroyed and 490 transfer events, 995 conversions, all identical.** `tools/orig_harness/order_harness` (`0x487BF0` with the runtime's conversions and the real unit-name and type lookups; intent resolution, order call and transport attach logging stubs): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 960 random strings (well-formed and malformed tokens of every letter) / 3,531 orders / 68 attaches, all identical.** `campaign_check` (smoke): all 14 campaigns list; AC01-AC03, CC01-CC02 load, place their units (e.g. AC01 15/19, AC03 13/79), give their orders and run 300-600 steps. Not differential-tested: the header and goal-key parsing (`0x435DA0`, `0x48E010`, `0x436C30`), the unit list, the start amounts and the provider-1 order of the battle start (read).
**Campaign front end (2026-10-20; checked against the original's own screens with gui_harness, the rest read):**
- *New campaign* (NEWGAME.GUI, set-up `0x478240`, handler `0x477AB0`): background newcampaign4, newcampaign4x (two campaigns or fewer) or playanygame4 (the any-mission form); the two side pictures and buttons; the campaign list holds the `camps\*.tdf` whose [HEADER] campaignside is the chosen side (`0x476A60`); the any-mission form adds the Missions list (the Campaign list moved to y 308, 48 high; Missions 62 high); the Difficulty switch is kept in setting Difficulty; the chosen side's picture is drawn through light row 0x1F. When a GUI loads (`0x4A81E0`) every switch's attributes become (attributes & 0x4000) | 1 and every button's colour word is cleared; the colour word is the light row the button's art is drawn through. Checked: 0 of 307,200 pixels differ (new, after Core, after Arm, any mission).
- *Briefing* (MSNBRIEF.GUI, `0x478E80`, handler `0x478CB0`): the palette from `bitmaps\mbrief<prefix>.pcx`; the planet from a table of 15 (`anims\<x>brief.GAF`: `<x>Pan` panorama with PanMask, `<x>Rotate`); wind a random walk between the mission's min and max wind; gravity shown as the value × 1/112 with one decimal; text colours per side ({0x35,0x33,0x40,0xD0} / {0x75,0x56,0x52,0xD4}, 0x5E for the blink-off phase); lines broken by `0x4AC4C0` (CR LF), colour spans carried over lines (`0x476CD0`), pages (`0x476EF0`); the MORE / BACK TO START label measured in the small picture font but drawn in the FNT font. Checked: first page 0 of 307,200 pixels with the same rand(); pages 2 and 3 text identical.
- *End of a mission* (end state machine `0x41F7F0`, states 5-7): a won mission shows its glamour picture (`bitmaps\glamour\<glamour>.pcx`, its palette faded in by fifths each tick, `camps\briefs\<glamoursound>.wav`), and "Click to continue." after 1 + 5 s, centred at h - 20, outlined in the side font's colours; the last mission won plays 3.zrb (ARM) or 4.zrb (CORE), then 5.zrb; otherwise the statistics screen (outcome1's palette) with the campaign buttons (`0x41F0A0` / `0x41F400` / `0x41EC50`): Start (the picked mission's briefing), Load Game, Save Game (a between-missions save), the mission list (each name prefixed by 0xFF lost, 0xFE won or 0xFD not played, then a space; the next mission picked after a win), Difficulty. Checked: 0 of 307,200 pixels against gui_harness endrun.
- *Restart* (exit menu, `0x460340`): the same mission from its start at the dialog's difficulty (the mission file read again for that difficulty).
- *Typed words* (the interface pass `0x4A9FD0` keeps the last 15 typed characters, upper-cased): DRDEATH on the single-player menu toggles setting AllMissions (and so the Any Mission button); "*III" … "*X" in the skirmish set-up set the player count.
- *Exit to Windows (2026-10-20, read)*: the main menu's EXIT (`0x425D80`): sound "exit", the pointer look 20 (hourglass, `0x491C80`), front-end state 8, whose next pass fills the back buffer with colour 0 (`0x4C69A0` / `0x4C6890(0, 0)`), presents it and calls `0x4B6230(0)` (display mode back for a full screen, WM_DESTROY posted; the message loop ends, `0x4916A0` saves the CD lists and frees everything, the window goes). The exit menu's question answered Yes (`0x4605C0`): sound "Exit", the music mood 4 (silent, `0x4CE690`), then for "return to main menu" the statistics, the black frame and the menu; for "exit to Windows" end flag 4 and `0x491C60`: the black frame (`0x4257A0`), `0x451B60` (network games: `0x452CC0` for every player this machine runs — its team killed by `0x486F10`; in the battle its record and session player stay — then `0x46C190` closes the session, which destroys this machine's players for the others), the statistics (`0x491B60`) and `0x4B6230(0)`. Another machine handles the session's "player destroyed" (system message 5, a player) for a record of the game (in use, controller 1-3, state ≠ 10) with `0x453010(id, 1)` (below: status 0x1B with reason 1 to everybody, `0x452CC0` by its rules — a remote human's whole machine group) and then `0x463C60(record, 0)` (controller 0); GS+0x2BEE bit 0; the host also refreshes the session description (`0x451090` → `0x4C9890`). In the room (GS+0x2A44 bit 2 clear) a leaving remote host also sends this machine's own player away with 10 ("The creator has left the game"). Corrected 2026-10-20: no 0x1C is sent there (`0x4550D5` is in the 0x1C receiver). `0x426200` ("Close Windows CD Player?") belongs to the CD check, not to the exit.
- *Closing the window*: in a battle the close handler (`0x4609A0`) sets quit intent 2 and opens YESORNO (`0x460680`) "Surrender this battle and exit to Windows?"; in every other state (`0x4578F0`) the program ends (end flag 4; a lobby status code chooses a message).
- *Settings read at start* (`0x42F9A0`): the key is "Anti-Alias" (with the hyphen); display bits of GS+0x37F06: Anti-Alias 2, Shadows 4, VehicleShadows 8, FeatureShadows 0x10, Shading 0x20 (all on by default), DitheredFog 0x40 (off), SwitchAlt 0x100; a Gamma of 10 reads as 12. Command line (`0x49EE30`): -s / /s starts without DirectSound, -w uses wave-out instead.
- *Lit button art* (`0x4B8310`, only while display flag 0x80 is set): a compressed frame's pixel p becomes T[level × 256 + p]; an uncompressed frame instead maps the screen pixel d to T[p × 256 + d] and skips source pixels equal to the level; a frame made of layers (subframe count > 0) is drawn unlit. The battle room's START button uses BUTTONS0's frames (compressed, one layer), so the first rule applies; the level is the interface clock's low five bits (`0x4B6340`: GetTickCount × rate / 1000).
- *Command line* (`0x49EE30`, from WinMain `0x49E830`; words split at space and tab by strtok, so quotes do not group): a word starting '-' or '/' is first offered to the debug library (`0x4DA0E0`: case-insensitive prefixes "-memfussy", "-memnofussy", "-memfrontalign", "-gonzo", "-memset", "-memnoset", "-fpufussy", "-fpunofussy", "-dprinton", "-dprintoff", "-dprintfile", "-memorystatus", "-performancestatus", "-disableimagehlp", "-enableimagehlp", "-disableimagehlplines", "-enableimagehlplines", "-debughelper", "-saveresources"; only with '-', so "/dprinton" is /d), which takes it; else the second character picks the switch (any case). **-d / -df**: 0x51FB48 = 3, or 2 when the third character is 'f'/'F' (the last such word counts); the window set-up keeps bit 0 of the display flags (display +0x202) set unless it is 3 — bit 0 is the full screen: `0x4B5510(1)` takes DirectDraw's exclusive mode at the display mode, `0x4B5510(0)` (only with -d) a GDI DIB section in a popup window at (0, 0) of the screen size, not topmost, and display +0xF0 bit 1 (full screen) clear. So -df is the same as no switch (0x51FB48 has no other reader). With -d: no movies at the start (state 0), INTRO / credits answer "Debug:  You must be in full-screen mode to play a movie.", and the screen-size list is the fixed windowed one (`0x4B5370`: display +0x44, the DIB, set). **A word without '-' / '/'** is copied whole into 0x51FB50 (64 bytes; a longer word runs on into the following globals) — the last one counts — and is the language; when none was given the registry value "language" (`0x42F980`, 64 bytes) is read, and "english" when that is empty. The language then loads `gamedata\translate.tdf` (`0x4C54F0`) and names the localized folders (`Hapi_BuildVirtualPath` `0x4290F0`: "<dir>-<language>\name" first) and unit fields ("<language>name", "<language>description"). The 3.1 data ships German, French, Italian, Spanish (and "piglatin" for some entries) in translate.tdf, `bitmaps-<language>\battleroom.pcx` and `CAMPS\Briefs-<language>\`. Client: ta_play parses both next to the other switches (a first word naming a folder is still the game folder); `client::FrontOptions::windowed_display`, `ta::MenuArt::full_screen`, `ta::Vfs::set_game_language`. Checked: `tools/command_line_check.ps1` (10 cases).
- *Translations* (`0x4C54F0` load, `0x4C5740` lookup; 2026-10-20): the table is reloaded only for a language other than the one loaded (stricmp). Every top-level section of translate.tdf: its name (255-byte buffer) is a base text, compared case-sensitively (byte order, binary search); its field named like the language (field names any case) is the translation; empty translations are skipped, a later section of the same name replaces the earlier one. Lookup: the translation, else the text itself (English has no fields: every text unchanged). **Where it applies** (98 calls): the GUI loader translates every button, text field, slider and label text when the file is read (`0x4AEAC0` / `0x4ADC70`; a label keeps 127 characters, a button 128); the screen set-up (`0x4A81E0`) cuts a staged button's text at '|' and translates each part (the "Off|On" check is made on the loaded text first); setting a gadget's text (`0x4A09C0`, the client's `LayoutScreen::set_caption`) translates it, and a staged button's parts again; the message box (`0x4ABD90`) translates its text. The common-section help text is read and then cleared before its translation (`0x4AD350` zeroes the buffer it just filled), so it is always empty. The other calls are texts written by code: see "Translation call sites" below. Client: `ta::load_translations` / `ta::translate` (`ta_compat/src/translate.cpp`), applied in `parse_gui`, `set_caption` (through `LayoutScreen::caption_translation`, set by ta_play) and the message box. Checked: gui_harness `--language german` (the original's own loader and screens with 0x51FB50 and the table set) against ta_play `german`: main menu, single player and options 0 of 307,200 pixels differ.
- *Translation call sites* (2026-10-20; all 175 calls of `0x4C5740`, get_xrefs_to): a string pushed just before the call is translated, mostly then formatted or handed to a routine that may translate again (`0x4A0BF0` → `0x4A09C0` captions, `0x4ABD90` message boxes: those texts are translated twice). Client: battle texts through `client::tr` (`client/include/client/content.h`, the hook ta_play sets), front-end texts through `ta::translate`. By routine — **GUI system**: `0x4AEC3C` / `0x4AEC99` (loader), `0x4ADCC4` (button), `0x4AE1FE` (slider), `0x4AD4AD` (common help, cleared), `0x4A8E68` (staged parts), `0x4A09F7` / `0x4A0B59` (captions), `0x4ABDC7` (message box; the client's MessageBox and now NetMessage) — done; `0x4A0120` / `0x4AA606` HELPTEXT: the help of the gadget under the pointer, translated when shown — done for the skirmish screen (the only one the client shows help on). **Maps**: `0x434DF9` the map list's names = translate(lower(name)), or the name when that equals the lowered name in any case (`ta::translate_lowered`; MenuArt::skirmish_maps, first_network_map); `0x435AD7` the map info's shown name +0xB14 (non-English: the same rule; skirmish MapName, room MAPNAME, view map); `0x4364B5` the description (+0xC24, the same rule; map choice); the map info opens Maps\<name>.ota through Hapi_BuildVirtualPath and, when that fails, the base text whose translation equals the name in any case (`0x4C5840`, `ta::untranslate` / `ta::map_file_name`), which then becomes +0xA14 (battle loader, sync checksum, map size, previews, battle_name). **Loading screen** `0x497F40`: "%s: %s" of translate("Map") and translate(+0xA14, 100 characters, lowered first in a language other than English — an untranslated name shows in lower case), the six labels, `0x497D3A` "Synchronization complete", `0x497EE5` / `0x497EF1` "%s.  %i %s" — done (client_loading.cpp). **Game settings page** `0x45F1D0` (22 calls: every name and value, the map's +0xA14 too, numbers not) and its help page `0x45F8C0` (key and text) — done. **Exit / game menu**: `0x46092E` "Restart", `0x460D92` "Settings" (then captions) — done. **Player names** `0x464290`: "Player" always; "Arm" / "Core" only in skirmishes (map info mode 2) — done (`start_player_name`). **Battle**: `0x4689C0` (Game Time, Total Units, Normal, Game Speed), `0x46A246` (clock), `0x46A860` (kills / kill / Veteran, status line, Weapon, feature description both branches, "Unidentified object" printed "%s%s" after "S: " for unit flag 0x200 else "R: "), `0x47F7C5` / `0x47F89E` (a notice's text when queued), `0x486C4B` (leader line format), `0x490E2D` / `0x490E6D` (speed lines), `0x4948E0` (Kills, Losses), `0x4942E0` (eight unit info headings), `0x489280` (m/s, m/s/s, deg/s, N/A), `0x41FF1A` ("Click to continue.") — done. **Skirmish** `0x4797E0` / `0x47A0E0` (Player / Computer / Open, then captions), `0x479C50` (three help texts), `0x47A0E0` / `0x47B88B` (option help texts), `0x47B0CB` (both refusals: "at least one player and one computer", "same allied group") — done; `0x47B008` "too many players enabled for this map" and `0x47AB10` "no skirmish maps" — done (see "Texts not shown before" below). **Campaign** `0x47710B` / `0x47712C` (BACK TO START, MORE..., then the caption), `0x47888A` / `0x47890A` (Wind Speed, Gravity), `0x49233F` / `0x492AED` / `0x49323A` (saved-game messages) — done. **Multiplayer**: `0x440F0D`, `0x440F98`, `0x443B0E` (name messages), `0x443F6D` (rejection reasons `0x452C40`), `0x444A85` ("%s  %s: %s" Players), `0x446150` ("%s %s?" Reject), `0x44725E` / `0x448020` (" %s %s" allied with / broke alliance with), `0x448321`, `0x4488AF`, `0x448A42`, `0x448F6C` (does not have this map), `0x4492BD` ("[%s]" BLOCKED; UNUSED not translated), `0x44BC80`, `0x44BECA` (No Limit), `0x44CA9D` (restriction row: the side), `0x45386A` (timeout format), `0x454FD6` (disconnect format), `0x451514` (add-player failure), the game list `0x441460` (`0x44172F` map name lowered + translated in other languages, `0x4417D3` / `0x4417F2` status, `0x441924` / `0x441964` / `0x4419A3` columns) — done. Formerly not shown, now built (2026-10-20, "Texts not shown before" below): `0x41F8F8`, `0x44152C`, `0x443EA2`, `0x444EDD` / `0x444F60`, `0x45530C`, `0x465803`, `0x4504A3` / `0x47BDAC`, `0x42B310`. N/A: CD prompts (`0x41EF47`, `0x41F6D2`, `0x425E92`, `0x425F33`, `0x42612A`, `0x4603AA`, `0x4603F2`, `0x477651`, `0x477764`, `0x477B94`, `0x478D4F`, `0x47AF06`, `0x4924F6`, `0x492545`), the Windows CD player question (`0x4262E7`, `0x42631A`, `0x42634F`), DirectX / sound driver / Revision.GPF warnings (`0x4266C2`, `0x426731`, `0x429098`, `0x4290C2`), online.dll button actions (`0x45B368`, `0x45B464`, `0x45B485`, `0x45B605`), DirectPlay lobby / registration / scores / service (`0x451742`, `0x4519E1`, `0x49F3EA`, `0x443776`, `0x4443D6`, `0x4514DF` the re-create variant, `0x443052`). **Other language uses** (0x51FB50 through `0x49F580`): `0x435430` mission files "%s-%s\%s" (dir-language\name, extension replaced) when it opens, else "%s\%s": Maps (TNT), camps\briefs (brief TXT, narration WAV, glamour sound WAV), camps\hints, camps\useonly (TDF) — client: Vfs::virtual_path in campaign.cpp / client_campaign.cpp / battle_loader.cpp; `Tdf_GetStringLocalized` `0x4C58A0` ("<language><key>" first, the table's language 0x51FDC0): campaign missionname (`0x435864`, `0x435F48`), brief, narration, missionhint (`0x4361F5` …) and the unit fields — client: campaign.cpp get_localized; `0x4777A0`: in Spanish SINGLE's "Skirmish" gets quick key 's' (`0x4A1530`); `0x4288D0` / `0x429290` bitmaps-<language> (only battleroom.pcx ships localized). The German battle room's "Kanalüberquerung": translate.tdf's key is "[canal crossing]" (lower case); the room shows +0xB14 = translate(lower("Canal Crossing")). Checked German with gui_harness `--language german`: battle room 87 pixels (the English caret / underline class), loading screen 0 (also "dumbbell", French Lava Run), game list 24 (quick-key underline colour, as English), message box text identical.
- *Texts not shown before* (2026-10-20). **Skirmish Start** (`0x47AE60`): "BigButton", archives rescanned, GS+0x2A3C = computer players + 1, the map info loaded with it (`0x435A20` → `0x435DA0` → schema `0x436860`, start positions `0x436C30`: the schema's specials named StartPos*). Refusals in this order, each `0x4ABD90(text translated, 0x1E0 = 480, OK, fitted)`: map info null "The terrain for the selected map does not exist."; no human or no computer "There must be at least one player and one computer opponent"; start positions (`0x437300`, entries of type 1) < GS+0x2A3C "There are too many players enabled for this map" (`0x47B008`); one allied group (`0x479760`). The client wrapped these at 320 (two lines where the original has one): fixed (`ta::map_start_position_count`, `show_message(..., width)`). **SelectMap** (`0x47AAF0`, "Skirmish" sound first): no map listed (`0x434BF0` = 0) → "There are no skirmish maps to choose from" (320, OK, fitted) instead of SELMAP. **Room MAP** (`0x444EA0`): the map info's name +0xA14 empty (`0x435C40`) or no map listed → "There are no multiplayer maps to choose from" (320). **Game list** (`0x441460`, from `0x443CB0` and UPDATE in `0x4437C0`): provider GUID TCP/IP ({36E95EE0-8577-11CF-960C-0080C7534E82}) → "Updating...", any other → "Connecting  (ESC to abort)"; `0x4ABD90(text, 0x96 = 150, OK 0 → hidden (0x4A0570), fitted)` (the box keeps the OK button's room), composited and presented once, then `0x4C9E50` enumerates (repeats while DPERR_CONNECTING 0x8877015E; Escape from the key queue → −1; any error → −1), the box closed (`0x4A9660`). Failure in `0x443CB0`: SELGAME closed, "Invalid TCP/IP Address" (200, OK, fitted, translated twice) whatever the provider, GS+0x2BC0 = 3: next pass sub-state 3 closes the service (`0x450DD0`) and goes to state 0x0F, whose next pass opens SELPROV (`0x444580`, flags 0x80) **over** the box: the box is seen in two passes, then lies under the connection screen (`0x4AB0B0` composites bottom-up only dirty layouts; a new layout always goes on top, `0x4AA8F0`). UPDATE ignores the failure (rows unchanged). Client: `FindGamesBox` (box drawn one pass before the session is asked; pumped per pass; Escape gives up), `show_invalid_address` (closes itself after two drawn passes). **Message box lines** (`0x4AC4C0`): a break writes "\r\n" in place of the space or hyphen; `0x4ABD90` cuts at "\n" with strtok (no empty lines), so a broken line keeps '\r', which `0x4A5030` counts (the hattfont12 frame 13) in the fitted width and the label's centring; client: `wrap_for_width(..., cr)`, `Font::text_width` counts control characters' frame widths (500-wide watch box 0 of 57,007 box pixels differ; was 12 pixels narrower). **Out of units** (`0x486C00`, the player record +0x144 reaching 0): skirmish `0x47BD70`: "%s %s" of "Core" (shared record +0x95 ≠ 0) / "Arm" and translate(table 0x507B88[rand() % 3]: "forces have been obliterated", "vermin have been exterminated", "forces have gone to a better place"); network `0x450380` (skipped while the local record +0x22 = 1): the record's name +0x2B and translate(table 0x5061B8[rand() & 7]: has left the scene / has been shown the door / has gone to a better place / has bowed out / has terminated / has been eradicated / has been liquidated / has been obliterated); both `0x463CA0(text, 4, 0, record +0x146)`. Client: `BattleOutside::player_out_notice` (the battle's crt_rand value) → `GameClient::on_player_out`. **Network end** (`0x41F7F0` case 0, map kind 3): the last frame copied; local record +0x22 not 0 or 2 → `0x4ABD90(translate(0x452C40(reason)), 320, OK, fitted)`, +0x22 cleared; case 1 waits for MSGBOX to close, each pass putting the copy back and the console (`0x464060`) under the GUI (so not dimmed). Client: `BattleOver::set_leaving_reason`. **Watch mode** (`0x464F80`, local player out, network, +0x22 = 0, watch bit 0x80 or computer players (`0x457BC0`)): with no computer player fighting, YESORNO.GUI flags 0x900, TITLE "You're out!  Continue Watching?", CHOICE1 Yes / CHOICE2 No (`0x4A0BF0` captions), handler `0x464DE0`: BigButton; Yes → `0x450F90`, end flags &= ~0x10; No → end flags |= 4, &= ~0x10. Else with a human still fighting (`0x457CB0`: controller 1 or remote human, state ≠ 10, units or none made, watch bit 0x40 clear) the box (`0x465803`, 500, OK, fitted) and end flags &= ~0x10. Client: `VictoryState::watch_prompt` → hook `watch_dialog` (`WatchQuestion`, `MessageBox::over_battle`: side panel dimmed). **Packet 0x27** (`0x453D40`, `0x45530C`): the named record's name and translate("has modified his executable.  Game integrity breached."), 12 console lines of kind 8 with the record's +0x146; TotalA.exe never sends 0x27 (the only byte store of 0x27 is in the key routine `0x4C1D50`) — client: NetBattle's `on_modified_executable` now shows them. **Incompatible units** (`0x42A8D0` end, called by `0x42BD10` at the top of every front-end pass `0x496BB0` when no table or GS+0x14397 set — which `GameData_LoadAll` sets: so at the first pass and after each battle): definitions without the version bit (Version above 3.1, a wrong Copyright line, a loose file under the CD switch 0x50289C, GS 0x511DE4) are dropped; when some were and none for the copyright / loose / 0x511DE4 reasons, `MessageBoxA(NULL, translate(text), "Error", MB_TOPMOST)` (`0x4B6B80`). Client: hook `enumerate_units` (first pass, first pass after a battle) → `unit_files_incompatible_notice` → `FrontEnd::system_message` (SDL_ShowSimpleMessageBox, no icon; headless printed). **Mission hint**: `0x435DA0` resolves `camps\hints\<missionhint>` (localized key, `0x4C58A0`; path kind 4 at map info +0x504) but nothing reads it: the path getter `0x4356C0` is called with kinds 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 only, and no instruction addresses +0x504 — the original never shows a mission hint (N/A). **HELPTEXT** (`0x4A0090`, `0x4AA606` in the GUI pass): when the gadget under the pointer changes, a layout with a HELPTEXT gadget gets translate(that gadget's help +0x33) and a redraw. The loader clears every gadget's help after reading it (`0x4AD350`); only the skirmish code writes +0x33 (`0x47A0E0`, `0x47AE60`). The GUIs with HELPTEXT are LOUNGE, LOUNGE2, SELGAME and SKIRMISH (all 188 guis\*.gui scanned), all with empty text: elsewhere the line stays empty — the client's empty line is already 1:1 (N/A). **Bottom bar** "R: " / "S: " Unidentified object (`0x46B6F3`, unit +0x110 bit 9, when `0x465AC0` says unseen): frame_harness FH_UNSEEN, 8 scenes (4 radar, 4 sonar), bottom bar 0 of 16,352 differing each.
- *Start glow compared* (2026-10-20; FRONTEND 11.1): `ta_compat/tests/fixtures/client/room_start_glow.txt` (everybody ready, the clock from 1725 in steps of 5) with gui_harness `stub:46E000:1:0 clock:T lounge:FILE` then `clock:T' frame shot:kN` per frame, against ta_play `--net-screen lounge:FILE`: the grill's opening stage by stage and START drawn through light rows 2, 7, 12, 17, 22, 27, 0 and 5: no difference in the grill and START (harness kN = our frame N − 1: its "frame" runs the interface pass before the room's state). What made earlier comparisons differ was the method: a room dump's "focus START" puts the focus ring around START (ta_play's room reader takes no focus line), the harness's interface clock ran free without `clock:` (another light row), without the stub 0x46E000 answers 0 and SYNCHING replaces START, and `loungeframe` + `shot:` does not redraw the grill. Left (not the glow): in that room ours draws the chat line's caret and the labels' quick-key underlines (89 pixels), the original neither — for the room's keys / focus owners.
- *SELVMODE.GUI never opens* (2026-10-20): the only reference to "SELVMODE.GUI" (0x506A78) is the `param_1 != 0` branch of the visuals page routine `0x45E5E0`, and its three callers all pass 0: `0x45E325` and `0x45E42D` in the visuals handler `0x45E100` (`push 0`) and `0x45FD17` in the options handler `0x45FC60` (`push edi`, EDI cleared at `0x45FC66`); nothing takes its address. The handler's "OK on selvmode.gui" test (`0x45E448`) is therefore dead too, and the 3.1 archives hold no SELVMODE.GUI. The screen size is chosen on the visuals page's VIDSLDR slider only.
- *Developer mode* (GS+0x37F2F bit 1, value 2; 2026-10-20): set at start only when the registry holds DisplaymodeDepth = 256 **and** Games = 1 (`0x42F9A0`; the writer `0x430F00` writes neither value, so only a hand-edited registry has them), or by the console's password command (`0x416E90`). It enables debugging only: battle keys 0x7E (`0x499890`: `0x4B5910`), '\\' re-running the last typed line, 0xD7 numbered movie-frame dumps, 0xEC toggling GS+0x3923B bit 1 (`0x495E90`), and the console command `0x417A60` (endless allocations / forced out-of-memory / a division by zero / DebugBreak). Nothing a player sees otherwise.



**Open:** the progress string's format, AiIgnore / AiPriorityTarget / InitialGroup / BuildPriority / CreationCountdown / MissionCriticalUnit (read but not used by anything built), killmul / timemul (score).

### Saved games — the bank container smoke-verified (bank_harness: 24 scenarios identical); exact save and load smoke-tested (save_check: 8 battles and 4 mission battles, every step after the load identical)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/journal.cpp` (`sim/journal.h`), `ta_compat/src/saved_game.cpp` (`ta/saved_game.h`), screens in `ta_compat/src/client_campaign.cpp`; tool `save_check`.
**The original's container** (`HapiBank_WriteFile` `0x4B39C0`, `HapiBank_WriteAccount` `0x4B3C60`, reader `HapiBank_OpenBank` `0x4B3770` / `HapiBank_LoadAccount` `0x4B4270`): header 0x22 bytes: "HAPIBANK", u32 offset of the bank name in the string directory (0), u32 file offset of the directory, u32 offset of the first account (0x22), u32 version (1), u8 directory squashed, nine zero bytes. Accounts follow back to back until the directory: header 8 × u32 (total size including the header, section name offset, counts of integer, double, string and blob entries, 1 = squashed, 0); entries: integers {name, value}, doubles {name, 8 bytes}, strings {name, value offset}; blob descriptors {name offset or −1, index, absolute file offset of the bytes as if stored, size} (only blobs with bytes); then the blob bytes. **A named blob's descriptor keeps the previous numbered blob's index in that account, else a stale stack word** (the engine writes 0 and reports the offset). Names and string values go into one directory in writing order: the bank name, then per account its name, the integer names, the double names, string name/value pairs, blob names. A squashed account stores one SQSH chunk of the entries and bytes (size = chunk + 0x20); the directory is squashed when that is smaller. Reading: entries are applied in that order; a blob's bytes are taken at (offset − account start − 0x20) of the (unsquashed) entries and appended to the blob of that name / index. Keys and sections are found case-insensitively. Saved games use the bank name "Total Annihilation 3.0".
**The original's saved game** (`GameSave_SaveAll` `0x4326B0`): section Summary: integer keys "BUILD DATE: <date>" and "BUILD TIME: <time>" (0; the executable's stamps, assumed "Jul 30 1998" / "11:16:36"), maxunits, Difficulty, Side, Players, Gametype, for skirmish CommanderDeath, Location, Mapping, LineOfSight, LineOfSightType; BetweenMissions (campaign saves between missions); "Game ID", "Game Time"; strings Campaign, Mission, Map, Thumbs, Description. During a battle also: the radar image, camera, Players (per player stored amounts and statistics, a 0x1C "GameTime" blob), Units (per unit a 0xB8 record: type name, id, position, facing, hit points, build state, orders count, flags, weapon slots …; its script — thread slots, statics, pieces —, its orders, its motion's velocity/speed/turn/flags but not its route goal, its resource slot), Mapping (explored map), Features, player features, metal plot map, Meteor, the victory conditions (Satisfied/Celebrated and counts per goal). **Not saved:** both random generators, projectiles, effects and particles, debris, wind, the route search, motion goals, the computer players' state, and more. Loading (`GameSave_LoadAll` `0x432610` from `0x497180`, which re-seeds both generators) re-creates every unit through unit creation and overwrites parts of it; the game does **not** continue as it would have — so the original's format cannot restore an exact state.
**The engine's saves** use the same container with the original's Summary keys (so a listing reads them like the original's) and three sections of its own: "Engine Start" (what the game was started from: the battle request — map, schema, sides, players, options, seeds — or the campaign mission and difficulty), "Engine Journal" (blob "Journal": the battle's input journal) and "Engine Check" (tick, `state_checksum`, both generators at the save).
**Journal** (`sim::Journal`): every input a running battle takes after its start, in order — commands with the step they are due before and the number of frame tails run before them, the frame tails (`Battle::end_frame`, which changes state) with the tick at each, the clock values the hover bob reads. `JournalRecorder` wraps `command::apply`, `Battle::step` and `end_frame`; `JournalPlayer::run_to` replays onto a battle made from the same start, interleaving commands and frame tails as recorded, then `apply_rest` the commands given after the save's last step. Loading = start the game again + replay to the save's step + check checksum and both generators. This restores everything (scripts' threads and locals, orders, motion, route search, computer players, features, effects, both generators) by construction; the cost is the replay time (smoke: the four 1,500-step Moon Quartet runs, both games each, took about 4 s in all). **Requirement for the client:** every input must reach the battle as a `sim::command::Command` through a recorder, with each frame's `end_frame`; the current client gives orders and selections directly, so its sessions cannot be saved exactly yet (`ta::JournaledSession`; the save screen says so).
**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/bank_harness` (random stores built in the original's store, `0x4B3750`/`0x4B4560`/`0x4B4630`/`0x4B46C0`/`0x4B4750`/`0x4B4BA0`/`0x4B4B50`/`0x4B4CF0`, written stored and squashed by `0x4B39C0`; the runtime's start-up run first, its fatal-error paths made to fault): **smoke: 24 scenarios, 270 keys, 98 blobs: header and accounts byte for byte identical with `ta::write_bank` (60,173 bytes, stale words excepted), both files read back by `ta::read_bank` identical to the content (18 squashed directories, every squashed account).** `save_check` (save at N, continue to M; load at N, continue to M; per step `state_checksum`, both generators and a deeper hash of scripts' thread memory, statics and piece animations, motion, economy, players): **smoke: Moon Quartet 4 × (900 → 1,500) and 4 × (600 → 1,200), AC03 2 × (300 → 600), CC02 2 × (300 → 600): every step identical.**
**Open:** restoring a game from the original's own sections (their layouts are read above, not the unit record in detail); writing the original's own sections from an engine battle; squashing on write; the client's input journal (see the requirement above); the original's save file names and directory.
